# WhoAdvice site audit - findings register - 2026-09-25

Companion to [[site-audit-2026-09-25]]. Every finding here survived an adversarial check: a second agent re-read the cited evidence and tried to disprove it. Where the checker corrected a detail, the correction follows the fix and overrides the finding's wording.

This register holds 366 of the audit's 374 findings. The other 8 describe live security holes, so they are kept in `audits/private/`, which git ignores, until they are fixed.

| Area | Critical | High | Medium | Low |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [[#Trust and site-level pages]] | 5 | 7 | 15 | 6 |

Severity: critical blocks traffic, revenue or trust, or is factually wrong to readers. High clearly costs rankings, conversions or readability. Medium is real but bounded. Low is polish.

## Trust and site-level pages

### TRUST-01 - The code gives every legacy article an 'Expert Tested' badge by default, and the CMS field help text suggests it too
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ArticleHero.tsx lines 29-45: if editorial_badge is empty and article_format is not 'editorial', getEditorialBadge returns `Expert Tested • ${year}`, with the year taken from publishedAt. Live: face-epilators.html shows 'Expert Tested • 2024' and bug-zapper.html shows 'Expert Tested • 2024'. The Sanity field help text primes editors with the same phrase: sanity/schemas/article/index.ts line 55 and sanity/schemas/post/index.ts line 51 read 'Shown above the post title in the hero, e.g. Expert Tested • 2026'. The vault says the opposite: reference/01-brand-and-audience.md line 34 ('does not claim ... to have personally used a product unless genuine first-hand testing notes are supplied'), 04 section 1 (do not use 'We tested'), 05 section 4 ('Without testing documentation, these are factual fabrications').
- **Why it matters:** This is a false claim of hands-on testing, placed on the most visible label of every legacy roundup. Rater guidelines and the vault's own trust contract both treat invented testing as deception. The claim is applied automatically, so no editor ever decides to make it.
- **Fix:** In ArticleHero.tsx, change the fallback so it never asserts testing: `return content.article_format === 'editorial' ? `Editorial Guide • ${year}` : 'Research-Based Buying Guide';` (this is the label the vault handoff uses in articles/hedge-trimmers/best-hedge-trimmers/publisher-handoff.md line 21). Change the Sanity help text in both schemas to 'e.g. Research-Based Buying Guide. Never use Tested/Expert Tested unless the package has hands-on testing notes (reference/06 section 1C).' Add a Sanity validation rule on editorial_badge that fails when the value matches /test(ed|ing)/i unless a new boolean field hands_on_testing_documented is true. Then run a GROQ query for posts where editorial_badge is empty and republish them.
- **Checker's correction:** The post schema's help text is on line 56 of sanity/schemas/post/index.ts, not line 51.

### TRUST-02 - A hard-coded '{year} TOP RATED' award sticker appears on every product-guide hero, but no rating system exists
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ArticleHero.tsx lines 168-177 render `{year} TOP RATED` with a filled Star icon whenever article_format is not 'editorial'. The year comes from publishedAt (line 53). Live: epilators.html '2026 TOP RATED', hedge-trimmers.html '2026 TOP RATED', face-epilators.html and bug-zapper.html '2024 TOP RATED'. sanity/schemas/product.ts and fragments/product-editorial-fields.ts contain no rating, score or award field, so there is nothing for 'rated' to refer to. The same wording appears in the epilators meta description ('Compare top-rated epilators'). The vault forbids this: reference/01 line 158 (never 'Invent an ... award'), 08 'Popularity' (allowed only with dated data).
- **Why it matters:** It reads as a third-party or editorial award that nobody gave. On the hedge-trimmer page it sits beside the vault-approved 'Research-Based Buying Guide' badge, so the page contradicts itself. On 2024 pages it also tells readers the guide is two years old.
- **Fix:** Delete the TOP RATED block (ArticleHero.tsx lines 168-177) and the unused Star import. If an overlay is wanted, make it an optional per-article CMS string (e.g. hero_overlay_label) with the same /rated|test/i validation, and leave it empty by default. Replace 'top-rated' in the epilators seo.meta_description with 'Compare seven epilators for legs, underarms and bikini line by head type, wet/dry use and comfort features.' Also sweep other meta descriptions for 'top-rated'.

### TRUST-03 - The sitewide footer tells readers 'We test' and cites a 'testing methodology' that does not exist
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The footer on every saved page (home.html, about.html, epilators.html, hedge-trimmers.html) has the tagline 'Expert advice for the discerning buyer. We test, review, and curate the best products so you can shop with confidence and clarity.' and the legal line 'Our testing methodology remains independent and unbiased.' The tagline comes from the Sanity siteLayout.footer.brandTagline field (sanity/schemas/siteLayout/siteFooter.ts line 9); the legal line comes from siteLayout.legalContent. No string in src/ or sanity/ matches, so both live only in CMS data. The hedge-trimmer article itself says 'WhoAdvice created this research-based guide by comparing exact models, manufacturer specifications and manuals...'.
- **Why it matters:** A testing claim on all 223 URLs conflicts with the vault's evidence model (01 line 34, 04 section 1, 05 section 4). It also contradicts the one article that follows the rules. A rater comparing footer and body sees an inconsistency that undermines every page.
- **Fix:** In Sanity Studio, siteLayout, Footer tab, set brandTagline to: 'Research-led buying guides. We compare exact models using manufacturer documentation, safety and recall records, and recurring owner feedback. We do not run a test lab.' Replace legalContent with: 'WhoAdvice is reader-supported. When you buy through links on our site, we may earn a commission. As an Amazon Associate, WhoAdvice earns from qualifying purchases. Commissions never decide which products we include or how we rank them. How we research: /how-we-research/.' Remove the duplicate 'Expert advice for the discerning buyer.' from the copyright line.
- **Checker's correction:** The same legalContent paragraph already carries a general sitewide affiliate disclosure ('WhoAdvice is a participant in various affiliate marketing programs...'). The fix should keep that disclosure and change only the testing sentence, adding the Amazon statement. I verified the footer on 8 pages, not all 223 URLs, but it comes from the shared layout.

### TRUST-04 - No page on the site carries Amazon's required Associate statement; the publishing pipeline drops the vault's approved disclosure
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** `grep -ic 'amazon associate'` returns 0 in every saved live page (home, about, category, author, epilators, face-epilators, hedge-trimmers, bug-zapper). The article disclosure is hard-coded in src/components/Post/AffiliateDisclosure.tsx lines 9-12: 'WhoAdvice is reader-supported. We may earn a commission when you buy through our links.' It has no Amazon sentence. The vault packages require one: articles/hedge-trimmers/best-hedge-trimmers/article.md line 14 ('As an Amazon Associate, WhoAdvice earns from qualifying purchases...'); its publisher-handoff.md line 149 says 'preserve the article-level Amazon Associate disclosure', yet the published hedge-trimmer page has 0 matches. The epilators package article.md line 10 has the same sentence. The vault rule is reference/08 section 5. All 18 epilator CTAs are Amazon links.
- **Why it matters:** The Amazon Associates Operating Agreement requires the Associate identification statement to appear clearly on the site. Leaving it out risks the account, which is the site's only revenue (AdSense is commented out). It is also the one disclosure the vault explicitly mandates.
- **Fix:** Change the AffiliateDisclosure.tsx text to: '<strong>WhoAdvice is reader-supported.</strong> We may earn a commission when you buy through links on this page. As an Amazon Associate, WhoAdvice earns from qualifying purchases. Product selection and ranking remain editorial decisions. <a href="/how-we-research/#affiliate-disclosure">How we make money</a>.' Render it as JSX with a Link instead of dangerouslySetInnerHTML. Add the same Amazon sentence to the sitewide footer legalContent (see TRUST-03). Better still, add an optional per-article `affiliate_disclosure` text field that defaults to this copy, so the package's own wording survives publishing.
- **Checker's correction:** A general affiliate disclosure does exist sitewide in the footer legalContent ('participant in various affiliate marketing programs'). What is missing is Amazon's own Associate identification statement, on every page, while the vault mandates it at article level.

### TRUST-20 - Legacy pages still show health claims the vault bans ('100% of dermatologists agree', 'Dermatologist Recommended', 'painless')
**critical** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** face-epilators.html body text: '100% of dermatologists agree that the product is gentle and safe, making it suitable for all skin types.' (twice), 'Dermatologist Recommended' (twice, as a feature label), 'Painless Precision', 'Gentle enough for daily use, the Duo causes no nicks, burns, or irritation while providing a painless experience.', plus Review JSON-LD positiveNotes 'Dermatologist recommended for safety and gentleness.' and 'Fast and painless hair removal.' The epilators FAQ says 'A true epilator is completely painless because it removes hair from the root.' Vault rules: 08 'Dermatologist tested' and 'Pain-free' sections, 05 section 14 (ban 'Dermatologists recommend' unless the study is identified), 04 section 7 (attribute manufacturer claims), 04 section 9 (never guarantee, never minimise a reaction).
- **Why it matters:** Unattributed medical-sounding claims ('suitable for all skin types', 'no ... burns, or irritation') are factually unsupportable for readers, and the epilator one is flatly false. They sit on pages that carry an 'Expert Tested' badge, which compounds the harm.
- **Fix:** Unpublish or noindex /skin-care/best-face-epilators/ until it is rebuilt through the vault workflow (the vault audit already excludes the FaceSpa 911 as stale). Meanwhile edit the Sanity doc: replace the dermatologist sentence with 'The brand says the device was dermatologist-tested; we could not find the study or its details.' Or delete it. Remove 'Dermatologist Recommended' and 'Painless' labels and the 'no nicks, burns, or irritation' clause. Epilators FAQ answer: 'No epilator is painless, because it pulls hair from the root. Wet use, a massage cap, a slower speed setting and regular sessions make it more tolerable for many users.' Add a vault/Studio check that fails on /dermatologist|painless|100%/i without a source.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity raised to critical. Under this audit's rubric, an unsourced '100% of dermatologists agree... suitable for all skin types' and a flatly false 'completely painless' are factually wrong statements to readers on health-adjacent pages.

### TRUST-05 - Every product card says 'Why we love it', which asserts first-hand affection the evidence model forbids
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/product-card/ProsCons.tsx line 77: `const heading = isPro ? "Why we love it" : "Points to consider";`. Live: 7 times on epilators, 5 on hedge-trimmers (a vault-approved research-based article), 5 on face-epilators, 6 on bug-zapper. The vault bans 'Our team loved' and 'In our experience' as fake hands-on authority (reference/05 section 4). 04 section 1 limits voice to 'We researched / We compared'.
- **Why it matters:** The component overwrites compliant article copy with an experiential claim, so even a fully audited article ships a banned phrase. The heading is also an H3, so the claim is repeated in the document outline for search engines.
- **Fix:** Change ProsCons.tsx line 77 to `isPro ? "Strengths" : "Drawbacks"` (or 'What stands out' / 'What to consider'). If a per-article override is wanted, add optional pros_heading/cons_heading fields to productEditorialFields and default to these neutral labels.

### TRUST-06 - 'Updated' dates come from Sanity's system _updatedAt, so any edit makes a page look freshly reviewed
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The hero 'Updated {formatMonthYear(post._updatedAt)}' is in ArticleHero.tsx line 118. JSON-LD `dateModified: post._updatedAt` is in src/lib/schema/article.ts line 38. The product-card date `Updated: {formatMonthYear(product._updatedAt)}` is in ProductReviewCard.tsx lines 89-93. The category date `Updated {formatShortDate(details._updatedAt)}` is in CategoryHeader.tsx line 71. Neither the article nor the post schema has a reviewed-date field. Live effect: epilators hero 'Updated Aug 2026' (dateModified 2026-08-06), but the vault audit of 2026-07-20 found the live product set stale and it was never replaced. The vault says 'Do not change the publication date merely to make old content appear new' (reference/12 Part 13) and 'On update, do not simply change the publication date. Reverify the evidence packet and ranking' (06 section 17).
- **Why it matters:** The page claims a freshness it has not earned: fixing a typo or swapping an image changes the visible review date and dateModified. Readers and Google both treat 'Updated' as 'the recommendations were rechecked'.
- **Fix:** Add a `last_reviewed_at` date field (required on publish) to sanity/schemas/article/index.ts and post/index.ts. Its help text should read 'Set only when the evidence packet, prices/availability and ranking were rechecked'. Render 'Last reviewed {Mon YYYY}' from it in ArticleHero, and use it for dateModified in src/lib/schema/article.ts (fall back to publishedAt, never _updatedAt). Backfill each existing doc's last_reviewed_at with its publishedAt, except articles republished from a vault package, which should use the package's audit date.
- **Checker's correction:** Add the 2025-08-22 bulk dateModified on the legacy skincare guides as direct evidence that system edit time is being published as freshness.

### TRUST-08 - Homepage positioning claims 'Expert Product Reviews' and 'Regularly reviewed guides' that the content cannot support
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/config/site-info.ts line 6 site_title 'Expert Product Reviews & Buying Guides | WhoAdvice'. The homepage H1 'Expert Product Reviews That Help You Choose Better' and trust badges 'Independent product research', 'Clear pros & cons', 'Regularly reviewed guides' come from Sanity homePage.hero.title / trustBadges (sanity/schemas/homePage/heroSection.ts, trustBadge.ts). The categories intro says 'Browse expert-researched reviews'. src/config/home-sections.ts line 16 says 'Latest Expert Reviews'. Against that: no expert is named anywhere; the author JSON-LD shows skincare pages last modified in 2024 (best-callus-remover 2024-08-21, best-face-epilators 2024-06-02, best-led-face-mask 2024-04-24, best-laser-hair-removal-2024 2024-08-07); 9 of 13 skincare guides have 2023-2024 dates on the category page. The vault bans vague authority without an identified expert (05 section 14).
- **Why it matters:** These are the first claims a rater checks, and the site's own dates refute 'regularly reviewed'. Unsupported expertise claims on a YMYL-adjacent site (skin devices, IPL, laser) lower the page-quality rating for the whole domain.
- **Fix:** site_title: 'Research-Led Product Guides & Comparisons | WhoAdvice'. Hero H1: 'Research-led buying guides that show their evidence'. Hero description: 'We compare exact models using manufacturer manuals, published specifications, recall records and recurring owner feedback, then explain who each pick suits and what it gets wrong. We earn commissions from some links; they never set the ranking.' Trust badges: 'Exact-model research', 'Drawbacks stated plainly', 'Sources on every guide'. Add 'Regularly reviewed' back only when last_reviewed_at (TRUST-06) is under 12 months on most guides. home-sections.ts line 16: 'Editor's Picks: Latest Buying Guides - WhoAdvice'.
- **Checker's correction:** 'Latest Expert Reviews' (home-sections.ts line 16) does not appear in home.html. It is the fallback title for the Editor's Pick page in EditorPick.tsx line 65, not visible homepage copy. The rest of the evidence stands.

### TRUST-09 - The About page is generic boilerplate: no owner, no people, no method, no money disclosure, and 'our team of experts' is unsupported
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/mdx/about.mdx lines 1-14 as rendered on about.html: 'your go-to source for insightful reviews and valuable tips', 'we dive deep into the world of culinary tools', 'elevate your kitchen game', 'our team of experts is here to guide you on your journey to self-care and confidence'. It names only four categories, not garden tools or home gadgets. The only ownership statement is commented out (lines 3-5, an MDX comment naming the owner and an email), so it never renders. about.html carries no JSON-LD (0 @type matches) and metadata robots includes 'nocache'. The vault bans empty importance statements and vague authority (05 sections 2 and 14).
- **Why it matters:** Raters look for 'who is responsible for this site and how do they make money' first on About. At the moment it answers neither, and it claims experts who are never named. That fails the E-E-A-T 'Trust' check on a site that makes skin-device recommendations.
- **Fix:** Rewrite src/mdx/about.mdx with these sections. (1) 'Who runs WhoAdvice': the real publisher name (person or registered business), country, and editor-in-chief with a link to their author page. (2) 'How we research': 'WhoAdvice is a research-led buying guide. We do not run a test lab and we do not say we tested a product unless we did. For each guide we compare exact models using manufacturer manuals and specifications, regulator and recall records, and patterns in recent owner reviews. Every guide lists its sources.' (3) 'How we make money': the affiliate text from TRUST-04. (4) 'Corrections': 'Found an error? Email corrections@whoadvice.com. We fix material errors and note the change and date on the page.' (5) 'Contact'. Cover all seven categories. Add Organization/AboutPage JSON-LD (name, url, logo at /images/logo-light.png, email, sameAs).

### TRUST-10 - No methodology, editorial-policy or corrections page exists, and the disclosure link that was meant to point there is dead code
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** sitemap-urls.txt lists only /about/, /contact/, /privacy/, /terms/, /cookies/ plus 7 author pages; there is no /how-we-research/, /editorial-policy/ or /corrections/. src/config/site-info.ts line 10 `editorial_disclosure_url: process.env.EDITORIAL_DISCLOSURE_URL ?? "#"`. AffiliateDisclosure.tsx line 7 computes disclosureUrl and imports Link (line 1) but never renders a link. The vault requires 'Transparent research methodology', 'Correction process' and 'Clear contact or editorial-policy access' (reference/12 Part 13), plus a written correction procedure (04 section 16, 07 section 18).
- **Why it matters:** The vault has a serious method: evidence ladder, two-source rule, exact-model rule. Readers and raters never see any of it. A public methodology page is the cheapest way to turn that internal rigour into visible trust, and it is where the 'how we rank' and 'corrections' promises should live.
- **Fix:** Create src/mdx/how-we-research.mdx plus a route at src/app/(frontend)/(marketing)/how-we-research/page.tsx (copy about/page.tsx). Sections: Evidence model (summarise reference/06 section 1A: research-based, no hands-on testing unless stated); Sources we use (reference/08 section 1 tiers, in plain language); How we rank (reference/04 section 3 criteria); What labels mean (Best Overall / Best Value definitions from 06 section 7); Affiliate disclosure (anchor id 'affiliate-disclosure'); Corrections policy (04 section 16 steps, plus a contact address); Update policy (06 section 17). Set EDITORIAL_DISCLOSURE_URL=/how-we-research/ in Vercel, render the Link in AffiliateDisclosure.tsx, add the page to the footer 'Company' group in Sanity siteLayout, and add it to the sitemap's static pages.

### TRUST-11 - Author pages show only a name and a post grid; bio and photo are fetched but never rendered, and the schema has no fields for credentials or links
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/(frontend)/author/[slug]/page.tsx lines 66-86 render `<Section title={author.name}>` plus PostGrid only, while src/lib/requests.ts lines 114-118 fetch `bio` and `image`. The Emily Cooper avatar URL (hash 497ce730...) appears in the epilators hero but 0 times on author-emily-cooper.html. Metadata: title 'Emily Cooper - WhoAdvice', description 'Posts created by Emily Cooper.' (lines 24-25), robots nocache. sanity/schemas/author.ts has only name, slug, image, bio; there is no jobTitle, expertise, credentials, sameAs/social links or email. The GROQ `posts` projection has no `| order(...)`, so the grid is unsorted (live: Sep 2023, Jul 2023, Aug 2023, Jul 2023, Aug 2024 ... Jun 2026, Mar 2024).
- **Why it matters:** The byline is the site's main E-E-A-T hook. Clicking it lands on an empty profile that says nothing about who the person is or why they are qualified to recommend IPL devices or hedge trimmers.
- **Fix:** Add fields to sanity/schemas/author.ts: jobTitle (string), expertise (array of category references), credentials (text, optional; 'leave empty rather than invent'), sameAs (array of url), and editorialRole (options: writer / editor / fact-checker). In author/[slug]/page.tsx render a header above the grid with the image (next/image 160px, not lazy), name, jobTitle, the bio via PortableTextComponent, expertise chips and sameAs links. Emit ProfilePage JSON-LD with mainEntity Person {name, jobTitle, image, description, sameAs, url:'https://whoadvice.com/author/<slug>/'}. Change the description to the first 155 characters of the bio. Add `| order(coalesce(publishedAt,_createdAt) desc)` to the posts projection. Remove nocache.
- **Checker's correction:** The author name is rendered as an H2 by Section.tsx line 17; the author page has no H1 (see TRUST-V03).

### TRUST-12 - Nothing on the site or in the vault establishes that the seven bylines are real people; the About page names nobody
**high** · effort M · hypothesis

- **Evidence:** sitemap-urls.txt: 7 author URLs (steve-mollison, max-collins, emily-cooper, jennifer-brooke, sophie-anderson, chris-jason, melissa-violet). `grep -rli 'Emily Cooper|Steve Mollison|Max Collins'` over the whole vault //wsl.localhost/ubuntu/home/arif/code/whoadvice returns nothing, so no brief, handoff or audit assigns or describes an author. Emily Cooper's byline covers skincare, Dyson hair styling and a cat fountain (author-emily-cooper.jsonld.txt). The vault packages are written to a research workflow (Codex skill), not by a named writer. The vault forbids inventing an expert (01 line 158; 12 Part 13 'must not invent credentials').
- **Why it matters:** If these are pen names attached to research-workflow output, a byline plus avatar implies a human reviewer who did not exist. That is the kind of 'fake author' pattern Google's reviews and site-reputation guidance targets, and it contradicts the vault's own trust contract. If they are real, the site fails to show it.
- **Fix:** Decision for the owner (this is a fact only Arif holds). If the bylines are real contributors, fill the TRUST-11 fields with verifiable bios and links. If they are not, reassign all content to one or two real, named editors (the owner at minimum) or to an organizational byline 'WhoAdvice Editorial Team' linked to /how-we-research/. Then 301 the retired /author/* URLs to the new author page. Add a vault rule to reference/01: 'The byline names the person who researched and edited the package; pen names are not used.'
- **Checker's correction:** The verified part is the absence of any identity evidence. Whether the personas are real stays unverifiable, and only the owner can settle it. The hypothesis label is appropriate.

### TRUST-07 - The product-card 'Updated' date sits next to the Amazon CTA and reads as a price check, but it is the shared product document's edit time
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ProductReviewCard.tsx lines 83-94 place `Updated: {product._updatedAt}` directly beside 'Check Price at Amazon'. Product documents are shared across articles (product.ts is a standalone document referenced by articleProduct). Live mismatches: on epilators all 7 cards say 'Updated: Jun 2026' while the hero says 'Aug 2026'. On face-epilators all 5 cards say 'Updated: Dec 2023' while the hero says 'Jun 2024' and datePublished is 2024-05-06, so the products look older than the article. On hedge-trimmers the cards say Jul/Aug/Sep 2026 on a page published 2026-08-24.
- **Why it matters:** Readers infer that price or availability was checked on that date. Nothing checks them: product.ts line 114 says the price is 'from Amazon at import time — verify before publish' and it is never shown. Three different date meanings on one page make the site look careless.
- **Fix:** Remove the Updated line from ProductReviewCard.tsx. If a date is wanted there, add `availability_checked_at` to productEditorialFields (per article, not per product) and render 'Availability checked {Mon YYYY}' only when it is set. The vault already records per-product check dates in articles/<cluster>/products/*.md.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to medium. The date mismatch is real and verified, but the claim that readers read it as a price check is an inference, and the harm is bounded. It overlaps TRUST-06.

### TRUST-13 - The newsletter form is fake: it validates the email, sends it nowhere, and tells the reader they are signed up
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/EmailSection/email-form.tsx lines 20-36: handleSubmit trims, regex-validates, then `setSubmitted(true)`. There is no fetch, action or API route (src/app/api has only draft, revalidate, email, products-audit). email-success.tsx line 5 says 'Thanks for signing up! We'll be in touch soon.' The section renders on every page (home, about, author, category, articles) with the promise 'We respect your inbox. Unsubscribe at any time.' The homepage text shows 'Get the best buying guides before you shop' twice (the CMS title and body hold the same string).
- **Why it matters:** The reader is told they subscribed to something that does not exist. That is a small but real deception, and it sits on every page next to the trust claims. If a list is ever added, the privacy policy must also cover it.
- **Fix:** Either (a) remove EmailSection from the layout until a provider exists (clear the siteLayout.emailSection title and body in Sanity; the component already returns null when both are empty), or (b) wire it to a real provider with a server action that posts to the provider API, double opt-in, and a success message 'Check your inbox to confirm your subscription.' Also set a distinct body in Sanity, e.g. 'One email when we publish or re-check a guide. No sponsored mail.', and update privacy.mdx with the provider as a processor.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to medium. The deception is real, but its harm is bounded: no data is collected or misused, and the fix is a CMS toggle.

### TRUST-15 - The privacy policy is an unedited generator template that omits every tracker the site actually runs
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/mdx/privacy.mdx: line 26 'Country refers to: Alabama, United States'. Lines 15, 119 and 157 describe user Accounts and 'signing in to Your Account'; line 120 describes purchase contracts; line 219 promises a 'Last updated' date 'at the top', which does not exist. Line 83 links to privacypolicies.com's template blog. It never names Google Analytics 4, Microsoft Clarity (session recording) or Vercel Analytics. src/components/Scripts.tsx lines 8-41 load GA4 G-CL05EM3735 and Clarity k2jh9qq4zt, and src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx line 42 loads Vercel Analytics. The policy also omits Amazon affiliate cookies, the contact form (name/email stored in Gmail), the newsletter and any controller identity or contact email (it says 'contact form' only). Lines 51-58 list 'Usage Data' as the only personal data.
- **Why it matters:** The policy does not describe the processing that happens, which fails GDPR/UK GDPR transparency (Art. 13) for the UK/EU visitors in GSC (United Kingdom 16 impressions, Germany 3, France 3) and CCPA notice-at-collection. Clarity's terms also require disclosing its use. For a rater, a template with an Alabama placeholder is a strong low-trust signal.
- **Fix:** Replace privacy.mdx with a short, accurate policy: controller (legal name, country, email); a data table (GA4: pages, device, approximate location, Google as processor, 14-month retention; Microsoft Clarity: clicks, scrolls, session replays with text masked, Microsoft; Vercel Analytics: anonymous page views, no cookies; Contact form: name, email, message kept in the WhoAdvice mailbox for 12 months; Amazon: when you click an affiliate link, Amazon sets its own cookies under its policy); legal bases (consent for analytics in the UK/EEA, legitimate interest elsewhere); rights and how to exercise them; a 'Last updated: <date>' line at the top. Remove the Accounts, purchases, SMS/push and 'business partners' clauses and the privacypolicies.com link.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to medium. The policy is inaccurate, but the UK and EEA exposure is small (22 impressions, 0 clicks in 3 months), and raters weigh legal-page quality less than the article and about-page claims. The 'contact form only' wording is right: the policy's Contact section links only to /contact/.

### TRUST-16 - GA4 and Microsoft Clarity load unconditionally, with no consent banner or Consent Mode, while the policies describe a 'notice acceptance' cookie that does not exist
**medium** · effort M · likely

- **Evidence:** src/components/Scripts.tsx lines 4-41: in production GA4 and Clarity inject on every page with no consent check. There is no consent/CMP code: `grep -i consent home.html` finds nothing, and there are no 'Accept' controls. privacy.mdx lines 94-100 describe 'Cookies Policy / Notice Acceptance Cookies' that 'identify if users have accepted the use of cookies'. GSC Countries.csv shows United Kingdom, Germany, France, Iceland visitors. Microsoft has required a consent signal for Clarity on EEA/UK/CH traffic since late 2025 (from memory of Microsoft's announcement; not re-verified in this audit).
- **Why it matters:** Analytics cookies and session replay without prior consent breach PECR/ePrivacy for UK/EEA visitors. Clarity may also stop recording or restrict data for those visitors without a consent signal. The policy describes a consent mechanism the site does not have, which is a false statement.
- **Fix:** Add a lightweight CMP (e.g. vanilla-cookieconsent from cdn.jsdelivr, or Cookiebot/Termly with Google Consent Mode v2) shown to EU/UK/CH visitors, using the geo header `x-vercel-ip-country` in middleware to set a cookie read at render time. In Scripts.tsx initialise `gtag('consent','default',{analytics_storage:'denied',ad_storage:'denied',ad_user_data:'denied',ad_personalization:'denied'})` before config, and call `window.clarity('consentv2',{analytics_Storage:'granted'|'denied', ad_Storage:'denied'})` on the user's choice. Load Clarity only after consent in those regions. Keep Vercel Analytics (cookieless). Update cookies.mdx and privacy.mdx to describe exactly this.
- **Checker's correction:** The code facts are verified. The statement about Microsoft's Clarity consent-signal requirement comes from memory and is not verified here. Severity lowered to medium because of the small UK and EEA audience.

### TRUST-18 - The terms page carries template placeholders, a non-existent comments system and no product/health disclaimer
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/mdx/terms.mdx line 10 'in accordance with and subject to, prevailing law of vi.' (unfilled jurisdiction). Lines 31-42 regulate user 'Comments', which the site does not have. Line 70 says 'you must inform us by sending an e-mail to WhoAdvice' but gives no address. Line 29 links 'Free Terms and Conditions Generator'. It has no affiliate or advertising clause, no statement that content is general information and not medical advice, and no product-safety/manufacturer-instructions clause, even though the site recommends IPL, laser hair removal, LED masks and electrical garden tools. The vault requires professional-care boundaries (reference/01 'Scope boundaries', 04 section 9).
- **Why it matters:** Unfilled placeholders show the legal pages were never read. For YMYL-adjacent device content, the missing health and safety disclaimer is both a trust gap and a liability gap.
- **Fix:** Edit terms.mdx: set the governing law to the operator's real jurisdiction; delete the Comments section (lines 31-42); replace line 70's 'e-mail to WhoAdvice' with the real address; delete the generator link. Add a section 'Product information and health': 'WhoAdvice guides are general information, not medical advice. Follow each manufacturer's instructions and safety warnings. Ask a doctor or dermatologist before using hair-removal, light-based or skin devices if you have a skin condition, are pregnant, take photosensitising medication, or have darker skin with an IPL device.' Add 'Affiliate links': the TRUST-04 wording.
- **Checker's correction:** Add to the evidence: terms.mdx line 96 says 'We do not ensure that the information on this website is correct... nor do we promise... that the material on the website is kept up to date'. That directly contradicts the homepage badge 'Regularly reviewed guides' (see TRUST-V01).

### TRUST-19 - No publisher identity anywhere: no legal entity, no address, a free Gmail inbox, and no Organization schema
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The homepage footer has `mailto:whoadvice@gmail.com` (siteLayout.footer.contactEmail). The contact page (src/app/(frontend)/(marketing)/contact/page.tsx lines 16-20) offers only 'Get in touch with us! Our contact page is your gateway to quick and easy communication.' plus a form. No page names a company, owner or country (privacy.mdx line 20 'Company ... refers to WhoAdvice'; line 26 Alabama placeholder). JSON-LD on the homepage is only an ItemList (home.jsonld.txt); there is no Organization or WebSite entity, and publisher.logo points to a 404 URL (lead fact). about.mdx lines 3-5 hold the owner's name and email in a comment that never renders.
- **Why it matters:** Google's rater guidelines ask 'who is responsible for the website' as the first trust question for sites that earn money from recommendations. A Gmail address and no named operator read as anonymous. The information exists but is hidden.
- **Fix:** Decide the public identity (owner name or registered business, country, business email on the domain, e.g. hello@whoadvice.com). Put it in about.mdx (TRUST-09), on the contact page ('WhoAdvice is published by <name>, <city, country>. Email: hello@whoadvice.com. We reply within two working days. Corrections: corrections@whoadvice.com.'), and in a sitewide Organization JSON-LD in the root layout: {"@type":"Organization","@id":"https://whoadvice.com/#org","name":"WhoAdvice","url":"https://whoadvice.com/","logo":"https://whoadvice.com/images/logo-light.png","email":"hello@whoadvice.com","founder":{...},"sameAs":[...]}. Reference that @id as publisher in BlogPosting. Replace the Gmail contactEmail in Sanity with the domain address.
- **Checker's correction:** Organization does appear in the homepage JSON-LD, but only as a nested publisher on each BlogPosting, with the broken logo URL. There is no standalone sitewide Organization or WebSite entity with an @id.

### TRUST-21 - The epilators roundup recommends IPL devices without the IPL safety boundaries the vault requires
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** epilators.txt line 134: 'Its skin tone sensor and precision head make it suitable for treating different body areas with more control.' The comparison table labels two IPL devices 'Best Premium IPL' and 'Best Cooling IPL' (Ubroo ICE-COOL, which has no card). The only caveat is the con 'Not suitable for every skin tone and hair color'. There is no professional-care or contraindication note anywhere in the 441-line page (a grep for doctor|dermatologist|pregnan|eye|mole returns only the IPL product lines). The vault's approved package covers this in 'Epilator vs. IPL' and 'Safety' sections (article.md lines 287-291). Required by reference/01 Scope boundaries ('Burns, wounds, or serious device reactions') and 06 section 16.
- **Why it matters:** IPL on unsuitable skin tones can burn. Presenting it as an epilator alternative with only a four-word caveat is the kind of YMYL gap raters mark down, and it is off-intent for an 'epilator' query anyway.
- **Fix:** Publish the vault package articles/epilators/best-epilators-at-home-hair-removal (mechanical epilators only, with its Safety and Epilator vs. IPL sections). If the live page must stay for now, remove both IPL rows and the IPL card, and add a Callout: 'IPL is not an epilator. Light-based devices are unsuitable for some skin tones and hair colours and can cause burns; check the maker's skin-tone chart and ask a dermatologist if unsure.'
- **Checker's correction:** There are two caveats, not one. Besides the con 'Not suitable for every skin tone and hair color' (line 156), the 'IPL Hair Removal Devices' type section (line 327) says 'it is not suitable for every skin tone or hair color'. What is missing is any burn risk, contraindication or professional-care note. Also, epilators.txt has 440 lines, not 441.

### TRUST-22 - The homepage promotes the unapproved legacy epilator page as the #1 Smart Pick and Editor's Pick
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** home.html: 'Today's Smart Picks' first item 'Best Epilators for Smooth Hair Removal at Home', and it is also the first Editor's Pick. home.jsonld.txt ItemList position 1 is /skin-care/best-epilators/ (dateModified 2026-08-06). The vault package for this URL (status ready_for_editorial_review, audit 2026-07-20) explicitly excludes the live BRE740/14 and FaceSpa 911 as stale, per the lead's measured facts. The 'Today's' label implies a daily refresh; sanity/schemas/homePage/heroSmartPicks.ts enforces a fixed hand-picked list of exactly 3 (validation length(3)).
- **Why it matters:** The site's most prominent endorsement goes to the page its own editorial process has judged out of date, and it carries 'TOP RATED' and 'Why we love it'. 'Today's' overstates how curated the list is.
- **Fix:** In Sanity homePage.hero.smartPicks and editorsPicks, feature only vault-approved, published packages (currently the hedge-trimmer cluster and digital clocks) until the epilator package is published. Rename the Smart Picks title in Sanity from 'Today's Smart Picks' to 'Recently researched guides'.
- **Checker's correction:** I did not verify whether the digital-clocks package is vault-approved (its folder has an audit.md but no publisher-handoff.md). 'Hedge trimmers and digital clocks' as the approved set should be checked before relying on it.

### TRUST-23 - The category page shows 'Updated Jul 1, 2026' over a list that is mostly 2023-2024 content, and the description promises products the category does not cover
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** category-skin-care.html: '13 articles Updated Jul 1, 2026', from CategoryHeader.tsx line 71 `details._updatedAt` (the category document's own edit time). The 13 cards listed are dated Sep 2023, Aug 2023, Jul 2023 (x2), Apr 2024 (x2), May 2024, Aug 2024 (x2), Jan 2025, Apr 2025, Jun 2026 (x2). The description says 'Explore skincare product and device reviews ... for cleansers, serums, moisturizers, masks' and 'find options for dry, oily, sensitive, combination, or mature skin'. The category contains only devices and tools; the moisturizer and micellar-water guides live under /beauty/ (sitemap: /beauty/best-cerave-moisturizer/, /beauty/best-micellar-water-2024/).
- **Why it matters:** The category header claims freshness and scope that the list below it disproves within one scroll. That is a small, clearly checkable overstatement.
- **Fix:** CategoryHeader.tsx: replace details._updatedAt with the max last_reviewed_at (TRUST-06) of the category's articles, labelled 'Latest guide reviewed {date}'. Edit the skin-care category description in Sanity to: 'Buying guides for at-home skin and hair-removal devices: epilators, IPL and laser hair removal, LED masks, facial steamers, callus removers and wax warmers. Each guide explains who a device suits, its drawbacks and what to check before buying.'

### TRUST-24 - The article disclosure is 12px muted text with no link and is injected through dangerouslySetInnerHTML
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** AffiliateDisclosure.tsx lines 15-23 style it `text-xs font-medium text-card-on-surface-variant` inside a low-contrast panel with a 60%-opacity icon. The Link import (line 1) and disclosureUrl (line 7) are unused. The placement itself is right: in epilators.html the disclosure is at byte 26,288 and the first Amazon href at 28,836; in hedge-trimmers.html 23,907 vs 26,446.
- **Why it matters:** The FTC's 'clear and conspicuous' standard concerns size, contrast and proximity. The position is good, but the smallest, lightest text on the page weakens it, and it offers no way to learn more.
- **Fix:** In AffiliateDisclosure.tsx use `text-sm text-card-on-surface` (at least 14px, full-contrast token), drop the opacity on the icon, render the TRUST-04 copy as JSX with `<Link href={info.editorial_disclosure_url}>How we make money</Link>`, and delete the dangerouslySetInnerHTML.
- **Checker's correction:** The byte offsets are 3 bytes off (26,285 and 23,904); the substance is unchanged.

### TRUST-25 - Every product carries Review JSON-LD authored by a named person, implying an individual review that the evidence model says did not happen
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** epilators.jsonld.txt: 7 Product nodes, each with `review: {@type: Review, reviewBody, positiveNotes, negativeNotes, author: {@type: Person, name: 'Emily Cooper'}}`. The same appears on face-epilators and hedge-trimmers (author Steve Mollison). The vault audit for the epilators package says Product markup is not recommended for this roundup, and the hedge handoff (publisher-handoff.md line 149) says 'without Google Product rich-result markup'.
- **Why it matters:** Beyond the structured-data policy risk (covered by the schema lens), marking up a research summary as a personal Review by a named person repeats the fake-hands-on claim in machine-readable form.
- **Fix:** In the PostJsonLD / article schema builder, drop the Product+Review nodes for roundups and emit an ItemList of the products (name, url anchor, image) as the vault handoffs specify. If Review markup is ever used, restrict it to articles whose evidence model is 'hands-on' (a new article field) and set the author to the actual tester.
- **Checker's correction:** The Review authors are Emily Cooper on face-epilators, Steve Mollison on hedge-trimmers and Max Collins on bug-zapper. The finding's wording implies Steve Mollison on face-epilators.

### TRUST-26 - Award labels are inconsistent within one page, and several are decorative labels the vault classifies as weak
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Epilators: the comparison table gives Silk-épil 7 'Best Wet & Dry Value' but its card badge is 'WIDE HEAD DESIGN' (lead fact). The table awards 'Best Cooling IPL' to a product with no card, and 'Best Premium IPL' / 'Best Premium Epilator' / 'Best Attachment Kit' appear. reference/06 section 7 lists 'Best Premium' and 'Best Design' as weak labels and says 'A product cannot receive two conflicting labels'.
- **Why it matters:** Contradictory or decorative awards signal that labels exist to justify affiliate slots, which is exactly what 04 section 2 warns against. A careful reader notices the table and card disagree.
- **Fix:** Derive the table's 'Best For' column from the same articleProduct.tagline field the card uses (one source of truth in ComparisonTable/DecisionComparisonTable), and fail publish (Sanity validation on the article document) when a table row references a product that has no card. Relabel per 06 section 7 when the vault package is published.

### TRUST-31 - The epilators hero badge 'Reviewed & Compared • 2026' implies a product review on a page with no method, sources or evidence statement
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The live epilators hero badge is 'Reviewed & Compared • 2026' (from editorial_badge). A grep of epilators.txt for how we|we chose|we selected|we researched|methodolog matches only the footer. There are 18 external links, all Amazon; there are no source, manufacturer or authority links (lead fact). By contrast, hedge-trimmers.html states 'WhoAdvice created this research-based guide by comparing exact models, manufacturer specifications and manuals, package contents, warranty support, current availability, and recall records.' The vault package has a full 'How we ranked the best epilators' section (article.md lines 193-209) that says 'WhoAdvice did not conduct laboratory or hands-on testing'.
- **Why it matters:** reference/01 says every article must answer 'What evidence supports the recommendation?'. The live epilator page answers nothing and cites nothing, yet claims to have 'Reviewed' the products.
- **Fix:** Publish the vault package (its method section and sources.md citations). Until then, set editorial_badge to 'Research-Based Buying Guide' and add a short method Callout under the disclosure: 'How we chose: we compared manufacturer specifications, head design, wet/dry use and recurring owner feedback. We did not test these devices.' Add a template-level 'Sources' block to ArticleContent fed by a new `sources` array (title, publisher, url, accessed date) on the article schema, populated from each package's sources.md.

### TRUST-V01 - The terms page disclaims accuracy and freshness, which contradicts the homepage badge 'Regularly reviewed guides'
**medium** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** src/mdx/terms.mdx line 96: 'We do not ensure that the information on this website is correct, we do not warrant its completeness or accuracy; nor do we promise to ensure that the website remains available or that the material on the website is kept up to date.' The homepage (home.html, visible text) shows the trust badge 'Regularly reviewed guides' and the hero says 'Get clear recommendations... and honest pros and cons before you buy.'
- **Why it matters:** A rater who checks the legal pages finds the site formally disowning the accuracy and freshness it advertises on its homepage. The template line also clashes with the vault's correction and update policy (reference/04 section 16, reference/06 section 17), which commits to fixing errors.
- **Fix:** Replace terms.mdx line 96 with: 'We check each guide against manufacturer documentation and other sources listed on the page, and we record the date a guide was last reviewed. Specifications, prices and availability change, so confirm details with the retailer or manufacturer before you buy. If you find an error, email corrections@whoadvice.com and we will correct material errors and note the change on the page.' Keep the liability-limitation paragraphs.

### TRUST-V02 - The contact form silently swallows send failures and clears the reader's message
**medium** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** src/components/Forms/ContactForm.tsx lines 33-45: after fetch('/api/email/'), it calls `setMessage(data.message)` and then `reset()` whatever the status. On failure, src/app/api/email/route.ts line 41 returns `{ error: err }` with status 500 and no `message`, so message becomes undefined, nothing is shown, and the typed form is wiped. A network error is only console.logged. This is the only contact channel that about.mdx line 14, privacy.mdx 'Contact Us' and terms point to.
- **Why it matters:** A reader reporting an error or asking a question gets no confirmation and no failure notice, and loses what they wrote. For a site whose only contact route is this form, silent failure undermines the promise of a correction and contact process.
- **Fix:** In ContactForm.tsx, check `res.ok`. On success, show 'Thanks, your message was sent. We reply within two working days.' and call reset(). On failure, keep the form values and show 'Your message could not be sent. Please email hello@whoadvice.com instead.' Catch network errors with the same message. Pair this with the server-side validation rewrite in TRUST-14, returning `{ ok: false, error }` with a proper status.

### TRUST-17 - The cookie policy names AdSense (disabled) but not Clarity or Vercel, advises readers not to disable cookies, and credits a generator
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/mdx/cookies.mdx line 31 says 'The Google AdSense service we use to serve advertising uses a DoubleClick cookie', but the AdSense script is commented out (Scripts.tsx lines 13-17). Line 10 says 'It is recommended that you leave on all cookies'; line 14 says 'Therefore it is recommended that you do not disable cookies' and links 'Cookies Policy Generator'. Line 18 describes 'Site preferences cookies', which the site does not set. There is no mention of Clarity or Vercel Analytics.
- **Why it matters:** The policy is inaccurate in both directions: it claims trackers that are not running and omits ones that are. The 'don't disable cookies' advice is a dark pattern regulators cite. The generator credit tells readers nobody wrote the policy for this site.
- **Fix:** Replace cookies.mdx with a table of actual cookies: _ga / _ga_<id> (Google Analytics, 2 years/analytics, consent required in UK/EEA); _clck, _clsk, CLID (Microsoft Clarity, analytics, consent required in UK/EEA); the CMP's own consent cookie (necessary); Amazon cookies set on amazon.com after an affiliate click (not set by WhoAdvice). Add a 'Change your choice' button that reopens the CMP. Delete the AdSense paragraph until AdSense is re-enabled, and delete the generator link and the 'do not disable' advice.
- **Checker's correction:** The cookie policy does name Google Analytics (line 27). Severity lowered to low because the fix is subsumed by TRUST-15 and TRUST-16.

### TRUST-27 - The footer 'social' icon is a share glyph that links to /contact; the socialLinks field exists but is never rendered
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Footer/Footer.tsx lines 72-78: `<Link href="/contact/" aria-label="Contact us"><Share2 .../></Link>`. sanity/schemas/siteLayout/siteFooter.ts lines 24-29 define socialLinks, and src/lib/queries/link-projection.ts line 109 fetches it, but Footer.tsx never reads data.socialLinks. The author and article metadata set twitter creator '@WhoAdvice' (author page.tsx line 45); whether that account exists was not verified.
- **Why it matters:** A share icon that goes to a contact form looks like a placeholder, and an unverified @handle may point to someone else's account. Real, linked profiles (sameAs) help entity recognition.
- **Fix:** Render data.socialLinks in Footer.tsx with platform icons and rel="me noopener", and remove the Share2 link (the Mail icon already covers contact). Set `twitter.creator` only from a configured `info.twitter_handle` that is confirmed to exist, otherwise omit it. Feed the same URLs into Organization.sameAs.

### TRUST-28 - The author ItemList JSON-LD points to a wrong URL (/emily-cooper/ instead of /author/emily-cooper/)
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/(frontend)/author/[slug]/page.tsx line 82: `url: `${info.base_url}/${author.slug.current}/``. Live author-emily-cooper.jsonld.txt: '"url":"https://whoadvice.com/emily-cooper/"'.
- **Why it matters:** The author entity's machine-readable URL points at a non-existent page, which weakens the author-to-content connection that bylines are supposed to build.
- **Fix:** Change line 82 to `${info.base_url}/author/${author.slug.current}/` and move to ProfilePage + Person as described in TRUST-11.

### TRUST-29 - The brand gets impressions for Which? queries and has no brand demand of its own; the badge language edges toward Which?'s
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** GSC Queries.csv: 'which? recommended' 5 impressions pos 85.8, 'which review' 2 pos 63, 'which advice' 1 pos 65, 'which recommendations' 1 pos 73, 'which website' 1 pos 61. There is no 'whoadvice' branded query in GSC or in the Bing KeywordReport (the only site: query is 'site:whoadvice.com'). The site uses 'Best Picks', 'TOP RATED' and 'Expert advice for the discerning buyer'.
- **Why it matters:** A name one letter-pattern away from Which?, a UK consumer-testing brand famous for 'Best Buy'/'Recommended' badges, combined with testing and award language, can look like imitation. That lowers trust for the UK visitors who do arrive. Zero branded searches means the brand has no recognition to lean on yet.
- **Fix:** Avoid 'Recommended'/'Best Buy'/'Top Rated' style badges entirely (TRUST-01/02). Add one line to the About page: 'WhoAdvice is an independent publisher and is not affiliated with Which? or any consumer-testing organisation.' Track branded queries in GSC monthly as the E-E-A-T fixes land.
- **Checker's correction:** The data is verified. The 'looks like imitation of Which?' consequence is an inference, and 'likely' is the right confidence.

### TRUST-30 - Homepage category cards show hard-coded 'Featured:' labels that fall back to repeating the category name
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/config/site-nav.ts lines 6-27 hard-code featuredLabel for five slugs only. Garden-tools and beauty-products fall back to category.title (category-section.tsx lines 55-58). Live home text: 'Garden Tools 9 Guides • Featured: Garden Tools' and 'Beauty Products 4 Guides • Featured: Beauty Products'. 'Featured: LED Masks' points to a guide dated Apr 2024.
- **Why it matters:** 'Featured: Garden Tools' under 'Garden Tools' is visibly unfinished copy on the homepage, and hard-coded features drift from the content.
- **Fix:** Add a `featuredArticle` reference to sanity/schemas/category.ts and render its short title (e.g. 'Featured: Hedge Trimmers'). Omit the 'Featured:' clause when it is empty. Delete featuredLabel from site-nav.ts and keep only the colours.

### TRUST-V03 - Author pages have no H1; the author's name is a generic Section H2 above a post grid
**low** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** author-emily-cooper.html contains 0 '<h1' elements. The name renders as `<h2 class="flex items-center text-xl font-bold uppercase ...">Emily Cooper` from src/components/Layout/Section.tsx line 17, used by src/app/(frontend)/author/[slug]/page.tsx line 69. The about, home and category pages each have one H1.
- **Why it matters:** The author page is where readers and raters check who wrote a guide. With no H1 or profile header, it reads as an archive listing, not a person's page, which weakens the byline's E-E-A-T value.
- **Fix:** In author/[slug]/page.tsx, render a profile header above the grid with `<h1>{author.name}</h1>`, plus the image, jobTitle and bio proposed in TRUST-11. Title the post grid 'Guides by {author.name}' as an H2 through Section.

## Content quality

### CON-01 - The live page still runs the legacy article. The approved article would fix most of the defects below and has been ready since 2026-07-20
**critical** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Live text (scratchpad/live/epilators.txt) ranks 3-270 / PL5137 IPL / FaceSpa Pro 911 / Silk-épil 7 / BRE740/14 / SES9-041 / BRE227/00 (lines 24-303). The vault's article.md ranks SES9-041 / BRE708/00 / SE7-041 / BRE728/00 / SES9-441 / BRE227/00 / 3-270 (article.md:36-42). audit.md:6 says 'ready_for_editorial_review' and audit.md:41 says 'No stale BRE740/14, 9-481, Panasonic, or FaceSpa product in the ranking.' JSON-LD dateModified is 2026-08-06, so the live post was edited AFTER the vault audit without taking the approved copy.
- **Why it matters:** Every live defect listed in CON-02 to CON-26 exists only because the approved package was never published. The approved package removes the non-epilators (article-contract.md:272 'No IPL device presented as an epilator'). It replaces BRE740/14 with BRE708/00 (products/unavailable-products.md:5). It drops the 'painless' claim (article-contract.md:269). It adds a research-only disclosure (article.md:7, 195), a methodology with weights (article.md:197-205), a safety section sourced to the Philips manual, NHS and AAD (article.md:287-303), a conclusion (article.md:331-333), 3 internal cluster links, and 4 authoritative external links. The live page has none of these.
- **Fix:** Publish article.md into the Sanity post for /skin-care/best-epilators/ after applying the approved-article fixes in CON-27 to CON-38. The minimum before publishing is CON-31 (evidence gaps) and CON-27 (internal jargon). Run the publication-day checks in publisher-handoff.md:191-200 on the same day: variation, seller, stock, redirects, CPSC. Publish the face, pubic-hair and underarm support pages at the same time, as audit.md:104 requires.
- **Checker's correction:** Status wording: the package is 'ready_for_editorial_review' (audit.md:6), and editorial QA was completed on 2026-07-20 (article-contract.md:338). Nothing in the vault records it as 'approved to publish'. Add as supporting evidence: live-cluster-competitor-audit-2026-07-20.md:12 had already flagged the live page's IPL mix and the painless answer.

### CON-02 - The page and site claim hands-on testing that the vault says never happened, and the page never says it is research-based
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Footer on this page: 'We test, review, and curate the best products' (epilators.txt:385) and 'Our testing methodology remains independent and unbiased.' (epilators.txt:401). The hero badge reads 'Reviewed & Compared • 2026' (epilators.txt:5). Every product card has the heading 'Why we love it' (hard-coded in src/components/Post/product-card/ProsCons.tsx:77). JSON-LD emits 7 Review nodes authored by 'Emily Cooper'. The About page says 'our team of experts'. There is no sentence anywhere in <main> saying the picks are research-based. The vault's own contract says 'evidence_model: research-based roundup; no hands-on testing claim' (article-contract.md:13). The published hedge-trimmer page proves the template can say it: 'Research-Based Buying Guide' plus 'WhoAdvice created this research-based guide by comparing exact models…' (live/hedge-trimmers.html).
- **Why it matters:** reference/01-brand-and-audience.md:153 says WhoAdvice must never 'Imply hands-on testing that did not happen'. 04-editorial-positions.md:17-23 bans 'We tested' and similar wording without testing records. 02-voice-and-tone.md:300-307 bans affective 'we loved' language without testing. This is a sitewide misrepresentation that sits on every Amazon-monetised page.
- **Fix:** 1) Change the site footer tagline (Sanity site settings or the footer component) to 'We research, compare and explain products so you can choose with confidence.' Replace 'Our testing methodology remains independent and unbiased.' with 'Product selection and rankings are editorial decisions.' 2) In ProsCons.tsx:77, change the headings to 'Pros' / 'Cons' or 'What stands out' / 'Watch-outs'. 3) Set editorial_badge on this post to 'Research-Based Buying Guide'. 4) Add the one-line evidence statement to the intro, as article.md:16 does. 5) Stop emitting Review nodes with a person author for research-only roundups (see also the JSON-LD lens).
- **Checker's correction:** Some rule line references are wrong. The 02-voice-and-tone.md 'we loved / We tested it in the shower' ban is at lines 106-113, not 300-307. The 04-editorial-positions.md 'We tested' ban is at lines 15-22. The substance is unchanged.

### CON-04 - The FAQ tells readers that a true epilator is 'completely painless', which is false, banned, contradicted two answers later, and repeated in FAQPage JSON-LD
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** epilators.txt:364: 'What is the least painful electric epilator? A true epilator is completely painless because it removes hair from the root.' epilators.txt:378: 'Epilators can cause discomfort because they remove hair from the root.' Both answers give the same cause for opposite outcomes. The painless sentence also appears in epilators.jsonld.txt (FAQPage Answer.text).
- **Why it matters:** This is factually wrong to readers and breaks 08-approved-claims-and-sources.md:700-706 ('Pain-free: Do not use for epilators'), 05-banned-ai-patterns.md §13 ('Pain-free', 'Completely safe') and 05 §12 (unsupported causal language). The page's first 'answer' to the core pain question is a falsehood, and it is also pushed into structured data.
- **Fix:** Delete this Q/A from the Sanity FAQ array. If a pain question is kept, use the approved wording: 'No epilator is painless. Wet use, a massage cap and a lower speed are intended to reduce discomfort, but comfort varies by person and body area' (this matches article.md:297 and 08 §Pain-free). Revalidate so the FAQPage JSON-LD updates.
- **Checker's correction:** The Pain-free rule is at 08-approved-claims-and-sources.md:366-372 (§3 'Pain-free'), not 700-706 (the file has 512 lines).

### CON-05 - The 'best epilators' ranking includes 2 IPL devices, a facial spa device and a stale model, and the top picks contain no body epilator except the budget one
**critical** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Top picks (epilators.txt:24-33) are the 3-270, 'Braun IPL Silk Expert Pro 5 PL5137' (described as 'instead of a root-plucking epilator') and 'Braun Face Epilator Facespa Pro 911'. The table adds 'Ubroo ICE-COOL IPL' (epilators.txt:59). Card #2 admits 'is not a traditional epilator' (epilators.txt:134, 150). Card #5 is BRE740/14 (epilators.txt:224), which products/unavailable-products.md:5 says to replace with BRE708/00. unavailable-products.md:14 says 'IPL devices: different hair-reduction mechanism; exclude from the mechanical epilator ranking.'
- **Why it matters:** 06-product-review-guidelines.md §6 (eligibility) and 04 §2 ('Best is conditional') are broken. A shopper who searched 'best epilator' is shown a light-based device as pick #2 and a 3-in-1 face spa as pick #3. The page answers a different question than the query. It also routes affiliate clicks to off-category products, which reads as commission-led inclusion (01 Editorial independence, lines 166-174).
- **Fix:** Remove the PL5137, the Ubroo CT05 and the FaceSpa Pro 911 from the product list, table and top picks. Handle IPL in one 'Epilator vs. IPL' buying-guide paragraph (article.md:291-293). Send facial shoppers to /skin-care/best-face-epilators/ with one contextual link (article.md:239). Replace BRE740/14 with BRE708/00 as approved. In practice: publish the approved lineup.

### CON-03 - The ArticleHero fallback badge prints 'Expert Tested • <year>' on any post without an editorial_badge, and it is live on the sibling face-epilator page
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ArticleHero.tsx:29-45: getEditorialBadge() returns `Expert Tested • ${year}` when editorial_badge is empty and article_format is not 'editorial'. Saved live HTML: face-epilators.html shows 'Expert Tested • 2024' (2 matches) and bug-zapper.html shows 'Expert Tested' (2 matches).
- **Why it matters:** The code manufactures a testing claim for every legacy post, including the facial-epilator page that the approved pillar links to. This breaches 01 trust contract line 153 and 02-voice-and-tone.md:510 ('Avoid claims WhoAdvice cannot substantiate: We Tested 100+').
- **Fix:** In ArticleHero.tsx, change the fallback to `Buying Guide • ${year}` or `Research-Based Guide • ${year}`, or render no badge when editorial_badge is empty. Then audit every post's editorial_badge in Sanity with a GROQ query (*[_type in ['post','article'] && !defined(editorial_badge)]).
- **Checker's correction:** The 'We Tested 100+' ban is at 02-voice-and-tone.md:316, not :510 (the file has 337 lines).

### CON-06 - The comparison table labels the FaceSpa Pro 911 'Wet/Dry', but the vault record says its epilation head is dry-use only and must not be rinsed
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Table row: 'Braun Face Epilator Facespa Pro 911 | Best for Face | Facial Epilator | Wet/Dry | 3-in-1 Face Care' (epilators.txt:66-70). The vault record products/braun-facespa-pro-911.md:13-14 says: 'facial epilation head is dry-use only; cleansing brush supports wet/dry use' and 'Do not rinse the facial epilation head'.
- **Why it matters:** This is a wrong specification with a safety and device-damage consequence. A reader can reasonably take 'Wet/Dry' to mean they can epilate their face in the shower. It breaches 07-fact-checking-policy.md §5 (exact-model rule) and 06 §16 ('Follow official warnings').
- **Fix:** If the FaceSpa stays anywhere on the site, change the Use cell to 'Dry (epilation head); wet/dry cleansing brush'. On this page, remove the row entirely (see CON-05).

### CON-07 - The ranking order makes no sense: the budget model is #1, the premium flagship #6, there is no Best Overall, and no ranking criteria are published
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Card numbers: 1 = Silk-épil 3 3-270 'Best Budget Epilator', 2 = IPL, 3 = FaceSpa, 4 = Silk-épil 7, 5 = BRE740/14, 6 = SES9-041 'Best Premium Epilator', 7 = BRE227/00 (epilators.txt:102-276). The page has no 'How we chose' section and no Best Overall label. The table uses a different order again (epilators.txt:45-98).
- **Why it matters:** 04-editorial-positions.md §3 says 'A ranking is an editorial conclusion' and requires weighing fit, evidence and drawbacks. 06 §4 says 'Define ranking criteria before selecting winners'. 01:15-21 says every article must answer 'Which option is most suitable'. Numbered cards with no logic tell the reader nothing about which to buy.
- **Fix:** Adopt the approved order and badges (article-contract.md:76-82) and the methodology section 'How we ranked the best epilators' (article.md:193-211). Until then, at minimum remove the rank numbers from ProductReviewCard, or reorder so that the flagship with the broadest fit leads with a 'Best Overall' badge.

### CON-08 - The table and cards disagree: a product with no card, 6 of 7 badge labels that don't match, a different order, and filler 'Key Feature' cells
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Ubroo ICE-COOL IPL CT05 appears in the table with its own affiliate link (amzn.to/4oh7U7O, used once in the HTML) but has no card. Table vs card badges: 'Best Budget Pick' vs 'Best Budget Epilator'; 'Best Premium IPL' vs 'Best Premium IPL Alternative'; 'Best for Face' vs 'Best Facial Epilator'; 'Best Wet & Dry Value' vs 'Wide head design'; 'Best Basic Corded Pick' vs 'Best Basic Corded Epilator'; 'Best Attachment Kit' vs 'Best Epilator Kit' (epilators.txt:46-96 vs 105-274). Key Feature cells include 'Corded Power', '5-in-1 Grooming Kit' and '3-in-1 Face Care'. The last column header is 'Check'. The Use column mixes 'Wet/Dry' and 'Wet & Dry'.
- **Why it matters:** 07-fact-checking-policy.md §17 says 'Tables match the prose'. 06 §7 says 'A product cannot receive two conflicting labels'. 06 §14 says a table should contain only decision-relevant fields, and 'Corded Power' is a format, not a differentiator. 'Best Premium' labels are listed as weak in 06 §7.
- **Fix:** Drive the table from the same product references and badge field as the cards: one `badge` field per product in Sanity, rendered in both places. Remove Ubroo. Use the approved 6 columns (Rank and product | Editorial badge | Power and use | Head and control | Key inclusions | Main tradeoff), as in article.md:34-42. Rename 'Check' to 'Price' or drop the table CTA, as the approved contract says (article-contract.md:124).
- **Checker's correction:** The Use column is consistent: every wet-capable row reads 'Wet/Dry' (69, 76, 90, 97). 'Wet & Dry' appears only in the Silk-épil 7 badge 'Best Wet & Dry Value' (74). Drop the claim that the Use column mixes the two forms.

### CON-10 - The intro opens with a generic category claim, piles up unsupported comparisons, dumps features, and never states its research basis
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** epilators.txt:20: 'Epilators are one of the most convenient hair removal devices for getting smoother skin at home…'. epilators.txt:21: 'an epilator can be cleaner, more affordable over time… Modern epilators have also become more comfortable and versatile, with features such as cordless use, wet and dry operation, built-in lights, massage caps, trimming heads, speed settings, and attachments for sensitive skin.' The intro (180 words) has no sentence on how products were chosen.
- **Why it matters:** The opening breaks 03 §3 (the intro must contain the reader's decision, one useful distinction, the research basis, and a preview; no sweeping statements), 05 §1 (trend claims: 'Modern epilators have also become more comfortable'), 05 §11 (feature dumping) and 05 §12 (unsupported comparisons: 'cleaner', 'more affordable over time' with no source or price). The reader gets nothing that helps a choice until the table.
- **Fix:** Replace it with the approved opening (article.md:12-16). That opening poses the decision question, explains why bigger kits and higher tweezer counts are not automatically better, flags the facial-device boundary, and states 'WhoAdvice researched exact models, manufacturer documentation…'. Before publishing, apply the jargon fix in CON-27 to its third paragraph.

### CON-12 - The page has no safety content: no damaged-skin warning, no ingrown-hair guidance, no professional-care note, and it implies facial or bikini suitability from head size
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Buying guide 'Head Size': 'A smaller head is better for precision areas like the face, underarms, and bikini line' (epilators.txt:351). The Types section says facial epilators work on 'upper lip, chin, cheeks, and jawline' with no manufacturer qualifier (epilators.txt:324), yet the FaceSpa record approves the upper lip and isolated brow hairs (braun-facespa-pro-911.md:10). The IPL card only says 'Not suitable for every skin tone and hair color' (epilators.txt:156), with no eligibility or eye-safety guidance. No text mentions broken or irritated skin, ingrown hairs, or when to see a professional.
- **Why it matters:** 01-brand-and-audience.md:176-188 (scope: escalate for irritation, infection and serious device reactions), 04 §9 (health boundaries), 06 §16 ('Do not advise use on broken, infected, or severely irritated skin', 'Follow official warnings') and 08 §Safety all apply. The approved article (article.md:287-303) covers this with the Philips manual, NHS and AAD. The live page does not cover it at all.
- **Fix:** Add a 'Safety' buying-guide subsection and a 'What to expect from epilation' section, using article.md:287-289 and 295-303 verbatim, with the Philips manual, NHS and AAD links. Replace the 'Head Size' sentence with: 'A narrow head or precision cap gives more control on small areas, but only use it on areas the manual approves.' Remove the unqualified facial-area list.
- **Checker's correction:** The 01 scope reference is lines 180-187, not 176-188 (minor).

### CON-13 - Brand claims and sensory effects are stated as facts, and several claims do not appear in the vault's evidence records
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Unattributed specs and brand claims: '40 minutes of cordless use' (epilators.txt:233), 'The close-grip technology is built to catch shorter hairs that may be missed by other methods' (198), '0.5mm Hair Capture' (77), 'Captures very short hair' (259), 'Skin tone sensor adjusts light intensity' (140), 'Smartlight helps with more thorough hair removal' (116). Sensory: 'for a gentler experience' (193, 198), 'Massage rollers help soften the sensation' (206), 'Massage cap helps reduce discomfort' (112). Not in the vault records: 3-270 'Smartlight' and 'Massage cap' (products/braun-silk-epil-3-3-270.md:8 lists 'massage rollers' only), BRE227/00 'cleaning brush' (article.md:41 lists 'Epilator and massage cap'), BRE740/14 'pedicure tools', PL5137 'Venus razor and vanity case'.
- **Why it matters:** The breaches: 03 §11 (attribution sits next to the claim), 04 §7 (manufacturer claims need attribution), 02 Sensory language ('must come from a source') and 08 §Pain-free ('Prefer: Intended to reduce discomfort'). 07 core rule: 'Every factual statement must be verified, attributed, clearly presented as analysis, or removed.' Package-content errors lead straight to 'not as described' returns.
- **Fix:** For every retained product, rewrite spec lines with attribution ('Braun says the SES9-041 runs for up to 50 minutes'). Change comfort lines to 'intended to reduce discomfort'. Delete package claims that the product file cannot support (3-270 Smartlight, BRE227/00 brush), or add the manufacturer source to the product .md first. The approved cards use 'stated runtime' consistently. See CON-30 for making that attribution explicit.

### CON-14 - Pros and cons are generic, duplicated or trivial, and no card reports a single recurring owner complaint
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Generic pros: 'Good option for legs and arms' (118), 'Good for larger body areas' (144), 'Cordless design is convenient' (202), 'Affordable Philips epilator' (281), 'Wet and dry use' (257). Duplicates within a card: 'Dry use only' + 'Not water resistant' (293, 297); '3-in-1 facial care system' + 'Includes cleansing and toning attachments' (172, 174). Trivial or vague cons: 'Not a traditional epilator' (150), 'Not ideal for sensitive areas' (124), 'Basic design compared with premium epilators' (301), 'Not the best choice as an only epilator' (188). Price cons with no price shown: 'May be too expensive for beginners' (265), 'Higher price than most epilators' (152).
- **Why it matters:** 03 §15 says pros and cons must be 'Specific, Evidence-based, Different from one another'. 06 §10 says 'Do not list price as a con without context'. 04 §10 says 'Do not manufacture a trivial con'. 01:73 lists 'Understand common complaints before buying' as a reader job. These lists give a shopper no basis for choosing between cards.
- **Fix:** Replace them with the approved pros and cons (article.md:54-189), which are parallel and tied to a specific distinction ('Does not include the SES9-441 package's body trimmer'). Also add at least one recurring owner-reported complaint per product from the research record (see CON-32).

### CON-22 - The page has no Amazon Associates statement; the only disclosures are a generic line and a footer that also claims testing
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** A search of epilators.html finds no 'Amazon Associate' string. Disclosure: 'WhoAdvice is reader-supported. We may earn a commission when you buy through our links.' (epilators.txt:17-18). Footer: 'participant in various affiliate marketing programs… Our testing methodology remains independent' (401). All 18 external links go to amzn.to.
- **Why it matters:** 08-approved-claims-and-sources.md:794 says 'When Amazon Associates links appear, include Amazon's required associate-identification statement… clearly and prominently.' The approved package already contains the wording (article.md:10).
- **Fix:** Add to the article-level disclosure component: 'As an Amazon Associate, WhoAdvice earns from qualifying purchases. Product selection and ranking remain editorial decisions.' Keep it above the first CTA. Replace the footer's 'testing methodology' line (see CON-02).
- **Checker's correction:** The rule is at 08:460, not 08:794 (the file has 512 lines). Severity raised to high: missing Amazon's required associate-identification statement is an Operating Agreement compliance gap. It puts the only monetisation on the page at risk, not just the page's copy quality.

### CON-31 - Three approved-article picks rest on competitor, marketplace or regional evidence, and one package claim is still unreconciled
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** 3-270: the only sources are Good Housekeeping (competitor) and the Amazon listing (sources.md:59-64, products/braun-silk-epil-3-3-270.md:5-7), yet the article asserts '20 tweezers', 'Narrower, older head design' (42, 176). SES9-441: Braun family support page plus Amazon only, with no exact-model manufacturer page (sources.md:46-51), yet it asserts 'Wide pivoting head; precision handle' and 'separate body trimmer' (40, 134). SE7-041: 'Verification gap: Current US manufacturer page, manual, and exact US package still required' (products/braun-silk-epil-7-se7-041.md:9). SES9-041 specs come from Braun's UK store (braun-silk-epil-9-ses9-041.md:18). BRE708/00: the table says 'Streamlined three-attachment package' (37) while sources.md:26 says 'reconcile retailer “two accessories” wording' and the product record status is 'pending_final_variation_check'.
- **Why it matters:** 07 §3 says a Tier 4 competitor source 'should lead to a stronger source, not become the final citation'. 08 §6 says marketplace seller copy cannot stand alone for consequential claims. 07 §5 is the exact-model/region rule, and 07 §4 requires two sources when sources conflict. These claims drive the ranking and the badges.
- **Fix:** Before publishing, get the Braun US product pages or manuals for 3-270, SE7-041, SES9-441 and SES9-041, record them in sources.md and the product files, and replace the Good Housekeeping citation. Settle the BRE708/00 package against the Philips US spec sheet. Until it is settled, change the table cell to 'Streamlined package (fewer attachments than BRE728/00)', with no count.
- **Checker's correction:** The finding understates the gap. Some asserted specs have no evidence record at all, not just a weak source. 'precision handle' (article.md:40) appears in no product file, only in the approval table (best-epilator-product-refresh-2026-07-20.md:40). The 3-270's 'Narrower, older head design' (42, 176) is not in braun-silk-epil-3-3-270.md. See CON-V02.

### CON-32 - The approved article says it analysed owner feedback but reports no owner complaint, so every con is a spec gap or 'costs more'
**high** · effort M · likely

- **Evidence:** The methodology cites 'recurring owner-reported patterns' (article.md:195, 207). The 14 cons across 7 cards (62-189) are all spec, package or price comparisons, such as 'Costs more than fixed- and pivoting-head alternatives' and 'Three-hour stated charging time requires planning'. None mentions durability, noise, battery ageing, hair breakage, or any other owner-reported complaint, although the research record lists 'hair breaking instead of being pulled' and 'Braun versus Philips' as recurring buyer topics (research/keyword-cluster-2026-07-15.md:55).
