- **Why it matters:** 03 §13 says a review section should 'State the dominant complaint'. 06 §10 lists 'Recurring complaint' as a good con source. 04 §10 says honest negatives improve trust, and 01:73 makes 'Understand common complaints before buying' a reader job. Claiming a review analysis without showing any result reads as decoration.
- **Fix:** For each product, add one attributed pattern from the retailer and forum review in the research record, for example 'A recurring complaint among owners is…', with sample and date recorded privately under 07 §10. Where no pattern was found, drop the claim of using owner patterns in the methodology rather than implying it.
- **Checker's correction:** Confidence can be raised to verified, and the finding is stronger than stated. The approved article's claim to have researched 'recurring owner-reported patterns' (article.md:16, 195, 207, 313) has no recorded per-product review analysis behind it. So the fix is not only to add complaints. Either do and record the review-pattern research per 07 §10, or delete the owner-pattern claims from the intro and methodology (and from article-contract.md:90 and :157). The article already covers hair breakage in its troubleshooting FAQ (315-317), so the gap is specifically product-level complaints in the cons.

### CON-33 - The approved article skips the reader intents the vault assigned to this pillar: beginners, sensitive skin, underarm and bikini picks, and a Braun-vs-Philips choice
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** article.md has 0 occurrences of 'beginner' and 0 of 'sensitive'. The keyword cluster assigns 'best epilator for sensitive skin' and 'best epilator for underarms' to this pillar (keyword-cluster-2026-07-15.md:130, 242) and lists 'Braun versus Philips; beginner model; budget' as the dominant broad-choice questions (line 55). The article tells readers to check the manual 14 times ('manual' x14) and 'confirm warranty coverage' (285), but never says which of the 7 picks has a bikini/precision cap, which manuals approve underarms, or what any pick's warranty is.
- **Why it matters:** 01:15 says 'The site should help readers make a decision, not merely give them more products to browse'. 16-human-centered-writing-and-editing.md:79 says to replace 'great for beginners' with the control that lowers the learning burden. It doesn't say to drop the beginner question. Handing the reader the manual check that the research team already did is unfinished work.
- **Fix:** Add a 'Which pick for which area' decision box after the table, for example: 'First epilator, legs only → BRE227/00 or 3-270; want a guided angle → BRE708/00; underarms → [picks whose manuals approve underarms, with the cap name]; bikini line → [picks with an approved bikini cap] (see our permission guide)'. Add a 'Braun or Philips?' comparison callout (02 Human-centered decision devices: 'Choose A for X; choose B when Y'). Put each pick's warranty length, from the manufacturer, in the product record and the table.
- **Checker's correction:** The cluster did not ask for sensitive-skin picks. It asked for a comfort/control criterion plus a safety note, which the article partly covers in 'Speed and comfort controls' (271-273) and Safety (287-289), though without the word. The concrete unmet specification is the 'intended areas' column the cluster assigned to the comparison table. The approved six-column table (article.md:34) has no area column, so the underarm and face-and-body intents have nowhere to land. Add that column, per exact manual, alongside the decision box.

### CON-V01 - The live page gives beginners contradictory advice: it tells them to buy a wet/dry model, but its only 'beginner' picks are dry-only corded models
**high** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** The #1 pick and verdict say 'A solid entry-level epilator for beginners' (epilators.txt:25, 103), yet its con is 'Dry use only' (122). The BRE227/00 pro is 'Compact and beginner-friendly design' (285), with cons 'Dry use only', 'Not cordless' (293-295). The same page says: 'A beginner-friendly epilator should have wet and dry use, speed settings, a sensitive cap, and a built-in light' (FAQ, 376). It also says 'A waterproof epilator is a better choice for many beginners' (buying guide, 336) and that wet/dry epilators 'are often better for beginners' (Types, 318).
- **Why it matters:** The page tells the reader to buy one thing and recommends the opposite at the decision point. The beginner FAQ is also emitted as FAQPage JSON-LD, so the contradiction reaches search results. This breaks 07 §17 ('Tables match the prose' and the consistency audit) and 16-human-centered-writing-and-editing.md:79 ('Replace “great for beginners” with the control… that lowers the learning burden').
- **Fix:** In Sanity, remove the 'for beginners' wording from the 3-270 top-pick and verdict and from the BRE227/00 pros. Delete the unsourced beginner claims at Types 318 and buying guide 336, and cut or rewrite the beginner FAQ. The approved article already avoids the label. When beginner guidance is restored (CON-33), phrase it as specific controls ('two speeds and a guide that holds the angle') and tie it to named picks.

### CON-09 - Top-pick blurbs copy the card verdicts word for word, in quotation marks that read like customer quotes
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Top pick 1: '“ A solid entry-level epilator for beginners who mainly want an affordable option for legs and arms. ”' (epilators.txt:25) is identical to the card 1 Verdict (epilators.txt:103). The same holds for picks 2 and 3 (lines 29/129 and 33/161). TopPickCard.tsx:48 and ProductReviewCard.tsx:51 wrap the text in &ldquo;…&rdquo;, and ProductReviewCard renders it as a <blockquote>.
- **Why it matters:** 03-writing-style-rules.md §3 says: 'Write original short descriptions for the top-pick cards… Do not reuse the… Verdict verbatim.' 03 §12 says: 'Do not create composite or synthetic quotes', and editorial verdicts in quotation marks look like third-party testimonials. The repetition also spends the reader's first screen on text they will read again.
- **Fix:** Give top-pick cards their own `topPickDescription` field in Sanity, filled from the approved Top 3 copy (article.md:20-30). Remove the curly quotes and the <blockquote> from TopPickCard.tsx and ProductReviewCard.tsx. Render the verdict as plain text with a 'Verdict' label.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to medium. This is a real rule breach (03 §3) and it wastes the first screen, but it misleads no one about the products. The 03 §12 argument ('composite quotes') is a stretch: the rule targets fabricated third-party quotes. The stronger point is that the quote styling makes editorial copy look like a testimonial.

### CON-11 - The intro and meta description promise picks and guidance for sensitive skin, beginners, underarms, bikini line, hair type and 'what to expect' that the page never delivers
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Meta description and hero dek: 'Compare top-rated epilators for legs, face, underarms, bikini line, and sensitive skin…' (epilators.txt:8). Intro: 'options for beginners, sensitive skin, facial hair, legs, underarms, and bikini-line touch-ups… how to choose the right model for your hair type… what to expect before buying' (epilators.txt:22). No card is labelled for sensitive skin, underarms or bikini. 'Hair type' never appears again, and there is no expectations section.
- **Why it matters:** 16-human-centered-writing-and-editing.md:34 says to avoid 'questions that the article never explicitly resolves'. 05 §18 (search-result mimicry) also applies: the list reads as competitor coverage, not delivered content. Readers who arrive for 'sensitive skin' or 'underarms' find nothing specific and bounce. It also overpromises in the SERP snippet.
- **Fix:** Rewrite the meta description to describe only what the page contains. The approved one works (publisher-handoff.md:9) if the colon rule in CON-37 is respected. Remove the list of undelivered use cases from the intro. If sensitive-skin and underarm intent stays on this pillar (keyword-cluster-2026-07-15.md:130), add the per-pick guidance described in CON-33.
- **Checker's correction:** The claim 'never delivers' is overstated. Beginners and sensitive skin get thin, generic feature advice rather than nothing. The undelivered promises are picks for underarms, the bikini line and sensitive skin, any hair-type guidance, and a real 'what to expect' section. Severity lowered to medium.

### CON-15 - Card verdicts repeat the summary and skip the tradeoff, and card 5 shows a doubled 'Verdict:' prefix
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Silk-épil 7: the Verdict 'Wet and dry functionality gives you the option to epilate in the shower for a gentler experience.' (epilators.txt:193) repeats the last sentence of its own summary (198). Card 5 renders 'The Verdict “ Verdict:\nBest for users who want…”' (219-220), so the CMS field contains the label text. Five of seven verdicts start 'Best for users who…' (129, 161, 220, 246, 272).
- **Why it matters:** 05 §10 bans identical product templates, and 05 §19 says 'State the verdict once'. 06 §9 says the verdict lead must state 'why it earned the recommendation' plus a tradeoff. These verdicts only restate the badge.
- **Fix:** In Sanity, strip 'Verdict:' from card 5's verdict field. Replace all verdicts with the approved 25-30-word verdicts (article.md:52-178), each of which names the buyer and the limitation. Add a Sanity validation rule on product.verdict that rejects values starting with 'Verdict'.
- **Checker's correction:** Four of seven verdicts start 'Best for users who…' (129, 161, 220, 272), not five. The SES9-041 verdict (246) starts 'The best premium epilator for users who…', which is a close variant. 06 §9 asks the verdict lead to say why the product earned the recommendation, and the tradeoff is a separate mini-review element (§9.4), so it should not be read as a verdict requirement.

### CON-16 - Product names follow Amazon listing titles, not official model identities, and 5 affiliate links differ from the publisher-confirmed links in the vault
**medium** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** 'Braun Epilator Silk-épil 7' has no model number (epilators.txt:197). The approved record is SE7-041 (products/braun-silk-epil-7-se7-041.md:4). 'Braun Silk-épil 9 Flex SES9-041 3D' adds '3D'. 'Braun Face Epilator Facespa Pro 911' is written 'Facespa', while its own summary says 'FaceSpa'. Live short links vs vault links: SES9-041 amzn.to/43t5UQs vs 4prmaeK; BRE227/00 4ok0WPw vs 4fa6R6V; 3-270 4eg9bro vs 4fkA25V; Silk-épil 7 4v4L8mn vs 4gIcrP5; FaceSpa 446pNwM vs 3Ro09kr (live HTML vs product .md files). I did not resolve the live short links, so their destinations are unverified.
- **Why it matters:** 03 §9 forbids assuming 'an Amazon title is the official product name' and changing model numbers. 07 §5 is the exact-model rule. 08 §5 says to 'Match each link to the exact model, variant, and package reviewed'. With no model number on the Silk-épil 7, the reader cannot tell which variant the link sells.
- **Fix:** Rename the product documents to their official names (for example 'Braun Silk-épil 7 SE7-041'). Replace every affiliate link with the vault's publisher-confirmed link and verify each redirect on publication day (publisher-handoff.md:193). Keep the raw ASIN in a separate field, as 08 §5 requires.
- **Checker's correction:** Drop the '3D' objection. Braun's own exact-model page for this product is 'silk-epil-9-flex-9-041-3d-epilator' (sources.md:12), and the vault record lists 'SE9 3D Flex epilator' (braun-silk-epil-9-ses9-041.md:17), so '3D' is part of the official name. The real identity problems are the missing SE7-041 model number and the 'Facespa' spelling.

### CON-17 - The 'Types' section opens with filler and a typo, mixes mechanisms with power and use formats, calls IPL a type of epilator and then says it is not one, and ties no type to a pick
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** epilators.txt:306: 'The technology of epilators evolved alot. There are many types of epilators. Many modern epilators now come in different types, such as cordless operation, wet and dry use, rotating disc epilators, tweezer-type epilators, facial epilators, and IPL devices.' epilators.txt:327: 'They are not mechanical epilators because they do not pull hair from the root.' The H2 reads 'Types of best epilators' (305). The spring and rotating-disc H3s (308-312) describe mechanisms that none of the 7 listed products uses. 'Wet and dry epilators… are often better for beginners because warm water can reduce discomfort' (318) is an unsourced causal claim.
- **Why it matters:** The section breaks 05 §2 (empty statements), 05 §12 (unsupported causal claims), 05 §17 and 03 §17 ('Adding “best” before every product name', awkward keyword grammar) and 03 §2 (every paragraph must do a job). The self-contradiction about IPL undermines trust in the whole taxonomy.
- **Fix:** Replace the section with the approved 'Types of epilators' (article.md:213-249). It groups type as mechanism / power and use / treatment area / head design, adds the 'Type shortcut' callout, and uses listed products as examples. At minimum, rename the H2 to 'Types of epilators', delete the first three sentences, and move IPL out to an 'Epilator vs. IPL' paragraph.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to medium. The section is sloppy and self-contradictory, but it is secondary content below the cards, and the reader-facing harm is bounded.

### CON-18 - The buying guide opens with an empty statement, has an empty H3, repeats the Types section, and omits the device criteria the vault requires
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** epilators.txt:330: 'When making a purchase, you must know certain aspects that determine if your choice is the best. Currently, we have listed the factors that we consider most important.' Empty '<h3></h3>' (356). 'Wet and Dry Use' (335-336) and 'Corded vs Cordless' (338-339) restate the Types H3s (317-321). 'Price and Long-Term Value' gives no prices and claims 'can… give smoother results' (359). 'Attachments' ends '…exfoliation brush, etc.' after 'such as' (342). The guide has no treatment-area, runtime/charging, replacement-parts, warranty, noise or safety factor.
- **Why it matters:** The opener is the banned 'There are many factors to consider' (05 §2). 05 §18 bans 'A buying guide that repeats product descriptions' and repeated sections. 06 §5 'Personal-care devices' requires runtime and charge time, replacement parts, warranty, noise, safety instructions and recalls. None of these appear.
- **Fix:** Replace the section with the approved 'How to choose the best epilator' (article.md:251-293): 10 pre-purchase checks, starting with treatment area and ending with safety and Epilator vs. IPL. Delete the empty heading block in the Sanity portable text. The site lens covers adding a validation rule against empty headings.
- **Checker's correction:** 05 §18 bans 'A buying guide that repeats product descriptions'. Here the guide repeats the Types section, not the product descriptions, so the rule applies only by analogy.

### CON-19 - The FAQ repeats the body, dodges its own question, and states unsourced durations and consensus
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** There are 9 FAQs. 'How does an electric epilator work?' (362), 'Are IPL devices the same as epilators?' (372) and 'Can I use an epilator in the shower?' (370) repeat the intro, Types and buying guide. 'What is the best epilator for beginners?' (376) never names a product. 'Results can last for several weeks… Some people need to epilate every two weeks' (366) has no source. 'Over time, many users find the process easier' (378) is consensus with no basis. The heading case is inconsistent ('Are Epilators Painful?').
- **Why it matters:** 16-human-centered-writing-and-editing.md:131 says 'remove questions already resolved clearly in the main body'. 05 §18 bans near-identical FAQs, 05 §16 bans manufactured consensus, 03 §21 requires the first sentence to answer the question, and 07 core rule requires sources. These answers also go into FAQPage JSON-LD.
- **Fix:** Cut to the 6 approved questions (article.md:305-329), which come from recorded buyer questions (sources.md:100-105). If a results-duration question is added (see CON-34), source it or phrase it as 'varies with your hair growth cycle'. Keep sentence-case headings.

### CON-20 - There is no conclusion and no methodology: the page ends on an FAQ, then a newsletter box and 2023-24 related posts
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** After 'Are Epilators Painful?' (epilators.txt:378-379) the page goes straight to 'Get the best buying guides before you shop' (381). No heading covers selection method, ranking weights or a final decision.
- **Why it matters:** 03 §20 says the conclusion should 'Restate the main decision, Name the strongest recommendation… Mention an alternative'. 06 §1 and §4 require declaring the evidence model and criteria. Without either, a reader who scrolled past the cards never gets a decision.
- **Fix:** Add 'How we ranked the best epilators' (article.md:193-211, after the jargon fix in CON-27) and 'Which epilator is best?' (article.md:331-333).

### CON-21 - The page advertises freshness it doesn't have: 'Updated Aug 2026', 'Reviewed & Compared • 2026' and '2026 TOP RATED' sit on top of a lineup the vault flagged as stale
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Hero: 'Updated Aug 2026' (epilators.txt:9) comes from `formatMonthYear(post._updatedAt)` (ArticleHero.tsx, byline block), and _updatedAt changes on any Sanity save. Cards say 'Updated: Jun 2026' (126 etc.). '2026 TOP RATED' is hard-coded (ArticleHero.tsx:175) and the page shows no ratings. JSON-LD dateModified is 2026-08-06, while BRE740/14 is still listed even though products/unavailable-products.md:5 marked it for replacement on 2026-07-20.
- **Why it matters:** 06 §17 says 'On update, do not simply change the publication date. Reverify the evidence packet and ranking.' 'Top rated' with no rating data is unsupported popularity language (08 §Popularity, 05 §16).
- **Fix:** Show a dedicated `lastReviewedAt` field that editors set only after a full evidence recheck, not _updatedAt. Remove the '{year} TOP RATED' overlay from ArticleHero.tsx, or show it only when a real rating source is configured. Make the card 'Updated' dates come from the same field.

### CON-26 - The live IPL card presents long-term hair reduction and skin-tone sensing as facts, with no eligibility or safety guidance
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** epilators.txt:134: 'a strong option for users who want long-term hair reduction at home. Its skin tone sensor and precision head make it suitable for treating different body areas with more control.' Pros: 'Premium IPL option for long-term hair reduction', 'Skin tone sensor adjusts light intensity' (138-140). The only limitation given is 'Not suitable for every skin tone and hair color' (156).
- **Why it matters:** 'Long-term hair reduction' is an efficacy claim that 08 §Efficacy says needs independent evidence or must be attributed as a brand claim. Light-based devices carry eligibility limits that 01 scope and 06 §16 require stating. The vault excludes IPL from this ranking altogether.
- **Fix:** Remove the IPL card (CON-05). If IPL coverage is wanted, create a separate researched IPL page with its own evidence packet and link to it from the 'Epilator vs. IPL' paragraph, as publisher-handoff.md:122-125 anticipates.

### CON-27 - The approved article leaks internal research and process jargon into reader-facing copy
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** article.md:16 'researched exact models, manufacturer documentation, current retailer identities, and recurring owner-reported patterns'. article.md:195 'We compared exact-model manufacturer documentation, current product identity… Retailer eligibility determined whether a product could enter this commercial roundup'. article.md:209 'Unresolved catalog or regional-source limitations remain in the private evidence record rather than appearing as artificial buyer drawbacks.' article.md:211 'Scores were comparative within this approved lineup'. article.md:313 'Recurring owner-reported patterns favor both methods'.
- **Why it matters:** 03-writing-style-rules.md:368 says: 'Replace internal research language such as exact-model evidence, manufacturer trail… or scraping-status wording with plain consumer language.' A shopper has no idea what a 'private evidence record' or 'retailer identity' is, and these lines make the methodology read as a compliance memo.
- **Fix:** Rewrite as follows. Intro: 'We compared manufacturer specs and manuals for each exact model and read what owners report most often.' Methodology: 'We only included models we could link to at a US retailer; that decided eligibility, not rank.' Delete line 209. Replace 'recurring owner-reported patterns' with 'what owners report most often' (the contract's mandated phrase at article-contract.md:157 should be relaxed to allow plain paraphrase).
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to medium. The problem is about five sentences in an unpublished draft, and the fix is a line edit. The fix must also amend article-contract.md:90 and :157, because the jargon is contract-mandated rather than a drafting slip.

### CON-28 - The approved article uses no contractions and reads formal and stiff against the voice guide
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** A search of article.md finds no reader-voice contractions: the only apostrophes are possessives (model's, manual's, Dermatology's, SES9-441's). Examples: 'A larger bundle or higher tweezer count is not automatically a better purchase' (12), 'Do not infer permission from a retailer category' (255), 'It may not be worth buying if…' (309).
- **Why it matters:** 02-voice-and-tone.md:219 says 'Use contractions and natural phrasing', and 03 §6 says 'Use contractions where they sound natural', keeping full forms for warnings. The article applies warning-register grammar to every sentence, which is part of why it reads machine-produced.
- **Fix:** In a line pass, contract the non-warning sentences ('isn't automatically better', 'doesn't decrease for everyone', 'you'll treat'). Keep 'Do not use on broken… skin' uncontracted in the Safety section.
- **Checker's correction:** The voice-guide rule is at 02-voice-and-tone.md:25 ('Use contractions and natural phrasing'), not :219.

### CON-29 - The approved article's summaries and verdicts are padded to hit word quotas and repeat specs across top pick, table, summary and pros
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The verdicts all land at 25-27 words and the summaries at 50-56 (article.md:50-178). The ends of the verdicts are filler: 'during body epilation' (136), 'during regular body sessions' (157), 'for regular body sessions' (178), 'with less clutter at home' (115). Spec repetition: BRE708/00 'up to 60 minutes' appears in the table (37), summary (71) and pros (78); BRE728/00 repeats 'up to 60 minutes' and 'ProGuide, two speeds, LED' in the table (39), summary (113) and pros (120-121). The SE7-041 top pick (30) repeats its summary's feature list (92). Types line 231 says 'predictable sessions' twice in two sentences.
- **Why it matters:** 03 §2 says 'Write for decisions, not word count'. 05 §11 (feature dumping), 03 §15 ('Do not repeat a pro or con word-for-word in the body') and article-contract.md:108 ('Do not copy the… Summary') all apply. The 25-30-word verdict quota in article-contract.md:141 is causing the padding.
- **Fix:** Change the contract to 'Verdict: at most 30 words' and 'Summary: at most 60 words'. Cut the padding tails. Let the table carry the runtime, tweezer count and LED specs, and use summary and pros for consequences, for example 'ProGuide keeps the head at the angle Philips recommends, which matters most for first-timers'. Rewrite the SE7-041 top pick around buyer fit, not specs.

### CON-34 - Four of the approved article's six FAQs repeat the body, while real buyer questions go unanswered
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** FAQ 'Does epilator discomfort decrease…' (article.md:319-321) repeats the expectations section (297). The ingrown-hair FAQ (307-309) repeats 303. The 'same epilator on face, underarms, bikini' FAQ (323-325) repeats the Treatment-area guide (255). 'Are wet/dry epilators worth paying more for?' (327-329) repeats 263-265. Missing: how long results last and how often to epilate, epilator vs. waxing (keyword-cluster-2026-07-15.md:96, 207), and whether regrowth is thicker. The wet/dry FAQ asserts 'dry use improves visibility and cleanup' (313) with no source.
- **Why it matters:** 16-human-centered-writing-and-editing.md:131 says to 'remove questions already resolved clearly in the main body'. 05 §18 bans repetitive FAQs, and 07 core rule requires a source for every claim. The FAQ is also the section most likely to be pulled into search snippets.
- **Fix:** Keep the ingrown-hair and hair-breakage FAQs, which add troubleshooting value. Replace the discomfort, same-epilator and wet/dry-worth-it FAQs with 'How long do epilation results last?' (sourced to the Philips or Braun manual or the AAD), 'Is epilating better than waxing?' (link the future comparison page), and 'Does hair grow back thicker after epilating?' (AAD or NHS). Source or delete 'improves… cleanup'.
- **Checker's correction:** Two notes. First, the six FAQs are fixed by article-contract.md:221-228 and come from recorded buyer questions (sources.md:100-105), so replacing them means amending the contract. Second, the fix is internally inconsistent: it keeps the ingrown-hair FAQ that the evidence lists as a repeat. Keep it only after trimming the overlap with line 303. A waxing FAQ must stay brief and link out, per the cluster's boundary.

### CON-35 - The approved article publishes no price anchors, yet three badges and several verdicts rest on 'value', 'budget' or 'premium'
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** '$' appears 0 times in article.md. Badges: 'Best Wet/Dry Braun Value' (28), 'Best Budget Braun Kit' (42). Examples: 'delivers Braun versatility below Flex pricing' (30), 'corded value' (157), 'Premium price' (36). The contract forbids undated prices (article-contract.md:123, 274) but offers no alternative.
- **Why it matters:** 04 §11 says 'Price is part of the verdict, not proof of quality', and 08 §Value says 'Calculate'. A 'value' badge without a price or price tier asks the reader to take it on faith. 04 §2 also says 'Best is conditional', so value against what?
- **Fix:** Add a 'Price tier' column ($ under $50 / $$ $50-100 / $$$ over $100) to the comparison table, backed by a dated price check recorded in each product .md, plus a table footnote 'Price tiers checked on <date>'. That is compatible with the contract's no-live-price rule.

### CON-36 - The approved article's lineup and badges were shaped by affiliate availability: 2 brands only, and brand-scoped 'Braun' awards
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** products/unavailable-products.md:37-39 removed Braun Silk-épil 5 5-810, Emjoi eRase e6 and Emjoi Epi Slim+ 'because no normalized Amazon destination or publisher-confirmed affiliate link' was recorded. The methodology says 'Retailer eligibility determined whether a product could enter this commercial roundup' (article.md:195). The final 7 are 4 Braun and 3 Philips. Two badges are brand-scoped: 'Best Wet/Dry Braun Value' and 'Best Budget Braun Kit' (article.md:28, 42).
- **Why it matters:** 01-brand-and-audience.md:166-174 says 'Commercial considerations must not determine the ranking… Affiliate availability can affect which retailer link is used'. It does not allow affiliate availability to decide inclusion. 04 §2 says to avoid 'decorative labels created only to include another affiliate product', and a brand-scoped award implies a better non-Braun option was left out.
- **Fix:** Re-open Part 1 for at least one non-Braun, non-Philips body epilator (for example a current Panasonic or Remington model) and link it from any authorised retailer if Amazon has none. Or disclose the limitation plainly: 'We only ranked models available from a major US retailer.' Rename badges without the brand: 'Best Wet/Dry Value', 'Best Budget Kit'.
- **Checker's correction:** Emjoi eRase e6 was removed from the facial archive, not from body-pillar candidacy, so it doesn't belong in this argument. The strongest evidence is Panasonic ES-EY30V. It was dropped only because it was unavailable on Amazon, though 'the exact model remained available from Panasonic's official US shop' (unavailable-products.md:32). That is affiliate availability deciding inclusion, where the rule only lets availability decide which retailer is linked. Note that 01:166-174 lists score, verdict, pros/cons, use case and order as protected; it does not name inclusion explicitly, so the breach rests on the rule 'included only when it earns a place through the article's stated criteria'.

### CON-V02 - The approved article states head and package specs that no vault evidence record supports
**medium** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** SE7-041: article.md:30, 38, 92 and 99 claim massage rollers / a 'massage roller cap', but braun-silk-epil-7-se7-041.md:8 lists '40 tweezers; wet/dry; two speeds; approximately 40-minute runtime', and :11 lists the package as 'epilator, shaver head, trimmer comb, skin-contact cap, cleaning brush, and pouch', with no massage cap. SES9-441: 'precision handle' (article.md:40) appears in no product file, only in the approval table (best-epilator-product-refresh-2026-07-20.md:40), while braun-silk-epil-9-ses9-441.md:12-15 lists only 'Wide pivoting epilator head', wet/dry and rechargeable. 3-270: 'Narrower, older head design' (article.md:42, 176) is absent from braun-silk-epil-3-3-270.md. BRE227/00: 'Compact fixed head; massage cap' (41, 155) rests on philips-series-2000-bre227-00.md, which records only the manufacturer URL and no specifications. Yet audit.md:65 reports 'Product specifications and package distinctions… Consistent with approved table and cards'.
- **Why it matters:** 07 core rule: every factual statement must be verified, attributed, presented as analysis, or removed. 03 §9 forbids mixing specifications. The SE7-041 massage-cap claim drives a pro ('Massage roller cap and two speeds add comfort control'), and the 3-270 'older head' claim drives its main tradeoff. The audit certified this consistency without the underlying record existing.
- **Fix:** Before publishing, add the exact-model manufacturer spec, or the US package list, for each claim to the product .md files and sources.md: the SE7-041 massage-roller cap, the SES9-441 precision handle, the 3-270 head width and generation, and the BRE227/00 head and package from the Philips US page. Remove any claim that can't be sourced. For example, change the SE7-041 pro to 'Two speeds and a skin-contact cap' if the massage cap isn't in the US package. Re-run the audit's claim table against the updated records.

### CON-V03 - The overlapping legacy /skin-care/braun-epilator/ roundup is still live and competes with the pillar, although the vault ruled it should be redirected or rebuilt
**medium** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** sitemap-urls.txt lists https://whoadvice.com/skin-care/braun-epilator/. In GSC Pages.csv it has 9 impressions at position 14.67, next to /skin-care/best-epilators/ with 15 impressions at position 14.27. The vault's research/live-cluster-competitor-audit-2026-07-20.md:14 says 'Do not refresh /skin-care/braun-epilator/ as another generic roundup. It overlaps the pillar… If it has no defensible branded demand or links, redirect it to the pillar.' The same file, at lines 134-150, records that the page 'repeatedly promises painless or virtually painless epilation', lists 'Braun 069055881460' (a UPC) as a product title, and claims 'America's #1 epilator brand' and savings of 'more than $700 per year'. The approved pillar package (publisher-handoff.md) never mentions this URL, so the publish plan does not resolve it.
- **Why it matters:** Both epilator pages rank at about position 14 for the same intent, which splits signals. The legacy page repeats the banned painless claims (08 §3 Pain-free, 05 §13) that the pillar refresh is meant to remove, so publishing only the pillar leaves the same false claim live one click away.
- **Fix:** Add /skin-care/braun-epilator/ to the pillar's publish checklist. The vault's Search Console gate is already answered: 9 impressions and 0 clicks is not defensible branded demand. Add a permanent redirect in next.config.mjs from /skin-care/braun-epilator/ to /skin-care/best-epilators/, unpublish the Sanity post so it drops out of the sitemap, and deploy both changes together with the pillar refresh.

### CON-23 - Typos, grammar slips and inconsistent heading case across the live copy
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** 'evolved alot' (epilators.txt:306); 'The technology of epilators evolved' is missing 'has'; 'types, such as cordless operation' treats a feature as a type (306); 'Currently, we have listed' (330); 'such as… an exfoliation brush, etc.' (342); empty H3 (356); 'Types of best epilators' / 'Buying Guide for Best Epilators' / 'Are Epilators Painful?' mix case (305, 329, 378) where 03 §16 wants sentence case for H2/H3; 'hair removal devices' (20) vs 'hair-removal devices' (354, 372); 'wet and dry' / 'Wet/Dry' / 'Wet & Dry' (48-98); 'Facespa' vs 'FaceSpa' (32 vs 166); card 5 'Verdict:' duplicate (219); 'May be too expensive for beginners ' has a trailing space (265).
- **Why it matters:** 16-human-centered-writing-and-editing.md Pass 6 requires checking 'grammar, spelling, punctuation, names… headings'. Visible sloppiness on a YMYL-adjacent affiliate page lowers trust at the moment of purchase.
- **Fix:** If the approved article is published, these go away. Otherwise fix each in Sanity: 'has evolved a lot' (or delete the sentence), drop 'etc.', delete the empty block, use sentence-case headings, 'hair-removal devices' as a modifier, 'wet/dry' everywhere, 'FaceSpa'.
- **Checker's correction:** The heading-case evidence is partly wrong. 'Types of best epilators' (305) is already sentence case, and its problem is keyword grammar, not case. The title-case offenders are 'Buying Guide for Best Epilators' (329), 'Are Epilators Painful?' (378) and the H3s such as 'Spring Type Epilators' and 'Rotating Disc Epilators' (308, 311).

### CON-24 - The related-posts block puts 2023-24 legacy excerpts with typos and banned beauty hype under the article
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** epilators.txt:413 'Blackhead Removal Tool T0 Get Rid of Blackheads'; 432 'A callus remover slows away those thick, complex layers'; 420 'a minor, vibrating head'; 426 'a device that Unlocks Natural Glow'; 437-438 'Best Face Epilators To Get Smooth And Shiny Skin… irresistibly smooth'. Dates run from Jul 2023 to Aug 2024.

### CON-25 - The hero dek is the SEO meta description, so readers see a decorative 'Looking for the best epilator?' hook as the page subtitle
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ArticleHero.tsx: `const excerpt = post.seo?.meta_description ?? post.excerpt;`, rendered as the hero <p>. Live dek: 'Looking for the best epilator? Compare top-rated epilators…' (epilators.txt:8).
- **Why it matters:** 05 §22 says to avoid questions that 'could introduce almost any article'. A meta description is written for a SERP, not as on-page copy, and coupling the two forces one text to serve both jobs.
- **Fix:** In ArticleHero.tsx, prefer `post.excerpt` and fall back to meta_description only when the excerpt is empty. Fill the excerpt from publisher-handoff.md:11 ('Compare seven research-based epilator picks, from flexible-head and wet/dry models to full grooming kits and straightforward corded choices.').

### CON-30 - The approved article uses brand jargon (ProGuide, Smart Touch, MicroGrip) without explaining it, and a badge is built on that jargon
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** 'ProGuide' appears 11 times, first in the top-pick heading 'Best Streamlined ProGuide Kit' (article.md:24), with no plain explanation until 'ProGuide system supports a consistent working angle' (77). 'Smart Touch' (3x) and 'MicroGrip' (1x) are never explained. 'stated' appears 13 times without naming who states it ('50-minute stated runtime', 58).
- **Why it matters:** 03 §1 says 'Explain technical terms on first use'. 06 §7 lists weak labels unless defined, and a trademark is not a use case. 03 §11 says attribution ('The brand says') should sit next to the claim, while 'stated' leaves the source implicit.
- **Fix:** Rename badge 2 'Best Philips Wet/Dry Pick' or 'Best for Guided Angle Control'. On first use write 'ProGuide, a clip-on guide that holds the head at Philips' recommended angle' and 'Smart Touch, which Braun says adjusts to how hard you press'. Replace 'stated runtime' with 'Braun says up to 50 minutes' or 'Philips says up to 60 minutes'.
- **Checker's correction:** Partly wrong. Smart Touch is glossed at its first body use: 'Smart Touch pressure control' (50) and 'Smart Touch adds pressure-responsive control' (57). The 'stated' convention is also explained once in the methodology (article.md:209: 'Manufacturer runtime and design statements are presented as stated claims'). What remains valid is a trademark-based badge ('Best Streamlined ProGuide Kit') that is not explained until line 77, and attribution that isn't next to each claim (03:151-163). Severity lowered to low.

### CON-37 - Approved article line-level issues: an awkward keyword question in the opening, repeated 'Choose' openers, a colon in the SEO title, and a 2026 year claim that is ageing
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Opening question: 'What matters more in the best epilator: a head that follows curves…?' (article.md:12). The phrase 'in the best epilator' is keyword-driven grammar. 'Choose' opens top picks 1-2 (22, 26), verdicts 3 and 6 (94, 157) and a conclusion sentence (333). SEO title 'Best Epilator in 2026: 7 Picks for At-Home Hair Removal' (article.md:3) uses 'X: Y'. The research date is July 20, 2026, and BRE708/00 is still 'pending_final_variation_check' (products/philips-series-8000-bre708-00.md:25).
- **Why it matters:** 03 §17 bans 'Awkward singular/plural grammar' and keyword-only phrasing. 03 §22 says 'Do not begin more than two cards with the same command verb'. Arif's recorded preference (memory no-colons-in-head-fields, 2026-09-23, 'by default for the other sites') is ' - ' in titles and no colons in meta. article-contract.md:284 allows '2026' only after a comprehensive recheck.
- **Fix:** Opening: 'What should decide your epilator: a head that follows curves, shower use, a bigger kit, or the lowest price?' Vary the openers ('Go for', 'Pick', 'Worth it when'). SEO title: 'Best Epilators 2026 - 7 Picks for At-Home Hair Removal', checking length. Re-run the product freshness checks before keeping 2026 in the H1.
- **Checker's correction:** The 'Choose' point is overstated. Only 2 of 7 verdicts open with 'Choose' (94, 157), which is within the 03:371 limit. The rule is breached only if the two top-pick cards (22, 26) count as cards. Line 333 uses 'Choose' mid-paragraph, not as an opener.

### CON-38 - The approved article's methodology publishes weights but no scores or per-product rationale, so readers can't see why #3 beats #4
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** article.md:197-205 lists weights totalling 100%. Line 211 says 'Scores were comparative within this approved lineup', but no score or rationale for rank position appears anywhere. The SE7-041 (#3) outranks the BRE728/00 (#4) although the BRE728/00 has longer stated runtime and more attachments (37-39).
- **Why it matters:** 06 §8 says that when scores are used, 'Record the reason for every score'. 04 §3 says a ranking is an editorial conclusion that should be explainable. Referring to scores the reader never sees invites distrust.
- **Fix:** Either delete 'Scores were comparative…' and add one sentence per adjacent pair explaining the order (for example 'SE7-041 ranks above BRE728/00 because most readers won't use nine attachments, and the Braun's wide head suits legs-first routines'), or publish whole-number category ratings per product, as 06 §8 allows.

## On-page SEO

### SEO-01 - The live page is the legacy roundup, not the approved vault article, so every on-page SEO decision in the vault is absent from the page
**critical** · effort L · verified

- **Evidence:** The vault handoff (articles/epilators/best-epilators-at-home-hair-removal/publisher-handoff.md:7-9) has seo_title "Best Epilator in 2026: 7 Picks for At-Home Hair Removal", h1 "7 Best Epilators for At-Home Hair Removal in 2026" and a separate meta. audit.md:41 says "No stale BRE740/14, 9-481, Panasonic, or FaceSpa product in the ranking". The live page ranks "Philips Epilator Series 8000 BRE740/14" (card 5) and "Braun Face Epilator Facespa Pro 911" (card 3). The live <main> has 2,352 words; the vault article has 3,778 words and 40 headings, with methodology, expectations and conclusion sections that live does not have.
- **Why it matters:** The whole vault SEO gate (keyword placement, heading plan, schema handoff, links, alt text) was spent on a document readers never see. Live shows products the vault's own audit judged stale. The 'Updated Aug 2026' label then presents that stale ranking as current.
- **Fix:** Publish the vault package into the `article` model: title, H1, meta, 7 products in the locked order, methodology, types, buying guide, expectations, 6 FAQs, conclusion, and links. Before that, run the publication-day checks in publisher-handoff.md:191-200 (variation, price, redirects, CPSC). Publish the face, pubic-hair and underarm support pages together with it, as audit.md:104 requires.
- **Checker's correction:** The word count needs a qualifier. 3,778 is the vault's own audit count (publisher-handoff.md:209). A raw `wc -w` of article.md gives 3,861 because it includes metadata. The vault's conclusion H2 is 'Which epilator is best?', and live has no conclusion section.

### SEO-02 - Site templates claim hands-on testing that the vault says never happened ('Expert Tested', 'TOP RATED', 'We test', 'Our testing methodology')
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ArticleHero.tsx:44 has the default badge `Expert Tested • ${year}`. It is live on /skin-care/best-face-epilators/ and /home-gadgets/best-bug-zapper/ as "Expert Tested • 2024". ArticleHero.tsx:175 hard-codes "{year} TOP RATED" over every non-editorial hero, and it is live on epilators as "2026 TOP RATED". The footer brandTagline and legalContent (Sanity footer settings) render "We test, review, and curate the best products" and "Our testing methodology remains independent and unbiased." GuideItemCard.tsx:21 labels tips "EXPERT TIP:". The vault states "Evidence model: Research-based; no hands-on testing" (publisher-handoff.md:6). Rule 12 Part 8 forbids "We tested"-style language, and 18 §6 line 63 says "Avoid implying hands-on testing".
- **Why it matters:** These are false statements to readers, on every page. Google's reviews-system guidance and FTC endorsement rules both treat invented testing as a trust problem, and any quality rater reading the footer next to the vault's evidence model would see the contradiction.
- **Fix:** In ArticleHero.tsx, change the default badge to `Researched & Compared • ${year}` (or require `editorial_badge`), and remove the `{year} TOP RATED` overlay at lines ~168-178. In Sanity footer settings, rewrite brandTagline and legalContent to describe research-based selection and drop 'test' and 'testing methodology'. Rename 'EXPERT TIP' to 'Tip'. Add a vault rule to 12 Part 13: site-wide template text must match the evidence model.
- **Checker's correction:** On epilators itself the hero badge is 'Reviewed & Compared • 2026', because editorial_badge is set. There, the false claims come from the '2026 TOP RATED' overlay and the footer. The 'Expert Tested' default shows only where editorial_badge is empty, as on the legacy face-epilator and bug-zapper pages. 'EXPERT TIP' does not render on epilators because it has no tips. It is a latent template label.

### SEO-03 - The page does not answer 'best epilator' first: no Best Overall, and the three top picks are a budget model, an IPL device and a face device
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The live top-pick H3s, in order, are "Braun Silk-épil 3 3-270" (Best Budget), "Braun IPL Silk Expert Pro 5 PL5137" and "Braun Face Epilator Facespa Pro 911". Card #1 is 'Best Budget Epilator'. The intro opens with the generic line "Epilators are one of the most convenient hair removal devices...", and the first 'best' appears at body word 131. The vault opening (article.md:12-16) is a decision question followed by a Best Overall (SES9-041).
- **Why it matters:** This breaks 18 §4 line 41 ("Give the leading recommendation or direct answer early"), 18 §4 line 39 (keyword in the first 100 words) and 12 Part 6 ("Avoid broad statements"). A searcher for 'best epilator' gets no single answer above the fold, which hurts both satisfaction and snippet or AI-answer extraction.
- **Fix:** Use the vault's Top 3 (Best Overall SES9-041, Best Streamlined ProGuide Kit BRE708/00, Best Wet/Dry Braun Value SE7-041) under an H2 'Top 3 epilator picks'. Replace intro paragraphs 1–2 with article.md:12-16. That opening names 'best epilator' in its first sentence and states the research method.
- **Checker's correction:** The keyword-placement part is overstated. The H1 'Best Epilators for...' and the visible dek directly under it ('Looking for the best epilator?') put the phrase within the first 100 visible words, so 18 line 39 is technically met. The real breaches are 18 line 41 (no leading recommendation) and 12 Part 6 (a broad opening statement). A further defect: the three picks shown first are a budget model, an IPL device and a face-only device.

### SEO-04 - Mixed search intent: a 'best epilators' ranking includes IPL devices, one of them ranked #2
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Card 2 is "Braun IPL Silk Expert Pro 5 PL5137", whose own cons say "Not a traditional epilator". The comparison table adds "Ubroo ICE-COOL IPL Hair Removal Device CT05 – Best Cooling IPL". content-brief.md:14 says "Keep IPL, shavers, trimmers ... out of the seven-product ranking". The site already has /skin-care/laser-hair-removal/ and /skin-care/best-laser-hair-removal-2024/, and GSC shows laser queries ('laser free hair removal' pos 92) reaching the site.
- **Why it matters:** This violates 12 Part 2 (one intent) and 18 §1 ("prevent keyword cannibalization"). IPL rows dilute topical focus and split relevance with the laser/IPL URLs. A reader comparing epilators gets a device that works differently.
- **Fix:** Remove both IPL products from the epilator table and cards; the vault ranking is 7 mechanical epilators. Cover IPL in one 'Epilator vs. IPL' H3 (vault article.md:291) that links contextually to a single consolidated IPL page (see SEO-19).
- **Checker's correction:** The content-brief rule is at content-brief.md:13, not :14.

### SEO-15 - The article body has no contextual internal links; all 42 internal links are navigation, jump links, footer or the related-posts block
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Link extraction of epilators.html found only header/nav (/, /#categories, /#editors-picks), breadcrumbs, jump anchors (#top-picks, #comparison, #product ids), footer category and legal links, and 6 related-post cards. There is no link inside any paragraph. The vault planned three contextual links: 'facial epilator guide' → /skin-care/best-face-epilators/, and links to /skin-care/can-you-use-an-epilator-on-pubic-hair/ and /skin-care/how-to-epilate-underarms/ (publisher-handoff.md:108-121). The latter two are not in the 223-URL sitemap. The renderer supports links (article-portable-text.tsx:45-79).
- **Why it matters:** This violates 18 §8 line 87 and 12 Part 9. The pillar passes no topical relevance to its cluster, and the cluster pages it needs do not exist. Bing long-tail queries hitting this page ask about underarms and legs ('what is the best epilator for women's underarms and legs', pos 2), which is exactly the support content that is missing.
- **Fix:** When publishing the vault article, keep its inline Markdown links as Sanity `inlineLink` annotations. Publish the pubic-hair and underarm support pages in the same release. Verify on the rendered page that each of the 3 links resolves (18 §8 line 91).
- **Checker's correction:** The 404s are now tool-verified, not only inferred from the sitemap. The Bing long-tail rows are site-level keywords: KeywordReport_9_25_2026.csv does not attribute them to this URL. That this page is the one hit is inference.

### SEO-18 - External authority links are stripped between vault and live: epilators has none, and the published hedge-trimmer page lost the CPSC links in its vault body
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Epilators: the only external links are 18 amzn.to 'Check Price' links. The vault handoff lists AAD, NHS ingrown-hair guidance, the Philips US manual and Philips troubleshooting (publisher-handoff.md:136-142). Hedge: the vault article.md:172 has [CPSC recall database](https://www.cpsc.gov/Recalls) and :254 has [CPSC extension-cord guidance]. The live /garden-tools/best-hedge-trimmers/ has zero non-affiliate external links, and none of the vault's 7 in-body internal links (e.g. /garden-tools/corded-vs-cordless-hedge-trimmer/, article.md:182).
- **Why it matters:** This violates 18 §7 line 79 and §8 line 90, and 12 Part 9 ("Keep attribution next to the claim"). On a safety-adjacent personal-care page, missing citations remove the main evidence of expertise. The hedge case shows the loss is systematic in how content is entered, not specific to one article.
- **Fix:** Add a markdown-to-Portable-Text import step (extend scripts/import-digital-clocks-draft.ts into a generic importer) that converts [text](url) into `inlineLink` or `link` marks, so links are never re-typed by hand. Then re-import the hedge-trimmer body and verify that the live link count matches the vault count (see SEO-29).
- **Checker's correction:** The hedge example is partly wrong. Of the 7 in-body vault links, 4 point to URLs that are not in the sitemap and so could not have been linked: corded-vs-cordless-hedge-trimmer (the finder's own example), best-pole-hedge-trimmers, what-size-hedge-trimmer-do-i-need and hedge-trimmer-vs-loppers-vs-pruning-saw. The stripping is proven for the two CPSC external links and for the links to the published /garden-tools/best-cordless-hedge-trimmers/ (vault lines 186 and 226) and /garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmers/ (190).

### SEO-21 - Weak E-E-A-T surfaces: the author page has no H1 or bio, the About page is generic, and there is no methodology on the article or site
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** /author/emily-cooper/ has 0 <h1> (the name is an <h2>), no bio, description "Posts created by Emily Cooper." and robots "index, follow, nocache". /about/ says "our team of experts is here to guide you" with no names, no editorial policy and no research method. The epilators page has no methodology section; the vault has '## How we ranked the best epilators' (article.md:193). AffiliateDisclosure.tsx:7 computes `disclosureUrl` but never renders it, and info.editorial_disclosure_url defaults to "#".
- **Why it matters:** This violates 18 §7 lines 80–81 (methodology; author, reviewer, updated date and disclosures in the template) and 12 Part 13 (author biography, correction process, editorial-policy access). For personal-care product reviews, Google's reviews guidance weighs evidence of expertise and method heavily.
- **Fix:** Add a `bio` (portable text) field and optional `credentials` to the Sanity author schema. Render the name as <h1> plus the bio on author/[slug]/page.tsx, with a description built from the bio. Publish a /how-we-research/ page stating the research-only evidence model, link it from AffiliateDisclosure (render disclosureUrl) and the footer, and set EDITORIAL_DISCLOSURE_URL. Publish the vault methodology section on the article. Do not invent credentials (12 Part 13).

### SEO-22 - The affiliate disclosure lacks the Amazon Associate statement required by the vault handoff and the Amazon programme
**high** · effort S · verified

'Amazon Associate' appears 0 times in any saved page. The vault handoff (publisher-handoff.md:100) says "Include the site's approved Amazon Associate identification wording", and the vault article's disclosure is "As an Amazon Associate, WhoAdvice earns from qualifying purchases."

- **Checker's correction:** The same gap exists on the already published hedge-trimmers page. Its handoff (line ~149) says 'preserve the article-level Amazon Associate disclosure', yet the live page has 0 matches, so the gap is template-wide and not specific to the epilators article.

### SEO-25 - Legacy sibling pages use ASINs and UPCs as product names in H2s and Product schema, plus generic headings and ungrammatical titles
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The /skin-care/best-face-epilators/ H2s include "Braun B07T7ZV9N5", "Finishing Touch 40754502029745" and "Finishing Touch 022600998655", and these strings are also Product.name in face-epilators.jsonld.txt. It has an H2 "Introduction", title "Best Face Epilators To Get Smooth And Shiny Skin" and badge "Expert Tested • 2024". /home-gadgets/best-bug-zapper/ has H2 "6 Best Bug Zapper" and H3 "Customer Reviews About Product or Brand". The hedge page has H2 "Frequently Asked Questions about Hedge Trimmer".
- **Why it matters:** This violates 12 Part 7 (entity clarity: exact product and model names), 18 §6 line 59, 12 Part 5 (no 'Introduction' headings) and 12 Part 4 (clear title). A retailer ID as a product name means neither readers nor search engines can tell which product is reviewed.
- **Fix:** Correct the product titles in Sanity: 'Braun Face Mini Hair Remover FS1000' style names, verified against each ASIN and the vault's products/*.md records. ### SEO-29 - No gate compares the rendered live page with the vault handoff, and the mechanical audit checks none of the SEO rules
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** /c/Users/marif/.codex/skills/whoadvice-article-workflow/scripts/audit_article.py checks the Markdown only: H1 count, metadata field presence, summary/verdict word counts, banned phrases, sentence length and heading skips. It does not check title or meta length, keyword placement, link counts or alt text, and it never looks at the live URL. Drift that passed 'Flags: 0': epilators was never published; hedge-trimmers was published with H1 "Best Hedge Trimmers for Small, Wide and Tall Hedges" (vault h1 "5 Best Hedge Trimmers for...", publisher-handoff.md:19), Product nodes instead of ItemList (handoff :94-97, :149), and zero of the vault's in-body links.
- **Why it matters:** 18 §11 and 12 Part 18 require checking the published page, but no step or tool does it. Every finding above passed the vault's gates, which is why vault quality and live quality have diverged.
- **Fix:** Add scripts/verify_live.py to the vault. It fetches the live URL with a browser UA, parses <title>, meta description, H1, heading outline, JSON-LD @types, internal/external link lists and img alts, diffs them against publisher-handoff.md YAML (seo_title, h1, meta_description, suggested_schema_types, internal_links, external sources, alt text), and exits non-zero on mismatch. Make 'verify_live passes' the last item in 18 §12 and in the handoff's 'Remaining CMS work'. Prove the gate fails on today's epilators page before relying on it.

### SEO-05 - Title tag is weak and off-plan: 39 chars, no differentiator, no brand, odd capitalisation, and the exact primary keyword is missing
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Live <title>: "Best Epilators for Hair Removal At Home" (39 chars). The vault primary_keyword is "best epilator" (singular, publisher-handoff.md:4), which appears nowhere in the title. The H1 is a different string, "Best Epilators for Smooth Hair Removal at Home". Post titles have no brand suffix, while /garden-tools/best-hedge-trimmers/ has "| WhoAdvice" typed into its CMS meta_title. getPostTitle (src/lib/post-seo.ts:42-44) returns meta_title raw, with no template.
- **Why it matters:** 18 §3 line 29 asks for about 50–60 chars with 'a clear reason to click'. Bing shows the page 131 times at pos 7.9 for 'epilator hair removal' with 0 clicks, and a bland title is a plausible factor (inference). An inconsistent brand suffix across posts is avoidable drift.
- **Fix:** Set seo.meta_title to the vault title. Under Arif's standing no-colon rule for head fields, recommended wording: "Best Epilator 2026 - 7 Researched Picks for At-Home Use" (recount to 50–60 chars). Pick one brand rule and put it in 12 Part 4: either all post titles end " - WhoAdvice", applied in code via generateContentMeta `title: {absolute}` with the suffix, or none do. Then strip the manual suffix from the hedge-trimmer meta_title.
- **Checker's correction:** Downgraded to medium. The 'exact primary keyword is missing' point is overstated, because 12 Part 3 ('Singular and plural forms', lines 286-297) says 'Use whichever form fits the sentence'. 'Best Epilators' therefore satisfies the placement rule. The defects are length, no differentiator, 'At Home' capitalisation, drift from the vault title, and an inconsistent brand suffix. The Bing 131 impressions are a site-level keyword row: the export does not say which URL was shown, so 'Bing shows the page 131 times' is inference. The proposed title is 55 characters.

### SEO-06 - The meta description opens with the pattern checklist 12 names as 'Avoid', claims 'top-rated', and also serves as the visible dek
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Meta (160 chars): "Looking for the best epilator? Compare top-rated epilators for legs, face, ...". 12 Part 4 lists under 'Avoid': "Looking for the best epilators? Discover the best epilators...". WhoAdvice collects no ratings (12 Part 14: "Do not mark up ratings WhoAdvice did not collect"). ArticleHero.tsx `const excerpt = post.seo?.meta_description ?? post.excerpt;` prints the meta under the H1. The vault supplies a separate article_excerpt (publisher-handoff.md:11).
- **Why it matters:** The opener spends the first ~30 characters on a question instead of the page's differentiator, and 'top-rated' is an unsupported claim. Tying the dek to the meta means neither can be edited without changing the other.
- **Fix:** Set meta_description to the vault text (148 chars, publisher-handoff.md:9), or a variant written with full stops per the no-colon rule. In ArticleHero.tsx, change to `const excerpt = post.excerpt ?? post.seo?.meta_description;` and fill `excerpt` from article_excerpt.
- **Checker's correction:** 'top-rated' is an unsupported superlative under 12 Part 4 ('Avoid unsupported superlatives') and 18 line 43. The 12 Part 14 rule the finder cited ('Do not mark up ratings WhoAdvice did not collect') is about structured data, not copy. Note also that the live meta repeats the 12 'Avoid' pattern's structure but not its keyword stuffing.

### SEO-07 - Heading outline defects: H3s before the first H2, an empty H3, a generic H2, and keyword-forced H2s
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** DOM order: H1, then 3 top-pick H3s, then the first H2. The cause is src/components/ui/article-portable-text.tsx:181, which renders `<TopPickCards ... showHeading={false}>`. There is an empty <h3></h3> between 'Ease of Cleaning' and 'Price and Long-Term Value', rendered by GuideItemCard.tsx:12 with no title guard. Live H2s include "Product List", "Types of best epilators" and "Buying Guide for Best Epilators". The vault equivalents are 'Best epilator reviews', 'Types of epilators' and 'How to choose the best epilator' (article.md:44,213,251).
- **Why it matters:** This violates 18 §5 line 50 ("Use H3s only for subsections within the preceding H2"), 12 Part 5 (avoid generic headings like 'Overview') and 12 Part 3 ("create grammatically unnatural headings to preserve exact-match wording"). The broken outline weakens section understanding for search engines, screen readers, and passage or AI extraction.
- **Fix:** In article-portable-text.tsx topPicksBlock, pass `showHeading` with a heading taken from a new `title` field on topPicksBlock (default 'Top 3 epilator picks'), and render it as H2. In GuideList/GuideItemCard, skip items whose title is blank, and add `validation: Rule => Rule.required()` to the guide item title in the Sanity schema. Rename the three H2s to the vault wording.

### SEO-08 - The renderer cannot express the vault's heading policy: product names are hard-coded H2, and every card injects the same two H3s
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The vault audit (audit.md:13, 74) says "Changed all seven review headings from H2 to H3 under `Best epilator reviews`" and "product reviews correctly nested as H3s". ProductReviewCard.tsx:65 hard-codes <h2> for product titles. ProsCons.tsx:77-81 renders H3 "Why we love it" / "Points to consider" on every card: 14 identical H3s on epilators, 10 on hedge-trimmers. 18 §6 line 69: "Do not add a repetitive heading to every card when the existing fields already answer it." 'Why we love it' also implies first-hand affection the evidence model does not support.
- **Why it matters:** The vault signs off a hierarchy the CMS cannot produce, so the 'H3 under H2' audit result is false for every published article (hedge-trimmers shows the same H2 products). Fourteen generic repeated headings add noise to the outline and to passage indexing.
- **Fix:** Add a `headingLevel` prop to ProductReviewCard, defaulting to 'h3' when rendered inside a productsIntroSection that has a title. Render Pros/Cons labels as <p class=...> (or <strong>) instead of <h3>, labelled 'Pros'/'Cons' as the vault writes them (article.md uses **Pros**/**Cons**). Add a line to 18 §5 recording which headings the site template renders automatically, so vault audits check the rendered outline.

### SEO-10 - Product+Review markup on a multi-product roundup contradicts the vault, is likely invalid, and there is no ItemList
**medium** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** src/lib/schema/post-page.ts:210-218 pushes buildProductNode for every non-editorial article. epilators.jsonld.txt has 7 Product nodes, each with a Review but no reviewRating, offers, aggregateRating, brand or mpn. Review.reviewBody equals Product.description because both come from `verdict` (schema/post.ts:69-79, 96-107). The live table's 8th product (Ubroo) has no node. 12 Part 14: "Do not recommend Google Product rich-result markup for a multi-product roundup ... use Article, BreadcrumbList, and a truthful ranked ItemList". The handoff asks for ItemList (publisher-handoff.md:17-20, 184-185). The GSC Search appearance export is empty.
- **Why it matters:** The markup earns nothing: no rich result has been recorded, and a Review without reviewRating is, to my knowledge, flagged as invalid by Google's product/review snippet requirements (not run through the Rich Results Test here). It also contradicts the site's own policy, and ItemList, the one type that fits a ranked list, is missing.
- **Fix:** In post-page.ts, stop pushing Product nodes for roundups. Add a new optional Sanity boolean `single_product_review`, emit Product/Review only when it is true, and require a reviewRating the site actually assigns. Add `buildItemListNode(meta.products)`: `{"@type":"ItemList",itemListOrder:"https://schema.org/ItemListOrderAscending",numberOfItems:n,itemListElement:[{"@type":"ListItem",position:i+1,name:product.title,url:`${canonical}#${anchorId}`}]}`, using the same anchor ids the cards render.
- **Checker's correction:** The code references are wrong. The Product push is at src/lib/schema/post-page.ts:40-48 (the file has 54 lines, not 210+). getProductDescription is at schema/post.ts:8-18 and buildProductNode at :29-70, where description feeds both `description` (41) and `reviewBody` (46). Downgraded to medium: an ItemList for a product roundup earns no Google rich result either. The gain is policy consistency and removing likely-invalid markup, not a new SERP feature. That the markup is invalid remains unvalidated (no Rich Results Test run).

### SEO-12 - The publisher entity is broken: the logo URL returns 404, and no site-level Organization or WebSite node exists
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/schema/article.ts:21 has `url: `${info.base_url}/logo-light.png`` (404; the real file is /images/logo-light.png per the measured facts). The same function feeds every BlogPosting on the home, category and author pages (14 Organization copies on /skin-care/). home.jsonld.txt has only an ItemList of BlogPostings: no Organization with url/sameAs/logo and no WebSite. Author Person nodes have only name and url, with no description, jobTitle or sameAs.
- **Why it matters:** Google's Article guidance uses publisher and author details to understand who stands behind content. A 404 logo and an entity with no url or sameAs weaken E-E-A-T signals for a YMYL-adjacent personal-care topic (18 §10 line 110: 'accurate author ... data').
- **Fix:** Change article.ts:21 to `${info.base_url}/images/logo-light.png`, and give the publisher `url: info.base_url` and `@id: `${info.base_url}/#organization``. On the homepage, emit one Organization node (with that @id, name, url, logo, sameAs for real profiles only) and a WebSite node. Reference the Organization by @id from the BlogPosting publisher. Add author `description` from a new Sanity author `bio` field (see SEO-21).

### SEO-16 - The pillar's only contextual inbound link uses the anchor 'Check This', and the closest sibling (braun-epilator) does not link to it at all
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** On /skin-care/best-face-epilators/ the text reads "For more details, <a href=\"https://whoadvice.com/skin-care/best-epilators/\">Check This</a>". The live /skin-care/braun-epilator/ links to 6 skin-care URLs (best-callus-remover, best-face-epilators, best-face-massager, best-facial-steamer, best-wax-warmer, blackhead-removal-tool) but not to /skin-care/best-epilators/.
- **Why it matters:** This violates 12 Part 9 ("Do not use 'click here' as the primary anchor", "Use descriptive anchor text") and 18 §8 line 88 ("Add links from relevant established pages back to the new page"). The page with the most topical overlap sends no signal to the pillar.
- **Fix:** On best-face-epilators, replace the sentence with the vault support-page return link, e.g. 'For legs, underarms and body use, compare our best epilator picks'. On braun-epilator, add one contextual link to the pillar in its intro, or consolidate the page (SEO-19).
