The face-epilators page also has a second 'Check This' link, pointing to /skin-care/braun-epilator/, which sends the same generic anchor to the page competing with the pillar. braun-epilator also links contextually to /skin-care/laser-hair-removal/.

### SEO-17 - Related posts are an unordered slice of the category, so every skin-care page links the same 2023–2024 legacy posts instead of topical siblings
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/requests.ts:136: `*[_type in ... && slug.current != $slug && $category in categories[]->slug.current][0...6]` has no order() and no topical filter. Epilators and face-epilators show the same first five cards (Wax Warmer 2023, 'Blackhead Removal Tool T0 Get Rid of Blackheads' 2023, Face Massager 2023, Facial Steamer 2023, Callus Remover 2024). None is braun-epilator or a hair-removal page. The block streams in via <Suspense> (page.tsx:94-96) and lands after the footer in the HTML.
- **Why it matters:** The block is the page's largest group of in-content links, and it spends them on off-topic, stale pages with typos. That weakens relevance signals and does not keep readers on topic.
- **Fix:** Add a `related` reference array (max 6) to the post and article Sanity schemas and use it first. Fall back to a GROQ query that scores shared tags and orders by publishedAt desc: `| order(count((tags[]->_id)[@ in ^.^.tags[]->_id]) desc, publishedAt desc)[0...6]`, or use a simpler `cluster` string field match. Also fix the 'T0' title in Sanity.
- **Checker's correction:** 'None is a hair-removal page' is wrong. The 6th card on epilators is 'Best Face Epilators To Get Smooth And Shiny Skin', and wax warmer is also hair-removal adjacent. The accurate claim is that the order is arbitrary and the block skews to 2023-2024 skin-care posts, not to the epilator cluster.

### SEO-19 - Cannibalisation inside skin-care: best-epilators, braun-epilator, best-face-epilators and two dated laser pages compete for overlapping hair-removal queries
**medium** · effort M · likely

- **Evidence:** The sitemap has /skin-care/best-epilators/, /skin-care/braun-epilator/ (title "Best Braun Epilator For Quick And Easy Hair Removal", 4 of the 7 live epilator picks are Braun), /skin-care/best-face-epilators/, /skin-care/laser-hair-removal/ (title "Best Laser Hair Removal 2025 To Get Hair-free Skin", double space, stale year) and /skin-care/best-laser-hair-removal-2024/. GSC Pages: best-epilators 15 imp pos 14.27, braun-epilator 9 imp pos 14.67, best-face-epilators 9 imp pos 17, laser-hair-removal 28 imp pos 71.25, best-laser-hair-removal-2024 7 imp pos 6.57. GSC query 'braun epilator' pos 57.
- **Why it matters:** This violates 18 §1 line 13, 12 Part 17 ("Create multiple near-duplicate pages for minor keyword variations") and 12 Part 18 ("Consolidating overlapping articles"). Two laser pages with 2024 and 2025 year titles are stale in September 2026, against 12 Part 13 (year only when maintained).
- **Fix:** (1) Choose one IPL/laser URL: keep /skin-care/laser-hair-removal/, 301 /best-laser-hair-removal-2024/ to it in next.config.mjs, retitle it without a year, and make it the vault's 'pending_relevant_IPL_page'. (2) Either 301 /skin-care/braun-epilator/ to /skin-care/best-epilators/, or re-scope it to a distinct comparison intent ('Braun Silk-épil 9 vs 7') with a link to the pillar. (3) Record the decision in the vault cluster doc. A GSC query-by-page export would confirm which queries overlap before choosing between 301 and re-scope.
- **Checker's correction:** Downgraded to medium. Overlap is inferred from titles and page-level rows. The GSC export has no query-by-page breakdown, and the impression counts are tiny (7-28 per page). Stale year titles and near-duplicate laser pages are proven; shared-query cannibalisation is not.

### SEO-20 - Freshness signals come from Sanity system timestamps, so 'Updated' dates are inconsistent and do not reflect editorial review
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The hero shows `Updated {formatMonthYear(post._updatedAt)}` (ArticleHero.tsx) = "Aug 2026". Product cards show `Updated: {formatMonthYear(product._updatedAt)}` (ProductReviewCard.tsx:91) = "Jun 2026". JSON-LD dateModified (article.ts:38), article:modified_time and sitemap lastmod all use `_updatedAt` (2026-08-06T07:40:33Z). The vault's 2026-07-20 audit judged the ranking stale, yet the page claims an August update. Legacy siblings show "Updated Jun 2024".
- **Why it matters:** _updatedAt moves on any mutation (a typo fix or migration) and does not move when a product record is refreshed alone. Two dates on one page confuse readers, and 12 Part 13 says "Do not change the publication date merely to make old content appear new"; a system-driven date does that automatically.
- **Fix:** Add a `last_reviewed_at` datetime (required on publish) to the article/post schema. Use it for the hero label, dateModified, article:modified_time and sitemap lastModified, falling back to publishedAt. Remove the per-card 'Updated' line, or show the same date. Add a line to 12 Part 13 on who sets last_reviewed_at and when.

### SEO-23 - The comparison table does not match the cards: a product with no card and a dead jump link, conflicting badges, and no limitation column
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The table row "Ubroo ICE-COOL IPL Hair Removal Device CT05" links to #ubroo-ice-cool-ipl-hair-removal-device-ct05; no element has that id. The Silk-épil 7 badge is "Best Wet & Dry Value" in the table and "Wide head design" on the card. The table columns are Product / Best For / Type / Use / Key Feature / Check. The vault table has 'Main tradeoff' (article.md:34), and 12 Part 8 suggests an 'Important limitation' column.
- **Why it matters:** This violates 18 §12 line 129 ("Confirm the title, H1, opening, table, product order, verdict, and conclusion agree") and 18 §6 line 64. Tables are a common source for comparison snippets and AI answers, and an inconsistent one feeds them wrong data.
- **Fix:** Generate table rows from the article's products array (by reference) instead of free-entry rows, so a row cannot exist without a card and the badge comes from the product's editorial_badge. Adopt the vault's six columns, including 'Main tradeoff'.
- **Checker's correction:** The table rows already reference product documents (the RSC shows {product:{...},values:[...]}). The fix is to restrict rows to products present in the article's products array and to derive the badge from the product, not to add references.

### SEO-24 - The FAQ is generic, answers its highest-value question without naming a product, and contradicts itself
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The FAQ "What is the best epilator for beginners?" answers with features only ("should have wet and dry use, speed settings...") and names no model. "What is the least painful electric epilator?" answers "A true epilator is completely painless", while "Are Epilators Painful?" says they "can cause discomfort". The vault FAQs come from community research (ingrown hairs, wet vs dry, hair breakage, discomfort over time, one epilator for face/underarm/bikini, wet/dry worth it; article.md:305-329).
- **Why it matters:** This violates 12 Part 12 ("Answer directly in the first sentence", community-led research) and 18 §12 line 138. Question-and-answer pairs are what AI answers and PAA-style features lift, and a contradictory pair is a factual error in the markup too.
- **Fix:** Replace all 9 FAQs with the vault's 6. Until then, change the 'least painful' answer to say no epilator is painless and name the comfort features, and make the beginner answer name the vault's pick for beginners.
- **Checker's correction:** The 'least painful' question also names no product.

### SEO-26 - Category pages put the brand suffix in the H1, and their BreadcrumbList points the category crumb at the homepage
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** CategoryHeader.tsx:26 uses `getCategoryTitle(details)` for the H1, and category-seo.ts:37 appends `"| " + info.site_name`, giving the live H1 "Skincare Product Reviews & Buying Guides| WhoAdvice". BreadCrumbs.tsx:59 calls `BreadCrumbsJsonLD({ links })` without currentPageUrl, so breadcrumbs.ts:27-33 falls back to the base URL. The live /skin-care/ BreadcrumbList has position 2 `{"@id":"https://whoadvice.com/","name":"Skincare"}`.
- **Why it matters:** The category hub is the parent every post links to. A malformed H1 and a breadcrumb that resolves to the homepage weaken the hub's topical signal and can surface a wrong breadcrumb trail.
- **Fix:** Split getCategoryTitle into getCategoryHeading (meta_title or title, no suffix) for CategoryHeader.tsx, and getCategoryTitle returning `${base} | ${info.site_name}` with the space, or the chosen ' - ' form, for metadata. In BreadCrumbs.tsx, accept and pass `currentPageUrl` (the category canonical from getCategoryCanonicalUrl).

### SEO-27 - Any category segment serves the article with 200, creating an unbounded duplicate URL space
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** curl (browser UA, 2026-09-25): https://whoadvice.com/garden-tools/best-epilators/ → 200; https://whoadvice.com/anything-at-all/best-epilators/ → 200; /kitchen-appliance/best-epilators/ returns the epilators title with canonical https://whoadvice.com/skin-care/best-epilators/. page.tsx calls `getContentBySlug(slug)` and never compares `categorySlug` with the document's category.
- **Why it matters:** The canonical limits the damage, but any stray or scraped link creates crawlable duplicates that waste crawl budget. GSC already reports 27 'Crawled – currently not indexed'. Correct URLs should be the only ones that resolve.
- **Fix:** In src/app/(frontend)/[categorySlug]/[slug]/page.tsx, after resolving, compare `meta.categories.slug.current` with `categorySlug`. If they differ, call `permanentRedirect(getPostPath(meta))` from next/navigation (or notFound()), and do the same in generateMetadata.
- **Checker's correction:** There is an extra consequence. page.tsx:95 passes the URL's categorySlug to RelatedPosts/getSimilarPosts. /garden-tools/best-epilators/ therefore renders garden-tools related posts, so each duplicate URL carries a different link block, not just a duplicate body.

### SEO-30 - The brief skips 12's required SERP fields, so word count and format against competitors cannot be judged, and the contract sets word targets that 12 and 18 forbid
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** 12 Part 1 requires `content_format_expected_by_serp`, `competitor_content_gaps`, `whoadvice_information_gain` and `search_freshness_requirements`; a grep of content-brief.md and article-contract.md finds none. article-contract.md:53 sets `target_length: 3000-3800 words` with per-section allocations (:298-310), while 18:144 says "WhoAdvice does not use ... minimum word counts" and 12 Part 7 says "not to reach an arbitrary word count".
- **Why it matters:** Without a recorded SERP snapshot there is no evidence for the page type, product count or depth, so 'live 2,352 words vs vault 3,778' cannot be judged against what ranks. The contradiction also lets length targets drive drafting.
- **Fix:** Add the Part 1 YAML block, filled from a dated SERP review (top 10 URLs, page type, product count, approximate word count, recurring subtopics), to every content-brief.md, and fail audit_article.py when it is missing. Either reword contract length fields as 'expected range from SERP review (not a target)' or delete them, and state in 12 Part 7 which rule wins.

### SEO-V01 - Legacy Product JSON-LD puts raw HTML (h3/h4 headings) into Product.description and Review.reviewBody
**medium** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** face-epilators.html at byte ~10148: `"@type":"Product","name":"Braun B07T7ZV9N5","description":"<h3>Features and Benefits...` followed by <h4> items ('Long-Lasting Results', 'World’s 1st Technology'...). Each block appears twice, once in description and once in reviewBody. Cause: src/lib/schema/post.ts:8-18, where getProductDescription falls back to `portableTextToHTML(product.content)` when verdict and summary are empty, and :41 and :46 reuse it for both fields.
- **Why it matters:** Structured data should carry plain text that matches visible content. The HTML blobs (with 'World's 1st' and 'Dermatologist Recommended' marketing claims) are raw product copy in markup, and a parser that strips tags can read the h3/h4 labels as heading-like text. It affects every legacy post with products but no verdict.
- **Fix:** In schema/post.ts getProductDescription, replace `portableTextToHTML(product.content)` with `contentToPlainText(product.content)` from src/lib/portable-text-plain.ts, truncated to about 300 chars. Better, stop emitting Product nodes on roundups (SEO-10), which removes the problem at the source.

### SEO-V03 - The face-epilator page routes its generic 'Check This' anchor to braun-epilator as well as to the pillar, and braun-epilator links on to the laser page, not the pillar
**medium** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** face-epilators.html has '<p>For more details, <a href="https://whoadvice.com/skin-care/braun-epilator/">Check This</a></p>' in addition to the one pointing to /skin-care/best-epilators/. A read-only curl of /skin-care/braun-epilator/ (2026-09-25) shows contextual links to /skin-care/best-face-epilators/ and /skin-care/laser-hair-removal/ and none to /skin-care/best-epilators/.
- **Why it matters:** Internal equity within the hair-removal cluster flows to the near-duplicate braun-epilator page and the stale laser page, not to the intended pillar. That reinforces the cannibalisation in SEO-19, and it breaks 12 Part 9 ('Do not use "click here" as the primary anchor') twice on one page.
- **Fix:** On best-face-epilators, remove the braun-epilator 'Check This' link, or replace it with a descriptive anchor to the pillar. On braun-epilator, add one contextual link to /skin-care/best-epilators/ in the intro, or 301 it to the pillar per the SEO-19 decision. Record the cluster's link map in articles/epilators/final-cluster-sync-2026-07-20.md.

### SEO-V04 - The vault's approved support pages exist as packages but return 404 live, so the pillar's handoff can never pass its own link test
**medium** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** The vault has articles/epilators/can-you-use-an-epilator-on-pubic-hair/ and articles/epilators/how-to-epilate-underarms/. A read-only curl on 2026-09-25 returned 404 for https://whoadvice.com/skin-care/can-you-use-an-epilator-on-pubic-hair/ and https://whoadvice.com/skin-care/how-to-epilate-underarms/. publisher-handoff.md:114-121 marks both links 'included_in_draft_publish_with_support_page'.
- **Why it matters:** If the pillar is published alone, as the hedge cluster was, its body will carry two internal links to 404s, violating 18 line 91 ('Confirm every link resolves'). Nothing in the vault's workflow blocks publishing a pillar before its dependencies.
- **Fix:** Add a 'dependencies' list to publisher-handoff.md YAML (URLs that must return 200 before publish) and have the proposed verify_live.py (SEO-29) fail when any returns non-200. Until then, publish the two support pages in the same release as the pillar, as audit.md:104 already requires.

### SEO-09 - Anchor-link '#' characters live inside heading elements, polluting heading text
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/ui/anchored-heading.tsx:36-43 renders `<a aria-label="Link to ${text}" href="#id">#</a>` inside the <Tag>. 8 live H2/H3s have textContent ending in "#", for example "Best Epilators Compared: Quick Overview #". The accessible name becomes 'X Link to X'.
- **Why it matters:** Search engines and AI extractors read heading text for section labels and jump links; a stray '#' and a doubled accessible name degrade both. This falls under 18 §11 line 123 ("Fix ... heading errors").
- **Fix:** Render the anchor as a sibling after the heading, inside a wrapper div with the group class, or keep it inside with `aria-hidden="true"` and put the '#' in a CSS ::after pseudo-element so it is not in the text node.
- **Checker's correction:** It is 5 headings on epilators, not 8. Hedge-trimmers has 5 as well: 'Our five recommended hedge trimmers#', 'Which type...#', 'How to choose...#', 'Frequently Asked Questions about Hedge Trimmer#' and 'Which hedge trimmer should you buy?#'.

### SEO-11 - FAQPage markup is emitted on every page with FAQs even though FAQ rich results no longer show for this kind of site, and Answer.text carries HTML debris
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** schema/post.ts:143-161 builds FAQPage from `portableTextToHTML(faq.answer)`. The live answers contain "<p>...<br/></p>" and an empty "<p><br/></p>" (Q2). Since August 2023 Google has shown FAQ rich results only for well-known government and health sites; the GSC Search appearance export for whoadvice is empty. 12 Part 14: "Do not recommend FAQ schema merely because the article has an FAQ." 18 §10 line 114: "do not assume FAQ rich results will appear."
- **Why it matters:** The markup has no SERP payoff for an affiliate site and adds maintenance risk: it must mirror visible text exactly, and today it carries the contradictory 'completely painless' answer. <p> and <br> are allowed tags, so this is hygiene, not an error.
- **Fix:** Either remove FAQPage from post-page.ts, which is the policy-consistent choice, or keep it for Bing/AI consumers and serialise answers with `contentToPlainText(faq.answer)` from src/lib/portable-text-plain.ts. Either way, remove empty blocks from the FAQ answers in Sanity.
- **Checker's correction:** The FAQ builder is at src/lib/schema/post.ts:82-100 (text at :96 via portableTextToHTML), not :143-161.

### SEO-13 - The Open Graph block misstates image dimensions and omits og:site_name and og:locale on posts
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/post-seo.ts:88-92 returns `width: 1280, height: 720` for every image; the epilators og:image is 1448x1086 (4:3). The live epilators head has no og:site_name or og:locale, but category and home pages do (metadata/category.ts:109-110). metadata/post.ts:50 sets `authors: post.author.name`, a name where OG expects a profile URL.
- **Why it matters:** Wrong declared dimensions can make Facebook, LinkedIn or Slack crop or reject the preview, which hurts share CTR. It is minor for ranking.
- **Fix:** In getPostOgImageMeta, request a fixed crop from the Sanity URL builder (`urlForImage(img).width(1200).height(630).fit('crop').url()`) and declare 1200x630, or read `asset->metadata.dimensions` in the GROQ projection and pass the real values. In generateContentMeta openGraph, add `siteName: info.site_name, locale: 'en_US'` and set `authors: [`${info.base_url}/author/${slug}/`]`.
- **Checker's correction:** The category siteName and locale are set at src/lib/metadata/category.ts:40-41 (and home.ts:23-24), not category.ts:109-110. Where locale is emitted, it is 'en-US'. Open Graph expects 'en_US', so the fix should use the underscore form on home and category too.

### SEO-14 - A meta keywords tag is emitted, and the same field is reused as article:tag
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** Live: <meta name="keywords" content="Best Epilators,Facial Epilators,Hair Removal Devices,Braun Epilators,..."> plus 7 identical article:tag entries. Source: metadata/post.ts:31,44,52 (`keywords` and `tags: keywords`). The category page emits 11 phrases.
- **Why it matters:** Google ignores meta keywords. Bing has said stuffed keyword tags can be a low-quality signal (not re-verified here). The tag also publishes the keyword map to competitors and does nothing for readers.
- **Fix:** Remove `keywords` from generateContentMeta and generateCategoryMeta. If tags are wanted, add a separate Sanity `tags` array that uses the vault's recommended_tags (publisher-handoff.md:13-16) and feed only openGraph.tags from it.

### SEO-28 - Image alt text copies titles: the hero alt equals the H1, and product alts repeat the product name in top picks and cards
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ArticleHero.tsx:159 `alt={post.title}` gives the live hero alt "Best Epilators for Smooth Hair Removal at Home". TopPickCard.tsx:28 and ProductReviewCard.tsx:35 use `alt={product.title}`, so 'Braun Silk-épil 3 3-270', 'Braun IPL Silk Expert Pro 5 PL5137' and 'Braun Face Epilator Facespa Pro 911' each appear twice. The vault supplies an image-specific hero alt, "Seven ranked epilators for at-home body hair removal", and per-model alts (publisher-handoff.md:148-159).
- **Why it matters:** This violates 12 Part 10 ("Do not repeat the same alt text across images", "describe what the image shows") and 18 §9 line 98. Title-as-alt is keyword repetition, not description.
- **Fix:** Use the Sanity image `alt` field when set, falling back to the title: `alt={post.thumbnail?.alt || post.title}` and `alt={product.image?.alt || product.title}`. Enter the vault alts in Sanity. In the top-pick cards, set `alt=""` (decorative), since the product name is printed beside the image.
- **Checker's correction:** The proposed fallback fix alone changes nothing for product images. The RSC payload shows the stored Sanity iimage `alt` already equals the product title (e.g. "alt":"Braun IPL Silk Expert Pro 5 PL5137"), so the stored alts must be rewritten as well as read. Related-post card images also use their post titles as alt.

### SEO-31 - The vault has no rule for title brand suffix or separator, its epilator title uses a colon, and Sanity does not validate title length
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** The epilators vault seo_title is "Best Epilator in 2026: 7 Picks for At-Home Hair Removal" (colon); the hedge vault seo_title is "5 Best Hedge Trimmers for 2026 | WhoAdvice" (brand and pipe). 12 Part 4 says nothing about suffixes or separators. Arif's standing preference (memory 'no-colons-in-head-fields', 2026-09-23) is ' - ' in titles and no colons in head fields, 'by default for the other sites'. sanity/schemas/components/seo.ts:12-17 has no validation on meta_title; meta_description has only a max(160) warning.
- **Why it matters:** Without a rule, each package invents its own format and live titles drift (SEO-05). Missing CMS validation means over-length or empty titles publish silently.
- **Fix:** Add to 12 Part 4: the separator (' - ' per Arif's preference, needs his confirmation for WhoAdvice), whether ' - WhoAdvice' is appended and by code or editor, and 'no colons in title or meta'. In seo.ts, add `validation: Rule => Rule.max(60).warning('Aim for 50–60 characters')` to meta_title and `Rule.min(120).warning(...)` to meta_description.

### SEO-V02 - og:locale is emitted as 'en-US' instead of the Open Graph format 'en_US'
**low** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** Live home.html and category-skin-care.html have <meta property="og:locale" content="en-US"/>. Source: src/lib/metadata/category.ts:41 `locale: "en-US"` and src/lib/metadata/home.ts:24 `locale: 'en-US'`.
- **Why it matters:** The Open Graph protocol defines og:locale as language_TERRITORY with an underscore. Consumers such as Facebook may ignore or misread the hyphen form. It is minor, but it is trivially wrong.
- **Fix:** Change both to `locale: 'en_US'`, and add the same value to generateContentMeta openGraph in src/lib/metadata/post.ts (as SEO-13 proposes).

## Technical SEO

### TECH-04 - 72 of 216 non-author URLs in the sitemap have no internal link from any non-author page, because category pages show only 20 posts and 'Load more' is a server action
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** I fetched every non-author sitemap URL, extracted its internal hrefs and counted inlinks. 72 URLs have zero: kitchen-appliance 36 of 68, home-gadgets 31 of 60, hair-grooming 4 of 30, personal-care 1 of 23. Examples: /kitchen-appliance/best-stand-mixer/, /kitchen-appliance/best-bread-maker/, /home-gadgets/best-smart-lock/. Cause: src/app/(frontend)/[categorySlug]/page.tsx:31-35 renders page 1 with limit 20. src/components/Category/PostsList.tsx:44-51 is a <button> that calls getPosts, which is a 'use server' action in src/requests/posts.ts:1, so no URL exists for page 2. Also, getSimilarPosts (requests.ts:136) takes `[0...6]` with no `order()`, so every article in a category links to the same 6 posts. Live /skin-care/page/2/ → 404.
- **Why it matters:** These URLs can only be found through the sitemap and the author pages. That is a likely contributor to GSC's 27 'Crawled – currently not indexed'. The largest money categories are the most affected.
- **Fix:** Add a crawlable route src/app/(frontend)/[categorySlug]/page/[page]/page.tsx that reuses getPosts({page, category, limit:20}). Give it a self canonical and noindex=false, and return notFound() when page > pagination.pages. In PostsList, render a real <a href={`/${slug}/page/${n+1}/`}> 'Next page' link, and keep 'Load more' as a progressive enhancement that intercepts the click. Page 1 stays at /[categorySlug]/. In getSimilarPosts, order by relevance instead of the default: for example `| order(count(tags[@._ref in ^.^.tags[]._ref]) desc, publishedAt desc)`, or rotate by slug hash, so links spread across the category.
- **Checker's correction:** The denominator is 215 unique non-author URLs, not 216.

### TECH-05 - No redirect for a 404 URL that still earns impressions, and stale dated or singular twins stay live and cannibalise
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Pages.csv (GSC): https://whoadvice.com/garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmer/, 11 impressions, position 6.27. Live it is 404 (noindex), and next.config.mjs has no rule for it. The evergreen twins are live in the sitemap alongside the dated slugs: best-ice-maker-2024 + best-ice-maker, best-string-trimmers-2024 + best-string-trimmer, best-laser-hair-removal-2024 + laser-hair-removal, best-cordless-hedge-trimmer + best-cordless-hedge-trimmers. The next.config.mjs redirect for /personal-care/best-pubic-hair-trimmer-for-silky-and-smooth-skin/ points to a stale category path (see TECH-02).
- **Why it matters:** A page-6 ranking is being thrown away on a 404. The twins split impressions and links between two thin URLs on the same intent (GSC shows both hedge-trimmer variants).
- **Fix:** In next.config.mjs, add permanent redirects: /garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmer/ → /garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmers/; /kitchen-appliance/best-ice-maker-2024/ → /kitchen-appliance/best-ice-maker/; /garden-tools/best-string-trimmers-2024/ → /garden-tools/best-string-trimmer/; /skin-care/best-laser-hair-removal-2024/ → /skin-care/laser-hair-removal/; /garden-tools/best-cordless-hedge-trimmer/ → /garden-tools/best-cordless-hedge-trimmers/. After each redirect, unpublish or delete the losing Sanity doc so the sitemap drops it. Change the pubic-trimmer destination to /hair-grooming/best-pubic-hair-trimmer/. Keeping 16 or more hand-kept rules in next.config.mjs is fragile, so move them to a Sanity `redirect` document type read by next.config's async redirects() (fetched at build) or by middleware. Editors can then add a redirect when they change a slug.
- **Checker's correction:** The fix needs care about direction. In GSC the performing twin is often the older or dated one: best-cordless-hedge-trimmer ranks at position 4 while the plural has no impressions, and best-laser-hair-removal-2024 ranks at 6.57 while laser-hair-removal sits at 71. Redirecting into the maintained 2026 article is still defensible, but the target must absorb the loser's content and intent, and the choice should be made per pair from GSC, not by 'evergreen slug wins'. The best-blow-dryer / best-blow-dryers pair is missing from the redirect list. The GSC evidence is corded (pos 6.27 and 22) and cordless (pos 4), not 'both hedge-trimmer variants' of one pair.

### TECH-02 - The article route ignores the category segment, so every /<anything>/<slug>/ returns 200 as a duplicate
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/(frontend)/[categorySlug]/[slug]/page.tsx:43-46 calls getContentBySlug(slug) with no check that categorySlug matches the document's category (src/lib/requests.ts:77-93 filters by slug only). Live: https://whoadvice.com/nonexistent-cat/best-epilators/ → 200, 'index, follow', canonical https://whoadvice.com/skin-care/best-epilators/. The same holds for /kitchen-appliance/best-epilators/. The 308 redirect in next.config.mjs for /personal-care/best-pubic-hair-trimmer-for-silky-and-smooth-skin/ lands on /personal-care/best-pubic-hair-trimmer/, which is a 200 whose canonical is /hair-grooming/best-pubic-hair-trimmer/.
- **Why it matters:** This creates an unbounded duplicate URL space that Vercel's ISR will also cache. A post whose category changes keeps serving 200 at its old URL forever instead of 308-ing, so links and signals stay split and rely on a canonical hint alone. Redirects can target stale category paths without anyone noticing. Related posts on the duplicate URL are also queried by the wrong category (getSimilarPosts uses the URL segment), so they come back empty.
- **Fix:** In page.tsx, after resolving, add `const canonicalPath = getPostPath(meta); if (categorySlug !== meta.categories.slug.current) permanentRedirect(canonicalPath);` (import permanentRedirect from next/navigation and getPostPath from src/lib/post-seo.ts). Pass meta.categories.slug.current, not the URL param, to RelatedPosts. Once generateStaticParams returns the real pairs (TECH-09), also set `export const dynamicParams = true`.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to medium. Every duplicate carries a correct self-pointing canonical to the real URL, and the duplicate space is only reachable when someone links to it. The damage is bounded: signals are consolidated by hint rather than by redirect, and the redirect destination is stale. The fix stands. Note that getPostPath already exists in src/lib/post-seo.ts:36.

### TECH-03 - Two different posts share the slug best-pubic-hair-trimmer, so one is unreachable and the sitemap lists the URL twice
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The saved sitemap.xml has <loc>https://whoadvice.com/hair-grooming/best-pubic-hair-trimmer/</loc> twice, with lastmod 2025-08-21T14:43:35Z and 2025-08-21T14:42:10Z (`sort | uniq -d`). The live /hair-grooming/ ItemList has two items with that same url: 'Best Pubic Hair Trimmer For Smooth And Silky Skin' (published 2025-08-21T14:43:32Z) and 'Best Pubic Hair Trimmer 2025 For Perfectly Groomed Bikini Line' (14:42:04Z). getPost (src/lib/requests.ts:61) takes `[0]` silently, while getArticle throws on duplicates (requests.ts:48-50). The slug fields in sanity/schemas/post/index.ts:40-49 and article/index.ts:25-33 have no cross-type isUnique.
- **Why it matters:** A second article exists in the CMS that no URL can serve. Its card sends users and crawlers to a different article. The sitemap contains a duplicate loc. If two *articles* ever collide, the page throws and returns a 500.
- **Fix:** Editorial: decide which pubic-trimmer post survives. Re-slug the other one (for example best-pubic-hair-trimmer-bikini-line), or delete it and fold its content in. Schema: on both the post and article slug fields, add `options.isUnique: async (slug, ctx) => !(await ctx.getClient({apiVersion:'2025-01-01'}).fetch('count(*[_type in ["post","article"] && slug.current == $s && !(_id in [$id, $draft])])', {s: slug, id: ctx.document._id.replace(/^drafts\./,''), draft: 'drafts.' + ctx.document._id.replace(/^drafts\./,'')}))`. Make getPost fetch the whole array and log or throw on length > 1, the same way getArticle does.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to medium: one URL and one hidden document. Sanity's default slug isUnique already blocks same-type duplicates in the Studio, so the gap is cross-type (post vs article) collisions or documents written by API or migration. The '500 if two articles collide' path needs an API-level write to trigger.

### TECH-06 - The sitemap lists a noindex,nofollow page and does not filter on the CMS indexable flag
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/sitemap.ts:43-49 pushes every doc returned by getAllPosts (src/lib/requests.ts:168-183: `*[_type in ${CONTENT_DOCUMENT_TYPES}]{_updatedAt, slug, categories[0]->{slug}}`), with no seo.indexable filter. I crawled all 222 unique sitemap URLs: exactly one is not 'index', https://whoadvice.com/beauty/best-elf-primer/ → robots 'noindex, nofollow'. That matches GSC Coverage 'Excluded by ‘noindex’ tag' = 1. src/lib/metadata/post.ts:65-67 maps indexable=false to {index:false, follow:false}.
- **Why it matters:** Search engines receive contradictory signals, and the page shows up in Coverage as an issue. 'nofollow' also cuts the internal links on that page, including links to its sibling reviews.
- **Fix:** In getAllPosts, add `&& seo.indexable != false` to the filter. Do the same in getAllCategories for categories used by the sitemap. In post.ts and src/lib/metadata/category.ts:52-54, change the non-indexable branch to `{ index: false, follow: true }`. Decide editorially whether best-elf-primer should be indexed at all.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to medium: one URL is affected today. The code defect is general and cheap to fix.

### TECH-07 - Sitemap metadata carries no signal: priority 1 everywhere, static lastmod is the build time, and lastmod comes from _updatedAt
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/sitemap.ts:23-28 gives the static pages lastModified: new Date() and priority 1. Lines 33-50 give every other URL priority 1 and changeFrequency 'yearly' (static pages get 'monthly'). The live sitemap shows /privacy/ lastmod 2026-09-19T08:57:03.428Z, but the page says 'Last updated: September 19, 2023' (src/app/(frontend)/(marketing)/privacy/page.tsx). Post lastmod is Sanity _updatedAt, which changes on any mutation, including migrations (sanity/migrations/move-product-editorial-to-articles.ts).
- **Why it matters:** Google ignores priority and changefreq, and learns to distrust a lastmod that moves without a content change. Accurate lastmod is the one field that helps recrawl of updated reviews, and that matters with 27 URLs in 'Crawled – currently not indexed'.
- **Fix:** Remove priority and changeFrequency from every entry. Give static pages a hard-coded lastModified per page, or read it from each MDX file's front-matter. Add a `contentUpdatedAt` datetime field to the post and article schemas that editors set only on substantive revisions. Use `coalesce(contentUpdatedAt, publishedAt)` for sitemap lastmod, BlogPosting.dateModified (src/lib/schema/article.ts:38), article:modified_time (src/lib/metadata/post.ts:49) and the visible 'Updated' label.
- **Checker's correction:** The static-page lines are sitemap.ts:21-25, not 23-28.

### TECH-08 - 9 dated slugs, 2 slugs with a trailing hyphen and 26 titles with stale 2024/2025 years remain, and nothing in the slug schema prevents new ones
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Sitemap: best-corded-string-trimmers-2024, best-cordless-string-trimmers-2024, best-string-trimmers-2024, best-ice-maker-2024, best-patio-heater-2025, best-micellar-water-2024, best-laser-hair-removal-2024, best-hair-waver-2025, best-waterpik-2024; plus kitchen-appliance/best-stanley-tumbler-/ and egg-bite-maker-/. Live-title crawl: 26 <title>s contain 2024 or 2025, e.g. 'Best Ice Maker 2024 For Making Ice Quickly And Easily' and 'Best Laser Hair Removal 2025 To Get Hair-free Skin' (double space). GSC: /kitchen-appliance/best-stanley-tumbler-/ has 13 impressions at position 68. The slug fields (sanity/schemas/post/index.ts:40-49, article/index.ts:25-33) have only Rule.required().
- **Why it matters:** A year in the slug makes the URL look stale in September 2026 and forces a redirect every year. Stale years in titles lower CTR, and the site's CTR is already 0%.
- **Fix:** Add a validation rule to both slug fields: `Rule.required().custom(s => /^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$/.test(s?.current ?? '') || 'lowercase words separated by single hyphens, no trailing hyphen')`, plus a warning when /-20\d\d$/ matches. Add options.slugify that trims leading and trailing hyphens. Re-slug the 11 URLs to evergreen slugs with next.config.mjs 301s, or merge them into the existing twins (TECH-05). Put a year in seo.meta_title only when the article was actually refreshed that year.

### TECH-10 - Production Sanity reads go through the API CDN, while on-demand revalidation caches the result with no expiry, so a webhook-triggered render can capture stale content
**medium** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** sanity/env.ts:14 `useCdn = process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production'`. sanity/lib/client.ts:39-42 creates the published client with that setting. sanity/lib/fetch.ts:28-33 caches tagged fetches with revalidate:false. The Sanity webhook (src/app/api/revalidate/route.ts) fires right after publish and calls revalidateTag and revalidatePath. The next render then queries apicdn.sanity.io, which can briefly return the pre-publish result.
- **Why it matters:** If the race hits, the old content is frozen in the ISR cache until the next edit. Editors see 'I published but the site didn't change', and IndexNow has already told Bing to recrawl.
- **Fix:** For the server-side Next client, set `useCdn: false`: Next's Data Cache and tags already do the caching, and this is what next-sanity recommends with tag-based revalidation. Keep useCdn true only for any browser-side client. Also set a fixed `apiVersion` such as '2025-02-19' in sanity/env.ts instead of the 2023-06-15 default.
- **Checker's correction:** The race is inferred from the configuration, not observed. The substance matches next-sanity's guidance to disable the CDN when using tag or ISR revalidation.

### TECH-11 - The category <title> is missing a space, and the category H1, WebPage name and ItemList name all repeat the branded title
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/category-seo.ts:37 `(category.seo?.meta_title?.trim() || category.title) + "| " + info.site_name`. src/components/Category/CategoryHeader.tsx:136,161-163 renders that same string as the <h1>. PostsList.tsx:31 and CategoryHeader.tsx:147 put it in the JSON-LD. Live: <title>Skincare Product Reviews &amp; Buying Guides| WhoAdvice</title>, and the category WebPage and ItemList names are the same.
- **Why it matters:** All 7 category hubs show a malformed title in SERPs, and an H1 that ends in the brand reads as boilerplate.
- **Fix:** Split the helper into getCategoryHeading(category) = category.seo?.meta_title?.trim() || category.title, used for the H1 and JSON-LD names, and getCategoryTitle(category) = `${getCategoryHeading(category)} | ${info.site_name}`, used for <title>, og:title and twitter:title. Better still, set `title: { template: '%s | WhoAdvice', default: info.site_title }` once in src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx metadata and return the bare heading from generateCategoryMeta (see TECH-12).
- **Checker's correction:** The line numbers are wrong: CategoryHeader.tsx is 109 lines. The title is at :26, the H1 at :51-52, and the WebPage JSON-LD at :32-43, not :136, :147 or :161-163.

### TECH-13 - Internal search results are indexable with any query in the title, and /search/ is a 307 to the home page that the canonical then points at
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/(frontend)/search/page.tsx:27-62 sets robots index:true, follow:true, nocache:true and canonical `${info.base_url}/search/`. Line 72 `if (!query) redirect('/')` issues a 307. Live: /search/?q=epilator → 200, <title>Search results for epilator</title>, robots 'index, follow, nocache', canonical https://whoadvice.com/search/. /search/?q=zzzqqq → 200, 'Search results for zzzqqq', indexable (a soft 404). /search/ → 307 to https://whoadvice.com/.
- **Why it matters:** Anyone can create unlimited indexable URLs with arbitrary text in the <title> by linking to ?q=spam, which is a known spam and soft-404 vector. The canonical points at a URL that redirects.
- **Fix:** In generateMetadata, return `robots: { index: false, follow: true }` and drop the canonical, both with and without q. Render an empty search form at /search/ instead of redirect('/'), or at least use permanentRedirect. The NoResults branch needs the same noindex. Optionally add `Disallow: /search/?` only after the noindex has been crawled.

### TECH-14 - The 404 page has no <title>, header, footer or links: a dead end for users and crawlers
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/not-found.tsx renders only `<PublicLayout>Not Found</PublicLayout>`, and PublicLayout is just a Container (src/components/Layout/PublicLayout.tsx:9-11). There is no src/app/(frontend)/not-found.tsx, so notFound() inside (frontend) routes falls back to the root boundary, which sits outside the Header and Footer layout. Live https://whoadvice.com/nonexistent-cat/: 404, <meta name="robots" content="noindex"/>, no <title>, and zero <header>, <nav>, <footer> or internal hrefs outside scripts.
- **Why it matters:** GSC reports three 404s, and /garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmer/ gets impressions. Visitors who land on a 404 have nowhere to go, and the lost link equity is not passed on.
- **Fix:** Create src/app/(frontend)/not-found.tsx so it inherits the Header and Footer layout. Give it an H1 ('Page not found'), the search form, links to the 7 categories and the 6 newest posts from getPosts({page:1, limit:6}). Export metadata there too: `{ title: 'Page not found | WhoAdvice', robots: { index:false, follow:true } }`. Keep a minimal root src/app/not-found.tsx with a <title> for truly unmatched paths.

### TECH-15 - The home page has no WebSite or Organization structured data, and every publisher logo points to a URL that returns 404
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Saved home.jsonld.txt contains only ItemList, ListItem, BlogPosting, WebPage, Organization (as a nested publisher), Person and ImageObject. There is no top-level WebSite or Organization node. The publisher logo URL is `${info.base_url}/logo-light.png` in src/lib/schema/article.ts:21 and src/lib/schema/home.ts:30. Live: /logo-light.png → 404, and /images/logo-light.png → 200 image/png.
- **Why it matters:** Google's site name and logo in SERPs come from WebSite and Organization markup on the home page. Every BlogPosting's publisher.logo is broken across roughly 200 articles.
- **Fix:** Add a `src/lib/schema/site.ts` exporting buildOrganizationNode() {"@type":"Organization","@id":`${base}/#organization`,name:'WhoAdvice',url:`${base}/`,logo:{"@type":"ImageObject",url:`${base}/images/logo-light.png`,width,height}} and buildWebSiteNode() {"@type":"WebSite","@id":`${base}/#website`,name:'WhoAdvice',url:`${base}/`,publisher:{"@id":`${base}/#organization`}}. Render both on the home page. In article.ts and home.ts, replace getPublisher() and the inline publisher with `{"@id": `${base}/#organization`}` so the logo path lives in one place. Pick a logo that is at least 112px and not the light-on-dark variant.

### TECH-16 - Roundup pages emit one Product node per item with a Review but no reviewRating, offers or aggregateRating, against the vault's own schema decision
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/schema/post-page.ts:40-48 adds buildProductNode for every non-'editorial' document. src/lib/schema/post.ts:38-69 builds a Review with no reviewRating, and the Product has no offers or aggregateRating. The live epilators JSON-LD has 7 Product and 7 Review nodes, bug-zapper has 6, hedge-trimmers 5. The GSC 'Search appearance' export is empty. The vault audit for this article (articles/epilators/best-epilators-at-home-hair-removal/audit.md, cited in the facts file) says Product rich-result markup is not recommended for this multi-product roundup and hands off Article + BreadcrumbList + ItemList.
- **Why it matters:** Without a rating, these nodes can't qualify for review snippets, and they produce Search Console warnings. Google's guidance reserves product snippets for single-product pages, so the markup adds risk and no benefit.
- **Fix:** For product_guide roundups, replace the Product loop in post-page.ts with one ItemList node: {"@type":"ItemList", itemListElement: products.map((p,i)=>({"@type":"ListItem", position:i+1, name:p.title, url:`${canonicalUrl}#${productAnchor(p)}`}))}, using src/lib/product-anchor.ts for the ids. Keep buildProductNode only for a future single-product review template that has a real reviewRating backed by the editorial scoring.

### TECH-17 - The category breadcrumb JSON-LD points its last item at the home page
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/breadcrumbs/post-breadcrumbs.ts:24-36 gives the category crumb no href. BreadCrumbs.tsx:92-97 calls BreadCrumbsJsonLD({links}) without currentPageUrl, so src/lib/schema/breadcrumbs.ts:27,33 falls back to the home URL. Live category-skin-care JSON-LD: position 2 {"@id":"https://whoadvice.com/","name":"Skincare"}.
- **Why it matters:** The breadcrumb trail tells Google that 'Skincare' is the home page, which breaks breadcrumb rich results on all 7 category hubs.
- **Fix:** In CategoryHeader.tsx:156, pass `<BreadCrumbs links={breadCrumbs} currentPageUrl={getCategoryCanonicalUrl(details)} />`. Add a `currentPageUrl?: string` prop to BreadCrumbs and forward it to BreadCrumbsJsonLD. Also add aria-label="Breadcrumb" to the <nav> and aria-current="page" to the last item.
- **Checker's correction:** The line numbers are wrong: BreadCrumbs.tsx is 67 lines and the JSON-LD call is at :55-61, not :92-97. The CategoryHeader call site is :46, not :156. Also, BreadCrumbs.tsx:19 has a <nav> with no aria-label.

### TECH-18 - Author pages are indexed and in the sitemap, but they are thin, their ItemList URL is wrong, and they have no Person or ProfilePage markup
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/(frontend)/author/[slug]/page.tsx:71-106: title 'Name - WhoAdvice', description `Posts created by ${author.name}.`, robots 'index, follow, nocache'. Line 130: ItemList url `${info.base_url}/${author.slug.current}/` is missing /author/. Live author-emily-cooper.jsonld.txt: "url":"https://whoadvice.com/emily-cooper/", which is a 404 path. The page renders no bio (the query fetches `bio`, lines 114-120, but the page never shows it). The sitemap lists 7 author URLs. These pages are also the only internal path to the 72 orphan URLs (TECH-04).
- **Why it matters:** The author pages are the site's E-E-A-T anchor for a review site. Right now they are post lists with a boilerplate description and broken structured data.
- **Fix:** Fix line 130 to `${info.base_url}/author/${author.slug.current}/`. Render the bio and credentials. Use a description built from the bio, truncated to 155 characters. Emit {"@type":"ProfilePage", mainEntity:{"@type":"Person", name, url, image, description: bio, sameAs:[...], worksFor:{"@id":`${base}/#organization`}}}. Drop 'nocache' (TECH-24). Once pagination exists, cap the post grid, or leave it uncapped, since this is currently a crawl path.

### TECH-20 - og:image width and height are hard-coded to 1280x720 regardless of the real image, and images are served at full original size
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/post-seo.ts:81-93 and src/lib/category-seo.ts:85-97 return {width:1280, height:720}. sanity/lib/image.ts:15-17 builds `.auto('format').fit('max')` with no width. Live epilators og:image is a 1448x1086 JPEG declared as 1280x720. The skin-care og:image is ...-1000x1000.jpg, also declared 1280x720.
- **Why it matters:** Platforms that trust the declared size crop or letterbox the preview wrongly. Unsized originals also make the social image heavier than needed.
- **Fix:** In getPostOgImageMeta and getCategoryOgImageMeta, build the URL as `urlForImage(src).width(1200).height(630).fit('crop').auto('format').url()` and return width 1200, height 630, plus `alt: post.title`.
- **Checker's correction:** urlForImage is at sanity/lib/image.ts:11-13, not :15-17.

### TECH-21 - images.unoptimized: true, a no-op formatImage and width-less Sanity URLs mean no responsive images anywhere
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** next.config.mjs `images: { unoptimized: true, ...}`. src/lib/format-images.ts:2-4 returns src unchanged. PostCard.tsx and CategoryHeader.tsx:198-206 pass width={1280} height={960} with a full-size Sanity URL. Measured facts: product images are 1254x1254 (106 KB) rendered at 346x346, and there is no srcset on the page.
- **Why it matters:** Mobile users download images 3 to 10 times larger than they need, which hurts LCP and crawl efficiency on image-heavy roundups.
- **Fix:** Keep Vercel Image Optimization off, since that is presumably the reason for unoptimized, and use a custom loader instead. In next.config.mjs set `images: { loader: 'custom', loaderFile: './src/lib/sanity-image-loader.ts' }` with `export default function loader({src,width,quality}){ const u=new URL(src); u.searchParams.set('w',String(width)); u.searchParams.set('q',String(quality??75)); u.searchParams.set('auto','format'); u.searchParams.set('fit','max'); return u.toString(); }`. Remove unoptimized and add a `sizes` prop to each <Image> (for example sizes="(min-width:1024px) 616px, 100vw" for product cards). Delete formatImage.
- **Checker's correction:** PostCard uses height 720, not 960. The CategoryHeader image is at :89-95, not :198-206.

### TECH-09 - generateStaticParams returns [], so after every deploy each article's first request is a cold render
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** src/app/(frontend)/[categorySlug]/[slug]/page.tsx:38-40 `return [];`. On Vercel, a new deployment starts with an empty ISR cache for on-demand paths. Measured facts: TTFB 916 ms from Pakistan on a cache HIT (Age ≈ 6 days). A cold render adds two to three Sanity round trips first: getArticle, then getPost, then getSimilarPosts, then the layout's getSiteLayout.
- **Why it matters:** After each deploy, Googlebot and Bingbot, which IndexNow has just pinged, meet the slowest version of every page. That wastes crawl budget, and deploys are frequent. There are only about 209 content URLs, so prerendering them all is cheap.
- **Fix:** Return real params: `export async function generateStaticParams(){ const posts = await getAllPosts(); return (posts ?? []).filter(p => p.categories?.slug?.current).map(p => ({ categorySlug: p.categories.slug.current, slug: p.slug.current })); }`. Keep dynamicParams true so new articles still render on demand. Do the same for [categorySlug]/page.tsx using getAllCategories, and for author/[slug] using getAllAuthor.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to low. The 'deploys are frequent' premise is not supported: 7 commits in six weeks, clustered on two days, and a 6-day-old cache entry. Cold renders after a deploy are real but rare. That Vercel starts each new deployment with an empty ISR cache was not verified here.

### TECH-12 - Post titles have no brand suffix and the site has no title template. Only 2 of ~202 post titles carry the brand, typed by hand into meta_title
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/post-seo.ts:42-44 `return post.seo?.meta_title?.trim() || post.title;`. There is no `title.template` in src/app/layout.tsx or src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx. In the live crawl, 22 titles contain 'WhoAdvice': the static, category and author pages plus exactly two posts, /garden-tools/best-hedge-trimmers/ and /garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmers/, whose meta_title includes ' | WhoAdvice'. Author titles use ' - WhoAdvice' (author/[slug]/page.tsx:72), and marketing pages use ' | ' (metadata/marketing.ts:16).
- **Why it matters:** Brand recognition in SERPs is inconsistent, and editors have to remember to type the suffix, which leads to duplicated or missing brands.
- **Fix:** Add `export const metadata = { title: { template: '%s | WhoAdvice', default: info.site_title } }` to src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx. Make getPostTitle, getCategoryTitle, the author title and generateMarketingMetadata return bare titles. The homepage should use `title: { absolute: info.site_title }`. Strip ' | WhoAdvice' from the two hedge-trimmer meta_title values. Add a Sanity validation warning on seo.meta_title when it contains 'WhoAdvice' or exceeds about 55 characters.
- **Checker's correction:** The author title line is :24, not :72. Severity lowered to low: a brand suffix is a consistency and CTR nicety, not a ranking defect, and Google often appends the site name itself.

### TECH-22 - The home and author pages point twitter:image at /og.png, which returns 404. The home page and marketing pages have no og:image at all
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/metadata/home.ts:33 and author/[slug]/page.tsx:94 set images: ['/og.png']. The public/ folder contains only 90af6523ccc1446cadb860608cc0fbf5.txt, ads.txt and images/. Live /og.png → 404. The saved home and about HTML have no og:image, and the about page declares twitter:card summary_large_image with no image.
- **Why it matters:** Every share of the home, about or author pages renders without an image.
- **Fix:** Add a 1200x630 public/og.png, or better, src/app/opengraph-image.png so Next injects it on every route without an override. Reference it from homeMetaData.openGraph.images and generateMarketingMetadata.
