### TECH-23 - Post Open Graph tags are missing og:site_name and og:locale, and locale uses an invalid format elsewhere
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/metadata/post.ts:46-57 has no siteName or locale. The saved epilators.html has no og:site_name or og:locale. Home, category, author and marketing pages emit og:locale 'en-US' (e.g. metadata/home.ts:24). OG expects language_TERRITORY, 'en_US'.
- **Why it matters:** This is minor social-preview hygiene, but the pages are the site's most shared, and the site name is missing on them.
- **Fix:** Add `siteName: info.site_name, locale: 'en_US'` to openGraph in post.ts, and change every 'en-US' to 'en_US' in metadata/home.ts, category.ts, marketing.ts, author/[slug]/page.tsx and search/page.tsx. Better, set openGraph defaults once in the (frontend) layout metadata.

### TECH-24 - The 'nocache' robots directive on author, about/marketing and search pages is not a Google directive
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/metadata/marketing.ts:42, author/[slug]/page.tsx:100 and search/page.tsx:194 set `nocache: true`. Live about.html and author-emily-cooper.html: <meta name="robots" content="index, follow, nocache"/>.
- **Why it matters:** Google ignores it: its supported equivalent was noarchive, and the cache feature is retired. Bing treats nocache like noarchive. So it does nothing useful and only suggests the pages are special. Harmless, but noise.
- **Fix:** Remove `nocache: true` from all three places. Also remove the redundant `googleBot` blocks, which only repeat index/follow.
- **Checker's correction:** The lines are author :52 and search :56, not :100 and :194.

### TECH-25 - BlogPosting headline uses the SEO title rather than the visible H1, and FAQ answers carry raw editor HTML including empty paragraphs
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/schema/article.ts:33-34: headline = getPostTitle(post), which is seo.meta_title; name = post.title. Live epilators: headline 'Best Epilators for Hair Removal At Home' but H1 'Best Epilators for Smooth Hair Removal at Home'. src/lib/schema/post.ts:96 sets Answer.text = portableTextToHTML(faq.answer), and the live output contains <p><br/></p>.
- **Why it matters:** Google compares headline with the on-page title, so a mismatch is a weak quality signal. Empty markup in answers is sloppy and can cause validator warnings.
- **Fix:** Set `headline: post.title` (the H1), truncated to 110 characters, and drop `name`. In buildFaqPageNode, use a sanitized HTML variant that strips empty <p> and <br> runs, or plain text from src/lib/portable-text-plain.ts. Keep only <p>, <ul>, <ol>, <li>, <a>, <b>, <strong>, <i>, <em>, <br>.

### TECH-26 - ItemList JSON-LD on home, category and author pages embeds full BlogPosting objects with a nested @context, and numberOfItems counts only the rendered page
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/schema/home.ts:37-45 uses item: ArticleJsonLD(post), which includes '@context'. Live category-skin-care.jsonld.txt shows {"@type":"ListItem","position":1,"item":{"@context":"https://schema.org","@type":"BlogPosting",... publisher, author ...}} repeated 13 times. numberOfItems = posts.length, which is 20 on kitchen-appliance, where the category has 68.
- **Why it matters:** This bloats the HTML and uses an unusual shape. Google's carousel guidance for summary pages expects ListItems carrying only position and url.
- **Fix:** In ListJsonLD, emit `{"@type":"ListItem", position, url: getPostCanonicalUrl(post), name: post.title}` and drop the nested ArticleJsonLD. Leave numberOfItems out, or set it to pagination.total.

### TECH-27 - A product webhook invalidates every post, article and listing cache site-wide
**low** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/revalidate-content.ts:169-196 (revalidateProductRoutes) always calls revalidateTags([product, article, post, content]) before the per-route loop. Every getPost, getArticle, getPosts and getSimilarPosts fetch carries one of those tags (requests.ts:43,66,138-143; requests/posts.ts:267-272), and so do the sitemap fetches.
- **Why it matters:** It works: articles are refreshed when a product changes, via the product tag and revalidatePath. But any bulk product update, such as Amazon link or price maintenance, becomes a full-site cold cache and a burst of Sanity reads.
- **Fix:** Tag article fetches with each referenced product id (for example tags: [slug, `product:${id}`...]; after the first fetch, add them with a cacheTag call or a second tagged fetch). In revalidateProductRoutes, call only revalidateTag(`product:${productId}`) plus the resolved dependent route slugs, and drop the global post, article and content tags.
- **Checker's correction:** The lines are 105-132, not 169-196 (the file is 170 lines). Also cite requests/posts.ts:52-66, not :267-272. The impact is overstated: with 'max' this is not a 'full-site cold cache'. Stale pages keep serving and each regenerates lazily on its next visit, so the cost is extra Sanity reads spread over time, plus one stale serve per page. The same global invalidation also happens on every content publish, not only on product edits (see TECH-V01).

### TECH-28 - revalidateTag(tag, 'max') is stale-while-revalidate, so the first request after a publish can still get old HTML. IndexNow pings Bing at that moment
**low** · effort S · hypothesis

- **Evidence:** src/lib/revalidate-content.ts:93 `revalidateTag(tag, "max")`. Lines 126-129 call bingIndexNow immediately after revalidateContent. Pages refreshed only through tags, not revalidatePath, include author pages, other articles' 'Related posts' and the sitemap. In Next 16, the 'max' profile serves stale content once and refreshes in the background.
- **Why it matters:** Bingbot, prompted by IndexNow, may fetch the stale version of a dependent page. revalidatePath on the article itself probably covers the main URL. I have not verified this on the running site.
- **Fix:** In a Route Handler, call `revalidateTag(tag, { expire: 0 })` for immediate expiry (Next 16 API), or keep 'max' and delay IndexNow until the pages are warm. For example, after revalidation, fetch the post URL once server-side with `await fetch(postUrl, {cache:'no-store'})`, then submit to IndexNow.
- **Checker's correction:** The lines are :29 and :62-65, not :93 and :126-129. The tag half is documented behaviour, so the confidence can rise from hypothesis to likely. Only the exact first-visit behaviour of revalidatePath on this deployment remains unmeasured.

### TECH-29 - IndexNow is wired and the key file is served, but a failed ping turns a successful revalidation into a 500, and noindex documents get submitted too
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/config/site-info.ts:11-12 (host whoadvice.com, key 90af6523...). public/90af6523ccc1446cadb860608cc0fbf5.txt is live and returns the key (200, verified). src/lib/index-now.ts:7-17 calls fetch with no try/catch. revalidateContent awaits it (revalidate-content.ts:126), and api/revalidate/route.ts:256-269 turns any throw into a 500, which makes Sanity retry the webhook. There is no check of seo.indexable before submitting.
- **Why it matters:** A Bing outage causes webhook retries and duplicate cache purges. Submitting noindex URLs wastes Bing's crawl on pages you exclude. Bing is where this site's clicks come from.
- **Fix:** Wrap the body of bingIndexNow in try/catch and log failures without rethrowing. Use api.indexnow.org/indexnow, which fans out to all participating engines. Add `keyLocation: `${base}/${key}.txt``. Skip submission when the webhook projection shows seo.indexable == false: add `"indexable": seo.indexable` to the Sanity webhook projection and pass it through handleRevalidateByType.
- **Checker's correction:** Only a network-level rejection (DNS failure, timeout, connection reset) throws and turns the webhook into a 500. A Bing HTTP error status (4xx/5xx) is logged and does not fail the webhook. The line is revalidate-content.ts:62, not :126. Every IndexNow endpoint already shares submissions with the other participating engines, so switching to api.indexnow.org is optional.

### TECH-32 - Search pagination drops one result per page (off-by-one with GROQ's exclusive range)
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/requests/search.ts:51-52 `const end = start + limit - 1;` used as `[$start...$end]` (line 29). In GROQ, '...' excludes the end index, so page 1 returns items 0–48 (49 items), and page 2 starts at 50, so item 49 is never shown. src/requests/posts.ts uses `[$offset...$limit]` with limit = offset+limit, which is correct.
- **Why it matters:** Search results silently skip documents. This is a small UX defect.
- **Fix:** Change to `const end = start + limit;` or use the inclusive `..` operator.

### TECH-33 - The unexplained /aadf8c3443d2e00c/script.js is Vercel Web Analytics served from a randomized first-party path. It appears nowhere in the repo
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** grep over src, sanity, next.config.mjs and sanity.config.ts finds no 'aadf8c3443d2e00c', and there is no vercel.json. The live script body contains the string `"/_vercel/insights"` and uses navigator.webdriver/Headless bot filtering. package.json:43 has "@vercel/analytics": "^2.0.1", and <Analytics /> is rendered in src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx:57. The saved page HTML does not contain the tag, so it is injected client-side.
- **Why it matters:** It is not a foreign or malicious script, but it is a third analytics beacon alongside GA4 and Clarity with no consent handling, and it was unexplained.
- **Fix:** Document it in AGENTS.md as Vercel Web Analytics (the randomized path is set by Vercel). If it is redundant with GA4, remove <Analytics /> from src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx and the dependency. List all three trackers in /cookies/.
- **Checker's correction:** The <Analytics /> line is :42, not :57.

### TECH-34 - A <meta name=keywords> tag is emitted from CMS keywords, and twitter:creator uses an unverified handle in two spellings
**low** · effort S · hypothesis

- **Evidence:** src/lib/metadata/post.ts:31,44 and category.ts:17,30 emit keywords, and live epilators shows 7 phrases. twitter:creator is `@${info.site_name}` = '@WhoAdvice' in post.ts:62, category.ts:49, home.ts:32 and the author page, but '@whoadvice' in marketing.ts:9. I did not verify whether either account exists or belongs to the site.
- **Why it matters:** Google ignores meta keywords, and the tag reveals the target phrases to competitors. Attributing cards to an account that isn't the site's own is a trust issue.
- **Fix:** Remove `keywords` from generateContentMeta and generateCategoryMeta. Keep the Sanity field for editorial use and the article:tag output only if it is wanted. Confirm the X handle; if the site has none, remove `creator`. Otherwise set `twitter: { site: '@<handle>' }` once in the (frontend) layout.
- **Checker's correction:** The keywords and handle-spelling parts are verified. Only the account's existence and ownership stay hypothesis.

### TECH-35 - JSON-LD is serialized with raw JSON.stringify, with no escaping of '<'
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/schema/json-ld-script.tsx:7, PostJsonLD.tsx:16-18, CategoryHeader.tsx:145-151 and BreadCrumbs.tsx:96 all use `dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: JSON.stringify(data) }}`. The payload includes CMS HTML (FAQ answers from portableTextToHTML, and product descriptions that fall back to HTML in post.ts:17).
- **Why it matters:** A '</script>' sequence in any CMS text would end the script block early. That breaks the markup, and with user-influenced content it becomes an XSS vector.
- **Fix:** In JsonLdScript, serialize with `JSON.stringify(data).replace(/</g, '\\u003c')` and route every JSON-LD emitter (PostJsonLD, CategoryHeader, BreadCrumbs) through JsonLdScript.
- **Checker's correction:** The CategoryHeader line is :34-42, not :145-151, and the BreadCrumbs line is :59, not :96. The content comes from logged-in CMS editors, so the risk is breakage rather than a realistic XSS vector unless editor accounts are compromised.

### TECH-V01 - Every single content publish marks the whole site's data stale, not just the edited article
**low** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** src/lib/revalidate-content.ts:43-51. revalidateContent calls revalidateTags([content, post, article, category, author, slug, categorySlug]) on every post or article webhook. getPosts (requests/posts.ts:52-66), getSimilarPosts (requests.ts:138-143), getAllPosts (requests.ts:176), search (search.ts:33-37) and getCategory/getPostByAuthor all carry one of those global tags. So one typo fix on one article invalidates every listing, every article's related-posts fetch, the sitemap and all search results.
- **Why it matters:** With the 'max' profile each affected page serves stale content once and then regenerates on its next visit. After any publish, crawlers that hit other pages first get stale HTML while Sanity reads multiply. Precise tags would let the edited URL, its category and its author refresh without touching ~220 unrelated pages.
- **Fix:** In revalidateContent, drop CACHE_TAGS.content, post, article and author from the list. Revalidate only slug, categorySlug and the author's slug tag (add the author slug to the webhook projection), plus revalidatePath('/'), the category path and the article path. Give getAllPosts (sitemap) its own 'sitemap' tag and revalidate that tag too. Keep the global tags for bulk operations only, for example a manual 'flush all' button.

### TECH-V03 - Neither the root nor the (frontend) layout exports default metadata, so any route that forgets generateMetadata ships without a title, description or OG defaults
**low** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** src/app/layout.tsx:1-15 and src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx:1-45 export no `metadata`. The results are visible live: /studio/ and every 404 page have no <title>, and posts get no og:site_name or og:locale (TECH-23), because each page builds its whole Metadata object by hand with duplicated values (home.ts, category.ts, marketing.ts, author and search pages).
- **Why it matters:** Every new route or boundary starts from zero SEO metadata. The per-page copies have already drifted: the '@whoadvice' vs '@WhoAdvice' spelling, missing siteName on posts, and ' - ' vs ' | ' title separators.
- **Fix:** Export one `metadata` from src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx with metadataBase: new URL(info.base_url), title: { template: '%s | WhoAdvice', default: info.site_title }, description: info.site_desc, openGraph: { siteName, locale: 'en_US', type: 'website', images: ['/og.png'] }, twitter: { card: 'summary_large_image' } and robots: { index: true, follow: true }. Then delete the repeated fields from each generate*Meta helper. This subsumes the fixes for TECH-12, TECH-22 and TECH-23.

## Search Console and Bing data

### DATA-01 - Google sends no traffic at all: 0 clicks from 481 impressions in 92 days, and most named-query impressions sit beyond page 5
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Performance/Chart.csv: 92 rows, clicks sum 0, impressions sum 481, 84 of 92 days with at least one impression. Queries.csv: 113 queries with 172 impressions (35.8% of 481; the other 64% are anonymised). 94 of those queries (148 impressions, 86.0%) average position >50, and only 7 queries (7 impressions) sit at position 20 or better. The only one in the top 10 is 'site:whoadvice.com' (pos 1). The top query by impressions is 'best epilator for chefs', 7 impressions at pos 208.43. Pages.csv: 121 URLs. Page-level impressions total 731 across position bands: <=10: 55 pages/370; 11-20: 16/75; 21-50: 28/90; >50: 22/196.
- **Why it matters:** The site has no organic revenue from Google. Almost every Google impression is either anonymised long tail or a result so deep that no human sees it. Planning built on 'we rank for X' queries in GSC would optimise noise.
- **Fix:** Work only on URLs that already reach page 1: the 55 page-level URLs at avg position <=10, above all garden-tools, best-smart-plugs and the dated-title pages (DATA-05, DATA-06, DATA-07, DATA-08). Use Bing as the second channel (DATA-09). Measure success in clicks, not impressions. Before any prune or merge, export 16 months of GSC Pages and Pages x Queries (the Search Console API gives more than the UI's 1,000-row, anonymised cut) so decisions don't rest on a 3-month, 36%-visible sample.

### DATA-02 - /garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmer/ ranked at position 6.27 and now returns 404 with no redirect
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Pages.csv row: 'https://whoadvice.com/garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmer/,0,11,0%,6.27'. The URL is not in sitemap-urls.txt. Live check 2026-09-25 (browser UA): /garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmer/ 404. next.config.mjs has 16 redirects, none for this source. Its successor, /garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmers/ (sitemap lastmod 2026-08-06, title '5 Best Corded Hedge Trimmers for 2026 | WhoAdvice'), has 1 impression at pos 22. GSC Coverage lists 3 'Not found (404)' with validation 'Started'.
- **Why it matters:** The corded hedge-trimmer page was one of only ~20 URLs on the site with page-1 Google visibility. The slug change threw that equity away and left a 404. This is almost certainly one of the 3 GSC 404s.
- **Fix:** Add to next.config.mjs `redirects`: { source: '/garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmer/', destination: '/garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmers/', permanent: true }. Deploy, run URL Inspection on the old URL, and continue the 404 validation in GSC. Also export the Coverage 'Not found (404)' example URLs (the export supplied has counts only) and redirect the other two to their nearest live equivalents.
- **Checker's correction:** Downgraded from critical to high. The page lost 11 impressions in 3 months with 0 clicks. That is real equity to recover with a one-line redirect, but it does not block the site's traffic or revenue. 'Almost certainly one of the 3 404s' remains an inference, because the Coverage export has counts only.

### DATA-03 - Legacy /best-cordless-hedge-trimmer/ (position 4) and the new /best-cordless-hedge-trimmers/ (0 impressions) are both live and self-canonical
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Pages.csv: '/garden-tools/best-cordless-hedge-trimmer/,0,11,0%,4'. /garden-tools/best-cordless-hedge-trimmers/ has no row (0 impressions). Live 2026-09-25: both return 200. The legacy page has title 'Best Cordless Hedge Trimmer For Your Lawn Care' and canonical to itself. The new page has title '5 Best Cordless Hedge Trimmers for Homeowners in 2026' and canonical to itself. Both are in the sitemap (lines 44 and 35). The vault's articles/hedge-trimmers/audits/cluster-audit.md line 27 says 'do not leave multiple indexable versions'.
- **Why it matters:** Google keeps ranking the stale legacy page and gives the researched 2026 page nothing. Readers who click land on the unrefreshed product set.
- **Fix:** Add a 308 in next.config.mjs from /garden-tools/best-cordless-hedge-trimmer/ to /garden-tools/best-cordless-hedge-trimmers/. Unpublish the legacy Sanity post document so it drops out of sitemap.ts's getAllPosts. Then request indexing for /garden-tools/best-cordless-hedge-trimmers/ and /garden-tools/best-hedge-trimmers/ in URL Inspection.
- **Checker's correction:** The new page was published on 2026-09-18, five days before the GSC window closed (2026-09-23), so its 0 impressions are expected. They are not evidence that Google 'gives the researched 2026 page nothing'. The defect is the two live, self-canonical, indexable duplicates, and the redirect fix stands.

### DATA-04 - The vault's publish workflow never inventories live legacy slugs, so the hedge-trimmer launch collided with three ranking URLs
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** cluster-audit.md line 27 only plans redirects for hypothetical '/home-garden/' or '/outdoor/' paths. grep of articles/hedge-trimmers finds no mention of best-cordless-hedge-trimmer/, best-corded-hedge-trimmer/ or best-hedge-trimmer-2025. The redirect for best-hedge-trimmer-2025 (Pages.csv: 18 impressions at pos 4, the site's best-positioned article URL) arrived only in commit 900ba2f on 2026-09-17, about 3.5 weeks after the hub went live (2026-08-24 per the lead's facts). The corded legacy URL 404s (DATA-02) and the cordless legacy URL still competes (DATA-03). publisher-cms-guide.md line 37 lists slug/canonical fields but no legacy-URL step.
- **Why it matters:** Every cluster the vault publishes next (bug zappers, string trimmers, epilators) has legacy near-duplicate URLs with impressions. Without a mandatory mapping step, each launch repeats this loss.
- **Fix:** Add a required 'Legacy URL map' section to the publisher-handoff.md template and to the whoadvice-article-workflow skill. Before publish, list every sitemap URL and every GSC Pages.csv URL whose slug shares the head term (for example grep 'hedge-trimmer'). Mark each one keep / 308-to / unpublish. The handoff cannot be marked ready until the next.config.mjs redirects are merged in the same deploy as the new article.

### DATA-06 - Four string-trimmer URLs, all on page 1 and all titled 2024 or 2025, split one intent
**high** · effort L · verified

- **Evidence:** Pages.csv: /garden-tools/best-string-trimmer/ 12 at 5.58, /garden-tools/best-string-trimmers-2024/ 10 at 6.8, /garden-tools/best-cordless-string-trimmers-2024/ 11 at 9, /garden-tools/best-corded-string-trimmers-2024/ 10 at 8.6. Live titles: 'Best String Trimmer 2025 For Weed and Grass Maintenance', 'Best String Trimmers 2024 For Easy Lawn Trimming', 'Best Cordless String Trimmers 2024 For Lawn Care', 'Best Corded String Trimmers 2024 for Your Lawn Care'. /garden-tools/best-string-trimmers/ returns 404. GSC query 'best weed eater trimmer' 1 at 19.
- **Why it matters:** Two generic 'best string trimmer(s)' pages compete for the same query. All four show a stale year in September 2026 SERPs. This is the second-strongest page-1 cluster on the site and has 0 clicks.
- **Fix:** Run the vault workflow for a string-trimmer cluster that mirrors hedge trimmers. The hub goes at /garden-tools/best-string-trimmers/ with 308s from /best-string-trimmer/ and /best-string-trimmers-2024/. Branches go at /garden-tools/best-cordless-string-trimmers/ and /garden-tools/best-corded-string-trimmers/ with 308s from the -2024 slugs. Add all redirects to next.config.mjs in the same deploy (DATA-04). Until the refresh ships, remove the year from each seo.meta_title rather than bumping it (DATA-08).

### DATA-07 - best-smart-plugs holds 15.6% of all page impressions at position 8.6 but serves a 2024 page with a definition as its snippet
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Pages.csv: '/home-gadgets/best-smart-plugs/,0,114,0%,8.63', which is 114 of 731 page-level impressions. No smart-plug query appears among the 113 exported queries, so all of its queries are anonymised long tail. Live 2026-09-25: title 'Best Smart Plugs to Control Your Devices Remotely'. Meta description: 'Smart plugs allow users to control and monitor their devices remotely through a smartphone or other connected devices.' JSON-LD datePublished 2023-11-30, dateModified 2024-04-22. Hero shows 'Updated Apr 2024'; product cards show 'Updated: Nov 2023'. Top picks: Kasa Smart HS103P4, GHome Smart WP3-4, Kasa Smart KP303. Sitemap lastmod 2024-04-22.
- **Why it matters:** This is the site's single biggest Google asset. The snippet gives no reason to click: no count, no year, no named pick, and a dated byline. Caveat: 0 clicks from 114 impressions at an average of 8.6 is within noise (expected clicks at that position are low single digits), so the ranking is the opportunity, not proof that the snippet failed.
- **Fix:** Make it the first home-gadgets package in the vault workflow: re-verify the product set, write sourced copy, and build a new publisher-handoff. Meanwhile set seo.meta_title to name the count and use case (no year until the refresh ships). Rewrite seo.meta_description to state what the reader gets (named top picks, energy-monitoring and outdoor options) without claims the page can't support.

### DATA-08 - 26 of 222 titles carry 2024 or 2025, and those pages hold 16.7% of page impressions (64 at page-1 positions)
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Live <title> fetch of all 222 sitemap URLs on 2026-09-25: 26 titles contain 2024/2025, only 3 contain 2026. The 19 dated-title URLs with GSC rows total 122 impressions (weighted pos 28.6), 10 of them at pos <=10 with 64 impressions. Examples: 'Best Hair Straighteners 2024 For Sleek and Frizz-Free Hair' (13 at 21.92), '10 Best Space Heaters 2025 to Warm You in Winters' (10 at 7.6), 'Best Laser Hair Removal 2024 For Smooth And Silky Skin' (7 at 6.57), 'Best Laser Hair Removal 2025 To Get Hair-free Skin' (double space), plus the four string-trimmer titles.
- **Why it matters:** A past year in a September 2026 product-roundup snippet tells searchers the page is stale. It suppresses CTR exactly on the page-1 impressions the site has.
- **Fix:** In Sanity, strip the year from seo.meta_title (and the H1 title where it repeats) on all 26 documents. Re-add a year only through the vault refresh that re-verifies products; never bump '2024' to '2026' without refreshed content, per the evidence rule. Separately, 9 slugs carry a year (best-*-2024, best-*-2025): leave those slugs until each page is refreshed or merged, then 308 them to year-free slugs.

### DATA-09 - Bing shows the site 3.9x more than Google, and epilator queries are 65% of Bing keyword impressions, still served by the unpublished legacy page
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Bing SearchPerformanceOverview: 91 days, 1,876 impressions, 4 clicks. Monthly impressions per day: Jun 20.3, Jul 21.4, Aug 18.9, Sep 21.9, which is stable. Google over the same window: 481, falling from 6.6/day in Aug to 3.4/day in Sep. Bing KeywordReport: 244 rows, 453 impressions, 3 clicks. Epilator-family queries: 111 rows, 293 impressions (64.7%), impression-weighted pos 7.2, 0 clicks; 'epilator hair removal' 131 at 7.87; 'epilator' 9 at 9.67; 'best epilators for home use' 5 at 8; 'number one best epilator' 5 at 9. Google for the same page: /skin-care/best-epilators/ 15 at 14.27. The vault package best-epilators-at-home-hair-removal (3,861 words, SEO title 'Best Epilator in 2026: 7 Picks for At-Home Hair Removal') is not live.
- **Why it matters:** The epilator pillar is the page search engines most associate with the site, and Bing already puts it on page 1. It is the cheapest route to first clicks, but the version shown is the legacy article the vault's own audit rejected (stale products, IPL devices in the table).
- **Fix:** Publish the vault's approved article to /skin-care/best-epilators/ using the handoff's SEO title, meta description and ranking. Set a single visible 'Updated' date. Then verify in Bing Webmaster URL Inspection and use IndexNow (Bing supports it; add a key file and ping it on Sanity publish via the existing /api/revalidate webhook) so Bing re-crawls within days. Track 'epilator hair removal' CTR in the Bing keyword report weekly.
- **Checker's correction:** The 64.7% share depends on counting misspelled variants as epilator queries. A strict 'epilat' match gives 276 impressions (60.9%). The conclusion is unchanged.

### DATA-05 - Garden-tools is the only category with real Google pull, but its impressions are split across duplicates and dated URLs
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Pages.csv aggregated by category (impressions, impression-weighted position). garden-tools: 9 URLs, 96 impressions, weighted pos 6.1, 95 of 96 impressions at pos <=10. home-gadgets: 200 impressions at 17.4, of which 114 are best-smart-plugs alone. skin-care: 105 at 35.4. kitchen-appliance: 78 at 51.5, only 6 at pos <=10 despite 67 sitemap URLs. hair-grooming: 76 at 37.4. personal-care: 23 at 44.7. beauty: 2. Inside garden-tools: string trimmers 4 URLs, 43 impressions; hedge trimmers 4 URLs (2 dead or redirected), 41 impressions; robotic-lawn-mower 12 at 5.08.
- **Why it matters:** The next editorial refresh should go where Google already puts the site on page 1. Kitchen-appliance, the largest category, has almost no pull, so refreshing it first would be the lowest-return work.
- **Fix:** Order the vault's next clusters as: (1) finish hedge trimmers (DATA-02/03), (2) string trimmers (DATA-06), (3) robotic lawn mower refresh (/garden-tools/robotic-lawn-mower/: title 'Robotic Lawn Mower For Easy Lawn Maintenance', dateModified 2025-09-01), then (4) the epilator pillar for Bing (DATA-09). Put kitchen-appliance refreshes behind the pruning gate in DATA-15.
- **Checker's correction:** 'The only category with real Google pull' is overstated. home-gadgets has 160 impressions at position <=10, more than garden-tools' 95, but 114 of them come from best-smart-plugs alone. Garden-tools is the only category whose page-1 pull is spread across most of its URLs. Downgraded to medium because this is a prioritisation observation, not a defect.

### DATA-10 - Bing's long conversational epilator prompts ask for need-to-model answers that neither the live page nor the vault draft gives directly
**medium** · effort M · likely

- **Evidence:** KeywordReport: 75 of 244 rows (99 impressions) are 8+ words or end in '?'; 30 of those rows (46 impressions) are epilator queries. Examples: 'recommend me epilator is good for removing the underarm hairs and also facial hair for woman...' 3 at 7; 'what epilator is easy enough for everyday use without requiring a complicated maintenance routine?' 3 at 6.7; 'what's the best beginner-friendly epilator for someone who has never epilated before?' 2 at 2.5; 'which affordable epilator is actually worth the price for leg and underarm use?' 1 at 6; 'what's the simplest epilator to maintain hygiene on a weekly basis?' 1 at 4; 'epilator that has a hair compartment' 6 at 4; 'best epilator device for blondes' 1 at 10. grep on live epilators.txt: 'compartment' 0, 'collect' 0, 'maintenance' 0. Vault article awards: 'Best Overall', 'Best Streamlined ProGuide Kit', 'Best Wet/Dry Braun Value'. Its FAQ (lines 307-329) covers ingrowns, wet/dry, breakage, pain, face/underarm/bikini and wet/dry value, but not beginners, low-maintenance cleaning, hair collection or light hair.
- **Why it matters:** These are Copilot-style prompts. AI answer surfaces quote pages that map a stated need directly to a named model with one reason and one limitation. The page ranks for the prompts but has no extractable answer to them.
- **Fix:** Add a 'Which epilator fits your situation?' block to the vault article, directly after the top-3 picks. Give 5-6 one-sentence answers, each mapping a need (first-time user; underarms plus face; easiest to clean weekly; legs on a budget; light or fine hair) to one ranked model, with its reason and its limit. Every claim must come from the products/*.md files and manuals. Where the evidence doesn't support an answer (for example, whether any ranked model collects hair in a compartment), say so plainly. Rephrase 2-3 FAQ H3s as the actual prompts ('What is the best epilator for beginners?', 'Which epilator is easiest to clean?').
- **Checker's correction:** Downgraded to medium: the long epilator prompts total about 42-46 impressions in 3 months. The finding's own evidence shows the pages rank for these prompts. Whether answer surfaces prefer need-to-model blocks is inference, so confidence 'likely' is correct.

The 3-month export satisfies neither, and it cannot see traffic that the older /skin-care/best-braun-epilator/ URL earned before the redirect. The 3-month data points to 'redirect', but the gate should be closed with the 16-month export and a backlink check first.

### DATA-14 - Near-duplicate URLs show up in the same SERPs: page-level impressions are 1.52x property impressions, and six bug-zapper URLs each got exactly 2
**medium** · effort L · likely

- **Evidence:** Page-level impressions total 731 vs property-level 481, i.e. 1.52 site URLs per impression. Bug zappers: best-bug-zapper 2 at 1.5, best-outdoor-bug-zapper 2 at 4, best-flowtron-bug-zapper 2 at 4.5, best-indoor-bug-zapper 2 at 5.5, best-solar-bug-zapper 2 at 6.5, best-bug-zapper-racket 9 at 6.11. Other groups: kitchen scales (best-kitchen-scale 2 at 15, best-digital-kitchen-scale 1 at 3, best-mini-kitchen-scale 1 at 10, best-nutritional-kitchen-scale 1 at 37); mini fridges (best-mini-fridge-with-freezer 4 at 56.75, best-mini-fridge 1 at 52, latest-mini-fridge 1 at 9); heaters (best-space-heater 10 at 7.6, best-indoor-electric-heater 2 at 72.5, best-electric-heater 0, best-electric-patio-heater 1 at 25, best-patio-heater-2025 0); hair dryers (best-blow-dryer 0, best-blow-dryers 1 at 8, best-hair-dryers 0); ice makers (best-ice-maker 1 at 11, best-ice-maker-2024 0); micellar (best-micellar-water-2024 0, best-garnier-micellar-water 0).
- **Why it matters:** Identical small counts across sibling URLs mean Google is testing several WhoAdvice pages for the same query and committing to none. Consolidating concentrates the signal.
- **Fix:** Merge map, each merge = one refreshed survivor plus 308s in next.config.mjs plus unpublishing the losers. best-blow-dryer and best-hair-dryers go to best-blow-dryers. best-ice-maker-2024 goes to best-ice-maker. best-patio-heater-2025 goes to best-electric-patio-heater. best-electric-heater and best-indoor-electric-heater go to best-space-heater. best-micellar-water-2024 goes to best-garnier-micellar-water (Bing shows Garnier-specific demand: 9 micellar rows, all Garnier-worded). The four kitchen scales become best-kitchen-scale plus at most one mini/precision branch. The three mini fridges become best-mini-fridge plus best-mini-fridge-with-freezer. Do bug zappers when the vault's bug-zapper cluster publishes, using its Legacy URL map (DATA-04).
- **Checker's correction:** The merge map would create a redirect chain. next.config.mjs:5-6 already sends /hair-grooming/best-hair-dryer/ to /hair-grooming/best-hair-dryers/. If best-hair-dryers is then 308'd to best-blow-dryers, that existing rule must be repointed to /hair-grooming/best-blow-dryers/ in the same change. 'Google is testing several pages and committing to none' is an inference from equal counts, and 'likely' is the right confidence.

### DATA-15 - 103 of 222 sitemap URLs (46%) got zero Google impressions in 92 days, concentrated in kitchen-appliance and home-gadgets
**medium** · effort M · likely

- **Evidence:** Join of the unique sitemap URLs against Pages.csv. Zero-impression URLs by section: kitchen-appliance 45/67, home-gadgets 27/59, hair-grooming 12/28, personal-care 12/22, beauty 3/4, garden-tools 2/9 (the two new 2026 hedge pages), skin-care 1/13, author 1/7. By sitemap lastmod year: 2023 5/11, 2024 29/56, 2025 65/130, 2026 4/25. GSC Coverage: 27 'Crawled - currently not indexed'.
- **Why it matters:** About half the site gets no search visibility. Large numbers of unvisited legacy roundups can drag down Google's site-level quality assessment, which fits Google ranking the same topics far below Bing (epilators 14.27 vs 7.87; corn roaster 67 vs about 5.8). The 27 crawled-not-indexed pages almost certainly come from this pool.
- **Fix:** Don't prune from a 3-month export. (1) Export 16 months of GSC Pages and the 'Crawled - currently not indexed' URL list. (2) Keep any URL with Bing keyword demand: best-coffee-beater (6 coffee-beater rows, 12 impressions), best-pedicure-tool and best-callus-remover (32 callus/pedicure rows, 34 impressions, 1 click), best-garnier-micellar-water, best-elf-primer (6 rows), best-meat-slicer, best-black-decker-dustbuster. (3) Any URL with 0 Google impressions over 16 months, no Bing demand and no merge target: set seo.indexable = false and unpublish, or merge per DATA-14. Record each decision in a vault prune log.
- **Checker's correction:** Coffee beater has 7 rows and 13 impressions in Bing ('what is a coffee beater?' 6, 'coffee beater' 2, and 5 singletons), not 6 and 12. The keep list also misses best-philips-trimmers and best-wahl-clippers, which have page-1 Bing demand (see DATA-V01). 'Unvisited roundups drag site-level quality' is inference, not measured.

### DATA-16 - Posts beyond the first 20 on a category page get far fewer impressions, which fits the client-only 'Load more' leaving them unlinked
**medium** · effort M · likely

- **Evidence:** Live category HTML 2026-09-25 vs Pages.csv. kitchen-appliance: the 20 posts linked from /kitchen-appliance/ have 8 zero-impression URLs and 56 impressions; the 47 unlinked posts have 37 zero-impression URLs (79%) and 22 impressions. home-gadgets: linked 20 have 6 zero-impression; unlinked 39 have 21 zero-impression (their 157 impressions are mostly best-smart-plugs' 114). The lead's facts note the category page renders 20 posts server-side, then a client 'Load more' with no paginated URLs.
- **Why it matters:** Unlinked posts depend on the sitemap and related-post links for discovery and internal PageRank. The pattern fits orphaning, though recency is a confounder because the category lists newest first.
- **Fix:** Add server-rendered pagination under src/app/(frontend)/[categorySlug]/: /[categorySlug]/page/[n]/ with <a href> next/previous links and a self-canonical per page. Keep 'Load more' as progressive enhancement over those links. Alternatively render every post title as a plain link list below the grid. Re-run this linked-vs-unlinked comparison 8 weeks later.

### DATA-17 - The post route ignores the category segment, so any /<anything>/<slug>/ returns 200
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/(frontend)/[categorySlug]/[slug]/page.tsx calls getContentBySlug(slug) and never compares categorySlug. Live 2026-09-25: /kitchen-appliance/best-epilators/ returns 200 with canonical https://whoadvice.com/skin-care/best-epilators/. /personal-care/best-pubic-hair-trimmer/ returns 200 with canonical /hair-grooming/best-pubic-hair-trimmer/. next.config.mjs redirects '/personal-care/best-pubic-hair-trimmer-for-silky-and-smooth-skin/' to '/personal-care/best-pubic-hair-trimmer/', a wrong-category URL, so it depends on the canonical to recover.
- **Why it matters:** Every old category move leaves a crawlable duplicate instead of a redirect. The canonical limits the damage, but Google spends crawl on duplicates and may pick them as 'crawled - not indexed' or alternates. Redirect targets can point at the wrong URL without anyone noticing.
- **Fix:** In page.tsx, after resolving: if (meta.categories.slug.current !== categorySlug) permanentRedirect(getPostPath(meta)) (from next/navigation). Do the same in generateMetadata's path. Change the pubic-trimmer redirect destination to '/hair-grooming/best-pubic-hair-trimmer/'.

### DATA-18 - The sitemap lists /hair-grooming/best-pubic-hair-trimmer/ twice because two Sanity documents share the slug
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** sitemap-urls.txt lines 58 and 150 are identical ('sort | uniq -d' returns only this URL). Both lastmods are 2025-08-21. src/app/sitemap.ts maps getAllPosts() one-to-one. src/lib/requests.ts getAllPosts queries *[_type in ["post","article"]] with no slug dedupe. getContentBySlug returns the article before the post, so the second document is unreachable. /hair-grooming/best-pubic-hair-trimmers/ returns 404. GSC: this URL has 14 impressions at 18.36.
- **Why it matters:** A shadowed duplicate document means edits may go to the copy readers never see, and the sitemap is not a clean URL list.
- **Fix:** In Sanity, find both documents with slug 'best-pubic-hair-trimmer' and unpublish the unreachable one, which is the `post` if an `article` exists. In getAllPosts, add `&& defined(slug.current) && seo.indexable != false` and dedupe by slug in sitemap.ts (a Set on the URL). Add a Sanity slug-uniqueness validation (isUnique across post and article) to both schemas.
- **Checker's correction:** Both documents may be the same type. If both are `post`, getPost's [0] makes one of them unreachable anyway. The Sanity-side fix is 'unpublish whichever document the page does not render', which is not necessarily the post.

### DATA-19 - Google visibility is falling while Bing holds: the indexed count dropped by 22 on 2026-08-22 and weekly impressions went from 65 to 14
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Coverage/Chart.csv: indexed 181 on 2026-07-01, 176 on 2026-08-21, 159 on 2026-08-22 (not indexed 25 to 39), 155 on 2026-08-29, recovering to 165 by 2026-09-15; not indexed 31 on 2026-09-21. Performance/Chart.csv, 7-day buckets from 2026-06-24: 67, 24, 25, 45, 19, 41, 44, 65, 29, 53, 31, 21, 14, then 3 (a 1-day remainder). 0-impression days on 2026-09-17, 09-18, 09-20 and 09-21. Bing daily average by month stayed at 18.9-21.9.

- **Fix:** In GSC Page indexing, open 'Crawled - currently not indexed' and 'Not found (404)', export the example URLs, and diff them against this sitemap. Check the site repo and Sanity history for deploys or bulk edits on 2026-08-20 to 08-22 (the git log visible here shows none for next.config.mjs between 2026-06-22 and 2026-09-17). Re-check weekly after the redirects in DATA-02/03/12/13 ship.
- **Checker's correction:** On 2026-08-22 the indexed count fell by 17 (176 to 159), not 22, and not-indexed rose by 14. The 22 figure is the drop from 181 on 2026-07-01 to 159. The total drop to the low point was 26 (181 to 155 on 08-29). 'Weekly impressions went from 65 to 14' compares a peak week with the last full week, so it is a selective framing of a noisy series.

**medium** · effort S · verified

### DATA-24 - Sitemap lastmod and JSON-LD dateModified carry Sanity _updatedAt bulk-touch dates, not real content changes
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/sitemap.ts uses lastModified: post._updatedAt. 116 of 222 URLs share 5 dates: 2025-08-21 (22 URLs), 2025-08-22 (39), 2025-08-26 (32), 2025-09-01 (6), 2025-09-11 (17). The 2025-08-26 batch: 32 URLs, 25 with zero impressions, 10 impressions total. best-smart-plugs has JSON-LD dateModified '2024-04-22T14:51:44Z', identical to its sitemap lastmod. Both hedge-trimmer pages show lastmod 2026-09-19, the date of revalidation commits 57a03a9/33fcd0f/f12c7d7.
- **Why it matters:** When lastmod moves without content changes, Google learns to ignore the site's lastmod. Real refreshes (the epilator publish, string trimmers) then won't be recrawled faster.
- **Fix:** Add an editor-set `contentUpdatedAt` datetime to the post and article schemas, changed only for substantive edits (the vault handoff can set it). Use it for sitemap lastModified and BlogPosting dateModified, falling back to publishedAt. Don't use _updatedAt.
- **Checker's correction:** The code path is verified. That these dates are bulk touches rather than content edits is inferred from the clustering, since Sanity history was not read.

### DATA-20 - The 469-desktop vs 12-mobile split reflects deep-position, probably automated impressions, not a mobile ranking penalty
**low** · effort S · hypothesis

- **Evidence:** Devices.csv: Desktop 469 impressions at avg pos 38.28; Mobile 12 at 32.42 (mobile ranks slightly better). Queries.csv: 86% of named-query impressions at pos >50, including 'best epilator for chefs' at 208.43 and 'electric toothbrush vs lunch box' at 271. Countries.csv: Japan 1 at 271.
- **Why it matters:** Product-review search is usually majority mobile. Mobile's better position rules out a mobile demotion. The remaining explanation is that desktop impressions are inflated by results on pages 5-20+, which rank trackers and scrapers generate on desktop, so real human visibility is below the 481 headline. Mobile's 12 is the honest baseline.
- **Fix:** In GSC, filter Position <=20 (via the API or a Pages x Device export) and compare devices. Report human-visible impressions (pos <=20) as the KPI instead of raw impressions. Run URL Inspection's live test with the smartphone crawler on /skin-care/best-epilators/ and /home-gadgets/best-smart-plugs/ to rule out a mobile rendering problem.
- **Checker's correction:** The data is confirmed. The cause (scraper or rank-tracker desktop impressions) cannot be verified from these exports and stays a hypothesis.

### DATA-21 - Brand-name collisions ('Which?', 'who app') produce noise impressions, and no query contains 'whoadvice'
**low** · effort S · verified

No query contains 'whoadvice' except 'site:whoadvice.com' (1 at 1). Homepage: 51 at 76.63. Static pages: privacy 8 at 5.88, contact 8 at 6.38, cookies 9 at 24, terms 6 at 4.17, about 6 at 4.83.

- **Fix:** Don't target these queries or read them as opportunities. Leave the homepage title alone for them. Exclude brand-collision and site: queries from reporting (a GSC regex filter like '^(which|who|site:)'). Track branded 'whoadvice' queries as a trust KPI; they are currently zero.
- **Checker's correction:** 'what brand is it' (position 21) is generic rather than a Which?/who collision. The collision set proper is 15 impressions.

- **Checker's correction:** The numbers are confirmed. 'In-house checks' cannot be verified from the export and remains a hypothesis. The effect on the site-wide average is small: 24 impressions out of 481.

### DATA-28 - GSC records no rich results even though FAQPage and Product/Review markup ships
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Performance 'Search appearance.csv' has a header row only (no rows). The lead's facts: the epilator page emits FAQPage (9 Q/A) and 7 Product nodes with Review but no reviewRating, aggregateRating or offers. The vault audit for the article recommends Article + BreadcrumbList + ItemList and no Product rich-result markup.
- **Why it matters:** Zero rich-result appearances confirms the current markup earns no SERP enhancements, so it can't explain or fix the 0% CTR. Carrying Product/Review nodes without ratings adds maintenance and validation risk for nothing.
- **Fix:** Follow the vault handoff: emit Article, BreadcrumbList and ItemList (ordered product names with URLs to their card anchors) from PostJsonLD for roundups, and drop the Product/Review nodes and FAQPage from roundup pages. Re-check Search appearance after 4 weeks.

## Vault rules and workflow

### RULES-01 - The lifecycle has no publish or live-parity stage, so approved articles can wait indefinitely while the legacy page stays live
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** 11-article-workflow.md stops at Stage 14 (L514-518). The audit statuses end at `ready_for_editorial_review`, which L463 says 'is not publication approval', and no status exists after it. articles/epilators/best-epilators-at-home-hair-removal/audit.md L3-6: 'Audit date: July 20, 2026 ... Outcome: ready_for_editorial_review'. final-cluster-sync-2026-07-20.md has the same status. The live /skin-care/best-epilators/ still shows the legacy title and products (BRE740/14 and FaceSpa, which the audit excluded) and the claim 'A true epilator is completely painless', which contradicts the approved article.md L297 ('Epilation can be uncomfortable'). Definition of done (11 L568-580) never requires the page to be live. The only rule saying 'Publishing is complete only when the live page matches article.md' is in articles/hedge-trimmers/publisher-cms-guide.md L147. That is a cluster-local file, not a reference document.
- **Why it matters:** Research and editing have been done, but none of it reaches readers or search engines. The ranking page has stayed wrong for 67 days, with a factual contradiction and excluded products, even though a corrected version exists in the vault.
- **Fix:** In 11-article-workflow.md, add a 'Stage 16: Publication and live parity' after Stage 14, and extend the status vocabulary to `approved_for_publication` -> `published` -> `live_verified` (plus `returned_to_editorial`). Stage 16 should require these records: the editor's name and approval date; the CMS document id; the publish date; the live URL; the live-parity check result (see RULES-10); and the article.md commit hash. Add an SLA: 'If a package sits in ready_for_editorial_review for more than 14 days while a legacy URL for the same keyword is live, escalate to the editor. Within 48 hours, hotfix any factually wrong or self-contradictory claims on the legacy page, even if the full republish waits.' Update the Definition of done: 'An article is done only when it is live_verified.' Promote the hedge-trimmer 'Publisher completion record' (publisher-cms-guide.md L128-145) into this stage as the standard record. Add a line to audit.md: 'next_action_owner: <name>, due: <date>'.
- **Checker's correction:** The lifecycle does not stop at Stage 14. Stage 15 (Update cycle) follows it, so the proposed publication stage belongs between 14 and 15, or should be numbered 14b. The 'not publication approval' quote is at 11 L516, not L463.

### RULES-02 - The site template adds trust labels that break the vault's evidence model, and no rule covers them
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ArticleHero.tsx L29-45 falls back to `Expert Tested • ${year}` whenever `editorial_badge` is empty. L168-177 hard-codes '{year} TOP RATED' with a star on every non-editorial hero. The live face-epilators and bug-zapper HTML both show 'Expert Tested • 2024'. The live epilators and hedge-trimmers pages both show '2026 TOP RATED'. product-card/ProsCons.tsx L77 labels pros 'Why we love it' (14 hits in the live epilators HTML). GuideItemCard.tsx L21 labels a tip '💡 EXPERT TIP:'. The rules forbid all of this: 01 trust contract ('Imply hands-on testing that did not happen', 'Invent ... award'), 16 L105 ('Do not write ... "we loved"'), and 12 L331 ('Tested, only when genuinely tested'). No handoff field or checklist item tells the publisher to set `editorial_badge` or check template labels. The 12 Part 14 YAML and skill deliverables.md have no badge field, and hedge publisher-cms-guide L56 ('Do not add ... hands-on labels') cannot be met because the labels are hard-coded.
- **Why it matters:** Research-only pages tell readers the products were expert-tested and 'Top Rated'. That is the most damaging trust failure possible for an affiliate review site, and it undercuts the honest method statements the vault works hard to produce.
- **Fix:** (1) In the new 19-article-design-and-rendering-spec.md (RULES-03), add a 'Template-owned labels' table: hero badge, image overlay, pros/cons headings, tip label, CTA text. Give each an approved value and a banned list: 'Expert Tested', 'Tested', 'Top Rated', 'Why we love it', 'Expert Tip', unless testing or expert records exist. Approved values: hero badge 'Research-based guide • {Month YYYY reviewed}'; pros/cons 'What stands out' / 'Tradeoffs'; tip 'Quick check'. (2) Add `editorial_badge` (required, from the approved list) to the 12 Part 14 handoff YAML and to skill references/deliverables.md. (3) Add to 18 §7: '[ ] Rendered page shows no template label implying testing, expert review, awards, or ratings the evidence model does not support.' (4) Open a code ticket: delete the `Expert Tested` fallback and the TOP RATED overlay in ArticleHero.tsx, and relabel ProsCons.tsx and GuideItemCard.tsx.
- **Checker's correction:** On epilators and hedge-trimmers the article-level editorial_badge was entered as 'Reviewed & Compared • 2026', so the 'Expert Tested' fallback shows only on pages without a badge (face-epilators, bug-zapper). The TOP RATED overlay and the 'Why we love it' labels are on every page. deliverables.md does have a per-product 'Editorial badge' field (L45). What is missing is the article-level hero badge.

### RULES-03 - No reference document specifies the rendered article design (section to Sanity block to component), so writers write for a page that doesn't exist
**high** · effort L · verified

- **Evidence:** README.md 'Document roles' (L25-45) lists 20 files and none covers rendering. 15's article contract lists `cms_owned: images, schema, ... mobile` (L71-78) and hands the design to the CMS. The Sanity article model has fixed blocks: articleContent.ts L9-31 (topPicksBlock, comparisonTableBlock, productReviewsBlock, typesSection, guideSection, faqSection, calloutGroup, decisionComparisonTableBlock, iimage). Each renders a fixed component with its own headings, labels, CTAs, and clamps (article-portable-text.tsx L174-315). The vault markdown uses devices that have no mapping to these blocks: `>` blockquote callouts, HTML comment image markers, '### Best Overall: ...' top-pick headings, and '**Pros:**' labels. See article.md L18-30 and L217-219.
- **Why it matters:** Every design decision made in the contract is lost or changed at CMS entry: award on top picks, heading levels, CTA placement, callout type, image placement and alt text. The editorial QA certifies a markdown file, not the page readers see.
- **Fix:** Create reference/19-article-design-and-rendering-spec.md with this outline. 1. Purpose and authority: owns rendered layout and must match the code in personal/blog; update in the same PR as component changes. 2. Page anatomy in DOM order: breadcrumbs, hero (H1, dek, badge, author, reviewed date, jump buttons), disclosure, body blocks, related posts, newsletter. 3. Block map table with columns markdown section | Sanity block | component | template-owned headings/labels | required fields | limits. Rows: intro → block H2-less paragraphs; 'Top picks' H2 block + topPicksBlock → TopPickCard; comparison H2 + comparisonTableBlock → ComparisonTable; 'Reviews' H2 + productReviewsBlock → ProductReviewCard; methodology → portable text; types H2 + typesSection → TypeItemCard; buying guide H2 + guideSection → GuideItemCard; callouts → calloutGroup; decision table → decisionComparisonTableBlock; FAQ H2 + faqSection → Faqs; conclusion H2 → portable text. 4. Heading plan: which headings the template emits (product H2 inside card, pros/cons H3, callout H3, guide/type H3) and what the writer must supply around them. 5. Per-block content contracts: top-pick description ≤ 22 words; table ≤ 4 spec columns and ≤ 5 words per cell; card fields; callout 1-3 bullets ≤ 20 words each; FAQ answer 40-80 words in one paragraph. 6. Image specs per slot: hero 4:3 ≥1600x1200; product 1:1 on white ≥1200; type card 1:1; in-body 16:9; alt text source field for each. 7. CTA rules: label, where CTAs appear, rel=sponsored. 8. Template-owned labels and banned labels (RULES-02). 9. Dates: published vs reviewed (RULES-08). 10. Reading design: prose measure 65-75ch, TOC threshold. 11. Markdown authoring conventions for each block, so article.md imports mechanically. 12. Change log. Route it in README (normal roundup: load at Part 2 and Part 4) and in SKILL.md 'Article contract and outline' and 'Final audit and handoff'.
- **Checker's correction:** The README roles table has 19 rows (01-18 plus the deprecated pointer), not 20.

### RULES-04 - The publisher-handoff YAML doesn't match the Sanity article/product schema, and several approved fields are silently lost
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The 12 Part 14 YAML (L903-924) and skill deliverables.md L68-89 were checked against sanity/schemas/article/index.ts, product.ts, fragments/product-editorial-fields.ts, and components/seo.ts. Lost: `article_excerpt`: the article schema has no excerpt field; ArticleHero.tsx L63 shows `seo.meta_description ?? excerpt`. `recommended_tags`: no tags field. `featured_image_filename`: Sanity serves hash URLs (live src cdn.sanity.io/.../<hash>-1280x720.png). `featured_image_alt_text` / `other_image_alt_text`: the `iimage.alt` field exists (components/image.ts L22-26), but ArticleHero L159, TopPickCard L195, ProductReviewCard L48, and TypeItemCard L56 all use `alt={title}` instead; live epilators alts are all product or post titles. `suggested_schema_types: [Article, BreadcrumbList, ItemList]`: lib/schema/post-page.ts L46 pushes Product+Review nodes and no page ItemList. `cta_label: Check price on Amazon`: CTA.tsx L30 hard-codes 'Check Price at Amazon' and TopPickCard/ComparisonTable hard-code 'Check Price'. The handoff has no field at all for things the CMS does hold: `editorial_badge`, `author`, `publishedAt`, `faqSection.enable_schema` (default true, sectionBlocks.ts L192-197), or per-product `tagline` (the epilators handoff products YAML L31-94 has no badge).
- **Why it matters:** The handoff looks complete, but a third of it has nowhere to go. Things the CMS actually needs (badge, author, dates, FAQ schema flag) are never specified. So alt text, schema, excerpt, and CTA copy on the live page come from template defaults, not editorial decisions.
- **Fix:** Replace the 12 Part 14 YAML and deliverables.md 'Publisher handoff' with a schema-mapped handoff whose keys are Sanity paths. Article: `title` (H1), `slug.current`, `categories[]`, `author` (reference by slug), `publishedAt`, `lastReviewedAt` (new field, RULES-08), `editorial_badge` (approved vocabulary), `seo.meta_title`, `seo.meta_description`, `seo.keywords: ''` (always empty), `seo.indexable: true`, `seo.open_graph_image` {file, alt}, `content[]` block list in order, `faqSection.enable_schema` (default false per 12 L942). Products (per `products[]` row): `product` → {asin, title ≤50 chars, brand, link, image {file, alt}}, `tagline`, `summary`, `verdict`, `pros[]`, `cons[]`. Move keyword/intent/freshness fields to a separate 'editorial record' block marked 'not entered in CMS'. Add a 'not implementable today' list (tags, image filename, ItemList, CTA label) with the code ticket that would fix each, so nobody treats those as done.
- **Checker's correction:** Line numbers for the alt props are wrong. They are TopPickCard.tsx L28, ProductReviewCard.tsx L35 and TypeItemCard.tsx L24, not L195, L48 and L56. ArticleHero L159 is correct.

### RULES-05 - The contract's top-pick design is overridden by the TopPickCard component: award dropped, CTA added, copy quoted and clamped
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Epilators article-contract.md: `top_pick_card_fields: Editorial badge, Exact product name, Original short description` and 'Do not add ASINs or affiliate links to the Top 3 cards'. 15 L68-69 has the same field list. TopPickCard.tsx L187-227 renders image, H3 product title, description wrapped in “ ” with `line-clamp-3` at text-sm, and a 'Check Price' AffiliateLink. It never renders the award, and topPickItem.ts has only `product` and `description`. The approved top-pick copy is 30-35 words (article.md L22, L26, L30). The live hedge-trimmer top pick is 38 words inside a 346px card clamped to 3 lines. No rule gives a length limit for the description.
- **Why it matters:** The top-pick block is the page's quick answer. Without the award the reader can't tell why each pick is there. The quotation marks present editorial copy as a quote with no speaker (against 03 §12). Truncation cuts off the tradeoff clause, which is the most useful part.
- **Fix:** In 19-article-design-and-rendering-spec.md §5 and in 15 'Introduction' (L117), state: 'Top-pick description: one sentence, 14-22 words, fit first then tradeoff. The award comes from articleProduct.tagline and must match the card and table. Top-pick cards carry one CTA only if the contract sets `top_pick_cta: true`.' Add `top_pick_description_max_words: 22` and `top_pick_cta` to the 15 contract YAML. Open a code ticket: TopPickCard should render `pick.product.tagline` above the title, remove the curly quotes, and drop line-clamp so a length rule is enforced instead. Extend audit_article.py to flag top-pick text over 22 words (see RULES-13).
- **Checker's correction:** The component lines are TopPickCard.tsx L37-59, not L187-227. The approved top-pick copy runs 29-32 words, not 30-35. I did not re-measure the 38-word hedge-trimmer top-pick figure.

### RULES-06 - Product heading level is specified three different ways, and template-owned headings are invisible to the writer
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Skill references/deliverables.md L41 template: '## [Rank]. [Exact product name]' (H2). Epilators audit.md L13: 'Changed all seven review headings from H2 to H3 under Best epilator reviews', and L74: 'product reviews correctly nested as H3s'. ProductReviewCard.tsx L78 always renders the product name as `<h2>`, and ProsCons.tsx L81 adds H3 'Why we love it'/'Points to consider'. article-portable-text.tsx L181 renders topPicksBlock with `showHeading={false}`, so product H3s come before the first H2 unless the editor adds an H2 block first. The measured facts confirm three H3 before the first H2, and 18 §5 L50 says 'Use H3s only for subsections within the preceding H2.'
- **Why it matters:** The audit certifies an H3 outline that the CMS can't produce. The live page ends up with a broken outline (orphan H3s, a flat run of product H2s, 14 generic H3s), which hurts accessibility and makes the page harder to scan.
- **Fix:** Make 19-article-design-and-rendering-spec.md §4 the single source for heading levels. Rule: 'Product review cards render their product name as H2 (template-owned). Place the reviews after an H2 only if the contract wants a grouping heading; otherwise do not add one. Top picks must be preceded by an editor-entered H2 ("Our top picks") block. Pros/cons, callout, type, and guide-item titles are template H3s; do not add H3s of your own inside those blocks.' Fix deliverables.md L41 to match. Remove 'product reviews correctly nested as H3s' from the audit template language. Add a check to the live QA list (RULES-10): 'No H3 before the first H2; no empty heading; H2/H3 text does not end in "#".'
- **Checker's correction:** The product H2 is at ProductReviewCard.tsx L65, not L78.

### RULES-07 - The comparison-table contract ignores what the renderer adds, so columns duplicate and badges drift
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Epilators contract: six columns 'Rank and product | Editorial badge | Power and use | Head and control | Key inclusions | Main tradeoff'. ComparisonTable.tsx L38-46 always adds a 'Product' first column and a 'Check' CTA last column, so 'Rank and product' would appear twice and the table would have 8 columns. Every cell is `whitespace-nowrap` (L90, L98), and cells like 'Shaver head, trimmer comb, skin-contact cap, brush, pouch' (article.md L36) force the measured 1,026px table inside a 353px viewport. comparisonTable.ts L268 allows up to 8 rows of any product, with no check that rows equal the ranked products. On live, the table lists 8 products (including an IPL with no card), and Silk-épil 7's table badge 'Best Wet & Dry Value' differs from its card badge 'WIDE HEAD DESIGN' because the badge is typed separately in each place. 06 §14 and 12 Part 8 list candidate columns but set no limits for width, cell length, or rows.
- **Why it matters:** On phones, the most-used decision tool becomes a wide scroll strip. Rows and badges can disagree with the ranked cards, which the reader sees as contradictions.
- **Fix:** Add a 'Comparison table contract' to 19 §5 and replace 06 §14 'Possible fields' with the following. 'The product column and CTA column are template-owned; do not specify them. Use at most 4 spec columns. The award/badge column is derived from articleProduct.tagline, never typed. Each cell: at most 5 words or one number with a unit, no lists. Rows = exactly the ranked products, in rank order; non-ranked alternatives never appear.' Update the 15 contract YAML with `comparison_columns: max 4` and `comparison_cell_max_words: 5`. Code ticket: remove `whitespace-nowrap` from value cells; validate in comparisonTable.ts that every row product is in `article.products`. Add an audit_article.py check that table product names equal card titles in order and table badges equal card badges.
- **Checker's correction:** The 8-row limit is at about L80 of comparisonTable.ts, which has 125 lines, not L268. 06 §14 does give one weak width rule ('Avoid tables with 15 columns that become unreadable on mobile'), but it sets no cell-length or row contract. With the schema's 6 spec columns plus the 2 template columns, a table can reach 8 columns.

### RULES-08 - The rules never define what the visible 'Updated' date means, and the template refreshes it on any edit
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ArticleHero.tsx L118: 'Updated {formatMonthYear(post._updatedAt)}'. ProductReviewCard.tsx L102-105: 'Updated: {formatMonthYear(product._updatedAt)}'. Sanity changes _updatedAt on any write, including a typo fix or an Amazon price import (product.ts L114: price 'from Amazon at import time'). Live: the hero says 'Updated Aug 2026' while the cards say 'Updated: Jun 2026'. The rules forbid cosmetic refreshes: 11 L535 'Do not refresh the date without substantive verification' and 12 L895 'Do not change the publication date merely to make old content appear new'. The article schema (article/index.ts) has only `publishedAt`, with no reviewed/verified field.
- **Why it matters:** The page shows a freshness claim the process never checked, and two different dates on one page look careless. Google's review guidance also treats unreal date changes as a quality problem.
- **Fix:** Add a 'Dates' section to 19 and to 12 Part 13: 'Displayed "Reviewed" date = `lastReviewedAt`, set only when Stage 15 completes a substantive verification (availability, variants, prices, recalls). Never display Sanity _updatedAt. Product cards show no date; the page-level reviewed date applies to all products.' Add `lastReviewedAt` (datetime, required for product_guide) and optionally `reviewedBy` (author reference) to article/index.ts. Add `last_reviewed_at` and `next_review_date` to the handoff YAML. Code ticket: ArticleHero shows 'Reviewed {lastReviewedAt}', and ProductReviewCard drops its date line.
- **Checker's correction:** The product-card date is at ProductReviewCard.tsx L89-93, not L102-105.

**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Epilators publisher-handoff.md L99-100: 'Keep the visible material-connection disclosure before the first affiliate CTA. Include the site's approved Amazon Associate identification wording.' The article.md L10 metadata line contains 'As an Amazon Associate, WhoAdvice earns from qualifying purchases.' ### RULES-10 - There is no post-publish live QA checklist; the CMS QA list in 13 is never loaded for normal roundups
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The only rendered-page checklist is 13 Step 20 (L1097-1145). README routing for a normal roundup (L49-51) and SKILL.md 'Final audit and handoff' (L77-91) load 11, 15, 16, 18, and deliverables, not 13. 18 §11 (L117-124) is framed as pre-publication. 12 Part 18 (L1153-1188) covers GSC performance, not rendered output. 17 L12 checks 'Run the mechanical audit on every complete article', i.e. markdown only. Live defects no current check would catch: an empty `<h3></h3>`, 8 headings ending in '#', 8 table rows vs 7 cards, a duplicated 'Verdict:' prefix, 'Updated' date mismatches, JSON-LD Product nodes where ItemList was handed off, a publisher logo 404, and all image alts equal to titles.
- **Why it matters:** Every defect listed is a rendering or CMS-entry error. Checks run only on markdown, so these ship and nobody sees them.
- **Fix:** Add reference/20-live-page-qa.md, routed as Stage 16 of 11 and loaded by SKILL.md after publication. Outline: 1. Inputs: live URL, article.md, handoff. 2. Parity: H1 = handoff h1; title and meta equal the handoff; H2/H3 sequence equals article.md headings (normalised, template headings whitelisted per 19 §4); product order, titles, and badges equal article.md in cards, top picks, and table; CTA count = ranked products (plus top-pick CTAs if the contract allows). 3. Structure: no H3 before the first H2; no empty headings; no heading text ending in '#'; one H1. 4. Schema: JSON-LD @types equal handoff `suggested_schema_types`; publisher.logo returns 200; FAQPage answers contain no empty `<p>`. 5. Links: every internal link returns 200 without redirect; affiliate links rel includes sponsored; disclosure appears before the first affiliate link. 6. Images: every img alt is non-empty and not equal to the title for product images; hero is not lazy. 7. Mobile 375px: no page overflow; table scroll wrapper present. 8. Record: date, checker, pass/fail per item, attached to the Stage 16 completion record. Add a skill script scripts/live_parity_check.py that fetches the URL, parses headings, JSON-LD, links, and alts, and diffs them against article.md. Show it fails on the current epilators URL before trusting it.
- **Checker's correction:** 18 §10-11 does contain rendered-page items (validate structured data, test mobile layout and table scrolling, fix heading errors). But they are generic, have no parity checks against article.md or the handoff, and nothing requires running them against the live URL after publication. The 17 mechanical-audit instruction is step 12 at L42, not L12. SKILL's final-audit list does not load 15.

### RULES-11 - audit_article.py reports 'Flags: 0' on the epilators article without running any card checks
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** audit_article.py L62 finds cards only by `^\*\*Title:\*\*`, and the approved article.md contains 0 such lines (grep count 0). The contract moved Title/Slug/Brand to 'structured_product_record_fields'. So card-field, ASIN/link, and Pros/Cons checks (L71-86) never run. The Pros/Cons regex requires `^\*\*Pros\*\*$`, while the article uses '**Pros:**' (article.md L54), so it would flag every card if cards were detected. audit.md L29-31 and publisher-handoff L23/L209 record 'Flags: 0' as QA evidence. The skill's own deliverables.md template (L43-46) still uses '**Title:**' fields.
- **Why it matters:** The mechanical gate says 'clean' because it silently skipped the checks, not because the article passed them. That breaks the principle that a check must fail when the thing it checks is broken.
- **Fix:** Rewrite card detection in audit_article.py to key on the product heading pattern that 19 §11 fixes (e.g. `^###? \d+\. `) and accept both `**Pros**` and `**Pros:**`. Print 'Cards found: N' and exit non-zero if N differs from the table row count or from a `--expect-cards` argument. Align deliverables.md L40-64 with the approved visible-card format (Editorial badge, Summary, Verdict, Pros, Cons; Title = heading). Before relying on it, show the script going red on a deliberately broken copy (a card missing a Verdict) and green on the fixed article. Record that red/green run in the skill README.
- **Checker's correction:** The contract did not move Title. It stays in visible_product_card_fields, and only Slug, Brand, ASIN and the link fields moved to structured_product_record_fields. Title is simply rendered as the heading, not as a '**Title:**' line. The global Summary/Verdict length checks (L55-60) do still run. Only the per-card field, Pros/Cons and ASIN/link checks (L71-86) are skipped.

### RULES-12 - The mechanical audit doesn't detect the [FACT CHECK: ...] markers that Stage 9 tells writers to insert
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** 11-article-workflow.md L341-345: 'When a needed fact is missing, insert: `[FACT CHECK: specific missing item]`'. Gate 10 (L378): 'No unresolved high-risk claim may remain in publishable copy.' audit_article.py L94 looks only for `\b(?:TODO|TBD|PLACEHOLDER)\b`. It also ignores `<!-- ... -->` HTML comments such as the epilators IMAGE BRIEF marker (article.md L219), which the handoff says must not render.
- **Why it matters:** An unresolved marker or an internal comment can pass every automated check and be pasted into the CMS as reader-facing text.
- **Fix:** Add `\[FACT CHECK:` and `\[CLAIM` / `\[C\d+\]` claim-ID patterns to the placeholder check as hard failures. Add a warning listing every `<!-- -->` comment, with its line number, as 'CMS instruction, strip before import'. Document both in 11 Stage 9 ('The mechanical audit fails while any marker remains').

### RULES-13 - audit_article.py enforces only a small share of the numeric and pattern rules the references set
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Rules with a number or pattern that the script ignores: intro 120-200 words (03 L45, 15 L112); conclusion 80-150 (03 L327, 15 L157); em dashes ≤ 1 per 250 words (03 L102); fake-testing phrases 'we tested', 'we tried', 'in our experience', 'we love' (12 L677-683, 16 L105); SEO title 50-60 and meta 140-160 characters (12 L324/L373); primary keyword in the first 100 words (12 L973); verdict text starting with 'Verdict:' (duplicated live on card 5); empty headings; comparison-table product set vs cards. BANNED (L6-11) has 13 phrases against the 27 patterns in 05. The script does check sentences over 30 words, Summary/Verdict length, and verdict openings.
- **Why it matters:** 'Mechanical audit returns no material flags' is a required check in 15 L188, but most mechanical rules are unchecked, so the gate gives little assurance.
- **Fix:** Add these checks to audit_article.py, each with a named rule reference in its message: intro/conclusion word windows (read from the article-contract YAML when present, falling back to 03); em-dash rate; FAKE_TESTING list; title/meta length from the metadata block; keyword in the first 100 words of body; `^(Verdict|Summary):` inside field bodies; empty `#` headings; table-vs-card name and badge parity (RULES-07); top-pick description ≤ 22 words (RULES-05). Keep each check in a table in the skill README with 'rule source' and 'shown failing on' columns.

### RULES-14 - Four documents give four different roundup section orders
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** 11 Stage 8 (L284-296): Quick picks, Comparison table, Product reviews, How products were selected, Buying guide, Safety, FAQs, Conclusion (no Types). 12 Part 5 (L414-424): quick recs, why picks fit, table, evaluations, method, buying, safety, FAQs, final (no Types). 15 Part 3 section contracts (L110-157): Introduction, top-pick cards, Product cards, Product types, Buying guide, FAQs, Conclusion (no methodology, no safety section; buying guide 'pre-purchase only'). Skill deliverables.md L21-30: direct answer, table, product H2 sections, Ranking methodology, Types, Buying guide, Expectations and safety, Community-led questions, conclusion. README L81 says 'The selected article-type profile owns section order', and SKILL.md L10 says the skill must not restate project rules. deliverables.md does both.
- **Why it matters:** Writers and auditors pick whichever list they loaded, so pages differ in structure for no editorial reason. Methodology, which the evidence model depends on, is optional in the controlling profile (15).
- **Fix:** Make 15 the single owner. Add '#### Section order (roundup default)' to 15: H1 → intro → Top picks (H2) → Comparison (H2) → Reviews (template H2 per product) → How we ranked (H2, required) → Types (H2, optional per contract) → How to choose (H2) → What to expect / safety (H2, when warnings exist) → FAQ (H2) → Verdict/conclusion (H2). Replace the lists in 11 L282-298, 12 L414-424 and deliverables.md L19-30 with 'See 15 §Section order'. Add `section_order:` to the 15 contract YAML.
- **Checker's correction:** The README ownership line is L80, not L81.

### RULES-16 - 12 and 18 have drifted, and 18 contains policy that 12, its declared owner, doesn't have
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** (a) Keyword in headings: 12 L242 'At least one relevant H2 or H3' and L975 'At least one useful heading', vs 18 L24 'at least one useful H2'. (b) TOC: 18 L54 'Add a table of contents or jump links when the published page is long enough', with no threshold; 12 has no TOC rule. (c) Price tiers: 18 L66 'Include cheap, mid-range, and premium choices', absent from 12. (d) Trust fields: 12 L862 'Where supported by the publishing system, include ... Reviewer', vs 18 L81 unconditional 'Include author, reviewer, updated date'. The schema has no reviewer field. (e) Decision boxes, callouts, emotional connection, and generated-image prompts (18 L69-74, L102-106) have no counterpart in 12. README L82 and 12 L5-7 say '12 owns SEO policy ... 18 must remain aligned'.

- **Fix:** (a) Use 'H2 or H3' in both. (b) Put the TOC rule in 12 Part 5 with a threshold, e.g. 'Articles with ≥ 6 H2s or ≥ 2,000 words render a jump-link list after the disclosure (see 19 §10)', and make 18 reference it. (c) Move the price-tier rule into 12 Part 8, qualified as 'when the market supports it'. (d) Make 18 L81 conditional on 19's field list. (e) Move the decision-device rules from 18 §6 into 16 'Decision-support components' and leave one-line checks in 18. Add a CI-style consistency script (skill scripts/check_reference_drift.py) that greps numbers and 'H2/H3' phrasing across 03, 11, 12, 15 and 18.

### RULES-17 - Heading case rules contradict each other and the template's own headings

Template defaults are Title Case: TopPickCards.tsx L157 'Top Picks at a Glance' and Faqs.tsx L215 'Frequently Asked Questions'. Live H2s are Title Case ('Best Epilators Compared: Quick Overview', 'Buying Guide for Best Epilators'), while the approved article.md uses sentence case ('Best epilators compared', 'How to choose the best epilator').
- **Why it matters:** Mixed case on one page is a visible sign of inconsistent editing, and it happens exactly where template headings sit next to editor headings.
- **Fix:** Keep sentence case (03 §16). Rewrite the 12 L453-461 examples in sentence case. In 19 §4, list every template heading and require sentence case ('Top picks at a glance', 'Frequently asked questions'), with a code ticket to change those strings. Add a heading-case check to the live QA script (flag H2/H3 where more than 50% of words after the first are capitalised, excluding proper nouns in the product list).
