- **Evidence:** The importer never writes image assets: no hero, no product.image, no inline iimage, and the '<!-- IMAGE BRIEF … -->' marker at best-epilators article.md line 219 is skipped (the comment filter in markdownLinesToPortableText). epilators/best-epilators-at-home-hair-removal/ has no images/ directory, while the handoff names best-epilators-at-home-2026.webp and epilator-types-decision-cards.webp (lines 148, 171). Only digital-clocks has an images/ folder. product.image is optional (product.ts lines 117-122).
- **Why it matters:** Every import produces an imageless page and product set that must be finished by hand, which is exactly where the hand-entry drift in PIPE-15 comes from. Image licence and exact-model checks have no record.
- **Fix:** Standardise articles/<cluster>/<slug>/images/manifest.yaml with entries {file, role: hero|product:<asin>|inline:<marker>, alt, license, source_url, exact_model_verified_by}. The importer uploads with client.assets.upload('image', ...) using the sha1 filename for dedupe, sets asset references only on empty fields, and places inline images at <!-- cms:image name --> markers. Add Studio validation requiring heroImage and product.image before publish.
- **Checker's correction:** A related hazard: the comment filter only drops single-line comments. A multi-line '<!-- CMS ... -->' block would be imported as visible paragraph text.

### PIPE-25 - Cluster rollout state is untracked: hedge trimmers are 3 of 10 live, and in the opposite order to the approved sequence
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** curl 2026-09-25 returned 200 for /garden-tools/best-corded-hedge-trimmers/ (datePublished 2026-08-06), /garden-tools/best-hedge-trimmers/ (2026-08-24) and /garden-tools/best-cordless-hedge-trimmers/ (2026-09-18). It returned 404 for best-pole-hedge-trimmers, corded-vs-cordless-hedge-trimmer, what-size-hedge-trimmer-do-i-need and how-to-use-a-hedge-trimmer. The vs-loppers, sharpen and clean pages are absent from sitemap-urls.txt. publisher-cms-guide.md lines 111-122 require the support pages first and the hub last. Epilators: 0 of 4 packages live (the pillar and face pages are legacy posts; the pubic-hair and underarm pages return 404). Bug zappers: 0 of 4 complete packages live (the audit's lines 26-35). Digital clocks: live as an article since 2026-09-16.
- **Why it matters:** The internal-link graph the cluster audits designed cannot exist while most nodes 404. The hub was published before its supports, which is why its links to them had to be stripped. No document says what is live.
- **Fix:** Maintain articles/<cluster>/publication-log.md, or better, derive it from each handoff's publication block (PIPE-13) with a `cms:status` script that prints cluster × page × cms_status × live HTTP code. Resume hedge trimmers in the guide's order: vs-loppers, what-size, sharpen, clean, use, corded-vs-cordless, pole. Then re-verify the hub's reciprocal links.
- **Checker's correction:** 'In the opposite order' is overstated. The actual order was corded (step 7), then the hub (step 10), then cordless (step 8): the hub went before cordless and before all six supports. 'Which is why its links to them had to be stripped' has no evidence. The live hub lacks even its link to the corded page, which was already live. A consequence the finder missed: the live cordless page links to the unpublished corded-vs-cordless page (PIPE-V01).

### PIPE-26 - The epilator package's time-sensitive checks have expired, and the legacy post was hand-edited after the package was approved for review
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The package's research and freshness date is July 20, 2026 (handoff line 196: 'A targeted CPSC exact-model search on July 20, 2026'). The live epilator post JSON-LD has datePublished 2026-06-12T03:36:00Z and dateModified 2026-08-06T07:40:33Z, and the sitemap lastmod is 2026-08-06. The handoff (lines 191-200) requires a publication-day recheck of variation, seller, price, redirect, recall and '2026' in the title.
- **Why it matters:** Importing the July copy as-is would publish two-month-old availability, price and recall assertions. The August edits to the live post were made outside the vault, so no one knows whether they contain corrections worth keeping.
- **Fix:** Before import, re-run the handoff's publication-day checks for all 7 ASINs and 7 short links, and record freshness_checked_at. Diff the live post's current copy against the vault article to confirm nothing added in June and August needs to move into the package. If the check changes any product, send it back through Stage 13 for the affected sections only.
- **Checker's correction:** 'Hand-edited after the package was approved for review' is not supported. dateModified is _updatedAt, which any write changes, including a schema or product-reference migration. It falls 14 minutes after the corded hedge page's publish (07:26), which suggests a Studio session or bulk operation, not necessarily a copy edit. The freshness expiry itself stands. Diffing the post's document history (Studio history) would settle what changed.

### PIPE-V03 - The editorial migration script converts post.products references into articleProduct objects, which the post schema and post query cannot read
**medium** · effort S · likely · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** sanity/migrations/move-product-editorial-to-articles.ts lines 136-142 select `*[_type in ["article", "post"]]`, and lines 108-121 replace every bare reference in products[] with an {_type:'articleProduct', product:{_ref}} object. sanity/schemas/post/index.ts line 142 declares post.products as `of: [{ type: 'reference', to: { type: 'product' } }]`. Posts are read with CONTENT_META_PROJECTION → LEGACY_PRODUCT_PROJECTION `products[] -> {...}` (content-queries.ts lines 5-18 and 88). The GROQ `->` operator on a non-reference object yields null. Live posts still render products, so the script has evidently not been run against posts since they were created.
- **Why it matters:** Anyone who follows the script header ('Run with: npx tsx ...') would silently strip product cards and Product JSON-LD from every legacy post, which is most of the site, with no dry run and no undo except document history.
- **Fix:** Restrict the query to `_type == "article"`, add dry-run by default with a JSON plan and a --commit flag, and add a guard that refuses to patch any document whose _type is not article. If the article backfill is complete (a read-only `count(*[_type=="article" && count(products[defined(_ref)])>0])` returns 0), delete the script.

### PIPE-V04 - The auto importer names products from the review H2 (award text included), ignores the package's **Title:** field, and writes that name onto shared product documents
**medium** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** Vault importer autoProducts (lines 981-1003) takes `title` from the `#{2,3}` heading with the rank stripped. It never reads `**Title:**`. The product payload (line 1062) sets `title: product.title` on drafts.<existing id> when an ASIN match exists. hedge-trimmers/best-hedge-trimmers/article.md line 50 '## 1. EGO POWER+ HT2601: Best overall for wide, mature hedges' with '**Title:** EGO POWER+ HT2601 26-Inch Hedge Trimmer' (line 52). The same **Title:** field appears in 5 product blocks in each of the three live hedge roundups.
- **Why it matters:** Importing any spec-shaped roundup would retitle shared product documents to strings like 'EGO POWER+ HT2601: Best overall for wide, mature hedges'. Once those product drafts are published, the new names appear on every page that uses the product (cards, tables, Product JSON-LD), including other live articles.
- **Fix:** In autoProducts, use `valueOrEmpty(lines, 'Title')` when present and strip a ': <award>' suffix from the heading otherwise. Better, take identity (title, brand, ASIN, link) only from handoff products[] (PIPE-14). Never set `title` on an existing product document. List title differences in the dry-run report for a human decision instead.

### PIPE-V05 - Slug and category inference reads the first '**Slug:**' line anywhere in the file, so packages that use '**Recommended slug:**' abort, or would pick up a garbage slug
**medium** · effort S · likely · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** Importer line 1022 `source.match(/^\*\*Slug:\*\*\s*`?\/?[^/]+\/([^/]+)\/?`?/m)` and inferCategory line 1006 `/^\*\*Slug:\*\*\s*`?\/?([^/]+)\//m` run on the whole source. The hedge hub, corded and cordless article.md files put the page slug under '**Recommended slug:**' (hub line 5), so the first '**Slug:**' is a product slug (hub line 53: `ego-power-plus-ht2601`). In JavaScript a negated class `[^/]+` also matches newlines, so the match runs past line 53 to the first '/' (the amzn.to URL at line 57). The category lookup then fails with 'Category not found: ego-power-plus-ht2601` ...'. Not executed here (no node on the Windows side); this follows from regex semantics and the file contents.
- **Why it matters:** The whole hedge-trimmer cluster (7 unpublished pages, same metadata style) cannot go through the auto importer at all. A package without any '**Slug:**' metadata falls back to the directory name, which can silently differ from the live URL.
- **Fix:** Read slug and category only from handoff YAML `recommended_slug` (PIPE-04 contract), or at minimum restrict the metadata parse to the preamble (parseMetadata's lines before the first H2) and accept both 'Slug' and 'Recommended slug'. Use `[^/\n]+` in any path regex. Fail the dry run when the resolved route differs from the handoff's canonical_url.

### PIPE-28 - The editorial-migration script writes immediately with no dry run and patches published documents directly
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** sanity/migrations/move-product-editorial-to-articles.ts lines 131 `await client.patch(doc._id).set(patch).commit()` inside a loop over every article/post (lines 147-153). It has no --commit flag. The header says 'npx tsx', but tsx is not installed. AGENTS.md line 3: 'Default every importer and migration to dry-run.'
- **Why it matters:** A re-run changes live documents without review. It is low severity only because it is idempotent for bare references.
- **Fix:** Add dry-run by default with a JSON plan output, a --commit flag gated on SANITY_API_WRITE_TOKEN, and a batch transaction with visibility 'sync'. Or delete the script if the one-time migration is confirmed done, which a read-only count of bare references in products[] would show.
- **Checker's correction:** 'tsx is not installed' does not block it, because `npx tsx` downloads tsx on demand. The more serious defect in this script is that it also rewrites post documents (PIPE-V03), so re-running it is not 'idempotent and harmless'.

## Design and UX

### DES-01 - The newsletter form on every page shows a success message but never sends the email anywhere
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/EmailSection/email-form.tsx lines 20-36: handleSubmit runs preventDefault, checks the email against a regex, then calls setError(null) and setSubmitted(true). There is no fetch, server action or API route. email-success.tsx line 5 then shows "Thanks for signing up! We'll be in touch soon." The block renders site-wide from src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx lines 33-35, and on the live epilators page under the H2 "Get the best buying guides before you shop" with the disclaimer "We respect your inbox. Unsubscribe at any time." The email input (email-form.tsx line 55) has only a placeholder and no <label>.
- **Why it matters:** Every reader who subscribes is told they are signed up, but nothing is stored. The page makes a promise to readers that it cannot keep, on the same pages that ask them to trust its product picks.
- **Fix:** Pick one of two options. (a) Remove <EmailSection> from src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx and unset emailSection in the Sanity siteLayout document until a list exists. (b) Wire it to a real provider through a server action in src/components/EmailSection/actions.ts that POSTs to the ESP. Set submitted only on a 2xx response, show EmailError on failure, and add a visually hidden <label for="newsletter-email">Email address</label>. While doing that, delete the body text that repeats the title, and cut the section's padding from py-24 to py-12 md:py-16.

### DES-02 - The hero template adds unsupported claims by default: an 'Expert Tested' badge and a 'TOP RATED' sticker
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ArticleHero.tsx line 44: when editorial_badge is empty the badge falls back to `Expert Tested • ${year}`. Lines 168-177 put a `{year} TOP RATED` star sticker on every non-editorial hero image, with year taken from publishedAt. Live pages: face-epilators.html and bug-zapper.html show "Expert Tested • 2024" and "2024 TOP RATED". hedge-trimmers.html shows the badge "Research-Based Buying Guide" next to the sticker "2026 TOP RATED". The vault forbids this: reference/01-brand-and-audience.md line 34 says "WhoAdvice does not claim to operate a testing laboratory or to have personally used a product unless genuine first-hand testing notes are supplied", and reference/05-banned-ai-patterns.md section 4 ("Fake hands-on authority") lists "We tested".
- **Why it matters:** The code puts a testing claim and a rating award on pages whose editors never made them. On legacy pages the '2024' year also tells readers the guide is stale. On the hedge-trimmer page the sticker contradicts the page's own research-based positioning.
- **Fix:** In ArticleHero.tsx, make getEditorialBadge return null when editorial_badge is empty, and render the badge <span> only when a value exists. Delete the TOP RATED block (lines 168-177) entirely. If an award overlay is wanted later, drive it from a CMS field such as article.hero_award that editors fill in explicitly. Also make editorial_badge required in sanity/schemas/article/index.ts and sanity/schemas/post/index.ts, with a description telling editors that 'tested' wording needs first-hand notes.

### DES-03 - Every product card is hard-coded with the heading 'Why we love it', including on the vault-approved hedge-trimmer article
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/product-card/ProsCons.tsx line 77: `const heading = isPro ? "Why we love it" : "Points to consider";`. The heading renders 7 times on epilators and 5 times on hedge-trimmers; the hedge-trimmer article is the vault-compliant one. The vault bans this wording in reference/05-banned-ai-patterns.md line 64 ("Our team loved"), reference/16-human-centered-writing-and-editing.md line 105 ("Do not write … 'we loved'") and reference/02-voice-and-tone.md line 108.
- **Why it matters:** The template adds an invented first-hand statement to copy that editors deliberately wrote without one. The cons card on the IPL product even says "Not a traditional epilator" directly under "Why we love it".
- **Fix:** Change the labels in ProsCons.tsx to "What it does well" and "Drawbacks", or better, read them from the article with those strings as defaults (add article.pros_label and cons_label to the Sanity article schema). Render them as <p class="text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-wider"> labels rather than <h3> (see DES-13).

### DES-05 - Body text runs about 123 characters per line on desktop because the prose container is max-w-max
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ArticleContent.tsx line 6 and LegacyPostContent.tsx line 12: `className="mt-5 w-full max-w-max prose … md:prose-lg lg:prose-xl"`. max-w-max overrides prose's 65ch cap, so paragraphs fill the Container (max-w-(--breakpoint-xl), px-4). The lead measured a 1,233px column at 20px/36px, about 123 cpl. The FAQ section is capped separately at max-w-4xl (Faqs.tsx line 19), so the article changes width part-way down.
- **Why it matters:** Around 45–75 characters per line is the comfortable reading range; at 123, readers lose their place moving from one line to the next. Wide prose also leaves no room for a table of contents or a sticky rail on desktop.
- **Fix:** Put the article inside a two-track grid in page.tsx: `grid lg:grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_280px] gap-12`, with the article in column 1 and a sticky TOC in column 2 (see DES-07). In ArticleContent and LegacyPostContent, replace `max-w-max` with `max-w-none` and constrain text blocks only: `[&>:is(p,ul,ol,h2,h3,h4,blockquote)]:max-w-[68ch]`. Wide components (.not-prose tables and cards) keep the full column. Use prose-lg as the top size; 20px on desktop is too large for a 68ch column.
- **Checker's correction:** Minor: Container padding is px-2.5 md:px-4, not px-4.

### DES-07 - A 23,884px mobile page has no table of contents, no jump navigation and no sticky element
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The lead measured no TOC, no aside, no sticky element and no skip link. The header is not sticky: Header.tsx line 18 has `bg-surface/95 glass-header` (backdrop blur) but no sticky or fixed class. A grep of the components for sticky or fixed finds only a commented-out block in components/Layout/TwoColumnLayout/Aside.tsx line 12. `scroll-anchor` (globals.css line 190) still offsets anchors by 72px+1rem as if a header were fixed. Every H2 already has an id (anchored-heading.tsx), so the page has the anchors to build a TOC from.
- **Why it matters:** A reader who wants 'the one for sensitive skin' or 'the FAQ about pain' has to scroll through 7 cards of about 1,700px each on a phone. There is no way to jump to 'Compare', a specific product or 'Buying guide', and no way back to the table after reading a card.
- **Fix:** Add src/components/Post/ArticleToc.tsx, a server component that builds [{id,text}] from the H2 blocks of article.content (plus product anchors from getProductAnchor). On lg, render it as the sticky right rail from DES-05 (`sticky top-6`), with the active section highlighted by an IntersectionObserver in a small client child. Below lg, render a sticky `top-0 z-30` horizontal chip bar with the entries Picks, Compare, Reviews, Guide and FAQ, h-11, overflow-x-auto, bg-surface/95. Add a 'Back to comparison' text link under each product CTA. Either make the header sticky or set scroll-margin to the chip-bar height rather than the header's.
- **Checker's correction:** 'No jump navigation' is overstated. The hero has two jump buttons, 'See Top Picks' (#top-picks) and 'Compare Models' (#comparison), on every article that has those blocks. What is missing is navigation to the reviews, the individual products, the guide and the FAQ, plus any persistent or return navigation. Desktop is equally affected (13,639px), and desktop is where GSC traffic is.

### DES-08 - Top-pick cards hide the award, repeat the verdict word for word, and sit under no heading
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** TopPickItemType.product is `Pick<ProductType, "title" | "link" | "image">` (src/lib/article-content.ts lines 34-38), so the product's tagline (the Sanity field titled 'Editorial badge', sanity/schemas/fragments/product-editorial-fields.ts lines 4-7) is never available to TopPickCard. resolve-top-pick-items.ts lines 4-14 use the product verdict as the pick description. On the live page, each quote in the three top-pick cards ("A solid entry-level epilator for beginners…") appears again as 'The Verdict' in that product's card further down. article-portable-text.tsx lines 179-182 pass showHeading={false}, which is why three H3s come before the first H2. Picks default to the first 3 products in array order (line 25). The vault's quick-pick spec (reference/14-article-prompt-library.md lines 1300-1306) requires an Award plus copy "written separately from the introduction, comparison table, product Summary, and Verdict".
- **Why it matters:** The picks strip is where a skimming reader decides, but it shows three images and three quotes with no 'Best budget / Best premium / Best for face' label. The IPL device is not visibly marked as a non-epilator. The same sentence then appears twice more further down.
- **Fix:** Add `tagline` to the Pick type and to the topPicksBlock GROQ projection. Render it as the first line of TopPickCard: `<p class="text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-wider text-primary">{tagline}</p>`. Add an `award` string and a `limitation` string to each topPicksBlock item in the Sanity schema, and remove the verdict fallback from resolve-top-pick-items.ts, rendering nothing when the description is empty. Always render a section heading: change the default to showHeading={true} with the text 'Our top picks', or read it from block.title. On mobile, lay the cards out as horizontal rows (64px thumbnail, award, name, one-line fit) instead of three stacked squares; that removes about 1,000px from the top of the page.
- **Checker's correction:** The verdict and first-3 fallback applies only when topPicksBlock.items is empty (line 20 returns the editor items otherwise). On epilators the text matches the verdicts either way. The measured 1,640px height supports the 'about 1,000px saved' estimate.

### DES-09 - The product-card layout puts the least useful content loudest and the decision content smallest, and pads each card heavily
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/product-card/ProductReviewCard.tsx: the left column holds a full-width square image (line 27, aspect-square, 616px on desktop) and then the verdict block (lines 45-54). So on mobile the order is rank → image → 'The Verdict' → badge pill → product H2. The verdict is `font-playfair text-2xl italic` inside `px-6` + `p-8`, which leaves about 235px of text width on a 353px card (inferred from the classes). The summary is text-base; pros and cons are `text-sm` (ProsCons.tsx line 70). Spacing on each card: my-12, mb-12 around the verdict, mb-12 after the header, space-y-10 plus mb-12 in the pros/cons, mb-8 under the badge. No price band, key specs (wet/dry, cordless, head width, attachments) or 'skip it if' appear anywhere in the card. The lead measured the page at 13,639px on desktop and 23,884px on mobile.
- **Why it matters:** The reader learns what the verdict says before learning which product it is. The pros and cons, the actual decision content, are the smallest text on the page (14px against 20px body). The facts a buyer compares on (cordless? wet/dry? price tier?) are only in the table, which is hard to use on phones (DES-10).
- **Fix:** Restructure ProductReviewCard as a single column on mobile and a 5/7 split on lg. Order: (1) rank + tagline award pill + H3 product name; (2) the verdict as one text-lg sentence in Inter semibold with no quote marks, dropping the 'The Verdict' label and the italic serif; (3) the image, 4:3 at max-h-64 on mobile and at most 360px square in the lg left column; (4) a spec chip row built from the comparison-table values for that product (Type · Wet/Dry · Power · Key feature); (5) the summary at text-base; (6) pros and cons side by side at md+ and text-base, labelled per DES-03; (7) one CTA. Replace the paddings with my-8 per card, p-5 md:p-8 inside, gap-5 between blocks. Target: a card under 1,000px on mobile.

### DES-10 - The comparison table is hard to use on phones, and its rows and labels drift from the product cards
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ComparisonTable.tsx line 35 has `min-w-[640px]`, and every cell has `whitespace-nowrap` (lines 90 and 98), so the lead measured the table at 1,026px inside a 353px scroller. It has no sticky first column, no scroll-shadow hint and no <caption>. Product names get an image tooltip on hover only (lines 57-76), which does nothing on touch. The 'Check Price' links are 14px underlined text (line 113). Colours are raw Tailwind emerald-900/800 and bg-[#F9F8F6] rather than tokens. On the live page the table lists 8 products; the row 'Ubroo ICE-COOL IPL Hair Removal Device CT05' links to #ubroo-ice-cool-ipl-hair-removal-device-ct05, but no element on the page has that id (grep count 0). 'Best For' is typed free text separate from the product tagline, so the Silk-épil 7 row says 'Best Wet & Dry Value' while its card badge says 'WIDE HEAD DESIGN'. The vault itself requires "The comparison table works on mobile" (reference/13-article-production-guide.md line 1138).
- **Why it matters:** On a phone the reader sees 1.5 columns at a time and loses the product name as soon as they scroll sideways. A row with no card and labels that disagree with the cards undermine the one at-a-glance view the page has.
- **Fix:** In ComparisonTable.tsx: (1) below md, render each row as a stacked card (a <dl> with name, Best for, Type, Use, Key feature, and one CTA); from md up, keep the table. (2) At md+, make the first column sticky (`sticky left-0 bg-white z-10 shadow-[1px_0_0_var(--color-outline-variant)]`), remove whitespace-nowrap from value cells, and add a visually hidden <caption>. (3) Derive 'Best for' from row.product.tagline, and delete the free-text column from the schema, or validate that it equals the tagline. (4) Add a Sanity validation rule on comparisonTableBlock requiring every row.product to also appear in article.products; in the renderer, render the name as plain text when there is no card. (5) Swap the emerald and hex colours for text-primary and bg-surface-container-low.

### DES-11 - The legacy renderer places the buying guide before the product reviews, and legacy product review text is counted and put in structured data but never shown
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/LegacyPostContent.tsx renders a hard-coded H2 'Introduction' (line 13), then top picks, then types (lines 29-39), then the products intro and table (41-53), then the guide (55-65), and only then the products (67-73). Faqs renders outside the article in a separate centred container (page.tsx lines 87-91; Faqs.tsx line 19, max-w-4xl mx-auto). Both renderers use ProductReviewCard (Product.tsx line 10), which never renders product.content. Yet getReadingTime counts that content (src/lib/reading-time.ts line 42; article-content.ts in the productReviewsBlock case), and it appears in JSON-LD reviewBody (face-epilators.jsonld.txt: "reviewBody":"<h3>Features and Benefits</h3>…"). On live face-epilators, the visible product titles are 'Braun B07T7ZV9N5' and 'Finishing Touch 40754502029745', i.e. an ASIN and a barcode used as names. The heading outline on that page confirms the order: Types, Products intro, Buying Guide, then the product H2s.
- **Why it matters:** On legacy posts, readers have to get through types and a buying guide before reaching any product. The '12 min read' promises text that isn't on the page. The structured data describes review content readers cannot see, and titles made of ASINs or barcodes look like a scraped catalogue.
- **Fix:** In LegacyPostContent.tsx, reorder to: body (drop the literal 'Introduction' H2) → TopPickCards → products intro + ComparisonTable → products → types → guide. Render <Faqs> inside the article, left-aligned. Either render product.content in ProductReviewCard, collapsed as 'Full review' in a <details> below the pros and cons, or remove it from reading-time.ts and from the Product review JSON-LD. The render-it option is better for legacy posts whose only substance is that field. Add a Sanity validation on product.title rejecting titles that match /\b[A-Z0-9]{10}\b|\d{11,}/, and fix the face-epilator product titles.
- **Checker's correction:** A third code-as-name title exists: 'Finishing Touch 022600998655'.

### DES-12 - The 'Types' and 'Buying guide' sections are card grids with body text in #888 at 14px, which fails AA contrast
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/GuideItemCard.tsx line 15: `[&_p]:text-sm [&_p]:text-text-light`, where --text-light is #888 (globals.css line 160), on a bg-white card. #888 on #fff is about 3.5:1, below the 4.5:1 WCAG AA minimum for 14px text (ratio calculated, not measured on the page). GuideList.tsx line 6 and TypesList.tsx line 6 lay these out as `sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3` grids. On the live page that is 7 type cards (a 3+3+1 orphan row) and 10 guide cards, including an empty `<h3>` between 'Ease of Cleaning' and 'Price and Long-Term Value'. TypeItemCard body is 15px (TypeItemCard.tsx line 35) and GuideItemCard is 14px, against 20px prose.
- **Why it matters:** The buying guide is the page's main 'how to choose' content, and it is the faintest, smallest text on the page, split into boxes the eye has to zig-zag across. Low-vision readers and anyone on a phone outdoors will struggle with it.
- **Fix:** Render guideSection items as prose in the article column: an H3 per item at body size, paragraph text in text-on-surface (#1c1b1b), and the item.tip as a single left-bordered callout. Delete GuideItemCard's text-text-light and text-sm. Render typesSection as a two-column definition list (<dl class="grid md:grid-cols-2 gap-x-8 gap-y-4">) with no images, or as the existing DecisionComparisonTable when types are compared on the same axes. In both PortableText renderers, skip heading blocks whose text is empty: in createAnchoredHeading, `if (!text.trim()) return null`, and add a matching blocks.h3 override in portable-text.tsx.
- **Checker's correction:** There are 9 guide cards, not 10. The empty <h3> is not a card: it is an empty heading block inside the portable-text content of the 'Ease of Cleaning' card, rendered by the legacy PortableTextComponent. 9 cards fill three rows, so only the types grid (7) has an orphan. Contrast is now measured on the live page, not just calculated.

### DES-20 - The footer lists 3 of 7 categories, has no editorial-policy link, and claims testing the site doesn't do
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The live footer Categories column links Kitchen, Hair Grooming and Skin Care only. The sitemap has home-gadgets (60 URLs; the only page with real GSC impressions, /home-gadgets/best-smart-plugs/, is here), personal-care (23), garden-tools (10) and beauty (5). The footer tagline says "We test, review, and curate the best products", and the legal line says "Our testing methodology remains independent and unbiased". Both conflict with reference/01-brand-and-audience.md line 34. Footer.tsx lines 72-78 use a Share2 icon for 'Contact us'. The contact address is whoadvice@gmail.com. There is no 'How we review' or affiliate-disclosure page link. The footer column titles are <h4> with no h2/h3 above them.
- **Why it matters:** Readers who check the footer for credibility find a testing claim the site doesn't back up and no method page. The categories that actually earn impressions have no footer link.
- **Fix:** In the Sanity siteLayout footer, list all 7 categories. Add a 'How we choose products' page (the vault's reference/01 positioning, written for readers) and an 'Affiliate disclosure' page, linked from the Company column. Rewrite brandTagline to 'We research, compare and explain products so you can choose with confidence.' Remove 'testing methodology' from legalContent. Swap Share2 for MessageSquare or Mail. Render the column titles as <h2 class="text-label-md">.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity raised to high. The footer makes the same unsupported testing claim as DES-02, and it does so on every page of the site. It is the same vault breach (01-brand-and-audience.md line 34).

### DES-34 - The article template has no defined component order for a buying decision; target layout given component by component
**high** · effort L · verified

- **Evidence:** Today the order depends on each document's portable-text array (article model) or is hard-coded to a guide-before-reviews order (LegacyPostContent.tsx lines 10-74). The live epilators page (article model) renders: hero → disclosure → 3 intro paragraphs → 3 unlabeled top picks → table H2 → 'Product List' → 7 cards → 7 type cards → 10 guide cards → FAQ → newsletter → footer, with related posts streamed into place before the newsletter. It has no methodology, conclusion or sources section, although the vault's roundup structure (reference/11-article-workflow.md lines 283-296) lists 'How products were selected', 'Conclusion' and 'Sources or editorial notes'.
- **Why it matters:** Without a fixed layout, each article ends up in a different order and the decision aids end up after long sections. The page cannot get the reader to a choice quickly and consistently.
- **Fix:** Standardise the roundup layout in the renderer. Enforce the order with Sanity validation on article.content, or have ArticleContent slot blocks into fixed regions. 1) ArticleHero (DES-02/06): badge, H1, one-line dek, byline with reviewed date, then a disclosure line linking the policy. 2) Top-picks strip under the H2 'Our top picks' (DES-08). 3) Sticky section chips on mobile and a TOC rail on desktop (DES-07). 4) 'Which kind do you need?' calloutGroup (DES-16). 5) Comparison: table at md+, stacked cards on mobile (DES-10). 6) Short intro prose (at most 120 words; move the current 3 paragraphs here). 7) Reviews: product cards (DES-09) as H3s under the H2 'The N epilators, reviewed', each ending with a 'Back to comparison' link. 8) 'How we chose' (a new methodologyBlock showing the article's evidence basis, with sources as external links). 9) Buying guide as prose (DES-12). 10) FAQ (DES-27). 11) 'Bottom line' conclusion block. 12) Related, 3 curated cards (DES-18). Then the footer, and the newsletter only once it actually works (DES-01). Everything text-based sits in the 68ch column; tables and cards use the full width.
- **Checker's correction:** The vault presents its roundup structure as a default to adjust, not a mandate. The fix should enforce the order of the blocks that are present rather than require every section.

### DES-04 - Unlayered heading CSS overrides the Tailwind weight, line-height and colour classes on every heading
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/globals.css lines 357-380 declare `h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6,strong { color: var(--text-dark); font-family: Poppins…; font-weight:700; line-height:1.3 }` outside any @layer. Lines 389-393 declare `.font-stitch :where(h1..h6) { font-family: playfair; font-weight: inherit; line-height: inherit; }`, also unlayered. Unlayered rules beat Tailwind's layered utilities whatever their specificity. The lead measured the effects: h1 font-weight is 400 despite its font-bold class, and the mobile H1 is 30px/45px, which is the inherited 1.5 line-height rather than leading-tight's 37.5px. By the same cascade, color #000 should override text-card-primary (#012d1d) on every heading, and <strong> (e.g. <strong>WhoAdvice</strong> in the disclosure) falls back to Poppins inside Inter text. Those last two are inferred from the CSS, not measured.
- **Why it matters:** The heading design in the components never actually renders. H1s and H2s look thin: a synthesized 400 weight drawn from the 600 face. They have loose leading: an inherited 1.5, or 1.8 inside prose-xl. They also lose the brand green. The hierarchy between section H2, product H2 and body text flattens, which is the main tool a scanning reader relies on.
- **Fix:** Move both blocks inside `@layer base { … }` in globals.css. Rewrite them as `.font-stitch :where(h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6){font-family:var(--font-playfair-display),ui-serif,Georgia,serif;font-weight:700;line-height:1.2;color:var(--color-primary)}`. Drop the h1–h6/strong Poppins rule entirely, and delete `strong` from the selector list so bold text stays in Inter. Then check computed styles in a real browser: h1 should report font-weight 700, line-height about 1.25 × font-size and color rgb(1,45,29).
- **Checker's correction:** The colour and <strong> claims are now measured, not inferred: every heading computes to rgb(0,0,0) rather than #012d1d, and the disclosure's <strong> renders in Poppins 700. One detail is wrong. Headings do not 'look thin' from a synthesized 400. The browser matches the requested 400 to the only loaded face, Playfair 600, so headings render semibold rather than the designed 700. The visible damage is black instead of brand green and very loose leading on wrapped headings (52.5px on a 30px H2 on phones, 45px on the H1). Hierarchy by size still survives, so medium, not high. The fix stands.

### DES-06 - On phones the first screen has no product, no picks and no image; the preloaded hero image starts at 714px
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** ArticleHero.tsx renders a one-column stack below lg in this order: breadcrumb (pt-8 pb-4), badge, H1 (text-3xl; 45px line-height per DES-04), a dek at `text-[19px] leading-[1.7]` (line 82), then an author row with py-6 and borders (line 87), the CTA row, and only then the image (lines 155-179). The lead measured the hero image top at 714px on an 812px viewport, and that image is the one with priority/preload. The dek is `post.seo?.meta_description ?? post.excerpt` (line 63), so the live page shows the SERP snippet "Looking for the best epilator? Compare top-rated epilators…" at a larger size than the body text (16px).
- **Why it matters:** A searcher on a phone sees a repeat of the snippet they just read and no answer. The preloaded LCP image mostly spends bandwidth below the fold. The first product name appears only after the three intro paragraphs.
- **Fix:** Rebuild the mobile hero as: badge, then H1 (text-[28px] leading-tight), then a one-sentence dek from a new article.dek field (stop falling back to meta_description), then a one-line byline (avatar 32px, name, Updated date; move the share icon into that line), then a compact 'Our picks' strip (see DES-08) that is visible inside the first 812px. Below lg, either hide the hero image or move it after the picks strip and drop `priority`: `hidden lg:block` on the image wrapper, with priority conditional on the desktop layout via a separate <link rel=preload media="(min-width:1024px)">. Set the dek to text-base on mobile and text-lg on lg.
- **Checker's correction:** The breadcrumb (pt-8 pb-4) comes from BreadCrumbs.tsx line 19, rendered by page.tsx lines 57-63, not from ArticleHero. The hero also contains 'See Top Picks' and 'Compare Models' buttons (ArticleHero.tsx lines 131-152) above the image. Severity lowered from high to medium: GSC shows 97.5% of impressions on desktop (469 vs 12), so the mobile-first-screen cost is currently bounded. The defect is still real for mobile-first indexing and for Bing mobile traffic.

### DES-13 - The heading outline is muddled: product names are H2s beside section H2s, pros/cons labels are H3s, and hidden '#' links are appended to headings
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ProductReviewCard.tsx line 65 renders each product name as <h2 class="font-playfair text-2xl">, a sibling of the section H2 'Product List', which prose-xl renders at 36px. The lead measured 7 product H2s. ProsCons.tsx lines 81-91 add two <h3> labels per card, 14 in all. anchored-heading.tsx lines 36-44 append <a …class="… opacity-0 … group-hover:opacity-100">#</a>. It stays invisible on touch devices and when focused by keyboard, because there is no focus-visible rule, and it adds '#' to the heading textContent, which the lead measured on 8 headings. The anchored heading's text-3xl class is also overridden by prose-xl's h2 size (measured at 36px), so the component's size classes have no effect.
- **Why it matters:** Screen-reader and outline users get a flat list of 30+ headings with no nesting. Keyboard users land on an invisible link. Reader mode, copy-paste and snippets pick up stray '#' characters.
- **Fix:** Render product names as <h3> inside a wrapping <section aria-labelledby> for the reviews section, and rename the CMS heading 'Product List' to something descriptive such as 'The 7 epilators, reviewed'. Replace the pros/cons <h3> with a <p> label (DES-03). In AnchoredHeading, give the link `aria-hidden="true" tabIndex={-1}` and draw the '#' with a CSS `::after` content, or add `focus-visible:opacity-100` and keep the '#' out of the accessible name with a <span aria-hidden>. Choose one type system for article headings: add `prose-headings:font-display prose-h2:text-3xl lg:prose-h2:text-4xl` on the <article> and remove the size classes from createArticleBlocks.
- **Checker's correction:** The size classes are not ineffective everywhere. At 375px text-3xl applies (30px), and prose-xl's 36px overrides it only at lg. The '#' link already has aria-label 'Link to {text}', so the fix should target focus visibility and tab order, not an accessible name made of '#'. Measured: 5 of these '#' anchors are among the sub-24px tap targets at 375px.

### DES-14 - Numbered rank badges suggest a ranking the order doesn't support
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ProductReviewCard.tsx lines 28-32 draw a 56px circle with {rank}; the rank is index+1 from article-portable-text.tsx lines 285-290. On the live page, #1 is the budget Silk-épil 3, #2 an IPL device, #3 a facial epilator, and the premium Silk-épil 9 Flex is #6. The section heading is the neutral 'Product List'. The vault package for this article defines a different, ranked order (per the facts file: SES9-041 first).
- **Why it matters:** Readers take '#1' as 'best'. Here #1 is the budget pick and #2 is not an epilator at all, so the numbering steers readers toward whatever sits at the top of the CMS array.
- **Fix:** Add `ranked: boolean` to productReviewsBlock in the Sanity schema. Render the numeral only when ranked is true; otherwise show the tagline award pill in its place. Set ranked=true only when the article contract declares a ranked roundup.

### DES-15 - 18 'Check Price' calls to action in three different visual styles, with no retailer or price context
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Three visual styles carry the 18 CTAs: TopPickCard.tsx line 53 (full-width rounded-xl filled button, 'Check Price'), ComparisonTable.tsx line 113 (14px emerald underlined text link, 'Check Price'), and CTA.tsx line 22, the editorial variant (rounded-full, px-10 py-5, shadow-lg, 'Check Price at Amazon' with an arrow). The lead counted 18 CTAs, all Amazon links with rel=sponsored. In ProductReviewCard.tsx lines 83-94 the main CTA sits beside an italic 'Updated: Jun 2026'. CTA.tsx lines 36-50 (a gray-700 legacy variant) and ProductCard/ProductTable (bg-blue-500, bg-gray-600) are further styles still in the codebase.
- **Why it matters:** Identical 'Check Price' buttons repeated 18 times make the page look like an affiliate funnel, not a guide. Readers have no sense of price tier until they leave the page, and the mix of button styles weakens the visual system.
- **Fix:** Keep one CTA per product in its card, one per row in the table (inside the stacked mobile card from DES-10), and none in the top-pick cards; make those name links jump to the review instead ('See review ↓'). Standardise on one AffiliateButton component with primary (card) and compact (table) sizes, using the bg-primary token, min-h-11 and text-sm font-semibold. Label it 'Check price on Amazon' so readers know where it goes. If an approved price_tier field exists ($, $$, $$$), show it next to the button rather than a live price. Delete the legacy variant from CTA.tsx.

### DES-16 - The decision-support components already exist but no published article uses them
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/CalloutGroup.tsx (section aria-label="Decision guide") and DecisionComparisonTable.tsx are wired into article-portable-text.tsx lines 158-173. A grep of the saved live HTML for 'aria-label="Decision guide"' returns 0 on epilators, hedge-trimmers, face-epilators and bug-zapper. The vault endorses 'Decision boxes' and 'Comparison callouts' (reference/02-voice-and-tone.md lines 190-196) and warns "Do not restate the same winners again in a second summary box" (line 192).
- **Why it matters:** The epilators reader's real first question is epilator vs IPL vs facial device, a branching decision. The page answers it only through a list of 7 type cards, even though the component for it has already been built.
- **Fix:** Do not add a separate 'quick verdict' box, because the vault forbids repeating the winners. Instead, add one calloutGroup right after the comparison table with 2-3 if/then branches (e.g. 'Want hair pulled from the root → epilator; want long-term reduction and have suitable skin tone → IPL; only upper lip/chin → facial epilator'), and replace the 7-card types grid with a DecisionComparisonTable (rows: Mechanism, Pain, How long results last, Best areas, Skin-tone limits). Add both block types to the article-contract template in the vault so editors plan for them.

### DES-17 - Per-card 'Updated' dates come from Sanity system timestamps and contradict the hero date
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ProductReviewCard.tsx lines 89-93 print `Updated: {formatMonthYear(product._updatedAt)}` ('Jun 2026' on all 7 cards), while ArticleHero.tsx line 118 prints `Updated {formatMonthYear(post._updatedAt)}` ('Aug 2026'). Both are Sanity system timestamps that change on any edit, including typo fixes and reference changes.
- **Why it matters:** Two different 'updated' dates on one page tell the reader that the products weren't rechecked when the article was. Neither date means 'an editor re-verified this'.
- **Fix:** Remove the per-card date from ProductReviewCard. Add an editor-set `reviewed_at` date field on the article schema and show 'Reviewed {Month YYYY}' in the hero byline, falling back to publishedAt and never to _updatedAt. Use the same field for dateModified in JSON-LD so the visible and structured dates match.
- **Checker's correction:** This affects every article, not only epilators. On legacy pages the card dates are older than the hero date (face-epilators Dec 2023 against Jun 2024), which reads as stale product data.

### DES-18 - Related posts are an unordered slice of the category, showing 2023 publish dates and legacy titles
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/requests.ts line 136: `*[_type in … && $category in categories[]->slug.current][0...6]` has no order(), so the result follows Sanity's default order. On the live epilators page the six related cards are Wax Warmer (Sep 21, 2023), Blackhead Removal Tool 'T0' (Jul 25, 2023), Face Massager, Facial Steamer, Callus Remover, and Face Epilators last. The date comes from publishedAt (src/components/Category/PostCard.tsx line 50). The block's H2 is a plain `text-3xl` (page.tsx line 112), unlike the styled article H2s. RelatedPosts returns the heading even when posts is empty.
- **Why it matters:** The most relevant next read, face epilators, comes last. Every card shows a 2023 date, which tells readers the site is stale, right after they finish a 2026 guide. The 'T0' typo appears in a card title.
- **Fix:** Add a `related` array of references on article and post (curated, max 3). Fall back to `| order(_updatedAt desc)` filtered to article-model documents first, and show 3 cards, not 6. In PostCard, show 'Updated {Mon YYYY}' from reviewed_at/_updatedAt instead of publishedAt, or hide dates older than 12 months. Return null from RelatedPosts when posts.length === 0. Rename the heading to 'Keep comparing', styled with the article H2 classes. Fix the 'T0' title in Sanity.
- **Checker's correction:** Not every card shows 2023. Four show 2023 and two show 2024.

### DES-19 - The header gives phone users no navigation, and the two remaining header actions both send readers to the homepage
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Header/Header.tsx lines 40-43: the menu is `hidden lg:flex`, with no hamburger or disclosure below 1024px. The live menu has one item, 'Explore Categories' → /#categories. The CTA 'Best Picks' → /#editors-picks (live header), and the search icon calls router.push('/#search') (SearchButton.tsx line 16). The logo renders an <img alt="WhoAdvice."> plus a visible 'WhoAdvice.' span on md+ (Logo.tsx lines 22-35), so the name is announced twice. The header is not sticky (DES-07).
- **Why it matters:** Phone readers, who make up nearly all real reading traffic, cannot reach a category from an article except through the breadcrumb. The loudest header button, a filled pill, takes readers away from the page they are on.
- **Fix:** Show the categories below lg too: a `<details>` 'Menu' button that opens a panel listing all 7 categories from siteLayout.header.menu, with no client JS needed. Put the actual category links in the CMS menu instead of a single '#categories' link. On article routes, drop the 'Best Picks' pill, or make it an outline style. Keep the search icon but send it to /search/ with an input, rather than the homepage anchor. In Logo.tsx, set alt="" when logo.text is rendered.
- **Checker's correction:** The why_it_matters claim that phone readers 'make up nearly all real reading traffic' is contradicted by the data. GSC shows 469 desktop impressions against 12 mobile over 3 months. The missing mobile menu is still a real defect. The logo name is announced twice only at md and up, since the span is hidden on phones.

### DES-21 - The affiliate disclosure is 12px and never links to the policy URL the component already computes
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/AffiliateDisclosure.tsx line 7 sets `const disclosureUrl = info.editorial_disclosure_url ?? "#"` but never uses it. Line 22 renders the text at `text-xs font-medium text-card-on-surface-variant` inside a pale box, followed by pb-16 (64px) of space (page.tsx line 73). The <strong>WhoAdvice</strong> in it is inserted with dangerouslySetInnerHTML.
- **Why it matters:** Placing the disclosure before the first CTA is right, but 12px text with no 'learn more' reads as fine print. A clear, linked disclosure makes the recommendations more credible.
- **Fix:** Render at text-sm, and add ` <Link href={disclosureUrl} className="underline">How we make money</Link>` when info.editorial_disclosure_url is set (create the page per DES-20). Replace dangerouslySetInnerHTML with JSX. Reduce the gap after it to pb-8.

### DES-22 - Colour tokens are defined three times over, and leftover legacy colours still ship
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** globals.css defines #012d1d as --color-primary (line 37), again as --color-card-primary (line 86), and hard-codes it a third time in `@utility text-primary { color:#012d1d }` (lines 286-288); bg-primary, bg-surface and similar are hard-coded the same way (lines 290-332). Legacy colours on live pages: PostsList.tsx line 46 'Load more posts' is `bg-red-400 hover:bg-red-500` (category pages); Section.tsx line 44 draws a #FE4F70 gradient squiggle (live on author pages and search results); search/page.tsx line 122 uses `bg-blue-500`; Author.tsx line 29 uses hover:text-brand-red; ComparisonTable uses emerald-900/800. There are also six near-identical off-whites: body #fcf9f8 (line 340), page-background #fafaf7, the newsletter's #F8F7F2 (EmailSection.tsx line 16), the table header's #F9F8F6, card-image-bg #f9f7f5 and surface-container-low #f6f3f2.
- **Why it matters:** A red load-more button and a pink squiggle on a green editorial site make the site look assembled from two themes. Triple definitions mean a brand-colour change will miss places, and the near-duplicate off-whites create visible seams between sections.
- **Fix:** Delete the --color-card-* aliases and replace the card-* classes with the M3 names (a mechanical rename: text-card-primary→text-primary and so on). Hand-edit, because some class lists mix names. Delete the hard-coded @utility colour blocks (lines 286-332), since @theme already generates those utilities. Replace bg-red-400 with `bg-primary text-on-primary`, and remove the squiggle SVG from Section.tsx. Collapse the off-whites into --color-surface (#fcf9f8) and --color-surface-container-low (#f6f3f2). Afterwards, check that `grep -rE 'card-|brand-red|red-4|blue-5|emerald-|#F[89]' src` returns nothing.
- **Checker's correction:** Two items listed as live are not visible on the audited pages. (1) Post/Author.tsx is dead code: nothing imports it except the Post barrel. (2) The red 'Load more posts' button renders only on categories with more than 20 posts (kitchen-appliance, home-gadgets, hair-grooming, personal-care), not on skin-care, where the saved HTML has 0 matches.

### DES-24 - 32 tap targets on the phone layout are smaller than 24px, concentrated in the breadcrumb, table and footer
**medium** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** The lead measured 32 links or buttons under 24px in one dimension at 375px. Likely sources in code: breadcrumb links at `text-xs uppercase` with no padding (BreadCrumbs.tsx line 20); table 'Check Price' as 14px inline text (ComparisonTable.tsx line 113); table product-name links (line 62); footer links `font-label-sm text-label-sm` (12px) at space-y-4 (Footer.tsx line 24); and the opacity-0 '#' heading anchors. BreadCrumbs.tsx line 19 also renders <nav> without an aria-label. The attribution of the 32 targets to these sources is inferred, not measured element by element.
- **Why it matters:** Small targets on a long mobile page cause mis-taps, and 24px is the WCAG 2.2 AA minimum (2.5.8). The affiliate links in the table are among the smallest.
- **Fix:** Give text links in the breadcrumb and footer `inline-flex min-h-6 items-center py-1`, raise the footer text to text-sm, give table CTAs min-h-11 (or move them into the stacked mobile cards per DES-10), and remove the focusable '#' anchors from tab order (DES-13). Add aria-label="Breadcrumb" and aria-current="page" on the last item. Then re-measure with the same 375px script; the pass condition is 0 targets under 24px.
- **Checker's correction:** The attribution is now measured element by element, not inferred. It also adds the hero byline author link (ArticleHero.tsx line 109) as a source. The affiliate links in the table are half of all failures.

### DES-25 - The author page, where the byline leads, has no H1, bio, photo or credentials, and still uses the legacy section style
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/(frontend)/author/[slug]/page.tsx renders `<Section title={author.name}>` followed by a PostGrid, and nothing else. Section.tsx line 17 makes the name an uppercase <h2> with the #FE4F70 squiggle. The live author-emily-cooper.html has no <h1>; its first heading is the H2 'Emily Cooper', followed by 2023 legacy post cards. The hero byline links there (ArticleHero.tsx line 111).
- **Why it matters:** Readers who click the byline to check who is advising them land on an unstyled list with no information about the author, which weakens every article's authorship signal.
- **Fix:** Build an AuthorHeader component: H1 name, 96px photo, role line, 2-3 sentence bio from the author schema's portable-text bio, a 'How this author researches' line linking to the method page, and a list of that author's articles sorted by _updatedAt with the new-model articles first. Remove the Section wrapper. Add bio and role fields to the Sanity author schema if they are missing.

### DES-26 - The page ends with over 400px of stacked padding from the related posts, newsletter and footer
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** After the FAQ (mb-12) come RelatedPosts `py-14` (page.tsx line 111), EmailSection `py-24` (EmailSection.tsx line 16) and the footer `py-section-gap`, which is 120px top and bottom (Footer.tsx line 49; globals.css lines 273-276). Inside the article, each ProductReviewCard adds my-12 plus internal mb-12 blocks, and the hero adds pb-12 and then pb-16 around the disclosure (page.tsx line 73).
- **Why it matters:** The padding adds a lot of height (13.6k desktop / 23.9k mobile) without adding content. Those pages are already long, and the uniform very large gaps make the page's sections harder to tell apart.
- **Fix:** Define a spacing scale in @theme: --spacing-section: 64px (desktop) / 40px (mobile) and --spacing-block: 32px / 24px. Use py-10 md:py-16 for RelatedPosts and EmailSection, and pt-16 pb-10 for the footer (replacing py-section-gap on article routes). Apply the card spacing from DES-09. Re-measure scrollHeight at 375px; the target is under 16,000px with unchanged content.

### DES-29 - Dead legacy product components are still in the bundle graph and keep the confusing colours alive
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** LegacyProduct.tsx, ProductCard.tsx (bg-blue-500 CTA), ProductSlider.tsx (imports swiper and swiper/css), ProductTable.tsx (bg-black header) and Post/Author.tsx are exported from product-card/index.ts and Post/index.ts. A grep of src shows no imports outside those index files and each other. Post/Header.tsx and Post/Content.tsx are alias re-exports. components/Layout/TwoColumnLayout and SidebarWidget.tsx (#FE4F70 gradient) have no importers. Product.tsx always returns ProductReviewCard, so LegacyProduct never renders even on legacy posts.
- **Why it matters:** The task brief asks for a LegacyProduct vs new renderer comparison, but LegacyProduct has no visible output at all. Keeping dead components with off-brand colours and a swiper dependency invites someone to wire them back in.
- **Fix:** Delete LegacyProduct.tsx, ProductCard.tsx, ProductSlider.tsx, ProductTable.tsx, the legacy branch of CTA.tsx, Post/Author.tsx, Post/Header.tsx, Post/Content.tsx, Layout/TwoColumnLayout/ and SidebarWidget.tsx. Remove their exports and uninstall swiper if nothing else imports it. Check with `pnpm build` and a grep for each name.
- **Checker's correction:** This is worse than 'in the bundle graph'. The dead ProductSlider's swiper stylesheet actually ships as a render-blocking <link rel=stylesheet> on the live article page. That happens because Faqs.tsx imports Faq through the Post barrel, which pulls in product-card/index.ts. Severity raised to medium. The fix should also change Faqs.tsx to `import Faq from "./Faq"` and stop re-exporting product-card components from Post/index.ts.

### DES-V01 - GuideItemCard hard-codes a '💡 EXPERT TIP:' label on every guide tip, an authority claim editors never make
**medium** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/GuideItemCard.tsx lines 18-26 render, whenever item.tip is set: `<p className="m-0 text-sm font-bold text-text-dark"><span aria-hidden="true">💡 </span>EXPERT TIP:</p>`, then the tip in `text-sm italic text-text-light` (#888). 0 occurrences on the 8 saved pages, so it is latent, but it is the same template-injected authority as DES-02/DES-03 and fires the moment an editor fills the tip field.
- **Why it matters:** The template labels ordinary editor copy as expert advice. That breaks 01-brand-and-audience.md line 34 and the 'fake hands-on authority' ban in 05-banned-ai-patterns.md section 4, and it prints the tip in the same failing #888 grey as DES-12.
- **Fix:** In GuideItemCard.tsx, replace the label with a neutral 'Tip' (or read it from a CMS field defaulting to 'Tip'), drop the emoji, and render the tip text in text-on-surface (#1c1b1b) non-italic. Fold this into the DES-12 rewrite that turns guide items into prose with one left-bordered callout.

### DES-V02 - On phones the top of the page is spent on three stacked square images: the first product review starts 4,537px down
**medium** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** Measured on the live epilators page at 375x812 (Browser pane, getBoundingClientRect + scrollY): hero image top 714px, #top-picks top 1,987px with height 1,640px (three stacked aspect-square TopPickCards, TopPickCards.tsx line 27 `grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-3`), #comparison top 3,923px, first ProductReviewCard top 4,537px (height 1,670px), second card 1,831px tall. Page scrollHeight 23,884px.
- **Why it matters:** It quantifies DES-06/08/09. A phone reader scrolls about 5.6 screens before the first review and about 2 screens per product. The comparison sits behind 1,640px of images that repeat what the table then shows.
- **Fix:** Below md, render TopPickCard as a horizontal row: `grid grid-cols-[64px_1fr] gap-3`, a 64px thumbnail, the tagline award, the name, and one line of fit, with no square image and no CTA (see DES-15). Target #comparison top under 1,800px and the first review under 2,600px at 375px, and re-measure with the same script.

### DES-23 - Three font sources load at once, and the verdict's italic is synthesized
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** globals.css lines 1-6 import @fontsource Poppins 400/500/600/700 and Playfair Display 600/700. src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx lines 10-21 also load next/font Inter and Playfair_Display 600/700 (normal style only). The Tailwind token `--font-playfair: var(--font-playfair-display)…` (globals.css line 111) is resolved at :root, where --font-playfair-display is the fontsource 'Playfair Display' (line 157). So the `font-playfair` class (rank numeral in ProductReviewCard line 29, verdict blockquote line 50) uses the fontsource copy, while headings get the next/font copy through .font-stitch. The verdict is `italic` but no italic face is loaded, so the browser slants the upright face. Poppins now reaches only <strong> via the unlayered rule (DES-04). The cascade is inferred from the CSS; network requests were not traced.
- **Why it matters:** Two copies of Playfair plus Poppins load for little visible benefit. A fake italic in the largest text on each card looks poor, and <strong> switching typeface mid-sentence looks like a bug.
- **Fix:** Delete lines 1-6 of globals.css and uninstall @fontsource/poppins and @fontsource/playfair-display. Set `--font-playfair: var(--font-playfair-display)` in `@theme inline` so it resolves per element, or apply the next/font variables on <html> in src/app/layout.tsx rather than on the .font-stitch div. Either drop italics on the verdict (per DES-09) or add style: ['normal','italic'] to Playfair_Display. Change the body font-family to var(--font-inter-family).
- **Checker's correction:** 'Two copies of Playfair load' was not observed. Both sources register the same family name, so the browser merges the @font-face rules and downloaded one Playfair file (600 normal). The real costs are: (a) a Poppins 700 file downloaded only for the one <strong> in the disclosure, (b) unused Poppins @font-face rules in the 94 KB main CSS, (c) a synthesized italic on the verdict, which is also drawn from the 600 face for a 400 request. The 'font-playfair class uses the fontsource copy' distinction is moot. Severity lowered to low.

### DES-27 - The FAQ accordion uses small type, an inconsistent width, and the legacy renderer for answers
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Faq.tsx line 9 sets questions at `text-base font-medium`, and the chevron has no focus-visible style. Answers render through the legacy PortableTextComponent (line 17), which outputs the empty <p><br/></p> blocks visible in the JSON-LD for Q2 and Q1. The section is max-w-4xl (Faqs.tsx line 19), narrower than the 1,233px prose above it, and centred (mx-auto) only when showTitle is true, which means on legacy pages.
- **Why it matters:** The FAQ is a common landing point for 'is epilating painful'-type queries. Blank paragraphs and a width that jumps make it look unfinished.
- **Fix:** Use ArticlePortableText (or a shared minimal renderer that drops empty blocks) for FAQ answers. Match the FAQ width to the text column from DES-05 and always left-align it. Set summary text to text-lg font-semibold with `focus-visible:outline-2 outline-primary`. Keep all items collapsed but give each <details> an id so the TOC and search snippets can link to it.
- **Checker's correction:** 'Centred only on legacy pages' is not quite right. article-portable-text.tsx lines 304-311 also pass showTitle when faqSection.title is set, so article-model pages with a titled FAQ block are centred too. Epilators is not centred because its FAQ H2 is a separate portable-text heading.

### DES-28 - The legacy PortableText renderer shows 'Unknown block type' text to readers
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/ui/portable-text.tsx lines 57-69: unknownType returns <div>Unknown block type</div>. The same fallback is in article-portable-text.tsx lines 317-330. The legacy renderer is still used for types and guide item content, FAQ answers, the footer legal text and category overviews, and it does not register calloutGroup or decisionComparisonTableBlock (lines 13-21), so those blocks would show this text if nested in any of those places. None is visible on the saved pages (grep count 0).
- **Why it matters:** The first editor to paste a new block type into a guide item or FAQ answer would put debug text on a live page.
- **Fix:** In both renderers, return null from unknownType when process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production' and keep console.warn for server logs. Better, merge portable-text.tsx into article-portable-text.tsx with an option that turns off section-level blocks, so one renderer handles all portable text.

### DES-30 - Motion has no reduced-motion guard, and some affordances are hover-only
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** globals.css line 335 sets `html { scroll-behavior: smooth }` unconditionally. PostCard.tsx line 27 applies `group-hover:scale-105 duration-500`, and category-section.tsx line 89 translates images on hover. The newsletter and header buttons use active:scale-95. A grep of src for prefers-reduced-motion, motion-reduce or motion-safe finds nothing. Hover-only affordances: the heading '#' anchors (opacity-0 until hover) and the comparison-table image tooltip (Radix tooltip on hover).
- **Why it matters:** Readers who set their OS to reduce motion still get smooth-scroll jumps across a 24k px page. Touch readers never discover the hover-only features.
- **Fix:** Wrap the smooth-scroll rule in `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference) { html { scroll-behavior: smooth } }`. Add `motion-reduce:transform-none motion-reduce:transition-none` to the card hover-transform classes. Replace the table tooltip with a 40px thumbnail in the first column (or the stacked mobile card), and remove the Radix tooltip and 'use client' from ComparisonTable.tsx.

### DES-31 - No dark mode and no colour-scheme declaration; hold off on dark mode until the tokens are consolidated
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** A grep of the .tsx files finds only 1 'dark:' usage. globals.css hard-codes the body background (#fcf9f8, line 340) and the .font-stitch background (line 384), and has no prefers-color-scheme block or color-scheme property. Browsers with automatic dark theming (e.g. Chrome Android's auto-dark flag, Samsung Internet) may then recolour the page themselves; this was not tested on devices.
- **Why it matters:** Dark mode is not what is costing readers or revenue here, but an algorithmically darkened page with product images on #f9f7f5 tiles can look broken, with no control over the result.
- **Fix:** For now, add `color-scheme: light` on :root and <meta name="color-scheme" content="light"> in the root layout. Build dark mode only after DES-22 has reduced the palette to the M3 tokens: redefine those tokens under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` guarded by :root:not([data-theme=light]), and give product image tiles a fixed light background.

### DES-32 - The homepage search section repeats the 'Today's Smart Picks' title and its search field has no label or submit button
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The live home.html search section (id="search") has the H2 "Today's Smart Picks" (CMS field searchSection.title), the same text as the Smart Picks panel H3 above it. search-section.tsx lines 81-92 render an <input type="text"> with a placeholder only, no <label> and no submit button; submission works only via Enter, and queries under 3 characters do nothing silently (line 38). The header search icon on every page routes to this section.
- **Why it matters:** Two blocks with the same title confuse readers. Search is the only site-wide finding tool on phones (see DES-19), and it gives no visible way to submit and no feedback.
- **Fix:** In Sanity, change searchSection.title to 'Search our guides'. In search-section.tsx, add a visually hidden <label>, make the input type="search" with enterKeyHint="search", add a visible submit button inside the field, and show 'Type at least 3 letters' text when the query is too short.

### DES-33 - The category page H1 shows the '| WhoAdvice' title-tag suffix as its visible headline, and category cards show 2023 dates
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** CategoryHeader.tsx line 165 renders `{title}` from getCategoryTitle. Live category-skin-care.html H1: "Skincare Product Reviews & Buying Guides| WhoAdvice" at text-6xl on desktop. PostCard dates come from publishedAt (PostCard.tsx line 50), so skin-care cards read 2023. The category page also uses the red 'Load more posts' button (DES-22).
- **Why it matters:** A 60px headline ending in a pipe and the brand name looks like a template leak, and a wall of 2023 dates makes the category look abandoned.
- **Fix:** Render category.title (or a dedicated h1 field) in the H1 and keep getCategoryTitle for <title> only. Adding the missing space before the pipe is a separate SEO fix. Show 'Updated Mon YYYY' on cards per DES-18.
- **Checker's correction:** The H1 is at CategoryHeader.tsx lines 51-53, not line 165 (the file is 109 lines). Skin-care cards are mixed (4 of 13 from 2023), not all 2023. The red 'Load more posts' button does not render on skin-care: 14 posts is under the 20-post page, so hasMore is false. It appears only on the larger categories.

### DES-V03 - Product anchors and heading anchors use two different slug functions; product ids keep '/' and accented characters
**low** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** Saved epilators.html ids include id="philips-epilator-series-8000-bre740/14", id="philips-epilator-series-2000-bre227/00" and id="braun-silk-épil-3-3-270" (from getProductAnchor in src/lib/product-anchor.ts). Heading ids come from slugifyHeading (anchored-heading.tsx lines 5-11), which strips everything outside a-z0-9 and '-'. GuideItemCard.tsx line 5 and TypeItemCard.tsx line 9 use a third rule, `title.replace(/\s/g,'-').toLowerCase()`.
- **Why it matters:** Shared deep links to products become percent-encoded fragments such as %C3%A9 and '/'. Any TOC (DES-07) or 'Back to comparison' link has to know three slug rules, and a title change in one place silently breaks table-to-card links, which is how the dangling Ubroo row (DES-10) goes unnoticed.
- **Fix:** Create one slugify in src/lib/slug.ts (NFKD normalise, strip diacritics, replace non [a-z0-9] with '-', collapse repeats). Use it in getProductAnchor, anchored-heading.tsx, article-portable-text.tsx slugifyHeading, GuideItemCard and TypeItemCard. Keep a one-release alias id on product cards for the old anchors if any have been shared.

## Competitors and search results

### COMP-01 - The site claims hands-on testing it does not do. Competitors that claim testing show the protocol; WhoAdvice shows nothing to back the claim
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Saved live HTML, the footer on every page (epilators, about, author-emily-cooper, home): "We test, review, and curate the best products so you can shop with confidence". The affiliate disclosure says "Our testing methodology remains independent and unbiased." face-epilators.html renders the hero badge "Expert Tested • 2024", and so does bug-zapper.html. Source: src/components/Post/ArticleHero.tsx lines 34-45 (getEditorialBadge) falls back to `Expert Tested • ${year}` whenever the CMS field editorial_badge is empty. Vault reference/04-editorial-positions.md section 1 forbids "We tested" unless genuine testing records exist. The vault article (article.md line 7 and line 195) states "WhoAdvice did not conduct hands-on testing". Competitors: Good Housekeeping (the AOL syndication of 17 Jul 2026) describes 50 women testers, lab tests and Quiet Mark noise testing. Emily's Review states a 4-week panel and 5 scoring criteria.
- **Why it matters:** The SERP rewards testing only when it is evidenced. An unevidenced "Expert Tested" badge beside a page that has no protocol, tester, photo or date is the weakest trust position available. It also contradicts the article's own research-only disclosure once the vault copy ships. That is a reader-facing false statement, and it is exposed if an affiliate or programme reviewer spot-checks it.
- **Fix:** 1) In src/components/Post/ArticleHero.tsx getEditorialBadge, change the fallback from `Expert Tested • ${year}` to `Research-Based Buying Guide` (the label the published hedge-trimmer page already renders). Do not derive a testing claim from a missing field. 2) Change the footer tagline source (it is not in src/components/Footer/*, so find it in the Sanity site-settings document) to "We research and compare products using manufacturer documentation, manuals and owner feedback." 3) Change the disclosure sentence "Our testing methodology remains independent" to "Our research and ranking remain editorial decisions." 4) Add an Evidence-model select field (research / hands-on / expert-led) to the post schema. Allow a hands-on badge only when the field is hands-on and a tester and test-date block is filled in.
- **Checker's correction:** The page under audit, /skin-care/best-epilators/, does not show 'Expert Tested'. Its hero badge is a CMS value, "Reviewed & Compared • 2026". The false testing claim reaches it through the sitewide footer tagline and footer legal line. The 'Expert Tested' fallback appears on the legacy pages (face-epilators, bug-zapper). The 'testing methodology' sentence is in the footer legal content, not in the in-article AffiliateDisclosure.tsx, which only says the site is reader-supported. For fix 2, the tagline is the footer document's `brandTagline` field (Footer.tsx), and the 'testing methodology' sentence is the footer legalContent. Both are Sanity edits. I did not verify Emily's Review's panel claim.

### COMP-02 - The live Top 3 fails the SERP's category test: two of three quick picks are IPL devices, and no fetched ranking page puts IPL in a best-epilator list
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** epilators.txt lines 24-34: the top picks are Braun Silk-épil 3 3-270, Braun IPL Silk Expert Pro 5 PL5137 and Braun FaceSpa Pro 911. The comparison table also lists Ubroo ICE-COOL IPL CT05. The competitor lists are all mechanical. NBC Select (updated 16 Dec 2025): Philips Series 8000, Remington Smooth & Silky, Braun Silk-épil 9 Flex SkinSpa, Panasonic Gentle Cap, Philips Series 2000, Leuxe, Epilady Speed. Good Housekeeping: Braun Silk-épil 9 Flex SkinSpa, Philips Series 8000, Panasonic Gentle Cap. Cyclepil: Silk-épil 7, Philips 8000, Philips 4000, Silk-épil 9 Flex. The vault's approved article.md (7 mechanical models, SES9-041 first) already fixes this, but it is unpublished.
- **Why it matters:** The quick picks are the part of the page a comparison shopper reads first. For them, and for search engines judging intent match, two of the three answers belong to a different product category. The page sits at position 14.3 in Google and 7.9 on Bing for "epilator hair removal" with 0 clicks. The first screen answers a question the searcher did not ask.
- **Fix:** Publish the vault article articles/epilators/best-epilators-at-home-hair-removal/article.md over the legacy post at the same URL, using the new `article` model as the hedge-trimmer cluster did. Carry over its Top 3 (SES9-041, BRE708/00, SE7-041) and the 7-row table. Move PL5137 and CT05 to an IPL page (the vault audit assigns them to a separate IPL cluster) and link to it from the Epilator vs. IPL paragraph.
- **Checker's correction:** The sentence "The page sits at position 14.3 in Google and 7.9 on Bing for 'epilator hair removal'" conflates two numbers. 14.27 is the page's average position across all its queries in GSC Pages.csv; GSC has no 'epilator hair removal' query row. 7.87 comes from the Bing keyword report, which is site-level, not page-level. Severity is lowered to high: the live quick-pick copy itself labels PL5137 as an IPL device "instead of a root-plucking epilator". That makes this a category and intent mismatch rather than a false statement. NBC's award list in the finding is inaccurate: NBC's Best overall is the Remington Smooth & Silky and Philips Series 8000 is 'Best for body'.

### COMP-03 - There is no method, trust or author-credential section. Every ranking competitor fetched has one, and the vault already wrote one
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The live page's H2 list (facts file, plus epilators.txt lines 36-381) has no 'How we chose', 'Why trust us' or 'Meet our experts' section. The author page author-emily-cooper.html has no bio: only a post list, meta description "Posts created by Emily Cooper." NBC Select headings: "How I picked the best epilators", "Meet our experts", "Why trust NBC Select?". Good Housekeeping summarises its lab protocol. Cyclepil states it has reviewed only epilators for 6 years. The vault's article.md lines 193-211 contain a complete 'How we ranked the best epilators' section with seven preset weights (25/20/15/15/10/10/5).
- **Why it matters:** A research-based publisher can match testing publishers only on transparency. The live page shows 18 'Check Price' CTAs and zero statements of how the picks were made. The weighted rubric is a real trust asset competitors lack: NBC's method is qualitative only. It is currently invisible.
- **Fix:** 1) Ship the vault 'How we ranked' section, rendered as a visible block directly after the comparison table with the weights shown as a small table. 2) Add a 'Why trust WhoAdvice' box (the Sanity author document gets `bio`, `expertise`, `credentials` and `sameAs` fields) to ArticleHero or the article footer. It should state the research scope for this page (7 exact models, manufacturer manuals, the dated Philips US manual, NHS and AAD) and the research date of 20 Jul 2026. 3) Fill in the Emily Cooper author bio with real, verifiable background. If none exists, assign the article to the editor who actually did the research.
- **Checker's correction:** The Sanity author document already has a `bio` field (sanity/schemas/author.ts, portable-text array). The defect is that src/app/(frontend)/author/[slug]/page.tsx never renders it and hard-codes the description `Posts created by ${author.name}.` at lines 25, 34, 44 and 81. The fix should render `bio` on the author page and in a byline box, use it for the meta description, and add only the missing fields (expertise, credentials, sameAs). I did not verify Good Housekeeping's protocol summary directly; the search snippet only.

### COMP-04 - No qualified-expert input on a skin-contact category where competitors lead with dermatologists and chemists
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** NBC Select quotes two board-certified dermatologists, Dr. Marisa Garshick (MDCS Dermatology) and Dr. Stacy Chimento (Riverchase Dermatology), and profiles them under 'Meet our experts'. Good Housekeeping quotes Danusia Wnek, Senior Chemist at the GH Institute Beauty Lab. The live whoadvice page has 0 non-Amazon external links (facts file). Vault reference/06-product-review-guidelines.md section 1A allows research roundups to use "credible expert guidance", and section 1D defines an expert-led explainer. The vault face-page audit (research/live-cluster-competitor-audit-2026-07-20.md, 'Recommended rebuild contract') already calls for "medical/safety review".
