- **Why it matters:** Pain, ingrown hairs, folliculitis and facial use are health-adjacent questions, and the ranking competitors answer them with named clinicians. Without attributed expertise, WhoAdvice's safety paragraphs read as unsourced opinion and lose to NBC Select and Good Housekeeping on the same questions.
- **Fix:** Commission one qualified reviewer (a dermatologist or a licensed esthetician with verifiable credentials) to review the 'What to expect from epilation' and FAQ sections. Render a 'Reviewed by <name>, <credential>, <date>' line and add `reviewedBy` to the BlogPosting JSON-LD. Until a reviewer exists, keep claims attributed inline to the AAD, the NHS and the exact Philips manual, as article.md lines 299-303 already do, and never paraphrase a clinician who was not consulted.
- **Checker's correction:** schema.org defines `reviewedBy` on WebPage, not on BlogPosting. Emit it on the page's WebPage node (mainEntityOfPage) with a Person that carries credentials, rather than on BlogPosting. I could not re-verify Good Housekeeping's Danusia Wnek quote because goodhousekeeping.com is not fetchable; the claim rests on NBC alone.

### COMP-05 - No like-for-like spec matrix. Competitors show tweezer count, runtime and charge time per model; the live table shows none, and the vault table leaves gaps
**high** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The live comparison table columns are Product / Best For / Type / Use / Key Feature / Check (epilators.txt lines 38-43). None of them holds a number, and the only runtime on the whole page is "40 minutes of cordless use" for BRE740/14 (line 233). NBC product cards carry fixed fields: Target areas, Cordless, Water-resistant, Number of tweezers, Attachments, Speed settings. Orchyd, KayakCambria and ThirstyBear tables show tweezer counts, runtimes and charge times. The vault article.md table (lines 34-42) mixes these into prose cells: SES9-041 row has no runtime although line 50 says 50 minutes; SES9-441 has no tweezer count; SE7-041, SES9-041 and SES9-441 have no charge time; BRE227/00 and 3-270 have no speed count. The data exists in the product packets, e.g. products/philips-series-8000-bre708-00.md: 32 ceramic tweezers, up to 60 min, 3 h charge, USB-A.
- **Why it matters:** A manual-derived spec matrix is the single most defensible piece of added information a research publisher can offer. Scanners ("top 10 true epilator brands from least to most expensive", "epilator with ceramic blades" in the search exports) come for exactly these comparisons, and a table with one number per cell is also what AI answer engines lift.
- **Fix:** Add a second table, 'Specifications from manufacturer documents', below the editorial comparison table. Build it from new structured fields on the Sanity product document: tweezer_count, tweezer_material, head_motion (fixed/pivoting/fully flexible), power (corded/rechargeable/AA), battery_user_replaceable, stated_runtime_min, stated_charge_time_h, runs_while_charging, wet_dry_per_manual, speeds, light, included_heads, warranty_years, source_url and checked_at. Render 'Not stated by manufacturer' for blanks; never leave a cell empty or estimate. Keep the six-column editorial table as specified in content-brief.md.
- **Checker's correction:** I did not re-verify the Orchyd, KayakCambria or ThirstyBear table contents.

### COMP-06 - The vault's differentiator (manual-approved body areas) is described but never delivered: the reader is told to 'check the manual' instead of being given the answer per model
**high** · effort M · likely

- **Evidence:** article.md line 243: "permission must come from the exact manual, not the attachment name". Line 255: "check the exact manufacturer's instructions". Line 325: "Only if the exact device and attachment are approved for every intended area. ... Check the manual". No per-model table of approved areas exists. The vault competitor audit records that competitors get this wrong: Vogue mixes body products into face intent, and Women's Health's "Series 2000 bikini award needs manual scrutiny". Bing KeywordReport rows: "best epilator for legs and face" (pos 9), "epilator for face and for legs", "what is the best epilator for women's underarms and legs" (pos 2), "which style of epilator are good for women under arms" (pos 2), "top-rated epilator for facial and underarm hair removal" (pos 5.67).
- **Why it matters:** Area suitability is the reader's actual decision, and the SERP gets it wrong. A cited area-approval matrix is information gain no competitor fetched offers, and it answers the multi-area queries whoadvice already ranks top-10 for on Bing.
- **Fix:** Add an 'Approved areas by exact model' table with columns Legs / Arms / Underarms / Bikini line (external) / Face, plus the attachment or cap required. Mark each cell ✔ with manual section, ✖ 'manual prohibits', or '— not addressed in manual'. Source it from the manuals already in sources.md (e.g. the Philips BRE708/BRE728 US manual dated 2 Jul 2026) and add the equivalent Braun manuals per model. Store it as an `approved_areas` array of objects on the product document so the face and pillar pages reuse the same record.
- **Checker's correction:** The Bing keyword report is site-level, so these rankings cannot be attributed to the pillar URL specifically. Confidence stays 'likely' because no one has confirmed that each Braun manual actually states approved areas.

### COMP-16 - The 'best epilator for face' page is the weakest page in the cluster and still carries 'Expert Tested • 2024'; face-intent competitors score by facial zone and hair type
**high** · effort L · verified

- **Evidence:** face-epilators.html headings: "Braun B07T7ZV9N5", "Philips BRR474/00", "Finishing Touch 40754502029745", "Finishing Touch 022600998655", with the hero badge "Expert Tested • 2024". The vault audit says 3 of 5 are shavers, not epilators. GSC Pages.csv: /skin-care/best-face-epilators/ 9 imp pos 17; query "best epilator for face" pos 95. Emily's Review (fetched) scores 'Facial Zone Coverage', 'Precision & Hair Capture' and 'Results Longevity', separating fine vellus hair on cheeks from coarser upper-lip and chin growth. The search for 'best epilator for face' returned mostly retailer and brand pages (Amazon, Braun, Target, Walmart) plus NBC Select.
- **Why it matters:** The face branch is the vault's highest-volume expansion (the keyword-cluster notes cite 1,000 searches, KD 38, for the head term). The live page misidentifies products by ASIN or UPC and claims testing, so it cannot rank against zone-by-zone competitors.
- **Fix:** Execute P2 of research/live-cluster-competitor-audit-2026-07-20.md: rebuild from articles/epilators/best-face-epilators/ with only manual-approved facial epilators (FaceSpa Pro 911, Remington EP1050FCDN if identity resolves). Add a 'Facial zone × hair type' matrix (upper lip / chin / cheeks / jawline × fine vellus / coarse), with cells filled only from manufacturer area statements. Label shavers and trimmers as 'not epilators — alternatives'. Set editorial_badge to 'Research-Based Buying Guide' now, before the rebuild.
- **Checker's correction:** I did not re-verify the Emily's Review scoring criteria or the composition of the 'best epilator for face' SERP.

### COMP-V01 - A hard-coded '{year} TOP RATED' star badge overlays every product-guide hero, including research-only and 2024 legacy pages, with no rating source
**high** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ArticleHero.tsx lines ~168-176: `{!isEditorial && (<div ...><Star .../>{year} TOP RATED</div>)}`, where year = getUTCFullYear(post.publishedAt). Saved HTML: epilators.html '2026 TOP RATED'; hedge-trimmers.html '2026 TOP RATED' (the research-based page); face-epilators.html and bug-zapper.html '2024 TOP RATED'.
- **Why it matters:** 'Top rated' is an unsourced rating claim that no WhoAdvice score or rating system backs. On 2024 legacy pages it also advertises staleness. Ranking competitors (NBC, Cyclepil) show dated update notes rather than decorative rating seals, and the badge undercuts the research-only disclosure even on the correctly published hedge-trimmer page.
- **Fix:** Delete the TOP RATED overlay from ArticleHero.tsx, or drive it from an explicit CMS field (e.g. `hero_seal`) that defaults to empty and is allowed only when a documented rating method exists. Never derive it from publishedAt.

### COMP-07 - Award labels describe packages, not reader needs. Competitors and the Bing query data are organised by need (beginner, sensitive skin, thick hair, bikini, legs and underarms)
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Vault article.md awards: "Best Streamlined ProGuide Kit", "Best Wet/Dry Braun Value", "Best Full Kit", "Best Body-Grooming Kit", "Best Budget Braun Kit". Competitor awards: NBC "Best for sensitive skin", "Best for thick hair", "Best compact", "Most gentle"; GH "Best for Bikini Area", "Best for Beginners". Bing rows: "what's the best beginner-friendly epilator for someone who has never epilated before?" pos 2.5; "leg hair removal safe at home electric epilator beginner" pos 5.5; "which affordable epilator is actually worth the price for leg and underarm use?" pos 6. Vault reference/04-editorial-positions.md section 2 itself says award labels "should identify a real use case: Best for fine hair, Best for sensitive skin, Best budget device".
- **Why it matters:** Readers and answer engines match by need. "Best Streamlined ProGuide Kit" means nothing to a first-time buyer, so the page cannot be the answer to the beginner and underarm queries where it already has Bing visibility.
- **Fix:** Re-label the awards where the evidence supports a need, e.g. SES9-041 'Best for knees and ankles (fully flexible head)', BRE227/00 'Best if you never want to charge it', 3-270 'Best budget kit'. Add a 'Quick answers by need' block under the Top 3 (First-timer / Legs and underarms / Face and body / Low maintenance / Shower use), each pointing to one exact model with a one-line manual-backed reason. Reopen Part 1 as content-brief.md requires, because it locks the awards.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity is lowered to medium. Three of the seven vault labels already follow reference/06 section 7 good-label patterns (Best Overall, Best Corded Epilator, Best Budget Braun Kit), and the article is not yet live. I could not verify the Good Housekeeping labels 'Best for Bikini Area' and 'Best for Beginners' (site blocked).

### COMP-08 - An Amazon-only eligibility rule removed the one model every major competitor ranks (Panasonic gentle-cap), and package variants fill 4 of the 7 slots
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** products/unavailable-products.md line 32: "Panasonic ES-EY30V: unavailable on Amazon in the publisher's check; the exact model remained available from Panasonic's official US shop". The competitor audit's overlap list names the "Panasonic 60-tweezer gentle-cap model" as a core repeat signal. GH ranks the Panasonic gentle-cap model 'Best for Beginners', and its lab top-scorer was a Panasonic cordless shaver-epilator. NBC ranks it 'Best for sensitive skin'. The approved lineup has 2 brands only: two Silk-épil 9 packages (SES9-041, SES9-441) and two Philips packages the article itself says share "the same core approach" (article.md line 113). Article line 195 claims "Retailer eligibility ... did not determine the ranking".
- **Why it matters:** The lineup is narrower than the SERP consensus. A reader comparing with GH or NBC sees the best-known sensitive-skin or beginner option missing without explanation, while two near-duplicate variants take up space. The 'did not determine ranking' statement is not accurate when an eligible-by-evidence product was excluded by retailer.
- **Fix:** 1) Change the eligibility rule in the vault workflow (reference/15 or the product-refresh checklist): a manufacturer's official US store or a second major retailer qualifies as a destination, even if non-affiliate. 2) Reopen Part 1 for Panasonic ES-EY30V (and a Remington body model if its exact identity verifies). 3) Merge SES9-041/SES9-441 into one card with a 'Package options' sub-table, and do the same for BRE708/BRE728. 4) Until then, add one honest sentence to the method: 'Products without a verified US Amazon listing were not ranked.'
- **Checker's correction:** The article does not claim retailer eligibility had no effect. Line 195 says "Retailer eligibility determined whether a product could enter this commercial roundup, but it did not determine the ranking", which is accurate as written. The honest-sentence fix (4) is largely already there; it only needs to name Amazon. The sharper point is that the cluster's Amazon-only gate is stricter than reference/06-product-review-guidelines.md section 6, which requires only that a product be 'available to the target audience'. I could not verify GH's Panasonic 'Best for Beginners' or its lab top-scorer claim. Severity is lowered to medium because the gate is a disclosed, deliberate commercial rule.

### COMP-09 - No price or price tier and a single retailer. Competitors show dated prices and several stores; Bing queries ask for price ordering
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Live page: 18 external links, all Amazon, all 'Check Price' (facts file). No price anywhere in epilators.txt. NBC Select shows a price per pick ($26.99-$279.99) and links Amazon, Walmart and Braun. Bing rows: "top 10 true epilator brands from least to most expensive" (pos 8), "top 10 leg and body epilator brands from least to most expensive" (pos 8), "which affordable epilator is actually worth the price". content-brief.md: "Do not include prices or affiliate links in the table". reference/04 section 11: "Price is part of the verdict". The page's awards (Budget, Value) imply price with no number.
- **Why it matters:** 'Budget' and 'Value' awards with no price anchor are unverifiable to the reader, and price-sorted queries cannot be answered. A single-retailer 'Check Price' wall reads as monetisation-first compared with NBC's multi-store display.
- **Fix:** Add `price_band` ($ under $50 / $$ $50-120 / $$$ over $120) and `price_checked_at` fields to the product document. Render the band in the comparison table and a 'Price checked <date>' line on each card, which keeps the 'no live prices in table' rule while giving an ordering. Where the manufacturer store sells the exact model, add a second CTA ('Check price at Philips/Braun') with rel="sponsored" only when it is affiliate.

### COMP-10 - The cost of an epilator against waxing, shaving or IPL is asserted but never calculated; lower-tier competitors already publish cost-per-use sections

Vault article.md line 293: "Choose by desired upkeep, eligibility, discomfort tolerance, and total cost" (no figures). Orchyd (fetched, 17 Sep 2026) includes a "cost comparison analysis vs razors/waxing". ThirstyBear includes "Cost-per-use analysis" sections. Bing row: "epilator hair removal pros cons".

Take salon-waxing prices only from a published, citable price survey, else omit the row. Show the formula and the date, and label every input with its source. Do not state a saving figure without those inputs.

### COMP-11 - Nothing on how long a device lasts: replaceable battery, replacement heads, warranty. A specialist competitor answers 'How long will my epilator last?'

The live page says nothing about lifespan or parts.

The approved vault article uses a neutral '**Pros:**' label (article.md, each card).
