## Accessibility

### A11Y-01 - The newsletter form sends nothing but tells every reader they are signed up
**critical** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/EmailSection/email-form.tsx lines 20-36: handleSubmit only validates, then `setError(null); setSubmitted(true);`. There is no fetch, no server action and no provider call anywhere in the component. Line 45-46: `if (submitted) { return <EmailSuccess />; }`. email-success.tsx line 5 then shows "Thanks for signing up! We'll be in touch soon." The form is mounted site-wide from src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx lines 33-35. The live epilators.html shows it as `<form class="flex flex-col md:flex-row gap-4"><input type="email" ...>...Sign Me Up`.
- **Why it matters:** Every visitor who subscribes is told something false and their address is discarded. This is a trust defect on every page, and on a review site credibility is the product. It is also an accessibility trap: a screen-reader user gets no announcement at all (see A11Y-05), so they cannot even tell what happened.
- **Fix:** Do the smallest thing first: remove `emailSection` from the Sanity siteLayout document, or drop the `<EmailSection>` render in src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx lines 33-35, until a real list provider exists. When one is wired up, POST to a route handler (for example src/app/api/newsletter/route.ts) and set `submitted` only on a 2xx response. Show a real error on failure. Render the result inside a persistent `role="status"` container.

### A11Y-02 - Buying-guide body text is #888 on white (3.54:1), below the 4.5:1 minimum for 14px text
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/GuideItemCard.tsx line 15: `[&_p]:text-sm [&_p]:text-text-light`. Line 23 styles the tip as `text-sm italic text-text-light`. `--text-light: #888` is set in src/app/globals.css line 160. Live epilators.html has 9 guide cards, each `<div class="[&amp;_p]:text-sm [&amp;_p]:text-text-light ...">`, 18 occurrences of text-text-light in total. Computed contrast: #888888 on #ffffff = 3.54:1. The EXPERT TIP (#888 on stone-50 #fafaf9) = 3.39:1.
- **Why it matters:** Fails WCAG 1.4.3. The whole 'Buying Guide for Best Epilators' section, the advice that justifies the page, sits below the AA threshold at 14px. Low-vision readers and anyone reading outdoors on a phone struggle with it.
- **Fix:** In GuideItemCard.tsx lines 15 and 23, replace `text-text-light` with `text-card-on-surface-variant` (#414844, 9.40:1 on white). Also darken the legacy token in globals.css line 160 from `--text-light: #888` to `#666` (5.74:1), so the other users of the token pass too: ComparisonTable.tsx line 118, the Section.tsx line 57 subtitle and the admin views.
- **Checker's correction:** The page renders 9 affected guide-card paragraph blocks, not 18: the other 9 matches are in the RSC flight payload. No EXPERT TIP renders on the epilators page, so the 3.39:1 tip failure is latent, affecting any guide item that has a tip. Post/Author.tsx line 29 is another public user of the token and should be added to the list.

### A11Y-03 - Heading '#' anchor links are focusable but fully invisible when focused (opacity-0, hover-only)
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/ui/anchored-heading.tsx lines 37-43: `<a aria-label={`Link to ${text}`} className="ml-2 text-card-primary/50 opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100 hover:text-card-primary" href={`#${id}`}>#</a>`. Nothing reveals the link on focus: no focus or focus-visible variant exists in the repo (grep for focus-visible returns no hits). opacity-0 also hides the element's own focus outline. Live epilators.html contains 5 of these links (aria-label="Link to Best Epilators Compared: Quick Overview", "Link to Product List", "Link to Types of best epilators", "Link to Buying Guide for Best Epilators", "Link to Frequently Asked Questions"). The same pattern appears 5 times on hedge-trimmers.html.
- **Why it matters:** WCAG 2.4.7 Focus Visible failure. A keyboard user tabbing through the article lands on 5 stops where nothing on screen shows focus, so they lose their place. On touch devices the invisible link is still a tap target inside every heading.
- **Fix:** Remove the anchor. In anchored-heading.tsx render only `<Tag className={className} id={id || undefined}>{children}</Tag>` and keep the id, so #fragment deep links still work. Readers of an affiliate roundup get almost nothing from copy-a-section-link. If the link must stay, move it out of the heading into a sibling wrapper and add `focus-visible:opacity-100` plus a visible ring. That fix also resolves A11Y-04 and the trailing '#' in heading textContent.

### A11Y-05 - The newsletter email input has no label, no name and no autocomplete, and its errors and success are not announced
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** email-form.tsx lines 55-62: `<input type="email" required value={email} ... placeholder={placeholder} className="... outline-none" />`. It has no id, no `<label>`, no aria-label, no name and no autoComplete. Live: `<input type="email" required="" placeholder="your@email.com" .../>`. The placeholder is #1c1b1b at Tailwind v4's 50% placeholder mix, about #8e8d8d on white = 3.31:1. Because the input has `required` and `type=email` and the form has no noValidate, the browser's native bubble fires before the custom EmailError (lines 24-32), so those messages are unreachable. The input has no aria-invalid or aria-describedby. email-success.tsx is a plain `<p>` with no role="status", and it replaces the form, so focus drops to the body.
- **Why it matters:** Fails 1.3.1 and 3.3.2: a placeholder is not a label and it disappears as the user types. Fails 1.3.5 Identify Input Purpose (AA), because the field collects the user's own email and has no autocomplete="email". A screen-reader user who submits hears nothing and loses their focus position.
- **Fix:** In email-form.tsx add `<label htmlFor="newsletter-email" className="sr-only">Email address</label>`. Give the input `id="newsletter-email" name="email" autoComplete="email" aria-invalid={!!error} aria-describedby={error ? "newsletter-error" : undefined}`. Put `noValidate` on the form so the custom messages actually render, and give EmailError `id="newsletter-error"`. Render the success text inside an always-present `<div role="status" aria-live="polite">`, or move focus to it with tabIndex={-1}. Apply this only after A11Y-01 is resolved.

### A11Y-06 - Contact form labels are not associated with their inputs, validation errors are never shown, and the server response is never rendered
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Forms/ContactForm.tsx: the labels use `htmlFor="name"`, "email", "subject" and "message" (lines 51, 71, 83, 99), but no input has an id. `register()` spreads only name, onChange, onBlur and ref. The rules define messages such as "Minimum 4 characters required" (lines 60-66), but `errors.*.message` is never rendered; only aria-invalid is set. `const [message, setMessage]` (line 8) is set at line 40 but never used in the JSX. The catch block (line 42-43) only calls console.log. The inputs have no autoComplete.
- **Why it matters:** The /contact/ page fails 1.3.1 and 4.1.2: fields are announced as 'edit text' with no name, apart from the placeholder fallback. It fails 3.3.1 Error Identification, because users are blocked by invisible errors, and 4.1.3 Status Messages. It fails 1.3.5 for the name and email fields. Everyone, not only assistive-tech users, gets no confirmation that the message was sent.
- **Fix:** Add `id="name"`, `id="email"`, `id="subject"` and `id="message"` to the matching inputs. Add `autoComplete="name"` and `autoComplete="email"`. Under each field render `{errors.name && <p id="name-error" role="alert" className="mt-1 text-sm text-error">{errors.name.message}</p>}` and wire it with `aria-describedby`. Render `{message && <p role="status">{message}</p>}`. Check `res.ok` and set a failure message in the catch block.
- **Checker's correction:** aria-invalid is set on name, email and message but not on subject (lines 84-95). The fix should add aria-invalid to subject too.

### A11Y-04 - Each anchored heading's accessible name is doubled: 'X Link to X'
**medium** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** anchored-heading.tsx lines 34-44 put the `<a aria-label="Link to ${text}">` inside the heading element. Live: `<h2 ... id="frequently-asked-questions">Frequently Asked Questions<a aria-label="Link to Frequently Asked Questions" ...>#</a></h2>`. The heading's accessible name comes from its content, and that content includes the child link's aria-label.
- **Why it matters:** The headings list is the main way screen-reader users skim a long roundup. It will read 'Frequently Asked Questions Link to Frequently Asked Questions' for 5 of the H2s. That is noisy and makes the heading outline harder to scan.
- **Fix:** The same fix as A11Y-03: remove the in-heading anchor from anchored-heading.tsx. If it is kept, place it outside the `<Tag>` element.

### A11Y-07 - The top-picks section has no heading, so three H3s come before the first H2
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/ui/article-portable-text.tsx line 181 renders `<TopPickCards ... showHeading={false} />` for every topPicksBlock. TopPickCards.tsx lines 22-26 only emit the H2 'Top Picks at a Glance' when showHeading is true. Live: `<section class="not-prose my-10 scroll-anchor" id="top-picks"><div class="grid ..."><article ...><h3 ...><a ... href="#braun-silk-épil-3-3-270">`. The hero CTA 'See Top Picks' (ArticleHero.tsx line 136) points at this headingless section.
- **Why it matters:** The heading outline goes H1 → H3 → H3 → H3 → H2, which breaks 1.3.1 best practice. A screen-reader user who follows 'See Top Picks' lands in an unnamed region.
- **Fix:** In article-portable-text.tsx line 181 pass `showHeading` (true), or add an optional `title` field to the topPicksBlock schema and render it as the H2. In TopPickCards.tsx give the H2 an id and set `aria-labelledby` on the `<section>`.

### A11Y-09 - The body contains an empty <h3></h3> from CMS content in the 'Ease of Cleaning' guide item
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Live epilators.html: `<article id="ease-of-cleaning" ...><h3 ...>Ease of Cleaning</h3><div class="[&amp;_p]:text-sm ..."><p>Choose an epilator with a washable or removable head...</p><h3></h3></div></article>`. GuideItemCard.tsx line 16 renders item.content through PortableTextComponent (src/components/ui/portable-text.tsx), which has no block renderer and no empty-block guard.
- **Why it matters:** Screen readers list an empty heading ('heading level 3, blank') in the headings menu. That fails 1.3.1 and 2.4.6 Headings and Labels, and it also signals unchecked content.
- **Fix:** Delete the empty heading block from that guide item in Sanity. Add a guard in portable-text.tsx: `block: { h2: H("h2"), h3: H("h3"), ..., normal: P }`, where each renderer returns null when `value.children.every(c => !c.text?.trim())`. Do the same in createAnchoredHeading (anchored-heading.tsx line 57), and bail out when `text.trim()` is empty. The same guard removes the empty `<p><br/></p>` in FAQ answers (A11Y-34).

### A11Y-11 - 18 affiliate links are named only 'Check Price' / 'Check Price at Amazon', which cannot be told apart out of context
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** TopPickCard.tsx lines 52-59: link text `Check Price`. The editorial CTA in product-card/CTA.tsx line 30 reads `Check Price at Amazon`. ComparisonTable.tsx lines 109-116: `Check Price` inside a `<td>` whose row has no row header (A11Y-13). The facts count 18 such links on the page, all rel=sponsored. AffiliateLink.tsx already receives `productName` (line 13) but uses it only for analytics.
- **Why it matters:** In a screen reader's links list, and for voice-control users ('click Check Price'), 18 identical names point to 18 different Amazon products. 2.4.4 Link Purpose (In Context) is not met for the top-pick and card CTAs: the card is neither a paragraph, list item nor header-associated table cell.
- **Fix:** Fix it once in AffiliateLink.tsx: after `{children}` append `<span className="sr-only"> for {productName} (opens in a new tab)</span>` whenever `target === "_blank"`. The accessible name then starts with the visible text, which satisfies 2.5.3 Label in Name, and all 18 CTAs become unique ('Check Price for Braun Silk-épil 3 3-270').
- **Checker's correction:** The 2.4.4 reasoning is partly wrong. The top-pick and product-card CTAs follow a heading (H3/H2) that names the product, and technique H80 (link text plus the preceding heading) is sufficient for 2.4.4 (Level A, in context). Only the comparison-table 'Check Price' clearly fails 2.4.4, because its cell has no associated row header. The other 17 fail only 2.4.9 (AAA) and the links-list or voice-control usability problem. The fix stays valid. AffiliateLink's handleClick reads `children` when it is a string, so adding the sr-only suffix inside the <a> does not alter the analytics linkText.

### A11Y-13 - The comparison table has no caption and no row headers, and its last column is just 'Check'
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ComparisonTable.tsx line 35: `<table className="w-full min-w-[640px]">` with no `<caption>`. Line 90: the product cell is `<td className="px-4 py-3 font-medium ...">`, not a th. Lines 44-46: `<th scope="col" className="text-right">Check</th>`. Live header row: |Product|Best For|Type|Use|Key Feature|Check|. Saved HTML: caption=0, scope="row"=0. By contrast, DecisionComparisonTable.tsx lines 40-43 already use `<th scope="row">`.
- **Why it matters:** Screen-reader users moving across a row hear 'Dry', 'Massage Cap' and 'Check Price' without the product name, because there is no row header. That fails 1.3.1 for a data table, and the table's purpose isn't announced.
- **Fix:** In ComparisonTable.tsx change line 90's `<td>` to `<th scope="row" className="px-4 py-3 text-left font-medium ...">`. Add `<caption className="sr-only">{table.title ?? "Comparison of the products in this guide"}</caption>` after line 35. Rename the 'Check' header to 'Price' (it can stay visually right-aligned).
- **Checker's correction:** The proposed caption uses `table.title`, but ComparisonTableType (src/types/types.ts lines 72-75) has only columns and rows, with no title. Either use a fixed string, or add a title field to the schema and type first. Renaming 'Check' to 'Price' would mislead, because the column shows no price, only a link. Use 'Where to buy' or 'Retailer', or keep 'Check price' as the header text.

### A11Y-15 - The comparison-table link for 'Ubroo ICE-COOL IPL' points to an anchor that does not exist
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Live epilators.html contains `href="#ubroo-ice-cool-ipl-hair-removal-device-ct05"` once and no element with `id="ubroo..."`. ComparisonTable.tsx lines 55-80 always wrap the row title in `<Link href={productHref}>`, whether or not a product card exists.
- **Why it matters:** Activating the link does nothing. Keyboard and screen-reader users get no feedback that the product has no review, and the link claims a destination it doesn't have (2.4.4).
- **Fix:** Code: pass the set of rendered product anchors (from `article.products`) into ComparisonTable, and render plain text instead of a Link when `getProductAnchor(title)` is not in the set. Editorial: remove the Ubroo row or add its card, which is already flagged in the content lens.

### A11Y-17 - Each related-post card is one giant link whose accessible name repeats the title twice and includes the description, date and 'Read article'
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Category/PostCard.tsx line 21 wraps the whole `<article>` in `<Link>`. Line 26 gives the image `alt={post.title}`, followed by the category span, the h3 title (line 40-42), the description (line 44-46), the date and 'Read article'. Live: `<a class="block no-underline" href="/skin-care/best-wax-warmer/"><article ...><img alt="Best Wax Warmer For Your Silky And Smooth Skin" .../>...<h3 ...>Best Wax Warmer For Your Silky And Smooth Skin</h3><p ...>The wax warmer heats wax...</p>...<span ...>Sep 21, 2023</span><span ...>Read article</span>`.
- **Why it matters:** The links list and tab stops announce roughly a 40-word name per card, 6 times on this page, and the same cards appear on the home and category pages. Headings inside a link are also inconsistently exposed by screen readers.
- **Fix:** In PostCard.tsx, set the image `alt=""` (line 26). Move the `<Link>` onto the h3 text, and make the whole card clickable with a stretched pseudo-element: `<h3><Link href={url} className="after:absolute after:inset-0">{post.title}</Link></h3>` with `relative` on the article. Mark 'Read article' aria-hidden or drop it.

### A11Y-18 - Related-post dates use text-outline #717973 at 12px: 4.28:1 on the card background
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** PostCard.tsx line 49: `<span className="text-label-sm text-outline">`. --color-outline is #717973 (globals.css line 75), and the card has `bg-surface` #fcf9f8 (line 22). Computed 4.28:1, and 4.48:1 even on pure white. The same token is used for text in src/components/Category/CategoryHeader.tsx line 61 (`text-label-sm text-outline`).
- **Why it matters:** Fails 1.4.3 for 12px text on the article, home and category card grids.
- **Fix:** Replace `text-outline` with `text-on-surface-variant` (#414844, 8.97:1) in PostCard.tsx line 49 and CategoryHeader.tsx line 61. Reserve the outline token for borders.

### A11Y-19 - The Share button advertises an ARIA menu but has no menu keyboard behaviour, no Escape, and a silent 'Copied!'
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ShareButton.tsx lines 68-73: `<button aria-expanded={open} aria-haspopup="true" ...>`. Lines 80-89: `role="menu"` containing `role="menuitem"`. There is no onKeyDown (arrows or Escape), focus is not moved into the menu, and it closes only on `mousedown` outside (line 34). Tabbing away leaves it open. The 'Copied!' swap (lines 90-94) has no live region. When navigator.share exists the popover never opens, but aria-haspopup still announces a menu (and it is server-rendered as `aria-haspopup="true"` in live HTML).
- **Why it matters:** role=menu tells screen readers to expect arrow-key navigation and application mode, and none of it works. Keyboard users cannot dismiss the popup with Escape. The copy confirmation is never announced, which fails 4.1.3 Status Messages.
- **Fix:** Drop the menu. When `canNativeShare` is false, make the Share button itself copy the URL and announce it: `<span role="status" aria-live="polite" className="sr-only">{copied ? "Link copied" : ""}</span>`. Remove aria-haspopup and aria-expanded and the popover (lines 79-103). If the popover is kept, use a disclosure pattern: no role=menu, `aria-controls`, Escape closes it and returns focus, and it closes on focusout.

### A11Y-23 - The newsletter input's boundary is nearly invisible: border 1.59:1 and fill 1.07:1 against the section
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** email-form.tsx line 61: `border border-outline-variant bg-white`. --color-outline-variant is #c1c8c2, and the section background is `bg-[#F8F7F2]` (EmailSection.tsx line 16). Computed: #c1c8c2 vs #f8f7f2 = 1.59:1, white fill vs #f8f7f2 = 1.07:1. The home search input uses the same class (screens/home/search-section.tsx line 12).
- **Why it matters:** Fails 1.4.11 Non-text Contrast: the visual indicator that identifies the text field is under 3:1. Low-vision users see no field, only a placeholder floating on beige.
- **Fix:** Use `border-outline` (#717973, above 3:1 on both white and #F8F7F2) in email-form.tsx line 61 and search-section.tsx line 12, keeping the focus ring.

### A11Y-25 - In each product card the rank, image and verdict come before the product's H2 in reading order, and the rank is an unlabelled number
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** ProductReviewCard.tsx: the left column (lines 26-56), holding the rank `<span ...>{rank}</span>` (28-32), the image (33-41) and 'The Verdict' blockquote (45-53), is in the DOM before the right column that contains the `<h2>` (65-67). Live: `<span class="bg-card-primary ... absolute top-8 left-8 ...">1</span><img alt="Braun Silk-épil 3 3-270" .../>...<span ...>The Verdict</span><blockquote ...>“A solid entry-level epilator...”</blockquote>...<h2 ...>Braun Silk-épil 3 3-270</h2>`.
- **Why it matters:** A screen-reader user who jumps to a product by heading lands after that product's verdict and misses it. Reading linearly, they hear the verdict attributed to the previous section. The rank is announced as a bare '1' (1.3.1, 1.3.2).
- **Fix:** Restructure ProductReviewCard.tsx so the heading block (tagline + H2 + summary) comes first in the DOM, and use `lg:order-last` / `order-first` for the visual two-column layout. Make the rank badge `aria-hidden` and add `<span className="sr-only">Number {rank}: </span>` inside the H2. Give the `<article>` `aria-labelledby` pointing at the H2's id.

### A11Y-28 - Article paragraphs run to about 123 characters per line because `max-w-max` overrides the typography plugin's 65ch cap
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ArticleContent.tsx line 6 and LegacyPostContent.tsx line 12: `<article className="mt-5 w-full max-w-max prose ... lg:prose-xl">`. `.prose` sets max-width: 65ch, and `max-w-max` (max-content) replaces it. Measured by the lead: prose width 1,233px, paragraphs 20px/36px, about 123 characters per line at 1280px.
- **Why it matters:** Lines well past 80 characters make it hard to find the start of the next line, especially for dyslexic and low-vision readers (1.4.8 guidance). This is the main reading surface of every article.
- **Fix:** Keep the wide container for cards and tables, but cap text blocks: in ArticleContent.tsx line 6 and LegacyPostContent.tsx line 12 add `prose-p:max-w-[70ch] prose-ul:max-w-[70ch] prose-ol:max-w-[70ch] prose-headings:max-w-[70ch]`, or wrap PortableText normal blocks in `max-w-[70ch]`. Leave the `not-prose` components (TopPickCards, ComparisonTable, ProductReviewCard) full width.

### A11Y-08 - The 'Product List' H2 has no content, and the 7 product H2s sit beside it as siblings instead of under it
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Live: `id="product-list">Product List<a ...>#</a></h2><article id="braun-silk-épil-3-3-270" ...>`. The card comes immediately after it, and ProductReviewCard.tsx line 65 hard-codes `<h2 className="font-playfair text-2xl ...">{product.title}</h2>`. Each card then has H3 'Why we love it' and H3 'Points to consider' (ProsCons.tsx line 81).
- **Why it matters:** An empty section heading followed by sibling H2s gives assistive tech a misleading outline: 'Product List' looks like a section with nothing in it. It is a real but bounded 1.3.1 issue.
- **Fix:** Editorial: delete the bare 'Product List' H2 block from the Sanity article content, since the product H2s stand on their own. Add a rule to the vault's article template: no heading without body content. If a wrapper heading is wanted, add a `headingLevel` prop to ProductReviewCard, and to ProsCons's UpdatedList, so products render as H3 with pros/cons as H4.
- **Checker's correction:** Downgraded to low. A section heading followed directly by same-level headings is an outline or best-practice problem, not a WCAG 1.3.1 failure: the product H2s are still correctly marked up.

### A11Y-10 - Footer column titles are H4 directly after H2s, skipping H3
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Footer/Footer.tsx line 21: `<h4 className="font-label-md text-label-md uppercase ...">{title}</h4>`. Live order: H2 'Related posts' → H3 cards → H2 newsletter 'Get the best buying guides before you shop' → H4, H4, H4 (the footer columns).
- **Why it matters:** The skipped level suggests a missing parent section to heading-list users. It is a 1.3.1 best-practice issue.
- **Fix:** Change the `<h4>` at Footer.tsx line 21 to `<h2>`. The classes already control the visual size.

### A11Y-12 - Affiliate and external inline links open a new tab with no warning
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** AffiliateLink.tsx line 24 defaults to `target = "_blank"`, and live epilators.html has 18 `target="_blank"` links. article-portable-text.tsx lines 66-74 set target=_blank on every external inline link. Neither gives a visual or programmatic 'opens in new tab' cue.
- **Why it matters:** Screen-reader and cognitive-disability users lose the Back button context without warning. This is advisory under G201 and 3.2.5 (AAA), but it happens on every monetised click.
- **Fix:** Use the sr-only suffix from A11Y-11 in AffiliateLink. In article-portable-text.tsx lines 69-76 add the same `<span className="sr-only"> (opens in a new tab)</span>` when isExternal. Optionally add a small visible ExternalLink icon with aria-hidden.

### A11Y-14 - The horizontally scrolling table wrapper cannot receive keyboard focus, so the middle columns may never scroll into view
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** ComparisonTable.tsx line 34: `<div className="overflow-x-auto">` with no tabIndex, role or label. Measured on mobile: the table is 1,026px wide inside a 353px wrapper. The only focusable children are the first-column product link and the last-column 'Check Price' link. Chrome only auto-focuses scrollers that have no focusable descendants, so this one gets no scroller focus.
- **Why it matters:** A keyboard-only user (or a switch or magnifier user) on a narrow screen can reach columns 1 and 6 by tabbing but has no way to scroll to 'Type', 'Use' and 'Key Feature'. That is a 2.1.1 Keyboard risk.
- **Fix:** At ComparisonTable.tsx line 34 use `<div className="overflow-x-auto focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-card-primary" tabIndex={0} role="region" aria-label="Product comparison, scroll sideways for more columns">`. Apply the same change to DecisionComparisonTable.tsx line 25.
- **Checker's correction:** Downgraded to low, and the keyboard claim is overstated. Once focus is on the product link or the 'Check Price' link inside the scroller, the arrow keys scroll the nearest scrollable ancestor in Chrome and Firefox, so the middle columns can be reached. Tabbing to the last-column link also scrolls the container. The real gap is discoverability: the region is unnamed and has no focus indicator of its own. axe's scrollable-region-focusable rule passes because the region contains focusable descendants. The tabIndex/role/aria-label fix is still good practice.

### A11Y-16 - Image alt text repeats the adjacent heading everywhere, and the Sanity 'alt' field is ignored
**low** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** The hero uses `alt={post.title}` (ArticleHero.tsx line 159), so its alt equals the H1. TopPickCard.tsx line 28 and ProductReviewCard.tsx line 35 use `alt={product.title}`, placed right beside an H3 or H2 with the same text. The same image is used again in the table tooltip (ComparisonTable.tsx line 70), so the product name is read up to 4 times. Header Logo.tsx line 15 does use `logo.image?.alt`. But sanity/schemas/components/image.ts line 23 defines an `alt` field on the `iimage` type used by product.image, post.thumbnail and seo.open_graph_image, and none of the article components read it. getPostPrimaryImage (src/lib/post-seo.ts lines 46-53) returns only the URL.
- **Why it matters:** Screen-reader users hear 'Braun Silk-épil 3 3-270' (image) then 'Braun Silk-épil 3 3-270' (heading), 7+3 times on this page. That is noise with no information, and it pushes real content further away. 1.1.1 is technically met but badly served.
- **Fix:** Where an image is adjacent to a visible name that already identifies it, use `alt={product.image?.alt ?? ""}`: TopPickCard.tsx line 28, ProductReviewCard.tsx line 35 and ComparisonTable.tsx line 70. For the hero, extend getPostPrimaryImage to return `{ url, alt }` and use `alt={alt ?? ""}` in ArticleHero.tsx line 159. Editorial rule for the vault: product and hero alt text describes what the photo shows (for example 'Epilator with massage cap and shaver head attached'), or stays empty. It never repeats the title.
- **Checker's correction:** Downgraded to low: 1.1.1 is met, so this is redundancy, not failure. The comparison-table image sits in a Radix Tooltip portal (src/components/ui/tooltip.tsx), which mounts only while the tooltip is open on hover or focus. It is therefore not in the linear reading order, although on keyboard focus it becomes the link's description and repeats the name. TypeItemCard.tsx line 24 (`alt={item.title}` next to an H3 of the same text) has the same pattern.

### A11Y-20 - The breadcrumb nav is unlabelled and has no aria-current; the main nav label duplicates the role
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/BreadCrumbs.tsx line 19: `<nav className="pt-8 pb-4">` has no aria-label. Line 45 renders the current page as `<span className="text-card-primary font-bold line-clamp-1">` with no aria-current. Header.tsx line 42: `aria-label="Main navigation"`. Live: two nav landmarks, `<nav aria-label="Main navigation">` and a bare `<nav class="pt-8 pb-4">`.
- **Why it matters:** The landmarks menu shows 'navigation' and 'Main navigation navigation', so users can't tell which one is the breadcrumb. The current page isn't identified programmatically (1.3.1).
- **Fix:** In BreadCrumbs.tsx line 19 use `<nav aria-label="Breadcrumb" ...>`. On line 45 add `aria-current="page"`. Mark the ChevronRight separators aria-hidden; they already are. In Header.tsx line 42 change the label to `aria-label="Main"`.
- **Checker's correction:** Downgraded to low: unlabelled duplicate navigation landmarks and a missing aria-current are ARIA best-practice issues, not AA failures. The fix's 'Mark the ChevronRight separators aria-hidden; they already are' is redundant and can be dropped.

### A11Y-21 - There is no skip link; the layout relies on the <main> landmark alone
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx lines 28-32 render Header and then `<main className="min-h-[...]">` with no id and no preceding skip link. Grepping saved HTML for 'skip to' finds 0 on epilators, hedge-trimmers, home and category-skin-care.
- **Why it matters:** 2.4.1 is technically met through the main landmark. But sighted keyboard users (who don't use landmarks) must tab through the logo, nav, Search, Best Picks, breadcrumbs, author and Share before reaching content, on every page.
- **Fix:** In layout.tsx, as the first child of the font-stitch div, add `<a href="#main-content" className="sr-only focus:not-sr-only focus:fixed focus:left-4 focus:top-4 focus:z-50 focus:rounded-lg focus:bg-white focus:px-4 focus:py-2 focus:text-primary">Skip to content</a>`. Give `<main>` `id="main-content" tabIndex={-1}`.

### A11Y-22 - The newsletter section sits between </main> and <footer>, outside every landmark
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** layout.tsx lines 30-35: `</main>` then `{siteLayout?.emailSection && <EmailSection .../>}` then `<Footer>`. EmailSection.tsx line 16: `<section className="bg-[#F8F7F2] py-24 ...">` has no accessible name, so it is not a region landmark. Live byte offsets: </main> at 117130, the newsletter h2 at 117329, <footer at 118187.
- **Why it matters:** Users who navigate by landmarks skip the signup entirely. It is an axe 'region' best-practice violation.
- **Fix:** In EmailSection.tsx give the h2 `id="newsletter-heading"` and the section `aria-labelledby="newsletter-heading"`, which makes it a named region. Alternatively, render it inside `<footer>`.

### A11Y-24 - Contact form focus ring and field borders fail non-text contrast
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** ContactForm.tsx lines 58, 73, 85 and 101: `border-gray-300 ... bg-gray-50 ... focus:ring-blue-500`. Line 118 on the submit button: `outline-hidden ... focus:ring-4 focus:outline-hidden focus:ring-blue-300`. Computed with Tailwind hex approximations: blue-300 #93c5fd on white = 1.80:1; gray-300 #d1d5db border on gray-50 = 1.41:1. The form also uses a blue palette, `dark:` classes the site doesn't otherwise use, and none of the site tokens.
- **Why it matters:** The submit button's only focus indicator is below 3:1 (1.4.11), and the field outlines are barely visible.
- **Fix:** On the submit button, replace `focus:ring-blue-300 focus:outline-hidden` with `focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-primary`. Switch the inputs to `border-outline bg-white focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary`, the same as the newsletter input after A11Y-23. Drop the unused `dark:` classes.

### A11Y-26 - The site's own verdict is marked up as <blockquote> with typographic quotes
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ProductReviewCard.tsx lines 47-52: `<span ...>The Verdict</span><blockquote className="font-playfair text-2xl ... italic ...">&ldquo;{product.verdict.trim()}&rdquo;</blockquote>`. TopPickCard.tsx line 48 also wraps the description in &ldquo; &rdquo;.
- **Why it matters:** Screen readers announce 'block quote', which implies a quotation from an outside source. The editorial verdict is WhoAdvice's own text (1.3.1 semantics), and the verdict also reads as a quote to reviewers checking trust signals.
- **Fix:** Replace the blockquote with `<p className="font-playfair text-2xl ...">` and drop the curly quotes in ProductReviewCard.tsx line 51 and TopPickCard.tsx line 48. Keep the 'The Verdict' label as `<p>` or `<strong>`.
- **Checker's correction:** The blockquote is at lines 50-52, with the curly quotes on line 51.

### A11Y-27 - No prefers-reduced-motion handling anywhere: smooth scrolling, hover lifts and image zooms always animate
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** globals.css lines 334-336: `html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }`. Lines 176-183 `card-hover` apply `transform: translateY(-2px)` with a transition. PostCard.tsx line 27: `group-hover:scale-105 transition-transform duration-500`. The header CTA and newsletter button use `active:scale-95 transition-all`, and Faq.tsx line 12 has `transition-transform group-open:rotate-180`. A grep for motion-reduce, motion-safe or prefers-reduced-motion over src returns 0 hits.
- **Why it matters:** The in-page jumps (See Top Picks, Compare Models, product-name links) scroll smoothly over thousands of pixels (the page is 23,884px tall on mobile). That can trigger vestibular symptoms. It is AAA (2.3.3), but it costs little to respect the OS setting.
- **Fix:** Append to globals.css: `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { html { scroll-behavior: auto; } *, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: .01ms !important; transition-duration: .01ms !important; scroll-behavior: auto !important; } }`. Prefix the transform effects with `motion-safe:` (for example `motion-safe:group-hover:scale-105` in PostCard.tsx line 27).

### A11Y-29 - line-clamp truncates titles and descriptions, with no way to reveal the hidden text at zoom
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** TopPickCard.tsx line 37 has `line-clamp-2` on the product H3 and line 47 `line-clamp-3` on the description. PostCard.tsx has line 40 `line-clamp-2` on the H3 and line 44 `line-clamp-2` on the description. BreadCrumbs.tsx line 45 has `line-clamp-1` on the current page. None has a title attribute or expand control.
- **Why it matters:** At 200-400% zoom or with 1.4.12 text-spacing overrides, sighted users lose words of titles and descriptions (1.4.10, 1.4.12: no loss of content). Screen-reader users still get the full text, but magnifier users do not.
- **Fix:** Remove line-clamp from the headings (TopPickCard.tsx line 37, PostCard.tsx line 40) and from the breadcrumb current item (BreadCrumbs.tsx line 45); let them wrap. Keep clamps only on teaser descriptions whose full text appears elsewhere. The top-pick description currently duplicates the verdict, so that one can stay.

### A11Y-30 - Small inline targets on mobile: breadcrumb links, table links and the invisible heading '#' links
**low** · effort S · hypothesis

- **Evidence:** The facts count 32 links/buttons under 24px in one dimension at 375px. Candidates from code: breadcrumb links `text-xs` (BreadCrumbs.tsx line 20, about 16px tall), table product and 'Check Price' links `text-sm` (ComparisonTable.tsx lines 90, 113), the hero author link `text-sm` (ArticleHero.tsx lines 106-113), footer links `text-label-sm` 12px (Footer.tsx line 24), and the 5 '#' heading anchors. I did not itemise the 32 or measure their spacing.
- **Why it matters:** 2.5.8 (AA) is probably met for most of these through the 24px spacing exception. Table rows have py-3 and footer items space-y-4. But the hit areas are small for motor-impaired touch users, and the invisible '#' anchors are accidental tap targets inside headings.
- **Fix:** Removing the '#' anchors (A11Y-03) takes 5 off the list. Add `inline-flex min-h-6 items-center` to the breadcrumb Link (BreadCrumbs.tsx line 39) and the table links (ComparisonTable.tsx lines 60-63, 113). Re-measure with a DOM script at 375px and treat any target with both a size under 24px and a neighbour within 24px as a failure.
- **Checker's correction:** This is advisory. The finder itself says 2.5.8 is probably met through the spacing exception, so this is not an AA defect until a spacing measurement shows otherwise. The 'breadcrumb Link (BreadCrumbs.tsx line 39)' reference is the className line of the Link opened at line 38.

### A11Y-31 - The logo link's accessible name reads 'WhoAdvice. WhoAdvice.' at md and wider
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Header/Logo.tsx: the image has `alt={logo.image?.alt || logo.text}` (lines 15, 26), followed by `<span className={imageSrc ? "hidden md:inline" : undefined}>{logo.text}</span>` (lines 31-35). Live: `<img alt="WhoAdvice." .../><span class="hidden md:inline">WhoAdvice.</span>`.
- **Why it matters:** The first link on every page is announced with a doubled name. Minor, but it is on every page.
- **Fix:** When `logo.text` is present, set the image `alt=""`, and change the span to `className="sr-only md:not-sr-only"` so the name is always 'WhoAdvice.' exactly once.

### A11Y-32 - Below 1024px the header has no navigation at all: the nav is `hidden lg:flex` and there is no menu button
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Header.tsx line 41: `<nav className="hidden lg:flex items-center gap-6" aria-label="Main navigation">`. Live, it holds a single link 'Explore Categories' (href /#categories). No alternative control is rendered for mobile. Desktop gets 469 of 481 GSC impressions, but readers arriving on phones get no category route from the header.
- **Why it matters:** On phones and tablets the nav landmark is display:none, so category browsing is available only through Search and the footer. 2.4.5 is still met through search and the footer, but consistency and findability suffer.
- **Fix:** The menu has one short link, so drop `hidden lg:flex` for `flex` at Header.tsx line 41 (it fits beside the logo at 375px). Add a disclosure menu button only if more links are added later.
- **Checker's correction:** The claim that the nav link 'fits beside the logo at 375px' was not measured. The row also holds the logo text (hidden below md), the Search icon and the uppercase 'Best Picks' pill with px-6, so check for overflow at 320-375px before switching to `flex`. A `hidden sm:flex`, or moving the link into the footer or the category chips, may be safer.

### A11Y-33 - The byline author link looks exactly like the surrounding text
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ArticleHero.tsx lines 107-114: `<span className="font-bold text-card-on-surface">By <Link className="hover:text-card-primary transition-colors" href=...>{post.author.name}</Link></span>`. The link inherits the same colour and weight as 'By' and has no underline until hover, which only changes the colour.
- **Why it matters:** The author page is the page's main E-E-A-T trust link, and nothing tells sighted users it is a link. Keyboard users see it only through the focus outline.
- **Fix:** Add `underline underline-offset-2 decoration-card-outline-variant hover:decoration-current` to the Link at ArticleHero.tsx line 110.
- **Checker's correction:** The Link is at lines 109-114, and its className is on line 110.

### A11Y-34 - FAQ answers contain an empty paragraph and trailing <br/> that screen readers announce as blank
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Live FAQ answer for 'What is the least painful electric epilator?': `...lower speed settings can make epilation more comfortable.</p><p><br/></p>`. The first answer ends `...removes them quickly.<br/></p>`. Faq.tsx line 17 renders the answer through PortableTextComponent, which does no normalisation.
- **Why it matters:** The reading cursor stops on 'blank' inside the disclosure. It is a small 1.3.1 noise issue, and the same markup also leaks into FAQPage JSON-LD.
- **Fix:** Clean the two answers in Sanity. The empty-block guard from A11Y-09 in portable-text.tsx will also skip them. Strip hard breaks at the end of a block with a `hardBreak` component that returns null when it is the block's last child.

### A11Y-35 - The home search field (target of the header Search link on every page) has no label and no submit button
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** screens/home/search-section.tsx lines 81-88: `<input ref={registerSearchInput} type="text" ... placeholder={placeholder} className={SEARCH_INPUT_CLASS} />`, with no label, name or type=search, and the form (lines 77-80) has no button. Line 38-40 silently return when fewer than 3 characters are entered, with no message. Header SearchButton.tsx sends every page's 'Search' click to /#search.
- **Why it matters:** The field is named only by its placeholder (3.3.2). Submission depends on knowing to press Enter, and short queries fail with no feedback (3.3.1).
- **Fix:** Add `<label htmlFor="site-search" className="sr-only">Search reviews</label>`. Give the input `id="site-search" type="search" name="q"` and the form `role="search"`. Add a visible submit button (the magnifier icon can become a `<button type="submit" aria-label="Search">`). Show 'Type at least 3 characters' in a role="status" element when the query is short.

### A11Y-36 - Uppercase micro-labels at 10-12px (category chips, product taglines, footer links)
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** PostCard.tsx line 36: `text-[10px] font-bold uppercase tracking-widest`. ProductReviewCard.tsx line 61 tagline: `text-[11px] ... tracking-[0.15em] uppercase`. BreadCrumbs.tsx line 20: `text-xs ... uppercase tracking-wider`. Footer links use `text-label-sm` (12px, Footer.tsx line 24), and the affiliate disclosure is `text-xs` (AffiliateDisclosure.tsx line 22).
- **Why it matters:** Contrast passes, but all-caps text at 10-11px is hard to read for low-vision and dyslexic users. The affiliate disclosure, a legal and trust statement, is rendered at 12px.
- **Fix:** Set a 12px floor: `text-[10px]` and `text-[11px]` become `text-xs`. Raise the disclosure paragraph to `text-sm` (14px) and footer links to `text-label-md` or `text-sm`. Limit uppercase to labels of 1-3 words.

### A11Y-37 - Table product-name links are marked only by colour (1.96:1 against neighbouring cell text) until hover
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** ComparisonTable.tsx lines 60-63: `<Link className="underline-offset-2 hover:underline whitespace-nowrap">` inside `text-emerald-900` (line 90). The other cells are `text-text-default` #111. Computed with emerald-900 approximated as #004f3b (Tailwind v4 oklch): 1.96:1 against #111.
- **Why it matters:** These are standalone links in cells, not links inside prose, so this is not a strict 1.4.1 failure. Still, users with colour-vision deficiency get no cue that the product names jump to the review cards.
- **Fix:** At ComparisonTable.tsx line 62 replace `hover:underline` with `underline decoration-emerald-900/40 hover:decoration-2`.

### A11Y-38 - The footer 'Contact us' link shows a Share icon
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Footer/Footer.tsx lines 72-78: `<Link href="/contact/" ... aria-label="Contact us"><Share2 className="w-5 h-5 text-primary" aria-hidden /></Link>`.
- **Why it matters:** Sighted users, including voice-control users who say what they see ('click share'), get an icon that contradicts its accessible name. The icon-only affordance becomes misleading.
- **Fix:** Swap `Share2` for `MessageCircle` (or `Send`) from lucide-react at Footer.tsx line 2 and line 77.

### A11Y-39 - The above-the-fold author avatar and header logo are lazy-loaded
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Live: `<img alt="WhoAdvice." loading="lazy" width="120" height="32" ...>` in the header, and `<img alt="Emily Cooper" loading="lazy" width="48" height="48" ...>` in the hero byline. Logo.tsx lines 23-29 and ArticleHero.tsx lines 90-96 use next/image without `priority`, and next/image defaults to lazy.
- **Why it matters:** Both are visible in the first viewport on desktop. Lazy loading delays them until layout runs, so the byline, the page's main trust signal, pops in late. The effect is small, since the widths and heights are reserved and CLS is 0.
- **Fix:** Add `priority` (or `loading="eager"`) to the Image in Logo.tsx line 23 and ArticleHero.tsx line 90.

### A11Y-V01 - The byline avatar's alt repeats the author name that the adjacent 'By Emily Cooper' link already announces
**low** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ArticleHero.tsx lines 90-96: `<Image alt={post.author.name} className="w-12 h-12 rounded-full ..." .../>`, immediately followed by lines 107-114 `By <Link ...>{post.author.name}</Link>`. Live epilators.html: `<img alt="Emily Cooper" loading="lazy" width="48" height="48" ...>` next to the byline.
- **Why it matters:** Screen-reader users hear 'Emily Cooper, graphic' and then 'By Emily Cooper, link' back to back in the hero, on every article. The photo adds no information beyond the name.
- **Fix:** In ArticleHero.tsx line 91 set `alt=""`, since the avatar is decorative next to the visible name. Alternatively, wrap the avatar and the name in the same author Link, so the whole block is one target with one name.

### A11Y-V02 - Type-card images use alt = the card's H3 text, so every 'Types of best epilators' card is announced twice
**low** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/TypeItemCard.tsx line 24 `alt={item.title}` inside the card, with line 32-34 `<h3 ...>{item.title}</h3>` directly after. The Sanity typeItem image is an iimage with an `alt` field (sanity/schemas/post/typeItem.ts line 23; sanity/schemas/components/image.ts lines 22-26), and the component never reads it.
- **Why it matters:** The same image-alt-equals-heading redundancy as A11Y-16, across the 7 type cards on this page. It also means editor-written alt text in Sanity is silently discarded.
- **Fix:** TypeItemCard.tsx line 24: `alt={item.image?.alt ?? ""}`. Fold this into the A11Y-16 editorial rule: alt describes the photo, or is left empty.

### A11Y-V03 - On browsers with navigator.share, a failed native share leaves the Share button doing nothing while announcing 'expanded'
**low** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ShareButton.tsx lines 38-51: if navigator.share throws anything other than AbortError (for example NotAllowedError), execution falls through to `setOpen((prev) => !prev)`. But the popover renders only when `open && !canNativeShare` (line 79), so nothing appears, while `aria-expanded={open}` (line 69) now reports true.
- **Why it matters:** Users of Safari and Android Chrome get a control that silently fails. Screen-reader users hear 'expanded' with no popup present (4.1.2 state mismatch), and the copy-link fallback is unreachable.
- **Fix:** In handleShare, on a non-abort error set a `forceFallback` state and render the popover when `open && (!canNativeShare || forceFallback)`. Simpler, combined with the A11Y-19 fix: on failure, copy the URL directly and announce 'Link copied' in a role="status" span.

### A11Y-V04 - The article body nests about 26 <article> elements for cards and guide tips inside the main <article>
**low** · effort S · likely · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** ArticleContent.tsx line 6 wraps the body in `<article>`. Inside it, TopPickCard.tsx line 24 (3), ProductReviewCard.tsx line 22 (7), TypeItemCard.tsx line 12 (7) and GuideItemCard.tsx line 8 (9) each render `<article>`. Live epilators.html shows `<article class="flex flex-col h-full bg-white p-6 rounded-3xl ...">` and `<article id="ease-of-cleaning" ...>` inside the prose article.
- **Why it matters:** Screen readers that expose the article role (NVDA and JAWS in browse mode) announce 'article' boundaries 26 times. A 2-sentence buying-guide tip is not self-contained, syndicatable content, so the semantics add noise to the reading flow.
- **Fix:** Keep <article> for ProductReviewCard only, where each review is a self-contained unit, and give it aria-labelledby pointing at its H2. Change GuideItemCard.tsx line 8, TypeItemCard.tsx line 12 and TopPickCard.tsx line 24 to <div>. Alternatively, render the guide and type grids as <ul>/<li> so the count is announced.

## Performance

### PERF-01 - No responsive images anywhere: images.unoptimized plus a do-nothing formatImage() serves every Sanity image at its full source resolution
**high** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** next.config.mjs:90-91 `images: { unoptimized: true, ...}`. src/lib/format-images.ts:2-4 returns `src` unchanged. sanity/lib/image.ts:16 builds URLs with `.auto('format').fit('max')` and no width. Saved HTML: 19 <img>, none with srcset. The `sizes` props in TopPickCard.tsx:31 and ProductReviewCard.tsx:38 are therefore ignored. CORRECTION to the facts file: the 106,443-byte JPEG is only what a client without AVIF support gets. I requested the same URL with a Chrome Accept header and got image/avif at 37,474 bytes. The hero comes back as 20,653 bytes of AVIF, not 103,573 of JPEG. So format is already handled; the waste is resolution. Measured with a Chrome Accept header: the 7 product-card images total 221,691 B at full size and 118,794 B at w=640 (-46%). With `w=` set, Sanity returned WebP rather than AVIF. All 16 unique images on the epilators page total about 314 KB of AVIF.
- **Why it matters:** Every image on every page is decoded at 1254-1672px, even when it renders at 48, 80, 192 or 346 CSS px. The waste is modest on the epilators page and very large on the homepage (PERF-02). It also adds decode memory and main-thread cost on phones.
- **Fix:** Keep Sanity as the transformer and add a custom next/image loader. (1) In next.config.mjs, replace `unoptimized: true` with `loader: 'custom', loaderFile: './src/lib/sanity-image-loader.ts'`. (2) Create that loader: `export default function sanityLoader({ src, width, quality }) { if (!src.startsWith('https://cdn.sanity.io/')) return src; const u = new URL(src); u.searchParams.set('w', String(width)); u.searchParams.set('q', String(quality ?? 75)); u.searchParams.set('auto', 'format'); if (!u.searchParams.has('fit')) u.searchParams.set('fit', 'max'); return u.href; }`. (3) Delete src/lib/format-images.ts and its call sites. (4) Correct the sizes: TopPickCard `(min-width:1024px) 346px, (min-width:768px) 33vw, 100vw`; ProductReviewCard `(min-width:1024px) 616px, 100vw`; hero `(min-width:1024px) 592px, calc(100vw - 32px)`. Fixed-size images (avatar 48, thumbs 80) get 1x/2x candidates from width/height automatically. Expected effect (estimate): -40 to -50% image bytes on article pages and about -80% on the homepage, with no Vercel image-optimization cost.
- **Checker's correction:** Detail: at w=750 I measured 13,320 B WebP, not 10,904 B (this also affects PERF-10). Because Sanity switches from AVIF to WebP once w= is set, savings on images already small in AVIF are smaller than the product-card figure suggests. The hero drops only about 35% (20.6 to 13.3 KB). Treat -40 to -50% as an upper-end estimate for article pages.

### PERF-V01 - All 14 category hubs and the author pages are rendered on every request, with no CDN or ISR caching
**high** · effort S · verified · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** Live 2026-09-25: `curl -D - https://whoadvice.com/skin-care/` returns `Cache-Control: private, no-cache, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, `X-Vercel-Cache: MISS`, `Age: 0`, `X-Matched-Path: /[categorySlug]`, with no X-Nextjs-Prerender header, on repeated requests. /author/emily-cooper/ and /skin-care/?page=2 return the same. By contrast /, /about/, /skin-care/best-epilators/ and /garden-tools/best-hedge-trimmers/ return `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` + `X-Nextjs-Prerender: 1` + HIT. In code, src/app/(frontend)/[categorySlug]/page.tsx has `export const revalidate = 600` but no generateStaticParams. src/app/(frontend)/author/[slug]/page.tsx has neither. The only route that is ISR-cached on demand is [slug]/page.tsx, which exports `generateStaticParams() { return [] }` (line 38). No headers(), cookies(), connection() or dynamic export was found in these routes. Cause (inference, not proven): a dynamic segment without generateStaticParams is rendered at request time.
- **Why it matters:** Every visit and every Googlebot crawl of the hubs that pass link equity to the articles costs a serverless invocation plus a full render. The response is never CDN-cached, so TTFB never benefits from the edge (measured 0.74-0.76 s from Pakistan with a MISS every time). The `revalidate = 600` the author wrote is silently ineffective, and PERF-21's reading of it was wrong for the same reason.
- **Fix:** In src/app/(frontend)/[categorySlug]/page.tsx add `export async function generateStaticParams() { const cats = await sanityFetch<{slug:{current:string}}[]>({ query: `*[_type=="category" && defined(slug.current)]{slug}`, tags: [CACHE_TAGS.category] }); return cats.map(c => ({ categorySlug: c.slug.current })); }` (or `return []`, matching the article route), and remove `export const revalidate = 600` so on-demand revalidatePath/revalidateTag (revalidate-content.ts:57, 83) is the only invalidation. Do the same for author/[slug]/page.tsx using the author slugs. Recheck with curl: both routes must return `X-Nextjs-Prerender: 1` and X-Vercel-Cache HIT on the second request, and the saved HTML should regain `<link rel="preload" as="font">` in the head.

### PERF-02 - Homepage downloads about 753 KB of AVIF for 80px thumbnails and 192px category tiles
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Saved home.html: CompactPostCard thumbnails (`width="80" height="80" class="w-20 h-20 ..."`, src/components/Post/CompactPostCard.tsx:34-40) point at full-size 1448x1086, 1672x941 and 1254x1254 sources. Measured with a Chrome Accept header: 20,653 / 69,339 / 93,452 / 135,516 B. The same four at `w=160` measured 1,092 / 3,172 / 4,262 / 7,296 B. Category tiles (src/screens/home/category-section.tsx:84-89, `size-48`, i.e. 192px) use 1000-1254px sources: 7 tiles = 429,905 B at full size and 88,578 B at `w=384`. All 15 home images are loading="lazy", so every one is fetched as the user scrolls.
- **Why it matters:** The homepage is the site's crawl hub and its brand first impression. About 20x more image bytes than needed hurts mobile users on metered data, and it inflates LCP and INP on low-end phones as they decode 1.2-1.6k px images.
- **Fix:** PERF-01's loader fixes this automatically, because CompactPostCard and category-section already pass width/height. Verify afterwards: each home <img> should carry a srcset whose 2x candidate is w=160 (thumbs) or w=384 (tiles). Expected (estimate from the measured w= sizes): home image weight drops from about 753 KB to about 130 KB.
- **Checker's correction:** The 753 KB total also includes the 488x488 header logo and the Editor's Pick LargeCard fill image (src/components/Home/EditorPick/LargeCard.tsx, sizes '(max-width: 768px) 100vw, 50vw', also lazy). It is not all thumbnails and tiles. This is a sub-case of PERF-01 with the same fix, so I lowered it to medium. All images are lazy, so the bytes arrive as the user scrolls rather than on the critical path.

### PERF-03 - gtag.js is the heaviest asset on every page (179 KB gzip, 537 KB raw), and <head> preloads it at high priority
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Scripts.tsx:8-11 `<Script async src="https://www.googletagmanager.com/gtag/js?id=G-CL05EM3735" />` uses the default strategy (afterInteractive), which emits `<link rel="preload" href="https://www.googletagmanager.com/gtag/js?id=G-CL05EM3735" as="script"/>`. That link is present in all 8 saved pages. curl measured Content-Encoding gzip, 179,174 B transferred, 536,982 B raw, Cache-Control private, max-age=900. For comparison, all first-party JS on the epilators page is about 199 KB brotli across 10 chunks.
- **Why it matters:** A script preload runs at High priority. It competes with the hero image, the two font preloads and the app chunks during the critical window. Parsing and running 537 KB of JS then lands on the main thread just after hydration, which is the main INP/TBT risk on phones. The site has 0 search clicks to protect, so the analytics payload currently costs more than it returns.
- **Fix:** In Scripts.tsx, set `strategy="lazyOnload"` on the gtag.js <Script>. With lazyOnload no preload is emitted and the script loads after window load. Keep the inline `id="ga"` stub (lines 31-41) as afterInteractive, so `window.gtag` exists early and affiliate_click events queue into dataLayer before gtag.js arrives. Recheck: the `rel="preload"` gtag link must disappear from the HTML. Expected effect (estimate): 179 KB leaves the critical path, with a lower TBT/INP on mid-range phones.
- **Checker's correction:** Raw size is now 539,118 B (it drifts with Google's builds). Severity lowered to medium: the cost is a script-preload priority contest plus post-hydration main-thread work, and nothing here shows it measurably costs rankings or conversions. 'Biggest TBT/INP gain' is an unmeasured estimate.

### PERF-04 - Three analytics stacks load on every page: GA4, Microsoft Clarity session recording and Vercel Web Analytics
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Scripts.tsx:19-29 injects the Clarity loader (afterInteractive). The loader pulls https://scripts.clarity.ms/0.8.70/clarity.js: 25,592 B compressed, 75,095 B raw (measured). (frontend)/layout.tsx:3,42 renders `<Analytics />` from @vercel/analytics 2.0.1. The browser pane's resource timing showed clarity.ms/tag at 1,728 ms, the Vercel analytics script at 1,751 ms and clarity.js at 2,610 ms, all within the page's load window (load event 1,597 ms, excluding script execution).
- **Why it matters:** Clarity observes DOM mutations and records sessions, which adds main-thread work on every page. GA4 and Vercel Analytics both count pageviews, so one of them is redundant. With 481 impressions and 0 clicks in 3 months (GSC), session recordings have almost nothing to show.
- **Fix:** Choose one pageview source. Recommendation: keep GA4, which already carries the affiliate_click key event (src/lib/analytics.ts:96-106), and remove `<Analytics />` plus the @vercel/analytics dependency. Load Clarity with `strategy="lazyOnload"`, or remove it until the site has real traffic. Its affiliate event hooks in analytics.ts:108-112 already no-op when window.clarity is absent. Expected effect (estimate): about 30 KB compressed and one session-recording observer fewer per page.
- **Checker's correction:** Wrong line numbers: analytics.ts has 76 lines. The GA event is at 59-60 and the Clarity hooks are at 71-74, not 96-106 and 108-112. 'Already carries the affiliate_click key event' is unverified: line 41 is only a comment telling someone to mark it as a key event in GA Admin. The Clarity resource timings came from the lead's browser session and were not re-measured.

### PERF-06 - Swiper JS and CSS ship on every article page, although the slider is never rendered
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ProductSlider (src/components/Post/product-card/ProductSlider.tsx:1-8, 'use client', imports swiper/react, swiper/css and swiper/css/navigation) is used nowhere. It is only re-exported by the barrel src/components/Post/index.ts:19,37, and the post route imports from that barrel ([slug]/page.tsx:11-17). Result on the live page: chunk 4907-127475cee1f01b50.js is 102,939 B raw / 33,547 B br. I split it by module: swiper modules 46214 + 75235 + 79175 = 68,622 + 3,608 + 3,411 B, about 75.6 KB raw. A render-blocking stylesheet 8d78c9d5fd7d522c.css (8,080 B raw / 3,239 B gz) holds the swiper classes and a base64 `swiper-icons` font. Both files are referenced from the hedge-trimmers, bug-zapper and face-epilators pages too. Neither appears on home or category.
- **Why it matters:** About 74% of a 103 KB shared chunk and a whole render-blocking stylesheet on every money page are dead code. The browser downloads, parses and evaluates them before hydration finishes.
- **Fix:** Delete src/components/Post/product-card/ProductSlider.tsx and remove it from product-card/index.ts:3 and Post/index.ts:19,37. Remove `swiper` from package.json and regenerate pnpm-lock.yaml in the same step. Longer term, import components from their files rather than the Post barrel, so a client component with CSS side effects can't be pulled in by accident. Expected effect (estimate): about 24 KB brotli of JS and one render-blocking CSS request fewer on every article.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to medium. It is about 24 KB br of dead JS and a 3 KB gz blocking stylesheet: real waste, but bounded, with no demonstrated ranking or conversion cost. Note that chunk 4907 also carries the Radix Tooltip/Popper (19 'Tooltip' and 8 'Popper' matches), so the chunk itself stays until PERF-07 is done too.

### PERF-07 - The comparison table is a client component only for a hover tooltip, which pulls Radix Popper and @sanity/image-url into the browser
**medium** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Post/ComparisonTable.tsx:1 'use client'. It wraps every product name in Radix Tooltip (lines 58-76) to show a 200px image on hover, and calls getImageUrl() on the client (line 54). Live bundle: chunk 4907 module 26931 (23,628 B raw) contains Tooltip/Popper/DismissableLayer/Portal. Chunk 6064-79fe4b2d113c6121.js (30,631 B raw / 11,923 B br) contains `createImageUrlBuilder` and loads only on article pages, not on home or category. The table's full row data is also serialized into the RSC payload as client props.
- **Why it matters:** About 12 KB br of image-URL code and about 24 KB raw of popper code are shipped and hydrated for a hover effect that touch users (the mobile audience) can never trigger. It also makes the 9-row table part of hydration.
- **Fix:** Remove 'use client' from ComparisonTable.tsx so it renders on the server. Pre-compute the image URL on the server. Either drop the tooltip (the product anchor link already jumps to the card with its image) or replace it with a CSS-only popover: `<span class="group relative">...<span class="invisible group-hover:visible group-focus-within:visible absolute ..."><img loading="lazy" ...></span></span>`. AffiliateLink stays a client leaf, or goes away per PERF-15. Then delete src/components/ui/tooltip.tsx and @radix-ui/react-tooltip if nothing else uses them. Expected effect (estimate): -12 KB br (chunk 6064) and most of the non-swiper part of chunk 4907.
- **Checker's correction:** The Radix tooltip also opens on keyboard focus, not only on hover. It is touch users who can never trigger it. Chunk 6064 holds three modules, so '-12 KB br' for the whole chunk is an upper bound.

### PERF-08 - A third font family (Poppins 700) is downloaded for a single <strong>WhoAdvice</strong> in the affiliate disclosure
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/app/globals.css:1-4 import @fontsource Poppins 400/500/600/700 (12 @font-face rules including devanagari and latin-ext, in stylesheet 4dd1d96ba31433c9.css). The unlayered rule at globals.css ~363-379 `h1,...,h6, strong { font-family: var(--font-poppins), ... }` is overridden for headings by `.font-stitch :where(h1..h6)` (line ~389), but not for `strong`. In the browser pane: the only <strong> on the page is the disclosure's `<strong>WhoAdvice</strong> is reader-supported...` at y=1917. Its computed family is Poppins. Resource timing shows `/_next/static/media/poppins-latin-700-normal.9a881e2a.woff2` fetched (7,816 B decoded), not preloaded. document.fonts loaded: Poppins 700, Inter 100-900, Playfair Display 600. The body's own font-family (lines ~341-352) also names Poppins, but everything visible sits inside .font-stitch (Inter).
- **Why it matters:** It costs an extra font request and a late font swap for one word. The disclosure also renders in a different typeface from the rest of the page. The other 11 Poppins faces are dead CSS.
- **Fix:** Delete globals.css lines 1-4. In the body and `h1..h6, strong` rules, replace `var(--font-poppins)` and the literal `Poppins` with `var(--font-inter-family)`, and delete `--font-poppins` at line ~155. Remove @fontsource/poppins from package.json and regenerate the lockfile. Recheck in a browser: document.fonts must list only Inter and Playfair Display.
- **Checker's correction:** The fetch of poppins-latin-700 and the computed family came from the lead's browser session and were not re-run here. The CSS cascade in the code supports them.

### PERF-10 - Hero image: preload with no fetchpriority on desktop, and on phones it preloads a full-size image that is mostly below the fold
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** ArticleHero.tsx:158-165 uses `priority`. In Next 16.2.6, node_modules/next/dist/shared/lib/get-img-props.js:584 maps it to `preload: preload || priority`. fetchPriority is only emitted when passed explicitly. Saved HTML: the hero <img> has no loading and no fetchpriority attribute, and `<link rel="preload" as="image" href="...fa3045...-1448x1086.jpg?fit=max&auto=format"/>` has no imagesrcset or fetchpriority. On a 375x812 viewport the hero's top is at 714px, so only about 98px is visible. The excerpt <p> at top 301 and 161px tall is the likely mobile LCP element (inference: the hidden pane could not collect LCP entries). The text column comes first in source order (ArticleHero.tsx:72 before :155).
- **Why it matters:** On desktop the hero is the probable LCP and loses the priority boost. On phones a full-size preload competes with the Inter and Playfair preloads that render the real LCP text. Both effects are small but free to fix.
- **Fix:** Once the PERF-01 loader is in, change the hero to `<Image preload fetchPriority="high" sizes="(min-width:1024px) 592px, calc(100vw - 32px)" ...>` (drop the deprecated `priority`). Next then emits `imagesrcset` and `imagesizes` on the preload link, so a phone preloads a ~750w file (measured 10,904 B WebP at w=750) instead of the full image. Recheck: the preload link carries imagesrcset and the <img> has fetchpriority="high".
- **Checker's correction:** The w=750 hero measured 13,320 B WebP today, not 10,904 B. The mobile LCP element is still an inference from geometry. The facts file's 'Hero has fetchpriority' is wrong, and this finding correctly says so.

### PERF-11 - The category-page hero, the likely desktop LCP there, is lazy-loaded
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/Category/CategoryHeader.tsx:88-95 `<Image alt={details.title} className="w-full h-full object-cover" height={960} src={formatImage(urlForImage(heroImage).url())} width={1280} />` has no priority or preload. Saved category-skin-care.html: `<img alt="Skincare" loading="lazy" width="1280" height="960" ... ab7b16b832...-1000x1000.jpg?fit=max&auto=format"/>` (28,098 B AVIF measured). The page has no image preload, only the webpack and gtag preloads.
- **Why it matters:** A lazy LCP image waits for layout before it starts downloading. That is a textbook LCP regression on the 14 category hubs, which are the pages that pass link equity to the articles.
- **Fix:** In CategoryHeader.tsx add `preload fetchPriority="high" sizes="(min-width:1024px) 50vw, calc(100vw - 32px)"` to that Image. Recheck in the saved category HTML: no loading="lazy" on the hero, and a `rel=preload as=image` link is present.
- **Checker's correction:** Related: the category route is not ISR at all but dynamically rendered on every request (see PERF-V01). The saved category HTML also has no font preloads, which compounds the late LCP on these hubs.

### PERF-20 - Mobile page is 23,884px; the 7 product cards take 12,339px, and the first affiliate CTA sits 3 screens down
**medium** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Browser pane at 375x812, epilators page: document height 23,884. Product-card heights 1,670 / 1,831 / 1,821 / 1,738 / 1,696 / 1,696 / 1,887 px. The first card splits into an image+verdict column of 707px (image 353, verdict block 354) and a text column of 961px (header 300, pros/cons 393, CTA row 108). First `a[data-affiliate-placement]` at y=2,448. Comparison-table H2 at y=3,666. Footer 1,079px. ProductReviewCard.tsx: `my-12` (24), verdict wrapper `px-6 py-2 md:p-12` holding a `p-8 mb-12` box with `text-2xl` italic Playfair (44-50), header block `mb-12` with tagline `mb-8` (59-61), text column `p-8 md:p-12` (58).
- **Why it matters:** Scroll depth to the decision content is about 29 screens on a phone. Desktop-scale padding (32-48px) on a 343px column pushes CTAs and the comparison table far down. Affiliate revenue depends on readers reaching a CTA.
- **Fix:** In ProductReviewCard.tsx use mobile-first spacing: article `my-8 md:my-12`; text column `p-5 md:p-12`; header block `mb-6 md:mb-12`; tagline `mb-4 md:mb-8`; verdict box `p-5 md:p-8 mb-6 md:mb-12`; verdict text `text-lg md:text-2xl`. Remove the empty-padding wrapper `px-6 py-2` when there is no verdict. Consider moving the hero's See Top Picks/Compare buttons, which already exist, above the excerpt on phones. Expected effect (estimate): -250 to -400px per card, about -2,000 to -2,800px total, and the first CTA inside the second screen.
- **Checker's correction:** The per-card heights and first-CTA y=2,448 came from the lead's browser pane and were not re-measured here. The code confirms the desktop-scale spacing on phones. The pixel savings are estimates.

### PERF-05 - The 'unknown' /aadf8c3443d2e00c/script.js is Vercel Web Analytics, served through Vercel's first-party observability path
**low** · effort S · verified

It is 4,469 B, Cache-Control public, max-age=2678400, X-Vercel-Cache HIT. node_modules/@vercel/analytics 2.0.1 dist/next builds its script URL from `VERCEL_OBSERVABILITY_BASEPATH`. It is rendered by `<Analytics />` in src/app/(frontend)/layout.tsx:42. It does not appear in the saved HTML because the client component injects it after hydration.
- **Why it matters:** This closes an open question in the facts file: the script is not third-party or unknown code, so there is no security or trust issue. It is only the redundancy covered in PERF-04.

### PERF-09 - Playfair is declared twice but downloaded once; the @fontsource copy is dead CSS and the next/font face is variable
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** globals.css:5-6 import @fontsource Playfair 600/700: 8 @font-face rules in 4dd1d96ba31433c9.css, family 'Playfair Display'. next/font (layout.tsx:16-21) declares the same family name in a06cbc124c51dbfe.css, which loads later and wins. Only `eaead17c7dbfcd5d-s.p.woff2` (38,460 B, next/font, preloaded) was fetched. In a06cbc12, the latin weight-600 and weight-700 rules both point at the same file eaead17c7dbfcd5d-s.p.woff2, so it is a variable font.
- **Why it matters:** The facts file's 'double-loaded Playfair' costs CSS bytes, not a second font download. The variable face also means the typography lens's fix for the 400-weight heading bug (letting font-bold apply) adds no font bytes.
- **Fix:** Delete globals.css lines 5-6 and remove @fontsource/playfair-display from package.json (lockfile in the same step). Optionally set `weight: 'variable'` (or omit weight) in the Playfair_Display() call, so the declared range matches the file actually served.

### PERF-12 - The header logo and the author avatar are lazy-loaded above the fold, from oversized sources
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Saved epilators.html: `<img alt="WhoAdvice." loading="lazy" width="120" height="32" class="shrink-0 h-8 w-auto object-contain" src="...7f832506...-488x488.jpg?fit=max&auto=format"/>`, a square 488x488 JPEG in a 120x32 slot. The author avatar `loading="lazy" width="48" height="48"` comes from a 512x512 PNG (13,761 B AVIF measured) and sits at y≈300-400 on desktop (ArticleHero.tsx:90-96).
- **Why it matters:** Lazy loading makes both images wait for layout and paint late. The avatar is roughly 6x the bytes it needs. This is visible jank in the first viewport of every page.
- **Fix:** In the Header component, render the logo with `loading="eager"` (or `preload` on its Image). Better: serve it as a local SVG/PNG from /public (the facts show /images/logo-light.png exists) so it doesn't depend on the Sanity CDN. In ArticleHero.tsx:90 add `loading="eager"`. With the PERF-01 loader, the avatar's srcset becomes w=48/96 automatically.
