### PERF-13 - The 292 KB HTML is 55% RSC flight payload; markup is inflated by 46.8 KB of class attributes and 91 inline SVGs
**low** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** Parsed saved epilators.html (292,708 B): 60 `self.__next_f.push` scripts = 160,734 B (144,275 B decoded flight data, 125 rows). Markup without scripts = 116,446 B, of which class attributes = 46,806 B (745 attrs) and 91 inline <svg> = 30,392 B (lucide icons, 90 'lucide' mentions also in the RSC data). Each ProductReviewCard row in the flight data is about 4.4-4.5 KB (for example `43:["$","article","Philips Epilator Series 8000 BRE740/14-4",...` 4,493 B). Brotli brings the whole document to 29,566 B.
- **Why it matters:** Over the wire it is fine (29.6 KB br). The cost is parse and hydration on phones: about 145 KB of flight JSON plus 116 KB of HTML parsed on the main thread. Every repeated icon and long class list is paid twice, once in HTML and once in RSC.
- **Fix:** (a) Replace repeated lucide icons in ProsCons and CTA with one inline `<svg><symbol id="i-check">...` sprite in the layout, and `<svg><use href="#i-check"/></svg>` at each use. (b) Collapse the long class lists repeated per card (ProductReviewCard.tsx:24, 46, 61) into @utility or component classes in globals.css, e.g. `@utility review-card { ... }`. (c) Apply PERF-14. The RSC baseline itself is inherent to the App Router. Expected effect (estimate): -20 to -30% raw HTML. Treat it as polish after PERF-01/03/06.
- **Checker's correction:** Count is 59 flight scripts by my regex (60 if the bootstrap script is included). Severity lowered to low: the wire cost is about 29.6 KB br, and the parse-cost argument is an unmeasured estimate. The finder itself ranks this as polish.

### PERF-14 - JSON-LD ships twice (about 27 KB raw), and most of it is Product/Review markup the vault audit says not to use
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** Saved HTML: one `application/ld+json` script = 13,569 B. The identical string is also in the RSC payload as row `1e:T34d5,{"@context":"https://schema.org","@graph":[{"@type":"BlogPosting",...` (13,640 B), because PostJsonLD is rendered by a server component ([slug]/page.tsx:55) and its text is serialized into the flight data. `reviewBody` appears 7 times in the JSON-LD and 7 times in the RSC. The facts file quotes the vault audit for this article: "Product rich-result markup is not recommended for this multi-product roundup" (Article + BreadcrumbList + ItemList instead).
- **Why it matters:** About 9% of the raw document is structured data that is paid for twice and that the editorial pipeline itself recommends dropping. It adds parse cost with no rich-result upside: GSC's Search appearance export is empty.
- **Fix:** In src/app/(frontend)/[categorySlug]/[slug]/components/PostJsonLD.tsx, emit BlogPosting + BreadcrumbList + ItemList (name + url per product anchor) + FAQPage, and remove the 7 Product/Review nodes. The same schema change is owned by the SEO lens; this finding adds the byte cost. Expected effect (estimate): each copy shrinks from 13.6 KB to about 4-5 KB, about -18 KB raw per article page.
- **Checker's correction:** Severity lowered to low for this lens: the duplication costs about 27 KB raw, but only a few KB after brotli. The substantive schema change belongs to the SEO lens.

### PERF-15 - 18 affiliate CTAs are separate client islands wrapped in a context provider, only to attach a click handler
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** src/components/AffiliateLink.tsx:1 'use client', using useArticleAnalytics() (ArticleAnalyticsContext.tsx:1, 'use client'). [slug]/page.tsx:78-92 wraps the whole article body in `<ArticleAnalyticsProvider>`. The decoded RSC payload holds 10 `"placement"` and 10 `"productName"` client-prop entries. The facts file counts 18 CTAs rendered. The page chunk (page-c7304d4d4546316c.js) contains `affiliate_click`.
- **Why it matters:** Each CTA needs hydration before its onClick exists, so an early click fires no affiliate_click event. That undercounts the only conversion the site measures. The provider and island props also add hydration work.
- **Fix:** Render AffiliateLink as a plain server `<a href rel="sponsored noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" data-affiliate-placement data-product-name data-content-slug>`. Add one tiny client component `<AffiliateClickTracker />`, mounted once in the post page, that registers `document.addEventListener('click', e => { const a = (e.target as Element).closest('a[data-affiliate-placement]'); if (a) trackAffiliateClick({...a.dataset, linkUrl: a.href, pagePath: location.pathname}) }, { capture: true })`. Delete ArticleAnalyticsContext.tsx. Clicks before hydration are still missed, but there are 18 fewer islands and no provider.
- **Checker's correction:** There are not 18 separate islands. The 8 comparison-table CTAs render inside the ComparisonTable client component (one island). The 10 RSC client-prop entries match 10 standalone AffiliateLink islands (7 cta + 3 top_pick).

### PERF-16 - generateStaticParams returns [], so after every deploy each article renders cold on its first request, crawlers included
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** [slug]/page.tsx:38-40 `export async function generateStaticParams() { return []; }`. Live headers: Age 530,337 s, X-Nextjs-Prerender 1, X-Vercel-Cache HIT, so the entry was created around 2026-09-19 10:42 UTC. The site repo's last commit is 57a03a9, 2026-09-19 13:55 +0500 (08:55 UTC), about 1.8 h earlier, consistent with first-visit rendering after that deploy. A cold render of a legacy post runs getArticle() and then getPost() in series (src/lib/requests.ts:77-93).
- **Why it matters:** The first visitor, often Googlebot, to each of about 200 articles after a deploy pays a full Sanity round trip plus render. The Sanity query is a GET under the size limit, so generateMetadata and the page share it, but the article-then-post waterfall stays. Inference: Vercel's ISR cache is scoped per deployment, which the timing above supports but does not prove.
- **Fix:** Return real params from `getAllPosts()`, which already exists at src/lib/requests.ts:168-184 and selects `slug` + `categories[0]->{slug}`: `export async function generateStaticParams() { const all = await getAllPosts(); return (all ?? []).filter(p => p.categories?.slug?.current).map(p => ({ categorySlug: p.categories.slug.current, slug: p.slug.current })); }`. Keep dynamicParams at its default (true) so new articles still render on demand. Optionally merge getArticle and getPost into one GROQ query (`*[_type in ["article","post"] && slug.current == $slug]`) to remove the waterfall. Expected effect: no cold TTFB after deploys. About 223 pages at build is an acceptable build-time cost (estimate).
- **Checker's correction:** The per-deployment cold render is plausible but still inferred. 'Pays a full Sanity round trip' is overstated: sanityFetch sets `revalidate: false` on every tagged fetch (sanity/lib/fetch.ts:28-32), and Vercel's Data Cache is not per deployment (from memory of Vercel docs, not verified here), so a cold render typically reads cached data. Supporting evidence: the dynamically rendered category page answered in 0.74-0.76 s TTFB, no slower than the CDN-HIT article. Lowered to low.

### PERF-17 - Every publish invalidates the data cache for the whole site through global tags
**low** · effort M · verified

- **Evidence:** src/lib/revalidate-content.ts:43-51. revalidateContent() revalidates the tags content, post, article, category and author for a single article. Lines 108-113: revalidateProductRoutes() revalidates product, article, post and content globally, even though it also computes the dependent routes (115-128). Per-page fetches carry those global tags: getArticle `tags: [slug, CACHE_TAGS.article, CACHE_TAGS.product]` (requests.ts:43), getPost `[slug, CACHE_TAGS.post]` (66), and getSiteLayout is tagged with `content` (src/lib/requests/site-layout.ts:11).
- **Why it matters:** With `revalidateTag(tag, 'max')`, visitors still get stale-while-revalidate, so there is no user-facing stall. But each edit forces a re-render and Sanity re-fetch of every page on its next hit. That multiplies ISR writes and API calls by the page count and defeats the precise product-to-route mapping that was just built (commits of 2026-09-19).
- **Fix:** Tag per-page fetches only with document-specific tags: `article:<slug>` / `post:<slug>` plus `product:<_id>` for each referenced product (add product ids to the projection). Keep the global tags only on listing queries (getPosts, getSimilarPosts, getAllPosts). Then revalidateProductRoutes revalidates `product:<id>` and the computed routes, not the global set. Remove `content` from the getSiteLayout tags.

### PERF-18 - The 916 ms TTFB is mostly location and TLS, not the app: the HTML cache HIT is answered from iad1
**low** · effort S · likely

- **Evidence:** Three curl runs from the lead's location: connect 0.155 s, TLS done 0.59-0.77 s, TTFB 0.88-1.07 s, 29,566 B br. The HTML response has `X-Vercel-Id: bom1::iad1::...` with X-Vercel-Cache HIT. The static /aadf8c3443d2e00c/script.js returned `bom1:bom1:bom1`. Cache-Control on the HTML is `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate` (Next's ISR default on Vercel). X-Nextjs-Stale-Time 300 is the client router's staleTimes.static default, unrelated to CDN freshness.
- **Why it matters:** About 440 ms of the figure is TLS and about 300 ms is the Mumbai-to-iad1 hop. For the actual audience (US: 310 of 481 GSC impressions) iad1 is local. Optimizing server code against a Pakistan TTFB would waste effort.
- **Fix:** No code change. When the PageSpeed quota resets, take TTFB and LCP from CrUX/PSI (US field data) rather than local curl. If a non-US market ever matters, revisit Vercel's function region. The CDN and ISR setup is otherwise correct.
- **Checker's correction:** I did not re-run the lead's connect/TLS breakdown, so the 440 ms / 300 ms split stays an estimate. The 'CDN and ISR setup is otherwise correct' strength does not hold for category and author routes, which are served with private, no-store (PERF-V01).

Add `@source not '../app/studio'` and `@source not '../app/(admin)'` if the admin UI classes show up in 4dd1d96. Expected effect (estimate): 2 blocking stylesheets, about 12 KB raw less CSS.

### PERF-22 - The editorial pipeline has one unenforceable performance rule and no image spec
**low** · effort S · verified

- **Evidence:** The only performance item in the vault is //wsl.localhost/ubuntu/home/arif/code/whoadvice/reference/18-on-page-seo-checklist.md:122 `- [ ] Check Core Web Vitals and compress or lazy-load noncritical media.` The site repo's AGENTS.md has no image or performance rules (a grep for image/perform/font/lcp found only line 8, about preserving images). Live sources vary: 1254x1254 JPEG product shots, 1280x720 PNG legacy thumbnails, a 1672x941 PNG thumbnail, a 488x488 JPEG logo.
- **Why it matters:** Editors can't check Core Web Vitals from a Markdown checklist, and nobody owns the check. The code, not the editor, should guarantee image weight.
- **Fix:** Replace checklist line 122 with a publisher rule the handoff can verify: 'Upload photos as JPEG or WebP at ≥1200px on the long edge (hero 4:3, product 1:1); never PNG for photographs; the site resizes automatically.' Then enforce weight in code (PERF-01) and add a Playwright check, shown red against a deliberate break, that fails if any <img> on an article page lacks srcset or if more than one image is loaded eagerly.
- **Checker's correction:** This is not the only performance item in the vault. There are also 18-on-page-seo-checklist.md:100 ('Set width and height ... compress files, and use modern formats'), 13-article-production-guide.md:1134 ('Image dimensions and compression are appropriate.') and 12-seo-content-checklist.md:762 ('Compress and size images appropriately before upload.'). The defect stands in a different form: four vague, unowned image rules and no concrete upload spec.

### PERF-V03 - Category and author pages ship no font preloads, unlike every other route
**low** · effort S · likely · found by the checker

- **Evidence:** Count of `as="font"` in the saved HTML: epilators 2, home 2, about 2, face-epilators 2, hedge-trimmers 2, bug-zapper 2, but category-skin-care 0 and author-emily-cooper 0. A live fetch of /skin-care/ today also returned 0. The font hint exists only inside the flight data (`:HL["/_next/static/media/e4af272ccee01ff0-s.p.woff2","font",...]`), not as a <link> in <head>. These are exactly the two routes that are dynamically rendered (PERF-V01).
- **Why it matters:** Without the preload, Inter and Playfair are discovered only after the CSS is parsed. The hub H1 (Playfair) and body text swap later, adding LCP delay and a visible font swap on the category hubs.
- **Fix:** Expect this to resolve once PERF-V01 makes these routes static/ISR (inference: the preload loss correlates with dynamic streaming). Recheck the saved category HTML for two `rel="preload" as="font"` links after the change. If they are still missing, investigate why the (frontend) layout's next/font hints are not flushed into the head on these routes.
